. Now let us fix an arbitrary
T>0
, and define on
\bar{\Omega}^{\prime}
a new process
Z=(Z_{t})_{t\in[0,T]}
by the following formula

Z_{t}=\phi_{0,T}^{-1}\circ\phi_{t,T},\;\;t\in[0,T].

Note that on the
\mathbb{P}
-full event
\bar{\Omega}^{\prime}\cap\Omega_{0}^{T}
,
Z_{t}=\eta_{0,t}
, for every
t\in[0,T]
.
Arguing as the first part, one can prove that
Z_{t}
is the inverse of
\phi_{0,t}
on
\bar{\Omega}^{\prime}
, for every
t\in[0,T]
, and it is càdlàg.

Finally, assertion (iii) of Theorem
3.5
implies that
for every
\omega\in\bar{\Omega}^{\prime}
and every
t\in[0,\infty)
,
the mapping

\mathbb{R}^{d}\ni x\mapsto\phi_{0,t}(x)(\omega)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

is continuous.

The proof of this lemma is complete.
∎

Remark 3.7
.

In view of the applications to the stochastic transport equation, we note that, by the previous result, the function:

u(t,x,\omega)=u_{0}(\phi_{0,t}^{-1}(x)(\omega))

(3.25)

is well defined for every
t\in[0,\infty)
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\omega\in\bar{\Omega}^{\prime}
.

In the sequel, we also set

\phi_{0,t}(x)=\phi_{t}x=\phi_{t}(x),\;\;\;t\in[0,\infty),\;x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

(3.26)

Theorem 3.8
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
3.5
, there exists an
\mathbb{P}
-full event
\bar{\Omega}^{\prime}
such that,
for each
\omega\in\bar{\Omega}^{\prime}
,
s,t\geq 0
,
the mapping:

\mathbb{R}^{d}\ni x\mapsto\phi_{s,t}(x)(\omega)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

is a
surjective homeomorphism.

Moreover, on
\bar{\Omega}^{\prime}
, for every
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we have

\phi_{s,t}^{-1}(y)=y-\int_{s}^{t}b(\phi_{r,t}^{-1}(y))dr-(L_{t}-L_{s}),\;\;0\leq s\leq t,

(3.27)

and

\phi_{s,t}^{-1}(y)=y,\;\;0\leq t\leq s.

Finally, for every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},

t\in[0,\infty)
, the mapping:
s\mapsto\phi_{s,t}^{-1}(x)
is càdlàg on
[0,\infty)
and,
for every
s\in[0,\infty)
, the mapping:
t\mapsto\phi_{s,t}^{-1}(x)
is càdlàg on
[0,\infty)
.

Before we embark on the proof of this result, let us formulate a simple but useful corollary of it.

Corollary 3.9
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
3.5
, there exists an almost sure event
\Omega^{\prime\prime}\subset\bar{\Omega}^{\prime}
such that,
for each
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime\prime}
and
T>0
there exists
C(\omega)>0
(may dependent on
T
) such that the following inequality holds

|\phi_{s,t}^{-1}(y)-y|\leq\|b\|_{0}|t-s|+C(\omega),\;\;y\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\;0\leq s\leq t\leq T.

(3.28)

Proof.

It is enough to observe that since almost surely the trajectory
L:[0,T]\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
is càdlàg,
there exists a
\mathbb{P}
-full event
\Omega^{\prime\prime}\subset\bar{\Omega}^{\prime}
such that for
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime\prime}
,

C(\omega):=\sup\{|L_{t}(\omega)-L_{s}(\omega)|:0\leq s\leq t\leq T\}
is finite.
∎

Proof of Theorem
3.8
.

It is easy to see that the case with
0\leq t\leq s
holds. In the following we prove the case with
0\leq s\leq t
.

By Lemma
3.6
,
\phi_{s}^{-1},s\geq 0
are well defined random variables on the same
\mathbb{P}
-full event
\bar{\Omega}^{\prime}
and
on
\bar{\Omega}^{\prime}
, for every
s\in[0,t]
, we have

\phi_{s}^{-1}=\phi_{0,t}^{-1}\circ\phi_{s,t}=\phi_{t}^{-1}\circ\phi_{s,t}.

(3.29)

It follows that
\phi_{s,t}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
is also a
surjective homeomorphism and

\phi_{s,t}=\phi_{t}\circ\phi_{s}^{-1},\;\;\;\;\phi_{s,t}^{-1}=\phi_{s}\circ\phi_{t}^{-1}.

(3.30)

Finally, since by Theorem
3.5
on
\Omega^{\prime}(\supset\bar{\Omega}^{\prime})
we have:

\phi_{s,t}(x)=x+\int_{s}^{t}b(\phi_{s,r}(x))\,dr+(L_{t}-L_{s}),

(3.31)

setting
x=\phi_{s,t}^{-1}(y)
we find

y=\phi_{s,t}^{-1}(y)+\int_{s}^{t}b(\phi_{s,r}\circ\phi_{s,t}^{-1}(y))\,dr+(L_{t}-L_{s}),

(3.32)

and using that

\phi_{s,r}\circ\phi_{s,t}^{-1}=[\phi_{r}\circ\phi_{s}^{-1}]\circ[\phi_{s}\circ\phi_{t}^{-1}]=\phi_{r}\circ\phi_{t}^{-1}=\phi_{r,t}^{-1},

(3.33)

we get (
3.27
). The regularity properties of
\phi_{s,t}^{-1}(x)
can be deduced by (
3.30
).
∎

3.2
The differentiability property

Here we prove the existence of a
\mathbb{P}
-full event
\Omega^{\prime}
(independent of
t
) related to the differentiability of the solutions with respect to
x
, see part (iii) of Theorem
3.1
and Theorem
3.2
.

In below we use the following notation. If
u=(u^{k})_{k=1}^{d}
, then we put

b\cdot Du:=(\sum_{j=1}^{d}b_{j}D_{j}u^{k})_{k=1}^{d}\mbox{ and }{\mathscr{A}}u:=({\mathscr{A}}u^{k})_{k=1}^{d}.

(3.34)

Recall that
{\mathscr{A}}
is introduced in Definition
2.3
.

In what follows we will use the following result, which is a special case of
[
8
, Corollary 2.54]
, see also
[
20
, Corollary 2.2]
.

Lemma 3.10
.

If
\theta\in[0,1]
, then the following inequality (with constant
1
on the RHS) holds

\displaystyle\|fg\|_{\theta}\leq\|f\|_{0}\|g\|_{\theta}+\|g\|_{0}[f]_{\theta},\ \ \forall f,g\in C_{\mathrm{b}}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

(3.35)

Theorem
3.11
below holds under weaker assumptions than Theorem
3.1
. Indeed, if condition (
2.25
) is satisfied, then there exists
\beta^{\prime}\in(1-\frac{\alpha}{2},\beta]
such that condition (
3.36
) with
\beta
replaced by
\beta^{\prime}
is satisfied as well.
This result is a slight reformulation of
[
77
, Theorem 4.3]
and
[
78
, Theorem 6.7 and 6.11]
. Recall that the proof of
[
78
, Theorem 6.11]
is based on
[
86
]
(an extension of
[
86
]
is given in
[
28
]
). Assertion (
3.38
) follows from Theorem 4.3 in
[
77
]
, whose proof is the same as of
[
76
, Proposition 3.2 and Theorem 3.4]
.

Please note that our assertion about the constant
c(\lambda_{0},r)
is new which can be proven by carefully examining the proof and checking the constants given in
[
78
, Theorem 6.7 and 6.11]
.

Theorem 3.11
.

Assume that
\alpha\in[1,2)
or
\alpha\in(0,1)
. Assume that
L=(L_{t})_{t\geq 0}
is a Lévy process satisfying
(H)
and
(R)
in Hypothesis
1
in the former case or Hypothesis
3
in the latter case. Assume also that
\beta\in(0,1]
is such that

1<\alpha+\beta<2.

(3.36)

Assume that
b\in C_{\mathrm{b}}^{\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

Then the following assertions hold.

(I)

For every

\lambda\geq 1
and
f\in C^{\beta}_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
there exists a unique

u=u_{\lambda}(f)\in C^{\alpha+\beta}_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})
which is a solution to the following uncoupled system of linear equations

\lambda u-{\mathscr{A}}u-b\cdot Du=f\;\;\mbox{ on }\mathbb{R}^{d}.

(3.37)

These solutions have the following two properties

\displaystyle\lambda\|u_{\lambda}\|_{0}\leq\|f\|_{0},

(3.38)

and

\displaystyle\lim_{\lambda\to\infty}\|Du_{\lambda}\|_{0}=0.

(3.39)

(II)

For every
\lambda_{0}\geq 1
,
there exists
r_{0}>0
and a continuous function

(0,\infty)\ni r\mapsto c(\lambda_{0},r)\in(0,\infty)

(M1)

which is constant on
(0,r_{0}]
and increasing on
[r_{0},\infty)

such that for every
\lambda\geq\lambda_{0}
, every
r>0
and every

b\in C^{\beta}_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})
:
\|b\|_{\beta}\leq r
, the unique element
u_{\lambda}
introduced in Part I above satisfies

\lambda\|u_{\lambda}(f)\|_{0}+[Du_{\lambda}(f)]_{\alpha+\beta-1}\leq c(\lambda_{0},r)\|f\|_{\beta},\;\;f\in C^{\beta}_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d}).

(3.40)

(III)

For every
\varepsilon>0
and every
M>0
there exist constants
\lambda_{0}{}_{,M}\geq 1
and
C_{M}>0
such that if
\lambda\geq\lambda_{0}{}_{,M}
and a vector field
b\in C^{\beta}_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})
satisfies
\|b\|_{\beta}\leq M
,
the unique solution
u_{\lambda}\in C_{\mathrm{b}}^{\alpha+\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})
of the equation (
3.37
)
satisfies the following two conditions

\displaystyle\|Du_{\lambda}\|_{0}\leq\varepsilon\|f\|_{\beta},

(3.41)

\displaystyle\|u_{\lambda}\|_{\alpha+\beta}\leq C_{M}\|f\|_{\beta}.

(3.42)

Remark 3.12
.

If
b=0
, the previous result implies that for every

f\in C^{\beta}_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
the solution
u_{\lambda}
of equation (
3.37
) belongs to the space
C^{\alpha+\beta}_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})
. This means that the resolvent operator
(\lambda I-{\mathscr{A}})^{-1}
maps the space
C^{\beta}_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})
into
the space
C^{\alpha+\beta}_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})
(and as such is bounded). But we do not claim that this operator is surjective. In fact, we do not know whether this is true.

Remark 3.13
.

Note that the Schauder estimates of the previous theorem require that
f
and
b
have the same regularity.
We do not know whether the above results can be formulated in terms of Besov spaces, see the recent paper
[
27
]
for a related result when
\alpha>1
.

Remark 3.14
.

One may wonder why do we assume that
\alpha+\beta<2
and exclude the case when
\alpha+\beta\leq 2
? Indeed, the statement of the theorem would still make sense.
However, in that case we would need to consider the class
C^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
in the Zygmund sense, i.e., there exists
c>0
such that for every
\gamma\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
with
|\gamma|=2
,

|D^{\gamma}g(x+h)+D^{\gamma}g(x-h)-2D^{\gamma}g(x)|\leq c|h|^{2},\;\;x,h\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

(3.43)

This case requires further analysis. For our purposes, the statement as above is completely sufficient.

The following result is taken from
[
77
, Lemma 5.2]
.

Lemma 3.15
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
3.11
,
if
\lambda\geq 1
and

u=u_{\lambda}\in C_{\mathrm{b}}^{\alpha+\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})

is the unique solution
of equation (
3.37
) with the RHS
f=b
, then
the following Itô formula holds for all
t\in[0,\infty)
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.,

\displaystyle u(\phi_{t}(x))-u(x)=

\displaystyle L_{t}-\bigl(\phi_{t}(x)-x\bigr)+\lambda\int_{0}^{t}u(\phi_{s}(x))\,ds

(3.44)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}[u(\phi_{s-}(x)+z)-u(\phi_{s-}(x))]\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz),

where
\phi_{t}(x)
,
t\geq 0
, is the solution of (
2.34
) with
s=0
, i.e., with vector field
b
, starting at
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

The Itô formula can also be written in the following form, with
I:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
being the identity map,

\displaystyle(u+I)(\phi_{t}(x))-(u+I)(x)=

\displaystyle L_{t}+\lambda\int_{0}^{t}u(\phi_{s}(x))\,ds

(3.45)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}[u(\phi_{s-}(x)+z)-u(\phi_{s-}(x))]\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz).

Theorem 3.16
.

Suppose that the assumptions of Theorem
3.11
are satisfied. Assume that
(b^{n})_{n=0}^{\infty}
and
(f^{n})_{n=0}^{\infty}
are two
C_{\mathrm{b}}^{\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})
-valued
sequences of vector fields such that

b^{n}\to b^{0}\mbox{ and }f^{n}\to f^{0}\mbox{ in }C_{\mathrm{b}}^{\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d}).

(3.46)

Let, for all
n\geq 0
and
\lambda\geq 1
,
u^{n}_{\lambda}\in C_{\mathrm{b}}^{\alpha+\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})
be the unique solution to equation (
3.37
) with the vector field
b
replaced by the vector field
b^{n}

and
f
replaced by
f^{n}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\lambda u^{n}_{\lambda}-{\mathscr{A}}u^{n}_{\lambda}-b^{n}\cdot Du^{n}_{\lambda}=f^{n}\;\;\mbox{ on }\mathbb{R}^{d}.

(3.47)

Then, there exists
\lambda_{0}\geq 1
and
R>0
such that

\sup_{\lambda\geq\lambda_{0}}\sup_{n\geq 0}\|u^{n}_{\lambda}\|_{\alpha+\beta}\leq R\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\sup_{\lambda\geq\lambda_{0}}\sup_{n\geq 0}\|Du^{n}_{\lambda}\|_{0}\leq\frac{1}{3},

(3.48)

\lim_{n\to\infty}\|u^{n}_{\lambda}-u^{0}_{\lambda}\|_{0}=0,

(3.49)

and the following results hold:
for any compact set
K\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
0<\beta^{\prime}<\beta
,

\lim_{n\to\infty}\|u^{n}_{\lambda}-u^{0}_{\lambda}\|_{\alpha+\beta^{\prime},K}=0.

(3.50)

Note that (
3.50
) implies that, for any compact set
K\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{x\in K}|Du^{n}_{\lambda}(x)-Du^{0}_{\lambda}(x)|=0.

(3.51)

This convergence will be useful in the sequel.

Theorem 3.17
.

Assume the framework of Theorem
3.16
.
If
f^{n}=b^{n}
for some
n\in\mathbb{N}
, then

u^{n}_{\lambda}
satisfies the following Itô formula.
For all
t\in[0,\infty)
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s., we have,

\displaystyle u^{n}_{\lambda}(\phi^{n}_{t}(x))-u^{n}_{\lambda}(x)=

\displaystyle x+L_{t}-\phi^{n}_{t}(x)+\lambda\int_{0}^{t}u^{n}_{\lambda}(\phi^{n}_{s}(x))\,ds

(3.52)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}[u^{n}_{\lambda}(\phi^{n}_{s-}(x)+z)-u^{n}_{\lambda}(\phi^{n}_{s-}(x))]\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz),

where
\phi^{n}_{t}(x)
,
t\geq 0
is the solution of (
2.34
) with vector field
b^{n}
, starting at
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
s=0
.

Proof of Theorem
3.16
.

We only need to prove claims (
3.48
), (
3.49
) and (
3.50
).
Since by assumption we can find
M>0
such that

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\|b^{n}\|_{\beta}+\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\|f^{n}\|_{\beta}\leq M.

(3.53)

Hence assertion (
3.48
) follows from Theorem
3.11
. Thus it remains to prove (
3.49
) and (
3.50
).

Let us fix
\lambda_{0}\geq 1
and
R>0
from (
3.48
). Taking the difference between the equation (
3.47
) for
u^{n}_{\lambda}
and
u^{0}_{\lambda}
we get, for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle(\lambda I-{\mathscr{A}})(u^{n}_{\lambda}-u^{0}_{\lambda})-b^{n}\cdot D(u^{n}_{\lambda}-u^{0}_{\lambda})=f^{n}-f^{0}+(b^{n}-b^{0})\cdot Du^{0}_{\lambda}.

(3.54)

By the maximum principle (
3.38
) we know that

\lambda\|u^{n}_{\lambda}-u^{0}_{\lambda}\|_{0}\leq\|f^{n}-f^{0}+(b^{n}-b^{0})\cdot Du^{0}_{\lambda}\|_{0}\to 0

(3.55)

as
n\to\infty
. Then we have (
3.49
).

Note that when
\alpha<1
we cannot deduce that the RHS of (
3.54
) converges to
0

in the space
C_{\mathrm{b}}^{\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})
. This is because in general the term
(b^{n}-b^{0})\cdot Du^{0}_{\lambda}
only belongs to
C_{\mathrm{b}}^{\alpha+\beta-1}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})
; in general this term is not in
C_{\mathrm{b}}^{\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})
, see also Remark
3.18
.

Now we use a useful property of Hölder spaces , see page 37 in
[
56
]
and page 431 in
[
76
]
) together with (
3.48
) and (
3.55
).
Let us fix
\lambda\geq\lambda_{0}
and a compact set
K\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Let us consider the Hölder space

C^{\alpha+\beta^{\prime}}(K,\mathbb{R}^{d})
for any
0<\beta^{\prime}<\beta
.
Let us consider any subsequence
u^{k}_{\lambda}

of
u^{n}_{\lambda}
. By (
3.48
), we have

\sup_{\lambda\geq\lambda_{0}}\sup_{n\geq 0}\|u^{n}_{\lambda}\|_{\alpha+\beta}\leq R.

Hence,
there exists a function
v_{\lambda}\in C^{\alpha+\beta}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
a further subsequence of
u^{k}_{\lambda}
still denoted by
u^{k}_{\lambda}
, possibly depending on
\lambda
and
K
, such that

u^{k}_{\lambda}
converges to
v_{\lambda}

in
C^{\alpha+\beta^{\prime}}(K,\mathbb{R}^{d})
. This function
v_{\lambda}
coincides with
u^{0}_{\lambda}
by (
3.55
). This implies that the whole sequence
u^{n}_{\lambda}
converges to
u^{0}_{\lambda}
in
C^{\alpha+\beta^{\prime}}(K,\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
This proves (
3.50
). The proof of the theorem is complete.
∎

Remark 3.18
.

Note that
if
\alpha\in[1,2)
, then we have the following stronger result:

\lim_{n\to\infty}\|u^{n}_{\lambda}-u^{0}_{\lambda}\|_{\alpha+\beta}=0.

(3.56)

The proof follows using (
3.54
) since in this case
(b^{n}-b^{0})\cdot Du^{0}_{\lambda}
belongs to
C_{\mathrm{b}}^{\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
By assumptions (
3.46
) and inequality (
3.35
), we deduce that the RHS of (
3.54
) converges to
0

in the space
C_{\mathrm{b}}^{\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. In view of the bound (
3.53
), by part (III) of Theorem

3.11
we infer (
3.56
).

In the next result, we will prove a stronger assertion than assertions (iii) in Theorem
3.1
and (iii) in Theorem
3.2
, see also Remark
3.4
.
This result seems to be new even for Lipschitz
b
, see
[
61
]
. Let us emphasize that the results in Kunita’s paper
[
61
]
are not sufficient for our purposes because he didn’t prove the existence of a “universal”
\mathbb{P}
-full set
\Omega^{\prime}
as we did. See also Remark
1.8
.

Theorem 3.19
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
3.5
,
there exists a
\mathbb{P}
-full event
\Omega^{\prime}
such that,
for every
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime}

for every
t\in[0,\infty)
,
the mapping (
3.2
) , i.e.,

\mathbb{R}^{d}\ni x\mapsto\phi_{t}(x)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

(3.57)

is differentiable;

the derivative function

[0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\ni(t,x)\mapsto D\phi_{t}(x)\in\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})

is continuous;

for any
0<\delta<\frac{\alpha}{2}+\beta-1
,
the derivative function

\mathbb{R}^{d}\ni x\mapsto D\phi_{t}(x)\in\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})

(3.58)

is locally
\delta
-Hölder continuous, locally uniformly in
t\in[0,\infty)
.

Proof of Theorem
3.19
.

Since Theorem
3.5
is given, see also Remark
1.8
, we
argue, with important modifications, as in Section 3 of Kunita
[
61
]
.
Indeed, the Itô formula
(
3.44
) is similar to a classical SDE with regular coefficients considered
in
[
61
, Section 3.3]
. However, applying directly the results from
[
61
]
, we can only assert that
the map (
3.57
) is differentiable on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
on some almost sure event
\Omega_{t}^{\prime}
which may depend on
t
.

Let us first define an auxiliary set
\mathscr{O}
by

\mathscr{O}:=\mathbb{R}^{d}\times([-1,0)\cup(0,1]).

(3.59)

Let us next define an auxiliary random field
N

\displaystyle N_{t}(x,\lambda)

\displaystyle=\frac{\phi_{t}(x+\lambda e_{i})-\phi_{t}(x)}{\lambda},\quad(t,x,\lambda)\in[0,\infty)\times\mathscr{O}.

(3.60)

By looking at our initial SDE (
2.34
), see also (
3.9
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\phi_{t}(x+\lambda e_{i})-\phi_{t}(x)

\displaystyle=\lambda e_{i}+\int_{0}^{t}[b(\phi_{s}(x+\lambda e_{i}))-b(\phi_{s}(x))]ds+L_{t}-L_{t}

(3.61)

\displaystyle=\lambda e_{i}+\int_{0}^{t}[b(\phi_{s}(x+\lambda e_{i}))-b(\phi_{s}(x))]ds,

from which we deduce that the paths on
\Omega^{\prime}
(from Theorem
3.5
)

[0,\infty)\ni t\mapsto N_{t}(x,\lambda)\;\;\text{are continuous}.

(3.62)

This fact will be important in the sequel. Here the situation is different from the more general case considered in
[
61
]
in which the process
N_{\cdot}(x,\lambda)
had only a.s. càdlàg paths.

Thus we can view
N(x,\lambda)
as a random variable with values in the separable Fréchet space
C([0,\infty),\mathbb{R}^{d})
whereas the author of
[
61
]
was unable to view
N(x,\lambda)
as a random variable with values in the Fréchet space of all càdlàg paths endowed with the local supremum norm, because such space is not separable.

We choose and fix numbers
T>0
.
Let us define an auxiliary parameter

\gamma=\alpha+\beta-1.

(3.63)

Let us observe that due to assumtion (
2.25
), there exists
\delta
satisfying the condition below.

0<\delta<\frac{\alpha}{2}+\beta-1<\gamma.

(3.64)

In what follows we choose and fix such parameter
\delta
.
Moreover, condition (
2.25
) implies that
\gamma>\frac{\alpha}{2}
and the number

\gamma^{\prime}:=\gamma-\delta\in(\frac{\alpha}{2},\gamma).

(3.65)

Therefore, by Hypothesis
(H)
and the definition of the Blumenthal-Getoor index we infer that

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{p\gamma^{\prime}}\nu(dz)<\infty,\quad\mbox{ for }p\geq 2.

(3.66)

Note that in particular inequality (
3.66
) holds for
p=2
and we use this fact.

To continue with the proof of Theorem
3.19
, following the approach used in proof of
[
61
, Theorem 3.3]
, let us first formulate two important results that are essential to the proof.

Proposition 3.20
.

Assume that
N
is the random field defined in
(
3.60
).
For every
p\geq 2
, there exists
C_{p}=C(T,\alpha,\beta,\nu,p,d)>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}|N_{s}(x,\lambda)|^{p}]

\displaystyle\leq C_{p},\;\;\;\mbox{for all $(x,\lambda)\in\mathscr{O}$}.

(3.67)

Proposition 3.21
.

Assume that
N
is the random field defined in
(
3.60
) and
\delta
is as in (
3.64
). Then, for every
p\geq 2
, there exists
C_{p}=C(T,\alpha,\beta,\nu,p,d)>0
such that
for all
(x,\lambda),(x^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime})\in\mathscr{O}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}|N_{s}(x,\lambda)-N_{s}(x^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime})|^{p}]

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}(|x-x^{\prime}|^{\delta p}+|\lambda-\lambda^{\prime}|^{\delta p}).

(3.68)

Assuming for the time being validity of Proposition
3.21
, we deduce that

{\mathbb{E}}[\|N_{\cdot}(x,\lambda)-N_{\cdot}(x^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime})\|^{p}_{C([0,T],\mathbb{R}^{d})}]\leq C_{p}(|x-x^{\prime}|^{\delta p}+|\lambda-\lambda^{\prime}|^{\delta p}),\quad(x,\lambda),(x^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime})\in\mathscr{O}.

(3.69)

Choosing
p
large enough and then applying the Kolmogorov Test, see e.g.
[
61
, Appendix]
, we deduce
that there exists an
C([0,T],\mathbb{R}^{d})
-valued continuous random field
\tilde{N}
defined on the closure
\bar{\mathscr{O}}=\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[-1,1]
of the set
\mathscr{O}
,
such that for every
(x,\lambda)\in\mathscr{O}
,
N(x,\lambda)=\tilde{N}(x,\lambda)
,
\mathbb{P}
-almost surely. Hence we obtain
the differentiability with respect to
x
of the process
\phi_{t}(x)
uniformly in
t\in[0,T]
with
D_{i}\phi_{t}(x)=\tilde{N}_{t}(x,0)
. Moreover,
we deduce that the process
D_{i}\phi_{t}(x)
is continuous w.r.t.
(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
. More precisely,
we get that,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s., for every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
i=1,\ldots,d,

\lim_{\lambda\to 0}\big\|\frac{\phi_{\cdot}(x+\lambda e_{i})-\phi_{\cdot}(x)}{\lambda}-D_{i}\phi_{\cdot}(x)\big\|_{C([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{d})}=0.

(3.70)

Moreover, since
\delta
can be any element in
(0,\frac{\alpha}{2}+\beta-1)
,
D_{i}\phi_{\cdot}(x)
is locally
\delta
-Hölder continuous w.r.t.
x
with values in
C([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{d})
, for any
0<\delta=\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}<\frac{\alpha}{2}+\beta-1
.

To finish the proof of Theorem
3.19
, it remains to prove Propositions
3.20
and
3.21
.

In the following proofs the constants
C
or
c
can change values from line to line. They may only depend on
\alpha
,
\beta
,
\nu
,
p,

d
and
T
.
We also choose and fix
\lambda_{0}>\lambda_{0}{}_{,M}
and
\varepsilon=\frac{1}{3M}
, where
M=\|b\|_{\beta}
and
\lambda_{0}{}_{,M}
is introduced in (III) of Theorem
3.11
. Set
u=u_{\lambda_{0}}
be the unique solution
of equation (
3.37
) with the RHS
f=b
and
\lambda=\lambda_{0}
. Hence, (III) of Theorem
3.11
implies that

\|Du\|_{0}\leq\frac{1}{3}\text{ and }\|u\|_{\alpha+\beta}\leq C_{M}M<\infty.

(3.71)

Proof of Proposition
3.20
.

Let us choose and fix
p\in[2,\infty)
and
(x,\lambda)\in\mathscr{O}
and
i\in\{1,\cdots,d\}
. Set

Y(t;x,\lambda)=N_{t}(x,\lambda)=\frac{\phi_{t}(x+\lambda e_{i})-\phi_{t}(x)}{\lambda}.

(3.72)

For simplicity of presentation, we write
Y(t)
for
Y(t;x,\lambda)
.
Let us recall that by (
3.44
), with
u=u_{\lambda_{0}}
we have

\displaystyle\phi_{t}(x+\lambda e_{i})=

\displaystyle x+\lambda e_{i}+L_{t}-[u(\phi_{t}(x+\lambda e_{i}))-u(x+\lambda e_{i})]+\lambda_{0}\int_{0}^{t}u(\phi_{s}(x+\lambda e_{i}))\,ds

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}[u(\phi_{s-}(x+\lambda e_{i})+z)-u(\phi_{s-}(x+\lambda e_{i}))]\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz),

(3.73)

and

\displaystyle\phi_{t}(x)=

\displaystyle x+L_{t}-[u(\phi_{t}(x))-u(x)]+\lambda_{0}\int_{0}^{t}u(\phi_{s}(x))\,ds

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}[u(\phi_{s-}(x)+z)-u(\phi_{s-}(x))]\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz).

(3.74)

Thus we have

\displaystyle Y(t;x,\lambda)=

\displaystyle e_{i}-\frac{1}{\lambda}[u(\phi_{t}(x+\lambda e_{i}))-u(x+\lambda e_{i})-u(\phi_{t}(x))+u(x)]

(3.75)

\displaystyle+\frac{\lambda_{0}}{\lambda}\int_{0}^{t}[u(\phi_{s}(x+\lambda e_{i}))-u(\phi_{s}(x))]ds+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}Z(s,x,z,\lambda)\,\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz),

(3.76)

where, for
s\geq 0
and
(x,\lambda)\in\mathscr{O}
,

\displaystyle Z(s,x,z,\lambda):=\frac{1}{\lambda}\Big[u(\phi_{s-}(x+\lambda e_{i})\!+\!z)\!-\!u(\phi_{s-}(x\!+\!\lambda e_{i}))\!-\!u(\phi_{s-}(x)\!+\!z)\!+\!u(\phi_{s-}(x))\Big].

(3.77)

Since by (
3.71
)
\|Du\|_{0}\leq\frac{1}{3}
, i.e., the Lipschitz constant of
u
is
\leq\frac{1}{3}
, we infer that

\displaystyle\Bigl|\frac{1}{\lambda}[u(\phi_{t}(x+\lambda e_{i}))-u(x+\lambda e_{i})-u(\phi_{t}(x))+u(x)]\Bigr|

(3.78)

\displaystyle\leq\Bigl|\frac{1}{\lambda}[u(\phi_{t}(x+\lambda e_{i}))-u(\phi_{t}(x))]\Bigr|+\Bigl|\frac{1}{\lambda}[u(x+\lambda e_{i})-u(x)]\Bigr|\leq\frac{1}{3}(|Y(t)|+1).

(3.79)

It follows, since
p\geq 2
, that for every
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle|Y(t)|^{p}\leq c_{p}+c_{p}\int_{0}^{t}|Y(r)|^{p}dr+c_{p}\Bigl|\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}Z(s,x,z,\lambda)\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)\Big|^{p}.

(3.80)

Now, we estimate the stochastic integral as in the proof of Theorem 4.3 in
[
76
]
. Using (
2.40
) and treating separately the small jumps part and the large jumps part, we obtain

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\Bigl|\int_{0}^{s}\int_{B^{c}}Z(r,x,z,\lambda)\tilde{\mu}(dr,dz)\Big|^{p}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}|Z(s,x,z,\lambda)|^{2}\nu(dz)ds\Big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\Big]+C_{p}\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}|Z(s,x,z,\lambda)|^{p}\nu(dz)ds\Big],

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\Bigl|\int_{0}^{s}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}Z(r,x,z,\lambda)\tilde{\mu}(dr,dz)\Big|^{p}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|Z(s,x,z,\lambda)|^{2}\nu(dz)ds\Big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\Big]+C_{p}\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|Z(s,x,z,\lambda)|^{p}\nu(dz)ds\Big].

In view of the fact that
|Z(s,x,z,\lambda)|\leq 2\|Du\|_{0}|Y(s-)|\leq\frac{2}{3}|Y(s-)|
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\Bigl|\int_{0}^{s}\int_{B^{c}}Z(r,x,z,\lambda)\tilde{\mu}(dr,dz)\Big|^{p}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}(1+t^{\frac{p}{2}-1})\Big[\int_{B^{c}}\nu(dz)+\Big(\int_{B^{c}}\nu(dz)\Big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\Big]\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq r}|Y(s)|^{p}dr,

where we also used the Hölder inequality.

By applying Lemma
2.1
, we infer
|Z(s,x,z,\lambda)|\leq\|u\|_{1+\gamma}|Y(s-)||z|^{\gamma}
with
\gamma=\alpha+\beta-1
, see (
3.63
), and hence we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\Bigl|\int_{0}^{s}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}Z(r,x,z,\lambda)\tilde{\mu}(dr,dz)\Big|^{p}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}\|u\|_{1+\gamma}^{p}\Big[\mathbb{E}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|Y_{s}|^{2}|z|^{2\gamma}\nu(dz)ds\Big)^{\frac{p}{2}}+\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|Y_{s}|^{p}|z|^{\gamma p}\nu(dz)ds\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}(1+t^{\frac{p}{2}-1})\|u\|_{1+\gamma}^{p}\Big[\Big(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{2\gamma}\nu(dz)\Big)^{\frac{p}{2}}+\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{p\gamma}\nu(dz)\Big]\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq r}|Y(s)|^{p}dr,

where
\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{2\gamma}\nu(dz)+\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{p\gamma}\nu(dz)<+\infty
, since
2\gamma>\alpha
and
p\gamma>\alpha
.
Combining the above estimates, finally, we obtain

\mathbb{E}[\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}|Y(s)|^{p}]\leq c_{p}+c_{p}(1+t^{\frac{p}{2}-1})\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}[\sup_{0\leq r\leq s}|Y(r)|^{p}]ds,\;\;\;\;t\in[0,T],

(3.81)

for some constant
c_{p}>0
independent of
x
and
\lambda
. Applying the Gronwall lemma we obtain (
3.67
).
Hence the proof of Proposition
3.20
is complete.
∎

Proof of Proposition
3.21
.

Let us choose and fix
p\in[2,\infty)
and
(x,\lambda),(x^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime})\in\mathscr{O}
.
We consider
a continuous process
\hat{X}
defined by

\hat{X}(t)=\hat{X}(t;x,\lambda,x^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime})=N_{t}(x,\lambda)-N_{t}(x^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime}),\;\;\;t\geq 0,

(3.82)

where
N
is the random field defined in (
3.60
).

Then, by (
3.2
) and (
3.2
), we have

\displaystyle\hat{X}(t)=J_{1}(t)+J_{2}(t)+J_{31}(t)+J_{32}(t),\;\;t\geq 0,

where

\displaystyle J_{1}(t)

\displaystyle=\!-\frac{1}{\lambda}[u(\phi_{t}(x+\lambda e_{i}))-u(x+\lambda e_{i})-u(\phi_{t}(x))+u(x)]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+\frac{1}{\lambda^{\prime}}[u(\phi_{t}(x^{\prime}+\lambda^{\prime}e_{i}))-u(x^{\prime}+\lambda^{\prime}e_{i})-u(\phi_{t}(x^{\prime}))+u(x^{\prime})],

\displaystyle J_{2}(t)

\displaystyle=\lambda_{0}\int_{0}^{t}\Bigl[\frac{u(\phi_{s}(x+\lambda e_{i}))-u(\phi_{s}(x))}{\lambda}-\frac{u(\phi_{s}(x^{\prime}+\lambda^{\prime}e_{i}))-u(\phi_{s}(x^{\prime}))}{\lambda^{\prime}}\Big]ds,

\displaystyle J_{31}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{{\mathrm{c}}}}H(s-,z,x,x^{\prime},\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz),

\displaystyle J_{32}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}H(s-,z,x,x^{\prime},\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz),

and where

\displaystyle H(s,z,x,x^{\prime},\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\lambda}[u(\phi_{s}(x+\lambda e_{i})+z)-u(\phi_{s}(x+\lambda e_{i}))-u(\phi_{s}(x)+z)+u(\phi_{s}(x))]

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{\lambda^{\prime}}[u(\phi_{s}(x^{\prime}+\lambda^{\prime}e_{i})+z)-u(\phi_{s}(x^{\prime}+\lambda^{\prime}e_{i}))-u(\phi_{s}(x^{\prime})+z)+u(\phi_{s}(x^{\prime}))]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\lambda}\bigl[u(\phi_{s}(x+\lambda e_{i})+z)-u(\phi_{s}(x)+z)\bigr]-\frac{1}{\lambda}\bigl[u(\phi_{s}(x+\lambda e_{i}))-u(\phi_{s}(x))\bigr]

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{\lambda^{\prime}}\bigl[u(\phi_{s}(x^{\prime}+\lambda^{\prime}e_{i})+z)-u(\phi_{s}(x^{\prime})+z)\bigr]+\frac{1}{\lambda^{\prime}}\bigl[u(\phi_{s}(x^{\prime}+\lambda^{\prime}e_{i}))-u(\phi_{s}(x^{\prime}))\bigr].

With
r_{1}=\phi_{s}(x+\lambda e_{i})+z
and
r_{2}=\phi_{s}(x)+z
, so that by (
3.60
)

\displaystyle r_{1}-r_{2}

\displaystyle=\phi_{s}(x+\lambda e_{i})-\phi_{s}(x)=\lambda N_{s}(x,\lambda),

(3.83)

\displaystyle r_{2}+\theta(r_{1}-r_{2})

\displaystyle=\phi_{s}(x)+z+\theta\lambda N_{s}(x,\lambda).

(3.84)

Hence by the mean value theorem we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda}\bigl[u(\phi_{s}(x+\lambda e_{i})+z)-u(\phi_{s}(x)+z)\bigr]=\frac{1}{\lambda}\bigl[u(r_{1})-u(r_{2})]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\lambda}\int_{0}^{1}Du(r_{2}+\theta(r_{1}-r_{2}))(r_{1}-r_{2})\,d\theta=\int_{0}^{1}Du(\phi_{s}(x)+z+\theta\lambda N_{s}(x,\lambda))(N_{s}(x,\lambda))\,d\theta.

Analogously we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda^{\prime}}\bigl[u(\phi_{s}(x^{\prime}+\lambda^{\prime}e_{i})+z)-u(\phi_{s}(x^{\prime})+z)\bigr]=\int_{0}^{1}Du(\phi_{s}(x^{\prime})+z+\theta\lambda^{\prime}N_{s}(x^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime}))(N_{s}(x^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime}))\,d\theta,

and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda}\bigl[u(\phi_{s}(x+\lambda e_{i}))-u(\phi_{s}(x))\bigr]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}Du(\phi_{s}(x)+\theta\lambda N_{s}(x,\lambda))(N_{s}(x,\lambda))\,d\theta,

(3.85)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda^{\prime}}\bigl[u(\phi_{s}(x^{\prime}+\lambda^{\prime}e_{i}))-u(\phi_{s}(x^{\prime}))\bigr]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}Du(\phi_{s}(x^{\prime})+\theta\lambda^{\prime}N_{s}(x^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime}))(N_{s}(x^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime}))\,d\theta.

(3.86)

Therefore,

\displaystyle H(s,z,x,x^{\prime},\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}Du(\phi_{s}(x)+z+\theta\lambda N_{s}(x,\lambda))(N_{s}(x,\lambda))\,d\theta-\int_{0}^{1}Du(\phi_{s}(x)+\theta\lambda N_{s}(x,\lambda))(N_{s}(x,\lambda))\,d\theta

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{0}^{1}Du(\phi_{s}(x^{\prime})+z+\theta\lambda^{\prime}N_{s}(x^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime}))(N_{s}(x^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime}))\,d\theta

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{1}Du(\phi_{s}(x^{\prime})+\theta\lambda^{\prime}N_{s}(x^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime}))(N_{s}(x^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime}))\,d\theta

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\Bigl[Du(\phi_{s}(x)+z+\theta\lambda N_{s}(x,\lambda))-Du(\phi_{s}(x)+\theta\lambda N_{s}(x,\lambda))\Bigr](N_{s}(x,\lambda)-N_{s}(x^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime}))\,d\theta

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{1}\Bigl[Du(\phi_{s}(x)+z+\theta\lambda N_{s}(x,\lambda))-Du(\phi_{s}(x)+\theta\lambda N_{s}(x,\lambda))

(3.87)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad-Du(\phi_{s}(x^{\prime})+z+\theta\lambda^{\prime}N_{s}(x^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime}))+Du(\phi_{s}(x^{\prime})+\theta\lambda^{\prime}N_{s}(x^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime}))\Bigr](N_{s}(x^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime}))\,d\theta.

In order to find estimates for
J_{31}(t)
and
J_{32}(t)
, we will proceed as in the proof of
[
76
, Theorem 4.3]
and use the Burkholder inequality (
2.40
) from Proposition
2.7
.

We will first deal with the term
J_{32}
. Let us choose and fix an arbitrary
z\in B
.
Using the fact that
Du
is
\gamma
-Hölder continuous and the notation (
3.82
), we infer that
the 1st term on the RHS of (
3.87
) is bounded by

C|z|^{\gamma}|\hat{X}(s)|,\;\;s\geq 0.

In order to estimate the 2nd term on the RHS of (
3.87
) we will argue as on
[
76
, pages 445 and 446]
.
By inequality (
2.9
) in Lemma
2.1
and (
3.71
), we deduce that there exists a constant
c
depending on
Du
,
\gamma
and
\gamma^{\prime}
such that
for all
y,y^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

|Du(y+z)-Du(y)-Du(y^{\prime}+z)+Du(y^{\prime})|\leq c|z|^{\gamma^{\prime}}\,|y-y^{\prime}|^{\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}}.

(3.88)

Applying (
3.88
) we find that the 2nd term on the RHS of (
3.87
) is bounded by

\displaystyle C|N_{s}(x^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime})|\,|z|^{\gamma^{\prime}}\,\int_{0}^{1}|\phi_{s}(x)+\theta\lambda N_{s}(x,\lambda)-\phi_{s}(x^{\prime})-\theta\lambda^{\prime}N_{s}(x^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime})|^{\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}}d\theta

\displaystyle\leq C|z|^{\gamma^{\prime}}|N_{s}(x^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime})|\,\,\Big[|\phi_{s}(x)-\phi_{s}(x^{\prime})|^{\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}}+|\phi_{s}(x+\lambda e_{i})-\phi_{s}(x^{\prime}+\lambda^{\prime}e_{i})|^{\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}}\Bigr].

Therefore, we deduce that

\displaystyle|H(s,z,x,x^{\prime},\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\leq C|z|^{\gamma}|\hat{X}(s)|+C|z|^{\gamma^{\prime}}|N_{s}(x^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime})|\Big[|\phi_{s}(x)-\phi_{s}(x^{\prime})|^{\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}}+|\phi_{s}(x+\lambda e_{i})-\phi_{s}(x^{\prime}+\lambda^{\prime}e_{i})|^{\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}}\Bigr],

and so,
we get

\displaystyle|H(s,z,x,x^{\prime},\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})|^{p}\leq C_{p}|z|^{p\gamma^{\prime}}|\hat{X}(s)|^{p}

\displaystyle+C_{p}|N_{s}(x^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime})|^{p}\,|z|^{p\gamma^{\prime}}\Big[|\phi_{s}(x)-\phi_{s}(x^{\prime})|^{p(\gamma-\gamma^{\prime})}+|\phi_{s}(x+\lambda e_{i})-\phi_{s}(x^{\prime}+\lambda^{\prime}e_{i})|^{p(\gamma-\gamma^{\prime})}\Bigr],

which is a useful estimate. Thus, since
\delta=\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}
, we infer that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{s\in[0,t]}|J_{32}(s)|^{p}\Big]\leq C_{p}\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{0}^{t}|\hat{X}(s)|^{p}ds\Big]

(3.89)

\displaystyle+C_{p}\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{0}^{t}\!\!|N_{s}(x^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime})|^{p}\,\Big[|\phi_{s}(x)-\phi_{s}(x^{\prime})|^{p\delta}+|\phi_{s}(x+\lambda e_{i})-\phi_{s}(x^{\prime}+\lambda^{\prime}e_{i})|^{p\delta}\Bigr]ds\Big].

We have used (
2.40
) and (
3.66
) to obtain the above estimate.

Let us treat
J_{31}(t)
. Since
Du
is
\gamma
-Hölder continuous and (
3.71
), by
(
2.10
), we have

|Du(y)-Du(y^{\prime})|\leq c|y-y^{\prime}|^{\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}}=c|y-y^{\prime}|^{\delta},\;\;y,y^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

(3.90)

We obtain by (
3.71
), (
3.87
) and (
3.90
), when
|z|>1
,

\displaystyle\hskip-22.76228pt|H(s,z,x,x^{\prime},\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})|\leq c|\hat{X}(s)|\!+\!c|N_{s}(x^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime})|[|\phi_{s}(x)\!-\!\phi_{s}(x^{\prime})|^{\delta}\!+\!|\phi_{s}(x+\lambda e_{i})\!-\!\phi_{s}(x^{\prime}+\lambda^{\prime}e_{i})|^{\delta}],

and so we arrive at, for
p\geq 2,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{s\in[0,t]}|J_{31}(s)|^{p}\Big]\leq

\displaystyle C_{p}\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{0}^{t}|\hat{X}(s)|^{p}\,ds

(3.91)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}|N_{s}(x^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime})|^{p}\,(|\phi_{s}(x)-\phi_{s}(x^{\prime})|^{p\delta}+|\phi_{s}(x+\lambda e_{i})-\phi_{s}(x^{\prime}+\lambda^{\prime}e_{i})|^{p\delta})\,ds\Big].

Now we consider
J_{1}
. By (
3.82
), (
3.85
) and (
3.86
),
J_{1}
can be expressed as

\displaystyle J_{1}(t)=

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{1}Du(\phi_{t}(x)+\theta\lambda N_{t}(x,\lambda))\,[\hat{X}(t)]d\theta+\int_{0}^{1}[Du(x+\theta\lambda e_{i})-Du(x^{\prime}+\theta\lambda^{\prime}e_{i})]e_{i}d\theta

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{1}\!\![Du(\phi_{t}(x^{\prime})\!+\!\theta[\phi_{t}(x^{\prime}\!+\!\lambda^{\prime}e_{i})\!-\!\phi_{t}(x^{\prime})])\!-\!Du(\phi_{t}(x)\!+\!\theta[\phi(x\!+\!\lambda e_{i})\!-\!\phi(x)])]N_{t}(x^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime})d\theta.

Recalling (
3.71
) and (
3.90
), it follows that

\displaystyle|J_{1}(t)|\leq

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{3}|\hat{X}(t)|+C|\lambda-\lambda^{\prime}|^{\delta}+C|x-x^{\prime}|^{\delta}

\displaystyle+C|N_{t}(x^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime})|\,[|\phi_{t}(x)-\phi_{t}(x^{\prime})|^{\delta}+|\phi_{t}(x+\lambda e_{i})-\phi_{t}(x^{\prime}+\lambda^{\prime}e_{i})|^{\delta}].

Arguing as above, we get

\displaystyle J_{2}(t)=

\displaystyle\lambda_{0}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{1}Du(\phi_{s}(x)+\theta\lambda N_{s}(x,\lambda))N_{s}(x,\lambda)d\theta\,ds

\displaystyle-\lambda_{0}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{1}Du(\phi_{s}(x^{\prime})+\theta\lambda^{\prime}N_{s}(x^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime}))N_{s}(x^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime})d\theta\,ds

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda_{0}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{1}Du(\phi_{s}(x)+\theta\lambda N_{s}(x,\lambda))[\hat{X}(s)]d\theta\,ds

\displaystyle+\lambda_{0}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{1}[Du(\phi_{s}(x)+\theta\lambda N_{s}(x,\lambda))-Du(\phi_{s}(x^{\prime})+\theta\lambda^{\prime}N_{s}(x^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime}))]N_{s}(x^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime})d\theta\,ds.

It follows that

|J_{2}(t)|\leq c\int_{0}^{t}|\hat{X}(s)|ds+c\int_{0}^{t}|N_{s}(x^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime})|\,[|\phi_{s}(x)-\phi_{s}(x^{\prime})|^{\delta}+|\phi_{s}(x+\lambda e_{i})-\phi_{s}(x^{\prime}+\lambda^{\prime}e_{i})|^{\delta}]\,ds.

(3.92)

Note that the following inequality is
useful estimates. We can use (
3.1
) and (
3.67
) to obtain, for
p\geq 2,
by the Hölder inequality

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\Big(|N_{t}(x^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime})|^{p}\,\big[|\phi_{t}(x)-\phi_{t}(x^{\prime})|^{p\delta}+|\phi_{t}(x+\lambda e_{i})-\phi_{t}(x^{\prime}+\lambda^{\prime}e_{i})|^{p\delta}\big]\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}[|\lambda-\lambda^{\prime}|^{p\delta}+|x-x^{\prime}|^{p\delta}],

(3.93)

for some constant
C_{p}>0
independent of
x,x^{\prime},\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}
.

By the previous estimates,
we easily obtain

\mathbb{E}[\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}|\hat{X}(s)|^{p}]\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}[\sup_{0\leq r\leq s}|\hat{X}(r)|^{p}]\,ds+C|\lambda-\lambda^{\prime}|^{\delta p}+C|x-x^{\prime}|^{\delta p},

(3.94)

where
C
depends on
\alpha
,
\beta
,
\nu
,
p
,
d
and
T
. From (
3.94
) we deduce easily (
3.68
) using the Gronwall Lemma. This completes the proof of Proposition
3.21
.
∎

The proof of Theorem
3.19
is now complete.
∎

As a byproduct of the previous proof we also get the following result.

Corollary 3.22
.

For every
p\geq 2
and
T>0
there exists
C_{p}=C_{p}(T)>0
such that for every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},

\mathbb{E}[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|D\phi_{t}(x)|^{p}]\leq C_{p},

(3.95)

3.3
The diffeomorphism property

To prove Theorem
3.19
, we have applied the standard Kolmogorov test in order to obtain
nice regularity properties for
D\phi_{t}(x)
. However, to establish the diffeomorphism property of

\phi_{s,t}(x)
, it seems that we cannot use directly such Kolmogorov test because of the presence of both
s
and
t
. More precisely, when analyzing the inverse flow
\phi^{-1}_{s,t}(x)
, see (
3.27
), the presence of a backward term requires a different approach and a stronger version of the Kolmogorov test is necessary (cf. Theorem
F.2
). This is why in the next Theorem
3.23
we will work differently to study
D\phi_{s,t}(x)
.

Theorem 3.23
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
3.5
,
there exists a
\mathbb{P}
-full event
\Omega^{\prime\prime}
such that,
for all
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime\prime}
and
T>0
,
0\leq s\leq t\leq T
,
the mapping:

\mathbb{R}^{d}\ni x\mapsto\phi_{s,t}(x)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

is a surjective diffeomorphism of
C^{1}
-class and,
for any
\delta\in(0,\frac{\alpha}{2}+\beta-1)
,
the function

\mathbb{R}^{d}\ni x\mapsto D\phi_{s,t}(x)\in\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})

is locally
\delta
-Hölder continuous uniformly in
(s,t)\in[0,T]\times[0,T]
.

Moreover, for every
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime\prime}
and for any
\delta\in(0,\frac{\alpha}{2}+\beta-1)
,
the function

\mathbb{R}^{d}\ni x\mapsto D(\phi_{s,t}^{-1})(x)\in\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})

is locally
\delta
-Hölder continuous uniformly in
(s,t)\in[0,T]\times[0,T]
.

Finally, for every
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime\prime}
and for every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},
the functions

\displaystyle[0,T]\times[0,T]\ni(s,t)\mapsto D\phi_{s,t}(x)

(3.96)

\displaystyle[0,T]\times[0,T]\ni(s,t)\mapsto D(\phi_{s,t}^{-1})(x)

(3.97)

are separately càdlàg, i.e., for each
s\in[0,T]
,

[0,T]\ni t\mapsto D\phi_{s,t}(x)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\mbox{ is c\`{a}dl\`{a}g}

and for each
t\in[0,T]
, the map

[0,T]\ni s\mapsto D\phi_{s,t}(x)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\mbox{ is c\`{a}dl\`{a}g}.

The same holds for
D(\phi_{s,t}^{-1})(x)
.

Remark 3.24
.

By applying an argument analogous to that used in Lemma
3.6
, one can extend the above result to the infinite time interval and construct a universal
\mathbb{P}
-full set (independent of
T
) on which the flow
\phi_{s,t}(x),s,t\in[0,\infty)
, is a
C^{1}
-diffeomorphism whose derivatives are locally
\delta
-Hölder continuous.

Proof.

Consider a function

\psi:\mathbb{R}^{d}\ni x\mapsto x+u(x)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

Here
u=u_{\lambda_{0}}
and
\lambda_{0}
appear above (
3.71
).
According to (
3.45
), we have

\displaystyle\psi(\phi_{t}(x))=Y_{t}(\psi(x)),\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\;t\geq 0,

(3.98)

where

\displaystyle Y_{t}(y)=

\displaystyle y+\int_{0}^{t}\lambda_{0}u(\psi^{-1}(Y_{s}(y)))\,ds

(3.99)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(\psi^{-1}(Y_{s-}(y))+z)-u(\psi^{-1}(Y_{s-}(y)))\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)+L_{t}.

(3.100)

Some simple calculations lead to

\displaystyle[(D\psi)(\phi_{t}(x))]D\phi_{t}(x)=

\displaystyle D\psi(x)+\int_{0}^{t}\lambda_{0}Du(\phi_{s}(x))D\phi_{s}(x)ds

(3.101)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big[Du(\phi_{s-}(x)+z)-Du(\phi_{s-}(x))\big]D\phi_{s-}(x)\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz),

(3.102)

which is comparable with equality (21) in
[
39
]
.

Before proceeding with the proof, we present several results and facts, derived from Theorem
3.16
, collected from (
3.71
), and supplemented by some simple calculations, that will be used in the subsequent analysis:

(F1)

D\psi(y)=I+Du(y)
,
\forall y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

(F2)

\|Du\|_{0}\leq\frac{1}{3}
.

(F3)

\|u\|_{\alpha+\beta}<\infty
.

(F4)

\frac{2}{3}\leq\|D\psi\|_{0}=\|I+Du\|_{0}\leq\frac{4}{3}
.

(F5)

\frac{3}{4}\leq\|(D\psi)^{-1}\|_{0}=\|(I+Du)^{-1}\|_{0}\leq\frac{3}{2}
.

(F6)

By the definition of
\|Du\|_{\gamma}
and (F3), for any
s\geq 0
and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\|Du(\phi_{s}(x)+z)-Du(\phi_{s}(x))\|\leq c(|z|^{\gamma}\mathds{1}_{B}(z)+\mathds{1}_{B^{c}}(z)),\ \ \ \forall z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{0\}.

(3.103)

Here and in the following
\gamma=\alpha+\beta-1
. Keep in mind that (
2.25
) implies that
2\gamma>\alpha
.

(F7)

For any
s\geq 0
and
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\phi_{s}(x)-\phi_{s}(y)=\int_{0}^{1}D\phi_{s}(y+\theta(x-y))(x-y)d\theta.

(3.104)

(F8)

For any
d\times d
real invertible matrices
A
and
B
,

A^{-1}-B^{-1}=A^{-1}(B-A)B^{-1}.

(3.105)

(F9)

For any
p>\alpha
,
\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{p}\nu(dz)+\nu(B^{c})<\infty
.

(F10)

By (F1) (F3) (F5) and (F8), for any
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\|(D\psi(x))^{-1}-(D\psi(y))^{-1}\|\leq\frac{9}{4}\|u\|_{\alpha+\beta}|x-y|^{\gamma}\leq c|x-y|^{\gamma}.

(3.106)

Consider the
\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})
-valued solution to the following SDE, for every fixed
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle K_{t}(x)=(D\psi(x))^{-1}-\lambda_{0}\int_{0}^{t}K_{s}(x)Du(\phi_{s}(x))((D\psi)(\phi_{s}(x)))^{-1}\,ds

(3.107)

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}K_{s-}(x)\big[Du(\phi_{s-}(x)+z)-Du(\phi_{s-}(x))\big]((D\psi)(\phi_{s-}(x)))^{-1}\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)\

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}K_{s-}(x)\big[Du(\phi_{s-}(x)+z)-Du(\phi_{s-}(x))\big]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\times((D\psi)(\phi_{s-}(x)))^{-1}\big[Du(\phi_{s-}(x)+z)-Du(\phi_{s-}(x))\big]((D\psi)(\phi_{s-}(x)+z))^{-1}\mu(ds,dz).

By (F2), (F5), (F6) and (F9), classical results imply that there exist unique solutions to the above SDEs. Using the Itô product formula, c.f.
[
4
, Theorem 4.4.13]
, we have

\displaystyle d\big(K_{t}(x)\big((D\psi)(\phi_{t}(x))D\phi_{t}(x)\big)\big)

\displaystyle=K_{t-}(x)d\big((D\psi)(\phi_{t}(x))D\phi_{t}(x)\big)+(dK_{t}(x))(D\psi)(\phi_{t-}(x))D\phi_{t-}(x)+d\big[K_{\cdot}(x),(D\psi)(\phi_{\cdot}(x))D\phi_{\cdot}(x)\big)\big]_{t}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{0}K_{t}(x)Du(\phi_{t}(x))D\phi_{t}(x)dt

\displaystyle\quad+\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}K_{t-}(x)\big[Du(\phi_{t-}(x)+z)-Du(\phi_{t-}(x))\big]D\phi_{t-}(x)\tilde{\mu}(dt,dz)

\displaystyle-\lambda_{0}K_{t}(x)Du(\phi_{t}(x))((D\psi)(\phi_{t}(x)))^{-1}(D\psi)(\phi_{t}(x))D\phi_{t}(x)\,dt

\displaystyle\quad-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}K_{t-}(x)\big[Du(\phi_{t-}(x)+z)-Du(\phi_{t-}(x))\big]((D\psi)(\phi_{t-}(x)))^{-1}(D\psi)(\phi_{t-}(x))D\phi_{t-}(x)\tilde{\mu}(dt,dz)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}K_{t-}(x)\big[Du(\phi_{t-}(x)+z)-Du(\phi_{t-}(x))\big]((D\psi)(\phi_{t-}(x)))^{-1}\big[Du(\phi_{t-}(x)+z)-Du(\phi_{t-}(x))\big]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad((D\psi)(\phi_{t-}(x)+z))^{-1}(D\psi)(\phi_{t-}(x))D\phi_{t-}(x)\;\mu(dt,dz)

\displaystyle\quad-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}K_{t-}(x)\big[Du(\phi_{t-}(x)+z)-Du(\phi_{t-}(x))\big]((D\psi)(\phi_{t-}(x)))^{-1}\big[Du(\phi_{t-}(x)+z)-Du(\phi_{t-}(x))\big]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\ D\phi_{t-}(x)\;\mu(dt,dz)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}K_{t-}(x)\big[Du(\phi_{t-}(x)+z)-Du(\phi_{t-}(x))\big]((D\psi)(\phi_{t-}(x)))^{-1}\big[Du(\phi_{t-}(x)+z)-Du(\phi_{t-}(x))\big]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad((D\psi)(\phi_{t-}(x)+z))^{-1}\big[Du(\phi_{t-}(x)+z)-Du(\phi_{t-}(x))\big]D\phi_{t-}(x)\;\mu(dt,dz)

\displaystyle=0,

where we applied (F1) to cancel out the last three terms.
This proves that for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.,

\displaystyle K_{t}(x)[(D\psi)(\phi_{t}(x))]D\phi_{t}(x)=I,\quad\forall t\in[0,T].

(3.108)

Now we shall prove that
x\mapsto K_{t}(x)
is continuous uniformly in
t\in[0,T]
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.
Prior to that, we need to prepare some preliminary results. In particular, there exists some
M_{p,T}>0
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|K_{t}(x)\|^{p}\leq M_{p,T}.

(3.109)

By using (F2), (F5), (F6) and applying the Burkholder inequality twice (c.f.
[
61
, Theorem 2.11]
), we can derive that for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
p\geq 2
and
T>0
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|K_{t}(x)\|^{p}

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}\big\|(D\psi)^{-1}\big\|_{0}^{p}+C_{p}{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}\lambda_{0}K_{s}(x)Du(\phi_{s}(x))[(D\psi)(\phi_{s}(x))]^{-1}\,ds\Big\|^{p}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{p}{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}K_{s-}(x)\big[Du(\phi_{s-}(x)+z)-Du(\phi_{s-}(x))\big][(D\psi)(\phi_{s-}(x))]^{-1}\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)\Big\|^{p}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{p}{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}K_{s-}(x)\big[Du(\phi_{s-}(x)+z)-Du(\phi_{s-}(x))\big][(D\psi)(\phi_{s-}(x))]^{-1}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\times\big[Du(\phi_{s-}(x)+z)-Du(\phi_{s-}(x))\big][(D\psi)(\phi_{s-}(x)+z)]^{-1}\mu(ds,dz)\Big\|^{p}

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}\big\|(D\psi)^{-1}\big\|_{0}^{p}+C_{p}\lambda_{0}^{p}T^{p-1}\|Du\|_{0}^{p}\big\|(D\psi)^{-1}\big\|_{0}^{p}\int_{0}^{T}{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\|K_{s}(x)\|^{p}\,dt

\displaystyle\quad+C_{p}{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Big\|K_{s}(x)\big[Du(\phi_{s}(x)+z)-Du(\phi_{s}(x))\big][(D\psi)(\phi_{s}(x))]^{-1}\Big\|^{2}\nu(dz)ds\Big)^{\frac{p}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{p}{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Big\|K_{s}(x)\big[Du(\phi_{s}(x)+z)-Du(\phi_{s}(x))\big][(D\psi)(\phi_{s}(x))]^{-1}\Big\|^{p}\nu(dz)ds\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+C_{p}{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|K_{s-}(x)\|\|u\|^{2}_{\alpha+\beta}(\mathds{1}_{B}(z)|z|^{2\gamma}+\mathds{1}_{B^{c}}(z))\big\|(D\psi)^{-1}\big\|_{0}^{2}\,\mu(ds,dz)\Big)^{p}

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}\big\|(D\psi)^{-1}\big\|_{0}^{p}+C_{p}\lambda_{0}^{p}T^{p-1}\|Du\|_{0}^{p}\big\|(D\psi)^{-1}\big\|_{0}^{p}\int_{0}^{T}{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\|K_{s}(x)\|^{p}\,dt

\displaystyle\quad+C_{p}\|u\|^{p}_{\alpha+\beta}\big\|(D\psi)^{-1}\big\|_{0}^{p}T^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\int_{0}^{T}{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\|K_{s}(x)\|^{p}dt\Big(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{2\gamma}\nu(dz)+\nu(B^{c})\Big)^{\frac{p}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{p}\|u\|^{p}_{\alpha+\beta}\big\|(D\psi)^{-1}\big\|_{0}^{p}\int_{0}^{T}{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\|K_{s}(x)\|^{p}dt\Big(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{p\gamma}\nu(dz)+\nu(B^{c})\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+C_{p}\|u\|^{2p}_{\alpha+\beta}\big\|(D\psi)^{-1}\big\|_{0}^{2p}\;{\mathbb{E}}\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|K_{s-}(x)\|(\mathds{1}_{B}(z)|z|^{2\gamma}+\mathds{1}_{B^{c}}(z))\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)\Big|^{p}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{p}\|u\|^{2p}_{\alpha+\beta}\big\|(D\psi)^{-1}\big\|_{0}^{2p}\;{\mathbb{E}}\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|K_{s}(x)\|(\mathds{1}_{B}(z)|z|^{2\gamma}+\mathds{1}_{B^{c}}(z))\,\nu(dz)ds\Big|^{p}

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}\big\|(D\psi)^{-1}\big\|_{0}^{p}+C_{p}\lambda_{0}^{p}T^{p-1}\|Du\|_{0}^{p}\big\|(D\psi)^{-1}\big\|_{0}^{p}\int_{0}^{T}{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\|K_{s}(x)\|^{p}\,dt

\displaystyle\quad+C_{p}\|u\|^{p}_{\alpha+\beta}\big\|(D\psi)^{-1}\big\|_{0}^{p}T^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\int_{0}^{T}{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\|K_{s}(x)\|^{p}dt\Big(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{2\gamma}\nu(dz)+\nu(B^{c})\Big)^{\frac{p}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{p}\|u\|^{p}_{\alpha+\beta}\big\|(D\psi)^{-1}\big\|_{0}^{p}\int_{0}^{T}{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\|K_{s}(x)\|^{p}dt\Big(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{p\gamma}\nu(dz)+\nu(B^{c})\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+C_{p}\|u\|^{2p}_{\alpha+\beta}\|\big\|(D\psi))^{-1}\big\|_{0}^{2p}M_{T}\int_{0}^{T}{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\|K_{s}(x)\|^{p}dt,

where
M_{T}:=T^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\Big(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{4\gamma}\nu(dz)+\nu(B^{c})\Big)^{\frac{p}{2}}+\Big(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{2p\gamma}\nu(dz)+\nu(B^{c})\Big)+T^{p-1}\Big(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{2\gamma}\nu(dz)+\nu(B^{c})\Big)^{p}<\infty
by (F6) and (F9).
By the facts listed above and using the Gronwall inequality, we obtain (
3.109
).

Applying (
3.109
), Corollary
3.22
, (F7), and the Hölder inequality,
for any
q,r,p>0
with
p\geq 1
and
rp\geq 1
, there exists some
C_{q,r,p,T}>0
such that for any
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|K_{t}(x)\|^{q}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}|\phi_{s}(x)-\phi_{s}(y)|^{r}ds\Big)^{p}\Big)\leq C_{q,r,p,T}|x-y|^{rp}.

(3.110)

Now it is in a position to prove that
x\mapsto K_{t}(x)
is continuous uniformly in
t\in[0,T]
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.
Let
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Consider

\displaystyle K_{t}(x)-K_{t}(y)=

\displaystyle(D\psi(x))^{-1}-(D\psi(y))^{-1}-\lambda_{0}\int_{0}^{t}\big(K_{s}(x)-K_{s}(y)\big)Du(\phi_{s}(x))((D\psi)(\phi_{s}(x)))^{-1}ds

\displaystyle-\lambda_{0}\int_{0}^{t}K_{s}(y)\big(Du(\phi_{s}(x))((D\psi)(\phi_{s}(x)))^{-1}-Du(\phi_{s}(y))((D\psi)(\phi_{s}(y)))^{-1}\big)ds

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big(K_{s-}(x)-K_{s-}(y)\big)G(s-,x,z)\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)

(3.111)

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}K_{s-}(y)\big(G(s-,x,z)-G(s-,y,z)\big)\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)

(3.112)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big(K_{s-}(x)-K_{s-}(y)\big)H(s-,x,z)\mu(ds,dz)

(3.113)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}K_{s-}(y)\big(H(s-,x,z)-H(s-,y,z)\big)\mu(ds,dz)

(3.114)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle I^{0}(x,y)+I^{1}_{t}(x,y)+I^{2}_{t}(x,y)+I^{3}_{t}(x,y)+I^{4}_{t}(x,y)+I^{5}_{t}(x,y)+I^{6}_{t}(x,y),

(3.115)

where

I^{0}(x,y):=(D\psi(x))^{-1}-(D\psi(y))^{-1},

G(s,x,z):=\big[Du(\phi_{s}(x)+z)-Du(\phi_{s}(x))\big]((D\psi)(\phi_{s}(x)))^{-1},

and

\displaystyle H(s,x,z):=

\displaystyle\big[Du(\phi_{s}(x)+z)-Du(\phi_{s}(x))\big]((D\psi)(\phi_{s}(x)))^{-1}\big[Du(\phi_{s}(x)+z)-Du(\phi_{s}(x))\big]

\displaystyle((D\psi)(\phi_{s}(x)+z))^{-1}.

By using (F2) and (F5), we infer, for any
p\geq 2
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|I^{1}_{t}(x,y)\|^{p}\leq C_{p}T^{p-1}\lambda_{0}^{p}\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq t\leq s}\|K_{t}(x)-K_{t}(y)\|^{p}ds.

(3.116)

By the definition of
[Du]_{\gamma}
, (F1), (F3), and (F8),

\displaystyle\|Du(\phi_{s}(x))-Du(\phi_{s}(y))\|\leq c|\phi_{s}(x)-\phi_{s}(y)|^{\gamma},

(3.117)

and

\displaystyle\|((D\psi)(\phi_{s}(x)))^{-1}-((D\psi)(\phi_{s}(y)))^{-1}\|

(3.118)

\displaystyle=\|((D\psi)(\phi_{s}(x)))^{-1}\big((D\psi)(\phi_{s}(y))-(D\psi)(\phi_{s}(x))\big)[(D\psi)(\phi_{s}(y))]^{-1}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{9}{4}\|Du(\phi_{s}(x))-Du(\phi_{s}(y))\|

\displaystyle\leq c|\phi_{s}(x)-\phi_{s}(y)|^{\gamma}.

(3.119)

By (
2.9
) and (F3), we can deduce that for any
z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{0\}

\displaystyle\|Du(\phi_{s}(x)+z)-Du(\phi_{s}(x))-Du(\phi_{s}(y)+z)+Du(\phi_{s}(y))\|

\displaystyle\leq c[Du]_{\gamma}|\phi_{s}(x)-\phi_{s}(y)|^{\delta}|z|^{\gamma^{\prime}}\mathds{1}_{B}(z)+c[Du]_{\gamma}|\phi_{s}(x)-\phi_{s}(y)|^{\gamma}\mathds{1}_{B^{c}}(z)

\displaystyle\leq c|\phi_{s}(x)-\phi_{s}(y)|^{\delta}|z|^{\gamma^{\prime}}\mathds{1}_{B}(z)+c|\phi_{s}(x)-\phi_{s}(y)|^{\gamma}\mathds{1}_{B^{c}}(z),

(3.120)

with
\delta\in(0,\frac{\alpha}{2}+\beta-1)
and
\gamma^{\prime}:=\gamma-\delta\in(\frac{\alpha}{2},\gamma)
.
It follows from (F5), (F6), (
3.119
), and (
3.120
) that for any
z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{0\}

\displaystyle\|G(s,x,z)\|\leq c\Big(|z|^{\gamma}\mathds{1}_{B}(z)+\mathds{1}_{B^{c}}(z)\Big),

(3.121)

and

\displaystyle\|G(s,x,z)-G(s,y,z)\|

\displaystyle\leq\|Du(\phi_{s}(x)+z)-Du(\phi_{s}(x))-Du(\phi_{s}(y)+z)+Du(\phi_{s}(y))\|\|((D\psi)(\phi_{s}(x)))^{-1}\|

\displaystyle\quad+\|Du(\phi_{s}(y)+z)-Du(\phi_{s}(y))\|\|[(D\psi)(\phi_{s}(x))]^{-1}-((D\psi)(\phi_{s}(y)))^{-1}\|

(3.122)

\displaystyle\leq c|\phi_{s}(x)-\phi_{s}(y)|^{\delta}|z|^{\gamma^{\prime}}\mathds{1}_{B}(z)+c|\phi_{s}(x)-\phi_{s}(y)|^{\gamma}\mathds{1}_{B^{c}}(z)+c|\phi_{s}(x)-\phi_{s}(y)|^{\gamma}|z|^{\gamma}\mathds{1}_{B}(z).

Similarly, for any
z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{0\}
, we have

\displaystyle\|H(s,x,z)\|\leq c|z|^{2\gamma}\mathds{1}_{B}(z)+c\mathds{1}_{B^{c}}(z),

(3.123)

and

\displaystyle\|H(s,x,z)-H(s,y,z)\|

(3.124)

\displaystyle\leq c\|Du(\phi_{s}(x)+z)-Du(\phi_{s}(x))-Du(\phi_{s}(y)+z)+Du(\phi_{s}(y))\|\|Du(\phi_{s}(x)+z)-Du(\phi_{s}(x))\|

(3.125)

\displaystyle+c\|Du(\phi_{s}(y)+z)-Du(\phi_{s}(y))\|\|((D\psi)(\phi_{s}(x))^{-1}-((D\psi)(\phi_{s}(y)))^{-1}\|\|Du(\phi_{s}(x)+z)-Du(\phi_{s}(x))\|

(3.126)

\displaystyle+c\|Du(\phi_{s}(y)+z)-Du(\phi_{s}(y))\|\|Du(\phi_{s}(x)+z)-Du(\phi_{s}(x))-Du(\phi_{s}(y)+z)+Du(\phi_{s}(y))\|

(3.127)

\displaystyle+c\|Du(\phi_{s}(y)+z)-Du(\phi_{s}(y))\|^{2}\|[((D\psi)(\phi_{s}(x)+z))^{-1}-((D\psi)(\phi_{s}(y)+z))^{-1}\|

(3.128)

\displaystyle\leq c|\phi_{s}(x)-\phi_{s}(y)|^{\delta}|z|^{\gamma^{\prime}+\gamma}\mathds{1}_{B}(z)+c|\phi_{s}(x)-\phi_{s}(y)|^{\gamma}\mathds{1}_{B^{c}}(z)+c|\phi_{s}(x)-\phi_{s}(y)|^{\gamma}|z|^{2\gamma}\mathds{1}_{B}(z).

(3.129)

Applying (
3.117
), (
3.119
) and (
3.110
), we have, for any
p\geq\frac{1}{\gamma}\vee 2
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|I^{2}_{t}(x,y)\|^{p}

(3.130)

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}\mathbb{E}\Big[\!\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\!\!\|K_{s}(y)\|^{p}\Big(\!\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\!\big\|Du(\phi_{s}(x))-Du(\phi_{s}(y))\big\|\!+\!\big\|((D\psi)(\phi_{s}(x)))^{-1}\!-\!((D\psi)(\phi_{s}(y)))^{-1}\big\|ds\Big)^{p}\Big]

(3.131)

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}\mathbb{E}\Big(\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\|K_{s}(y)\|^{p}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}|\phi_{s}(x)-\phi_{s}(y)|^{\gamma}ds\Big)^{p}\Big)\leq C_{p,\gamma,T}|x-y|^{\gamma p}.

(3.132)

Using the
\rm It\hat{o}
formula and (
3.121
), we infer

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|I^{3}_{t}(x,y)\|^{p}\leq

\displaystyle C_{p}{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big\|K_{s}(x)-K_{s}(y)\big\|^{2}\|G(s,x,z)\|^{2}\nu(dz)ds\Big)^{\frac{p}{2}}

(3.133)

\displaystyle+C_{p}{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big\|K_{s}(x)-K_{s}(y)\big\|^{p}\|G(s,x,z)\|^{p}\nu(dz)ds\Big)

(3.134)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{p,T}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\|K_{s}(x)-K_{s}(y)\|^{p}dt,

(3.135)

where
C_{p,T}=C_{p}T^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\big(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{2\gamma}\nu(dz)+\nu(B^{c})\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}+C_{p}\big(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{p\gamma}\nu(dz)+\nu(B^{c})\big)<\infty
by (F6) and (F9).

For the fourth term
I^{4}
, applying (F9), (
3.122
) and (
3.110
), we have, for any
p\geq\frac{1}{\delta}\vee\frac{1}{\gamma}\vee 2
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|I^{4}_{t}(x,y)\|^{p}

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big\|K_{s}(y)\big\|^{2}\|G(s,x,z)-G(s,y,z)\|^{2}\nu(dz)ds\Big)^{\frac{p}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{p}{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big\|K_{s}(y)\big\|^{p}\|G(s,x,z)-G(s,y,z)\|^{p}\nu(dz)ds\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}\Big(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{2\gamma^{\prime}}+|z|^{2\gamma}\nu(dz)+\nu(B^{c})\Big)^{\frac{p}{2}}

(3.136)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\cdot\mathbb{E}\Big(\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\|K_{s}(y)\|^{p}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}|\phi_{s}(x)-\phi_{s}(y)|^{2\delta}+|\phi_{s}(x)-\phi_{s}(y)|^{2\gamma}ds\Big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+C_{p}\Big(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{p\gamma^{\prime}}+|z|^{p\gamma}\nu(dz)+\nu(B^{c})\Big)

(3.137)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\cdot\mathbb{E}\Big(\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\|K_{s}(y)\|^{p}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}|\phi_{s}(x)-\phi_{s}(y)|^{p\delta}+|\phi_{s}(x)-\phi_{s}(y)|^{p\gamma}ds\Big)\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C_{p,\delta,T,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\Big(|x-y|^{\delta p}+|x-y|^{\gamma p}\Big).

(3.138)

Using the
\rm It\hat{o}
formula and (
3.123
), we infer

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|I^{5}_{t}(x,y)\|^{p}\leq

\displaystyle C_{p,\gamma}{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big\|K_{s}(x)-K_{s}(y)\big\|^{2}\|H(s,x,z)\|^{2}\nu(dz)ds\Big)^{\frac{p}{2}}

(3.139)

\displaystyle+C_{p,\gamma}{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big\|K_{s}(x)-K_{s}(y)\big\|^{p}\|H(s,x,z)\|^{p}\nu(dz)ds\Big)

(3.140)

\displaystyle+C_{p,\gamma}{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big\|K_{s}(x)-K_{s}(y)\big\|\|H(s,x,z)\|\nu(dz)ds\Big)^{p}

(3.141)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{p,\gamma,T}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\|K_{s}(x)-K_{s}(y)\|^{p}dt.

(3.142)

where

\displaystyle C_{p,\gamma,T}=

\displaystyle C_{p}\Big[\Big(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{4\gamma}\nu(dz)+\nu(B^{c})\Big)^{\frac{p}{2}}T^{\frac{p-2}{2}}

\displaystyle+\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{2p\gamma}\nu(dz)+\nu(B^{c})+\Big(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{2\gamma}\nu(dz)+\nu(B^{c})\Big)^{p}T^{p-1}\Big]<\infty.

By using (F9), (
3.124
), and condition
\gamma^{\prime}+\gamma>\alpha
, see (
2.25
), we infer that, for any
p\geq\frac{1}{\delta}\vee\frac{1}{\gamma}\vee 2
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|I^{6}_{t}(x,y)\|^{p}\leq

\displaystyle C_{p}{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big\|K_{s}(y)\big\|^{2}\|H(s,x,z)-H(s,y,z)\|^{2}\nu(dz)ds\Big)^{\frac{p}{2}}

(3.143)

\displaystyle+C_{p}{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big\|K_{s}(y)\big\|^{p}\|H(s,x,z)-H(s,y,z)\|^{p}\nu(dz)ds\Big)

(3.144)

\displaystyle+C_{p}{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big\|K_{s}(y)\big\|\|H(s,x,z)-H(s,y,z)\|\nu(dz)ds\Big)^{p}

(3.145)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{p}{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\big\|K_{s}(y)\big\|^{p}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}|\phi_{s}(x)-\phi_{s}(y)|^{2\delta}+|\phi_{s}(x)-\phi_{s}(y)|^{2\gamma}ds\Big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\Big)

(3.146)

\displaystyle+C_{p}{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\big\|K_{s}(y)\big\|^{p}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}|\phi_{s}(x)-\phi_{s}(y)|^{p\delta}+|\phi_{s}(x)-\phi_{s}(y)|^{p\gamma}ds\Big)\Big)

(3.147)

\displaystyle+C_{p}{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\big\|K_{s}(y)\big\|^{p}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}|\phi_{s}(x)-\phi_{s}(y)|^{\delta}+|\phi_{s}(x)-\phi_{s}(y)|^{\gamma}ds\Big)^{p}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{p,\delta,T,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\Big(|x-y|^{\delta p}+|x-y|^{\gamma p}\Big).

(3.148)

Here

\displaystyle C_{p,\delta,T,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}

(3.149)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{p}\Big[\Big(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{4\gamma}\nu(dz)+\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{2(\gamma^{\prime}+\gamma)}\nu(dz)+\nu(B^{c})\Big)^{\frac{p}{2}}T^{\frac{p-2}{2}}

(3.150)

\displaystyle+\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{2p\gamma}\nu(dz)+\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{p(\gamma^{\prime}+\gamma)}\nu(dz)+\nu(B^{c})+\Big(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{2\gamma}\nu(dz)+\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{\gamma^{\prime}+\gamma}\nu(dz)+\nu(B^{c})\Big)^{p}T^{p-1}\Big]

(3.151)

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\infty.

(3.152)

Combining the above estimates ((F10), (
3.3
), (
3.116
), (
3.130
), (
3.133
), (
3.3
), (
3.139
), (
3.143
)) and applying the Gronwall inequality, we obtain, for any
p\geq 2\vee\frac{1}{\delta}\vee\frac{1}{\gamma}
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|K_{t}(x)-K_{t}(y)\|^{p}\leq

\displaystyle C_{p,\delta,T,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\Big(|x-y|^{\delta p}+|x-y|^{\gamma p},\;\;x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\Big).

Note that
\delta<\gamma
by (
3.64
). This fact plays an important rôle below.

In order to continue, let us recall useful notation. By
D_{\mathrm{Skor}}([0,T];\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d}))
we denote the space
D([0,T];\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d}))
of
\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d}))
-valued càdlàg functions endowed with the Skorokhod metric while by

D_{\mathrm{sup}}([0,T];\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d}))
we denote the same space endowed with the sup norm.

Next we apply the Kolmogorov criterion in the form of Theorem
F.2
and deduce that there exists a family

\{K^{\prime}(x),x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\}
of
D_{\mathrm{Skor}}([0,T];\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d}))
-valued random variables such that

K^{\prime}
is a modification of
K
, i.e., for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\mathbb{P}(K(x)\neq K^{\prime}(x))=0
,

for every
\omega\in\Omega
, the function

\mathbb{R}^{d}\ni x\mapsto K^{\prime}(x)\in D_{\mathrm{sup}}([0,T];\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d}))

(3.153)

is continuous,

if

\delta^{\prime}<\delta-\frac{d}{p}
, then the function
K^{\prime}
from (
3.153
) is locally

\delta^{\prime}
-Hölder continuous;

in particular, for every
\omega\in\Omega
and every
R>0
, there exists a constant
J_{R}(\omega)
, such that

\sup_{|x|\leq R}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|K^{\prime}_{t}(x,\omega)\|\leq J_{R}(\omega).

(3.154)

Since
\delta
can be any element in the open interval
(0,\frac{\alpha}{2}+\beta-1)
and
p
can be any sufficiently large constant, (ii) implies that

for any

\delta\in(0,\frac{\alpha}{2}+\beta-1)
, the function
K^{\prime}
from (
3.153
) is locally

\delta
-Hölder continuous. That is, for every
\omega\in\Omega
,
\delta\in(0,\frac{\alpha}{2}+\beta-1)
and every
R>0
, there exists a constant
J_{R,\delta}(\omega)
, such that

\sup_{|x|\vee|y|\leq R}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|K^{\prime}_{t}(x,\omega)-K^{\prime}_{t}(y,\omega)\|\leq J_{R,\delta}(\omega)|x-y|^{\delta}.

(3.155)

Therefore, in view of (
3.108
), (iii) of Theorem
3.5
and (iii) of Theorem
3.19
, there exists a
\mathbb{P}
-full measure set
\Omega^{0}
independent of
x,t
such that

K^{\prime}_{t}(x)[(D\psi)(\phi_{t}(x))]D\phi_{t}(x)=I\mbox{ for every }x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\;\;t\in[0,T]\mbox{ on }\Omega^{0}.

(3.156)

By using the Inverse Mapping Theorem, see e.g.
[
54
, Theorem 6.2.8]
,
[
50
, Appendix A]
, we infer that on
\Omega^{0}
, the function
\phi^{-1}_{t}
is also a
C^{1}
-class map and

D\phi^{-1}_{t}(x)=((D\phi_{t})(\phi_{t}^{-1}(x)))^{-1}=K_{t}^{\prime}(\phi^{-1}_{t}(x))[D\psi(x)],\ \forall(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[0,T].

(3.157)

By Theorems
3.5
,
3.8
and
3.19
, on some
\mathbb{P}
-full subset of
\Omega^{\prime}
, for any
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
,

•

the mapping
[0,T]\ni t\mapsto\phi_{t}(x)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
is càdlàg,

•

the mapping
[0,T]\ni t\mapsto\phi_{t}^{-1}(x)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
is càdlàg,

•

the mapping

[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\ni(t,x)\mapsto D\phi_{t}(x)\in\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})

is continuous.

Let
\Omega^{\prime\prime}=\Omega^{\prime}\cap\Omega^{0}
.
Hence, we infer that on
\Omega^{\prime\prime}
for every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
D\phi^{-1}_{t}(x)
is càdlàg on
[0,T]
.

Now we prove that, for every
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime\prime}
and any
\delta\in(0,\frac{\alpha}{2}+\beta-1)
,
the function

\mathbb{R}^{d}\ni x\mapsto D\phi_{t}^{-1}(x)\in\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})

is locally
\delta
-Hölder continuous uniformly in
t\in[0,T]
.

Fix
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime\prime}
and
N\in\mathbb{N}
. By Corollary
3.9
