, there exists a constant
M:=C_{\omega,N,T}
such that, for any
|y|\leq N
,

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\phi^{-1}_{t}(y)|\leq M.

Hence, by (ii’), (
3.154
), (
3.157
), and (F1)(F3)(F4), for any
t\in[0,T]
and
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
with

|x|\vee|y|\leq N
,

\displaystyle\|D\phi_{t}^{-1}(x)-D\phi_{t}^{-1}(y)\|

(3.158)

\displaystyle=\|K_{t}^{\prime}(\phi^{-1}_{t}(x))[D\psi(x)]-K_{t}^{\prime}(\phi^{-1}_{t}(y))[D\psi(y)]\|

(3.159)

\displaystyle\leq C\|K_{t}^{\prime}(\phi^{-1}_{t}(x))-K_{t}^{\prime}(\phi^{-1}_{t}(y))\|+\|K_{t}^{\prime}(\phi^{-1}_{t}(y))\|\|[D\psi(x)]-[D\psi(y)]\|

(3.160)

\displaystyle\leq CJ_{M,\delta}|\phi^{-1}_{t}(x)-\phi^{-1}_{t}(y)|^{\delta}+J_{M}\|u\|_{\alpha+\beta}|x-y|^{\gamma}

(3.161)

\displaystyle\leq CJ_{M,\delta}\Big(\int_{0}^{1}\|D\phi^{-1}_{t}(y+\theta(x-y))\|d\theta\Big)^{\delta}|x-y|^{\delta}+J_{M}\|u\|_{\alpha+\beta}|x-y|^{\gamma}

(3.162)

\displaystyle\leq CJ_{M,\delta}\Big(\int_{0}^{1}\|K_{t}^{\prime}(\phi^{-1}_{t}(y+\theta(x-y)))[D\psi(y+\theta(x-y))]\|d\theta\Big)^{\delta}|x-y|^{\delta}+J_{M}\|u\|_{\alpha+\beta}|x-y|^{\gamma}

(3.163)

\displaystyle\leq CJ_{M,\delta}J_{M}^{\delta}|x-y|^{\delta}+J_{M}\|u\|_{\alpha+\beta}|x-y|^{\gamma},

(3.164)

which proves that
D\phi_{t}^{-1}
is locally
\delta
-Hölder continuous uniformly in
t\in[0,T]
on
\Omega^{\prime\prime}
.

Recall that from (
3.30
) we have

\phi_{s,t}=\phi_{t}\circ\phi_{s}^{-1},\;\;\;\;\phi_{s,t}^{-1}=\phi_{s}\circ\phi_{t}^{-1},\;\;\;0\leq s\leq t\leq T.

(3.165)

By (
3.165
) we can differentiate:

\displaystyle D\phi_{s,t}(x)

\displaystyle=D\phi_{t}\,(\phi_{s}^{-1}(x))D\phi_{s}^{-1}(x),\;\;\;0\leq s\leq t\leq T,\,x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},

(3.166)

\displaystyle D\phi_{s,t}^{-1}(x)

\displaystyle=D\phi_{s}\,(\phi_{t}^{-1}(x))D\phi_{t}^{-1}(x),\;\;\;0\leq s\leq t\leq T,\,x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

(3.167)

By (iii) in Theorem
3.19
and the above results on
D\phi_{t}^{-1}
, using similar arguments as proving (
3.158
),
D\phi_{s,t}(x)
and
D\phi_{s,t}^{-1}(x)
are also locally
\delta
-Hölder continuous uniformly in
(s,t)\in[0,T]\times[0,T]
on some
\mathbb{P}
-full event. From (
3.166
) and (
3.167
), it is easy to conclude that for every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},
the functions

\displaystyle[0,T]\times[0,T]\ni(s,t)\mapsto D\phi_{s,t}(x)

(3.168)

\displaystyle[0,T]\times[0,T]\ni(s,t)\mapsto D\phi_{s,t}^{-1}(x)

(3.169)

are separately càdlàg.
∎

4
Stability

In this section, we use all the notations and assumptions introduced in the previous Section
3
, and let
T>0
be any fixed constant.

Below we denote by
\|\cdot\|
the Hilbert-Schmidt norm on
d\times d
matrices. In the next result we recall the assumptions of Theorem
3.5
.

Theorem 4.1
.

Assume that
\alpha\in[1,2)
or
\alpha\in(0,1)
. Assume that
L=(L_{t})_{t\geq 0}
is a Lévy process satisfying Hypotheses
1
in the former case or Hypothesis
3
in the latter case. Assume that
\beta\in(0,1)
satisfies condition (
2.25
) and
b\in C_{\mathrm{b}}^{\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
Let
(b^{n})_{n=1}^{\infty}\subset C_{\mathrm{b}}^{\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})

be a sequence of vector fields and
\phi^{n}
the corresponding
stochastic flows.
Let
b^{n}\to b
in
C_{\mathrm{b}}^{\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

Then, for every
p\geq 1
and any compact set
K\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we have:

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}[\sup_{t\in[s,T]}|\phi_{s,t}^{n}(x)-\phi_{s,t}(x)|^{p}]=0.

(4.1)

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}[\sup_{t\in[s,T]}\|D\phi_{s,t}^{n}(x)\|^{p}]+\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}[\sup_{t\in[s,T]}\|D\phi_{s,t}(x)\|^{p}]<\infty.

(4.2)

\displaystyle\,\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{x\in K}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}[\sup_{t\in[s,T]}\|D\phi_{s,t}^{n}(x)-D\phi_{s,t}(x)\|^{p}]=0.

(4.3)

Remark 4.2
.

The previous result remains valid with the same proof under the following weaker assumption.

Assume that numbers
0<\beta^{\prime}\leq\beta
satisfy

\frac{\alpha}{2}+\beta^{\prime}>1.

(4.4)

Assume also that
(b^{n})_{n=1}^{\infty}
is
a sequence of vector fields from the set
C_{\mathrm{b}}^{\beta^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})

such that
b^{n}\to b
in
C_{\mathrm{b}}^{\beta^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

Before we embark on the proof of the above important result, let us first formulate the following
essential corollary in which we use notation
B_{M}=\{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:|y|\leq M\}
.

Corollary 4.3
.

Under the same assumptions as in Theorem
4.1
.
There exists a subsequence of the sequence
\phi^{n}
, still denoted by
\phi^{n}
, and a
\mathbb{P}
-full set
\Omega^{\prime}\subset\Omega
such that for all
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime}
,
p\geq 1
and
M>0
,

\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{B_{M}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\,\Bigl[|\phi_{0,t}^{n}(x)-\phi_{0,t}(x)|^{p}+\|D\phi_{0,t}^{n}(x)-D\phi_{0,t}(x)\|^{p}\Bigr]dx=0,

(4.5)

and, for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\int_{B_{M}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\,\Bigl[\|D\phi_{0,t}^{n}(x)\|^{p}+\|D\phi_{0,t}(x)\|^{p}\Bigr]dx<\infty.

(4.6)

There exists a subsequence of the sequence
\phi^{n}
, still denoted by
\phi^{n}
, and a
\mathrm{Leb}\otimes\mathbb{P}
-full set
A\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\Omega
such that for all
(x,\omega)\in A
,

\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\,\Bigl[|\phi_{0,t}^{n}(x)-\phi_{0,t}(x)|+\|D\phi_{0,t}^{n}(x)-D\phi_{0,t}(x)\|\Bigr]=0.

(4.7)

Proof of Corollary
4.3
.

This follows immediately from the Fubini Theorem and Theorem
4.1
.

∎

Remark 4.4
.

According to Theorem
3.8
, the inverse flow
(\phi_{s,t})^{-1}
is the unique solution of the backward stochastic equation (
3.27
), analogous to the original equation (
2.34
), with the only difference being that the drift has an opposite sign.
Therefore, based on Theorem
3.23
, Theorem
4.1
and Corollary
4.3
remain valid
when
\phi_{s,t}^{n}(x)
and
\phi_{s,t}(x)
are replaced respectively by
(\phi_{s,t}^{n})^{-1}(x)
and
(\phi_{s,t})^{-1}(x)
, with slight adjustments to the proofs. However, it is important to note that the backward equation governs paths in reverse time
(\phi_{s,t})^{-1},s\in[0,t]
, unlike the forward equation
\phi_{s,t},t\in[s,T]
. Consequently, the formulation of the results differs accordingly:

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}[\sup_{s\in[0,t]}|(\phi_{s,t}^{n})^{-1}(x)-\phi_{s,t}^{-1}(x)|^{p}]=0,

(4.8)

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}[\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\|D(\phi_{s,t}^{n})^{-1}(x)\|^{p}]+\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}[\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\|D\phi_{s,t}^{-1}(x)\|^{p}]<\infty,

(4.9)

\displaystyle\,\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{x\in K}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}[\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\|D(\phi_{s,t}^{n})^{-1}(x)-D\phi_{s,t}^{-1}(x)\|^{p}]=0,

(4.10)

where
K\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
is any compact set.

Remark 4.5
.

By Remark
3.18
,
if
\alpha\in[1,2)
, we could obtain the following global stability of the flow:

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}[\sup_{t\in[s,T]}\|D\phi_{s,t}^{n}(x)-D\phi_{s,t}(x)\|^{p}]=0,

(4.11)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}[\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\|D(\phi_{s,t}^{n})^{-1}(x)-D\phi_{s,t}^{-1}(x)\|^{p}]=0.

(4.12)

Proof of Theorem
4.1
.

Let us fix
p\geq 2.
In view of Theorem
3.5
,
it is enough to verify that
(
4.1
), (
4.2
) and (
4.3
) of Theorem
4.1
hold for
\phi_{0,t}^{n}=\phi_{t}^{n}
and
\phi_{0,t}=\phi_{t}
, i.e.,
for any compact set
K\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\mathbb{E}[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}|\phi_{s}^{n}(x)-\phi_{s}(x)|^{p}]=0,

(4.13)

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\mathbb{E}[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|D\phi_{s}^{n}(x)\|^{p}]+\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\mathbb{E}[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|D\phi_{s}(x)\|^{p}]<\infty,

(4.14)

\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{x\in K}\mathbb{E}[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|D\phi_{s}^{n}(x)-D\phi_{s}(x)\|^{p}]=0.

(4.15)

We will prove the assertions stated above in several installments.

Proof of assertions (
4.13
).
.

According to Lemma
3.15
and Theorem
3.16
, there exists
\lambda_{0}{}_{,M}\geq 1
such that,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.,

\displaystyle u(\phi_{t}(x))-u(x)=

\displaystyle x-\phi_{t}(x)+L_{t}+\lambda_{0}{}_{,M}\int_{0}^{t}u(\phi_{s}(x))\,ds

(4.16)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}[u(\phi_{s-}(x)+z)-u(\phi_{s-}(x))]\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz),

and

\displaystyle u^{n}(\phi_{t}^{n}(x))-u^{n}(x)=

\displaystyle x-\phi_{t}^{n}(x)+L_{t}+\lambda_{0}{}_{,M}\int_{0}^{t}u^{n}(\phi_{s}^{n}(x))\,ds

(4.17)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}[u^{n}(\phi^{n}_{s-}(x)+z)-u^{n}(\phi^{n}_{s-}(x))]\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz).

Recall that by Theorem
3.16
and (
3.51
) we have, for any compact set
K\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq 0}\|u^{n}\|_{\alpha+\beta}<\infty\;\text{which implies}\;\sup_{n\geq 0}[Du^{n}]_{\alpha+\beta-1}<\infty,

(4.18)

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq 0}\|Du^{n}\|_{0}\leq\frac{1}{3},\quad\lim_{n\to\infty}\|u^{n}-u^{0}\|_{0}=\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}|u^{n}(x)-u^{0}(x)|=0,

(4.19)

and

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{x\in K}\|Du^{n}(x)-Du^{0}(x)\|=0.

(4.20)

Here
u^{n}
is the solution to (
3.47
) with
\lambda=\lambda_{0}{}_{,M}
and
f^{n}=b^{n}
,
and
u=u^{0}
is the solution to equation (
3.37
) with the RHS
f=b
and
\lambda=\lambda_{0}{}_{,M}
.

Then, we have

\mathbb{P}
-a.s.,

\displaystyle\phi_{t}(x)-\phi^{n}_{t}(x)=

\displaystyle[u(x)-u^{n}(x)]+[u^{n}(\phi^{n}_{t}(x))-u(\phi_{t}(x))]+\lambda_{0}{}_{,M}\int_{0}^{t}[u(\phi_{s}(x))-u^{n}(\phi^{n}_{s}(x))]ds

(4.21)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}[u(\phi_{s-}(x)+z)-u(\phi_{s-}(x))-u^{n}(\phi^{n}_{s-}(x)+z)+u^{n}(\phi^{n}_{s-}(x))]\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz).

The above equality can be rewritten as,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.,

\displaystyle\phi_{t}(x)-\phi^{n}_{t}(x)=

\displaystyle[u^{n}(\phi^{n}_{t}(x))-u^{n}(\phi_{t}(x))]+\lambda_{0}{}_{,M}\int_{0}^{t}[u^{n}(\phi_{s}(x))-u^{n}(\phi^{n}_{s}(x))]\,ds

(4.22)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}[u^{n}(\phi_{s-}(x)+z)-u^{n}(\phi_{s-}(x))-u^{n}(\phi^{n}_{s-}(x)+z)+u^{n}(\phi^{n}_{s-}(x))]\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)

\displaystyle+[u(x)-u^{n}(x)]+[u^{n}(\phi_{t}(x))-u(\phi_{t}(x))]+\lambda_{0}{}_{,M}\int_{0}^{t}[u(\phi_{s}(x))-u^{n}(\phi_{s}(x))]ds

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}[u(\phi_{s-}(x)+z)-u(\phi_{s-}(x))-u^{n}(\phi_{s-}(x)+z)+u^{n}(\phi_{s-}(x))]\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz).

Since

\|Du^{n}\|_{0}\leq\frac{1}{3}
, we have

\displaystyle|u^{n}(\phi_{t}(x))-u^{n}(\phi^{n}_{t}(x))|\leq\frac{1}{3}|\phi_{t}(x)-\phi_{t}^{n}(x)|.

(4.23)

Combining (
4.22
) and (
4.23
) leads us to the estimate

|\phi_{t}(x)-\phi^{n}_{t}(x)|\leq\frac{3}{2}\Lambda^{n}_{1}(t)+\frac{3}{2}\Lambda^{n}_{2}(t)+\frac{3}{2}\Lambda^{n}_{3}(t)+\frac{3}{2}\Lambda^{n}_{4}(t),

where

\displaystyle\Lambda^{n}_{1}(t)

\displaystyle=\Bigl|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}[u^{n}(\phi_{s-}(x)+z)-u^{n}(\phi_{s-}(x))-u^{n}(\phi^{n}_{s-}(x)+z)+u^{n}(\phi^{n}_{s-}(x))]\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)\Bigr|,

\displaystyle\Lambda^{n}_{2}(t)

\displaystyle=\lambda_{0}{}_{,M}\int_{0}^{t}|u^{n}(\phi_{s}(x))-u^{n}(\phi^{n}_{s}(x))|ds,

\displaystyle\Lambda^{n}_{3}(t)

\displaystyle=\Bigl|\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}[u^{n}(\phi_{s-}(x)+z)-u^{n}(\phi_{s-}(x))-u^{n}(\phi^{n}_{s-}(x)+z)+u^{n}(\phi^{n}_{s-}(x))]\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)\Big|,

\displaystyle\Lambda^{n}_{4}(t)

\displaystyle=\Big|[u(x)-u^{n}(x)]+[u^{n}(\phi_{t}(x))-u(\phi_{t}(x))]+\lambda_{0}{}_{,M}\int_{0}^{t}[u(\phi_{s}(x))-u^{n}(\phi_{s}(x))]ds\Bigr|

\displaystyle\quad+\Bigl|\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}[u(\phi_{s-}(x)+z)-u(\phi_{s-}(x))-u^{n}(\phi_{s-}(x)+z)+u^{n}(\phi_{s-}(x))]\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)\Bigr|.

Now set
p\geq 2
. Note
that,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.,

\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}|\phi_{s}(x)-\phi^{n}_{s}(x)|^{p}\leq C_{p}\sum_{j=1}^{4}\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\,\Lambda^{n}_{j}(s)^{p}.

The main difficulty is to estimate
\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\,\Lambda^{n}_{4}(s)^{p}
. Let us first
consider the other terms. By (
4.23
) and the Hölder inequality, we see

\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\Lambda^{n}_{2}(s)^{p}\leq c_{1}(p)\,t^{p-1}\int_{0}^{t}\sup_{0\leq s\leq r}|\phi_{s}(x)-\phi^{n}_{s}(x)|^{p}\,dr.

(4.24)

By (
2.40
) with
C=B^{c}

we find

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\Lambda^{n}_{1}(s)^{p}]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c(p)\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}|u^{n}(\phi_{s}(x)+z)-u^{n}(\phi^{n}_{s}(x)+z)+u^{n}(\phi^{n}_{s}(x))-u^{n}(\phi_{s}(x))|^{2}\nu(dz)ds\Big)^{p/2}\Big]

\displaystyle+\,c(p)\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}|u^{n}(\phi_{s}(x)+z)-u^{n}(\phi^{n}_{s}(x)+z)+u^{n}(\phi^{n}_{s}(x))-u^{n}(\phi_{s}(x))|^{p}\nu(dz)ds.

Using the following inequality, by (
4.19
),

\displaystyle|u^{n}(\phi_{s}(x)+z)-u^{n}(\phi_{s}^{n}(x)+z)+u^{n}(\phi_{s}^{n}(x))-u^{n}(\phi_{s}(x))|\leq\frac{2}{3}|\phi_{s}(x)-\phi_{s}^{n}(x)|,

and the Hölder inequality, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\Lambda_{1}(s)^{p}]\leq

\displaystyle C_{1}(p)\,(1+t^{p/2-1})\Bigl(\int_{B^{c}}\nu(dz)\,+\big(\int_{B^{c}}\nu(dz)\big)^{p/2}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\cdot\,\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}[\sup_{0\leq s\leq r}|\phi_{s}(x)-\phi_{s}^{n}(x)|^{p}]dr.

(4.25)

Let us treat
\Lambda^{n}_{3}(t)
. Set
\gamma=\alpha+\beta-1
. Note that (
2.25
) implies that
2\gamma>\alpha
. By using (
2.40
) with

C=B\setminus\{0\}
and Lemma
2.1
, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\Lambda^{n}_{3}(s)^{p}]\leq c(p)\|u^{n}\|_{1+\gamma}^{p}\,\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|\phi_{s}(x)-\phi^{n}_{s}(x)|^{2}|z|^{2\gamma}\nu(dz)ds\Big)^{p/2}\Big]

\displaystyle+\,c(p)\|u^{n}\|_{1+\gamma}^{p}\,\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|\phi_{s}(x)-\phi^{n}_{s}(x)|^{p}|z|^{\gamma p}\nu(dz)ds.

We obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\Lambda^{n}_{3}(s)^{p}]\leq

\displaystyle C_{2}(p)\,(1+t^{p/2-1})\,\|u^{n}\|^{p}_{1+\gamma}\,\cdot\Bigl(\big(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{2\gamma}\nu(dz)\big)^{p/2}+\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{\gamma p}\nu(dz)\Bigr)

(4.26)

\displaystyle\;\times\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}[\sup_{0\leq s\leq r}|\phi_{s}(x)-\phi^{n}_{s}(x)|^{p}]\,dr,

(4.27)

where
\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{2\gamma}\nu(dz)+\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\,|z|^{p\gamma}\nu(dz)<+\infty
,
since
p\geq 2
and
2\gamma>\alpha
. By (
4.18
),

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq 1}\|u^{n}\|_{\gamma+1}=\sup_{n\geq 1}\|u^{n}\|_{\alpha+\beta}\leq C<\infty.

(4.28)

Collecting the previous estimates, we arrive at

\displaystyle\quad\ \mathbb{E}[\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}|\phi_{s}(x)-\phi^{n}_{s}(x)|^{p}]

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}\,\mathbb{E}[\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\Lambda^{n}_{4}(s)^{p}]\,+\,C_{3}(p)\,(1+t^{p/2-1}+t^{p-1})\cdot\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}[\sup_{0\leq s\leq r}|\phi_{s}(x)-\phi^{n}_{s}(x)|^{p}]\,dr.

Applying the Gronwall Lemma we obtain, for any
t\geq 0
,

\mathbb{E}[\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}|\phi_{s}(x)-\phi^{n}_{s}(x)|^{p}]\leq C(t)\,C_{p}\mathbb{E}[\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\Lambda^{n}_{4}(s)^{p}],

(4.29)

with
C(t)=\exp\big(C_{3}(p)\,(t+t^{p/2}+t^{p})\big)
.
It remains to prove that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbb{E}[\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\Lambda^{n}_{4}(s)^{p}]=0.

(4.30)

By applying the Burholder inequality, see (
2.40
) and the Hölder inequality, we get

\displaystyle\hskip-73.97733pt{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}|\Lambda^{n}_{4}(s)|^{p}

(4.31)

\displaystyle\hskip-73.97733pt\leq C_{4}(p)|u(x)-u^{n}(x)|^{p}+C_{4}(p){\mathbb{E}}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}|u^{n}(\phi_{s}(x))-u(\phi_{s}(x))|^{p}

\displaystyle\hskip-73.97733pt+\lambda_{0}{}_{,M}C_{4}(p)T^{p-1}{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\sup_{0\leq s\leq r}|u(\phi_{s}(x))-u^{n}(\phi_{s}(x))|^{p}dr

(4.32)

\displaystyle+C_{4}(p){\mathbb{E}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|u(\phi_{s}(x)+z)-u(\phi_{s}(x))-u^{n}(\phi_{s}(x)+z)+u^{n}(\phi_{s}(x))|^{2}\nu(dz)\,ds\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}

\displaystyle+C_{4}(p){\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|u(\phi_{s}(x)+z)-u(\phi_{s}(x))-u^{n}(\phi_{s}(x)+z)+u^{n}(\phi_{s}(x))|^{p}\nu(dz)\,ds.

By using (
4.19
) we deduce that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}

\displaystyle\Big(C_{4}(p)|u(x)-u^{n}(x)|^{p}+C_{4}(p){\mathbb{E}}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}|u^{n}(\phi_{s}(x))-u(\phi_{s}(x))|^{p}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ +\lambda_{0}{}_{,M}C_{4}(p)T^{p-1}{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\sup_{0\leq s\leq r}|u(\phi_{s}(x))-u^{n}(\phi_{s}(x))|^{p}dr\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{n\to\infty}C_{p}(1+T^{p})\|u^{n}-u\|_{0}^{p}=0.

(4.33)

By applying again (
4.18
) and (
4.19
), there exists a constant
C
such that, for any
s\in[0,T]
and
|z|>1
,

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}|u(\phi_{s}(x)+z)-u(\phi_{s}(x))-u^{n}(\phi_{s}(x)+z)+u^{n}(\phi_{s}(x))|^{2}\leq C<\infty,

(4.34)

and, for any
s\in[0,T]
and
|z|\leq 1
,

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}|u(\phi_{s}(x)+z)-u(\phi_{s}(x))-u^{n}(\phi_{s}(x)+z)+u^{n}(\phi_{s}(x))|^{2}

(4.35)

\displaystyle\leq C(\|Du\|_{0}^{2}+\|Du^{n}\|_{0}^{2})|z|^{2}\leq C|z|^{2}.

We also have

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}|u(\phi_{s}(x)+z)-u(\phi_{s}(x))-u^{n}(\phi_{s}(x)+z)+u^{n}(\phi_{s}(x))|\leq\lim_{n\to\infty}2\|u-u^{n}\|_{0}=0.

(4.36)

In view of (
4.34
), (
4.35
), (
4.36
) and the Dominated Convergence Theorem, we infer

\displaystyle\quad\ \lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|u(\phi_{s}(x)+z)-u(\phi_{s}(x))-u^{n}(\phi_{s}(x)+z)+u^{n}(\phi_{s}(x))|^{2}\nu(dz)\,ds\Big)^{\frac{p}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{n\to\infty}{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}|u(\phi_{s}(x)+z)-u(\phi_{s}(x))-u^{n}(\phi_{s}(x)+z)+u^{n}(\phi_{s}(x))|^{2}\nu(dz)\,ds\Big)^{\frac{p}{2}}

\displaystyle=0.

(4.37)

Using the similar argument as (
4
), we have

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\!\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\!{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|u(\phi_{s}(x)+z)-u(\phi_{s}(x))-u^{n}(\phi_{s}(x)+z)+u^{n}(\phi_{s}(x))|^{p}\nu(dz)\,ds

\displaystyle=0.

(4.38)

By (
4.32
), (
4.33
), (
4
), and (
4
), we infer (
4.30
). Hence the proof of (
4.13
) is complete.
∎

Proof of assertion (
4.14
) and (
4.15
).
.

Define functions
\psi
and
\psi^{n}
by

\psi(x)=x+u(x)\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\psi^{n}(x)=x+u^{n}(x)\quad\mbox{ for }x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

Since
\|Du\|_{0}\leq\frac{1}{3}
and
\|Du^{n}\|_{0}\leq\frac{1}{3}
, the classical
Hadamard theorem, see
[
80
, p.330]
, implies that
\psi
and
\psi^{n}
are
C^{1}
-diffeomorphism from
\mathbb{R}^{d}
onto
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and

\displaystyle\|D\psi\|_{0}\leq 4/3,\ \|D(\psi^{n})\|_{0}\leq 4/3.

(4.39)

Noting that for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle D\psi^{-1}(x)

\displaystyle=\big(I+Du(\psi^{-1}(x))\big)^{-1}=\sum_{k\geq 0}(-Du(\psi^{-1}(x)))^{k},

(4.40)

\displaystyle D(\psi^{n})^{-1}(x)

\displaystyle=\big(I+Du^{n}((\psi^{n})^{-1}(x))\big)^{-1}=\sum_{k\geq 0}(-Du^{n}((\psi^{n})^{-1}(x)))^{k},

(4.41)

we have

\displaystyle\|D\psi^{-1}\|_{0}\leq 3/2\text{, and }\|D(\psi^{n})^{-1}\|_{0}\leq 3/2.

(4.42)

It follows that for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\quad\ |(\psi^{n})^{-1}(x)-\psi^{-1}(x)|

\displaystyle=|(\psi^{n})^{-1}\circ\psi\circ\psi^{-1}(x)-(\psi^{n})^{-1}\circ\psi^{n}\circ\psi^{-1}(x)|

\displaystyle\leq\|D(\psi^{n})^{-1}\|_{0}|\psi\circ\psi^{-1}(x)-\psi^{n}\circ\psi^{-1}(x)|\leq\|D(\psi^{n})^{-1}\|_{0}\|\psi-\psi^{n}\|_{0}\leq 3/2\|u-u^{n}\|_{0},

and hence

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\|(\psi^{n})^{-1}-\psi^{-1}\|_{0}=0.

(4.43)

Let us fix any compact set
K\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
. By using (
4.40
), (
4.41
) and the fact that for any
d\times d
real matrices
A
and
B
with
(I+A)
and
(I+B)
being invertible,

\displaystyle(I+A)^{-1}-(I+B)^{-1}=(I+A)^{-1}(B-A)(I+B)^{-1},

(4.44)

we deduce that, for any
x\in K
,

\displaystyle\quad\ \|D\psi^{-1}(x)-D(\psi^{n})^{-1}(x)\|

(4.45)

\displaystyle=\big\|\big(I+Du(\psi^{-1}(x))\big)^{-1}\big(Du^{n}((\psi^{n})^{-1}(x))-Du(\psi^{-1}(x))\big)\big(I+Du^{n}((\psi^{n})^{-1}(x))\big)^{-1}\big\|

(4.46)

\displaystyle\leq\|D\psi^{-1}\|_{0}\|Du(\psi^{-1}(x))-Du^{n}((\psi^{n})^{-1}(x))\|\|D(\psi^{n})^{-1}\|_{0}

(4.47)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{9}{4}\Big(\|Du(\psi^{-1}(x))-Du((\psi^{n})^{-1}(x))\|+\|Du((\psi^{n})^{-1}(x))-Du^{n}((\psi^{n})^{-1}(x))\|\Big)

(4.48)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{9}{4}\Big([Du]_{\gamma}\|\psi^{-1}-(\psi^{n})^{-1}\|_{0}^{\gamma}+\|Du((\psi^{n})^{-1}(x))-Du^{n}((\psi^{n})^{-1}(x)\|\Big),

(4.49)

where
[Du]_{\gamma}<\infty
according to (
4.18
).
We have to study the term
(\psi^{n})^{-1}(x)
. Note that
K\subset B_{M}
for some
M>0
, where
B_{M}
is the closed ball as before. We write, for any
n\geq 1
,

\displaystyle|(\psi^{n})^{-1}(x)|\leq|(\psi^{n})^{-1}(x)-(\psi^{n})^{-1}(0)|+|(\psi^{n})^{-1}(0)|\leq 3M/2+|(\psi^{n})^{-1}(0)|,

(4.50)

by (
4.42
). Let
y_{n}=(\psi^{n})^{-1}(0)
. We have

\psi^{n}(y_{n})=y_{n}+u^{n}(y_{n})=0.

Hence
|y_{n}|\leq|u^{n}(y_{n})|\leq\sup_{n\geq 1}\|u_{n}\|_{0}=C<\infty
by (
4.18
). It follows that

|(\psi^{n})^{-1}(x)|\leq 3M/2+C=:M^{\prime},\;\;n\geq 1,\,x\in K.

By (
4.20
) and (
4.43
), we obtain

\sup_{x\in K}\|D\psi^{-1}(x)-D(\psi^{n})^{-1}(x)\|\leq\frac{9}{4}\Big([Du]_{\gamma}\|\psi^{-1}-(\psi^{n})^{-1}\|_{0}^{\gamma}+\sup_{y\in B_{M^{\prime}}}\|Du(y)-Du^{n}(y)\|\Big)\to 0

as
n\to\infty
. Therefore, we get

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{x\in K}\|D\psi^{-1}(x)-D(\psi^{n})^{-1}(x)\|=0.

(4.51)

Using again (
4.44
), we also have the following a priori estimates:

\displaystyle\|D\psi^{-1}(x)-D\psi^{-1}(x^{\prime})\|

\displaystyle\leq\|D\psi^{-1}\|_{0}\|Du(\psi^{-1}(x))-Du(\psi^{-1}(x^{\prime}))\|\|D\psi^{-1}\|_{0}

(4.52)

\displaystyle\leq 9/4[Du]_{\gamma}\|D\psi\|_{0}^{\gamma}|x-x^{\prime}|^{\gamma},\quad\ \forall x,x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d},

(4.53)

which gives

\displaystyle[D\psi^{-1}]_{\gamma}\leq\frac{9}{4}(\frac{4}{3})^{\gamma}[Du]_{\gamma}.

(4.54)

Similarly,

\displaystyle[D(\psi^{n})^{-1}]_{\gamma}\leq\frac{9}{4}(\frac{4}{3})^{\gamma}[Du^{n}]_{\gamma}.

(4.55)

We know from
[
76
, p. 442 and (4.14)]
that

\displaystyle\psi(\phi_{t}(x))=Y_{t}(\psi(x)),

(4.56)

where

\displaystyle Y_{t}(y)=y+\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{b}(Y_{s}(y))\,ds+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}h(Y_{s-}(y),z)\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)+L_{t},

(4.57)

with
\tilde{b}(x)=\lambda_{0}{}_{,M}u(\psi^{-1}(x))
and
h(x,z)=u(\psi^{-1}(x)+z)-u(\psi^{-1}(x))
;
and

\displaystyle\psi^{n}(\phi^{n}_{t}(x))=Y^{n}_{t}(\psi^{n}(x)),

(4.58)

where

\displaystyle Y^{n}_{t}(y)=y+\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{b}^{n}(Y^{n}_{s}(y))\,ds+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}h^{n}(Y^{n}_{s-}(y),z)\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)+L_{t},

(4.59)

with
\tilde{b}^{n}(x)=\lambda_{0}{}_{,M}u^{n}((\psi^{n})^{-1}(x))
and
h^{n}(x,z)=u^{n}((\psi^{n})^{-1}(x)+z)-u^{n}((\psi^{n})^{-1}(x))
.

Observe that by (
4.56
) and (
4.58
), we have

\displaystyle D\phi_{t}(x)=(D\psi^{-1})(Y_{t}(\psi(x))DY_{t}(\psi(x))D\psi(x),

(4.60)

and

\displaystyle D\phi^{n}_{t}(x)=(D(\psi^{n})^{-1})(Y^{n}_{t}(\psi^{n}(x)))DY^{n}_{t}(\psi^{n}(x))D\psi^{n}(x).

(4.61)

Thanks to the above observations, to establish (
4.14
) and (
4.15
), it is sufficient to prove the following formulae (recall that
K\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
can be any compact set)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|Y^{n}_{t}(y)-Y_{t}(y)|^{p}=0,

(4.62)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|Y_{t}(y)-Y_{t}(y^{\prime})|^{p}\Big)\leq C_{T}|y-y^{\prime}|^{p},\;\;\forall\;y,y^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d},

(4.63)

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq 1}\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|DY^{n}_{t}(y)\|^{p}+\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|DY_{t}(y)\|^{p}<\infty,

(4.64)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\,\sup_{y\in K}{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|DY^{n}_{t}(y)-DY_{t}(y)\|^{p}=0,

(4.65)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|DY_{t}(y)-DY_{t}(y^{\prime})\|^{p}\leq C\Big(|y-y^{\prime}|^{p\gamma}+|y-y^{\prime}|^{\delta p}\Big),\;\ \forall\;y,y^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

(4.66)

Here
\delta>0
is a constant, which we will determine later.
Indeed, (
4.60
)-(
4.61
) combined with (
4.39
), (
4.42
) and (
4.64
) implies

\displaystyle\quad\ \sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|D\phi_{t}(x)\|^{p}+\sup_{n\geq 1}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|D\phi^{n}_{t}(x)\|^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\|(D\psi^{-1})\|^{p}_{0}\|D\psi\|_{0}^{p}\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|DY_{t}(y)\|^{p}+\sup_{n\geq 1}\|(D(\psi^{n})^{-1})\|^{p}_{0}\|D\psi^{n}\|_{0}^{p}\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|DY_{t}^{n}(y)\|^{p}

\displaystyle<\infty,

which establisheds (
4.14
).

Next, we will show that the above five inequalities imply assertion (
4.15
).
Owing to (
4.60
)-(
4.61
) and (
4.54
), we deduce for
x\in K
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|D\phi_{t}(x)-D\phi^{n}_{t}(x)\|^{p}

(4.67)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{p}\,{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big\|\big((D\psi^{-1})(Y_{t}(\psi(x)))-(D\psi^{-1})(Y_{t}(\psi^{n}(x)))\big)\cdot DY_{t}(\psi(x))\cdot D\psi(x)\big\|^{p}

(4.68)

\displaystyle+C_{p}\,{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big\|\big(D\psi^{-1})(Y_{t}(\psi^{n}(x)))-(D\psi^{-1})(Y^{n}_{t}(\psi^{n}(x)))\big)\cdot DY_{t}(\psi(x))\cdot D\psi(x)\big\|^{p}

(4.69)

\displaystyle+C_{p}\,{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big\|\big((D\psi^{-1})(Y^{n}_{t}(\psi^{n}(x)))-(D(\psi^{n})^{-1})(Y^{n}_{t}(\psi^{n}(x)))\big)\cdot DY_{t}(\psi(x))\cdot D\psi(x)\big\|^{p}

(4.70)

\displaystyle+C_{p}\,{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big\|(D(\psi^{n})^{-1})(Y^{n}_{t}(\psi^{n}(x)))\cdot\big(DY_{t}(\psi(x))-DY_{t}(\psi^{n}(x))\big)\cdot D\psi(x)\|^{p}

(4.71)

\displaystyle+C_{p}\,{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big\|\big((D(\psi^{n})^{-1})(Y^{n}_{t}(\psi^{n}(x)))\cdot\big(DY_{t}(\psi^{n}(x))-DY^{n}_{t}(\psi^{n}(x))\big)\cdot D\psi(x)\big\|^{p}

(4.72)

\displaystyle+C_{p}\,{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big\|(D(\psi^{n})^{-1})(Y^{n}_{t}(\psi^{n}(x)))\cdot DY^{n}_{t}(\psi^{n}(x))\cdot\big(D\psi(x)-D\psi^{n}(x)\big)\big\|^{p}

(4.73)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{p}[Du]_{\gamma}^{p}\|D\psi\|_{0}^{p}\,{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big(|Y_{t}(\psi(x))-Y_{t}(\psi^{n}(x))|^{\gamma p}\|DY_{t}(\psi(x))\|^{p}\Big)

(4.74)

\displaystyle+C_{p}[Du]_{\gamma}^{p}\|D\psi\|_{0}^{p}\,{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big(|Y_{t}(\psi^{n}(x))-Y^{n}_{t}(\psi^{n}(x))|^{\gamma p}\|DY_{t}(\psi(x))\|^{p}\Big)

(4.75)

\displaystyle+C_{p}\|D\psi\|_{0}^{p}\,\,{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big(\big\|(D\psi^{-1})(Y^{n}_{t}(\psi^{n}(x)))-(D(\psi^{n})^{-1})(Y^{n}_{t}(\psi^{n}(x)))\big\|^{p}\big\|DY_{t}(\psi(x))\big\|^{p}\Big)

(4.76)

\displaystyle+C_{p}\|D(\psi^{n})^{-1}\|_{0}^{p}\|D\psi\|_{0}^{p}\,{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big(\|DY_{t}(\psi(x))-DY_{t}(\psi^{n}(x))\|^{p}\Big)

(4.77)

\displaystyle+C_{p}\|D(\psi^{n})^{-1}\|_{0}^{p}\|D\psi\|_{0}^{p}\,{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big(\|DY_{t}(\psi^{n}(x))-DY^{n}_{t}(\psi^{n}(x))\|^{p}\Big)

(4.78)

\displaystyle+\,C_{p}\|D(\psi^{n})^{-1}\|_{0}^{p}\|D\psi(x)-D\psi^{n}(x)\|^{p}\,{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big(\|DY^{n}_{t}(\psi^{n}(x))\|^{p}\Big).

(4.79)

Note that by the definition of
\psi^{n}
and (
4.18
) we have

\sup_{n\geq 1}\sup_{x\in K}|\psi^{n}(x)|=:M_{K}<\infty.

(4.80)

By choosing
q>1
large enough such that
\gamma pq>2
, and setting
\frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}=1
, we have

\displaystyle\,\sup_{x\in K}{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|D\phi_{t}(x)-D\phi^{n}_{t}(x)\|^{p}

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}\,[Du]_{\gamma}^{p}\|D\psi\|_{0}^{p}\Big(\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Big({\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|DY_{t}(y)\|^{pq^{\prime}}\Big)\Big)^{\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}}\Big(\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|Y_{t}(\psi(x))-Y_{t}(\psi^{n}(x))|^{\gamma pq}\Big)\Big)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{p}\,[Du]_{\gamma}^{p}\|D\psi\|_{0}^{p}\Big(\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Big({\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|DY_{t}(y)\|^{pq^{\prime}}\Big)\Big)^{\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}}\Big(\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|Y_{t}(y)-Y^{n}_{t}(y)|^{\gamma pq}\Big)\Big)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{p}\|D\psi\|_{0}^{p}\,\,\Big(\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Big({\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|DY_{t}(y)\|^{2p}\Big)\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\cdot\Big(\sup_{x\in K}{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big(\big\|(D\psi^{-1})(Y^{n}_{t}(\psi^{n}(x)))-(D(\psi^{n})^{-1})(Y^{n}_{t}(\psi^{n}(x)))\big\|^{2p}\Big)\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\,+C_{p}\,\|D(\psi^{n})^{-1}\|_{0}^{p}\|D\psi\|_{0}^{p}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big(\|DY_{t}(\psi(x))-DY_{t}(\psi^{n}(x))\|^{p}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+C_{p}\,\|D(\psi^{n})^{-1}\|_{0}^{p}\|D\psi\|_{0}^{p}\sup_{y\in B_{M_{K}}}{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big(\|DY_{t}(y)-DY^{n}_{t}(y)\|^{p}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+C_{p}\,\|D(\psi^{n})^{-1}\|_{0}^{p}\sup_{x\in K}\|Du(x)-Du^{n}(x)\|^{p}\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big(\|DY^{n}_{t}(y)\|^{p}\Big).

Since by (
4.19
)
\lim_{n\to\infty}\|\psi^{n}-\psi\|_{0}=0
, (
4.63
) and (
4.66
) imply that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|Y_{t}(\psi(x))-Y_{t}(\psi^{n}(x))|^{\gamma pq}\Big)=0,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|DY_{t}(\psi(x))-DY_{t}(\psi^{n}(x))\|^{p}\Big)=0.

And once we prove that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{x\in K}{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big(\big\|(D\psi^{-1})(Y^{n}_{t}(\psi^{n}(x)))-(D(\psi^{n})^{-1})(Y^{n}_{t}(\psi^{n}(x)))\big\|^{2p}\Big)=0,

(4.81)

then, using (
4.18
), (
4.20
), (
4.39
), (
4.42
), (
4.64
), and
(
4.65
), we conclude that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{x\in K}{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|D\phi_{t}(x)-D\phi^{n}_{t}(x)\|^{p}=0.

which implies (
4.15
).

Now it remains to prove (
4.81
). By (
4.58
),

\displaystyle Y^{n}_{t}(\psi^{n}(x))=\psi^{n}(\phi^{n}_{t}(x))=\phi^{n}_{t}(x)+u^{n}(\phi^{n}_{t}(x)).

(4.82)

Combining (
4.18
) and (ii) in Theorem
3.5
,

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq 1}\sup_{x\in K}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|Y^{n}_{t}(\psi^{n}(x))(\omega)|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{x\in K}|x|+\sup_{n\geq 1}\|b^{n}\|_{0}T+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|L_{t}(\omega)|+\sup_{n\geq 1}\|u^{n}\|_{0}

(4.83)

\displaystyle=:C_{K,T}(\omega)<\infty,\ \ \mathbb{P}\text{-}a.s.\ \omega\in\Omega.

(4.84)

For any
J>0
, set
A_{J}=\{\omega\in\Omega:C_{K,T}(\omega)\leq J\}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in K}{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big(\big\|(D\psi^{-1})(Y^{n}_{t}(\psi^{n}(x)))-(D(\psi^{n})^{-1})(Y^{n}_{t}(\psi^{n}(x)))\big\|^{2p}\Big)

(4.85)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in K}{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big(\big\|(D\psi^{-1})(Y^{n}_{t}(\psi^{n}(x)))-(D(\psi^{n})^{-1})(Y^{n}_{t}(\psi^{n}(x)))\big\|^{2p}\mathds{1}_{A_{J}}\Big)

(4.86)

\displaystyle+\sup_{x\in K}{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big(\big\|(D\psi^{-1})(Y^{n}_{t}(\psi^{n}(x)))-(D(\psi^{n})^{-1})(Y^{n}_{t}(\psi^{n}(x)))\big\|^{2p}\mathds{1}_{A^{c}_{J}}\Big)

(4.87)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{y\in B_{J}}\|(D\psi^{-1})(y)-(D(\psi^{n})^{-1})(y)\big\|^{2p}+C_{p}\Big(\sup_{n\geq 1}\|D(\psi^{n})^{-1}\|_{0}^{2p}+\|D\psi^{-1}\|_{0}^{2p}\Big)\mathbb{P}(A^{c}_{J}).

(4.88)

Then by (
4.42
) and (
4.51
), we have

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{x\in K}{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big(\big\|(D\psi^{-1})(Y^{n}_{t}(\psi^{n}(x)))-(D(\psi^{n})^{-1})(Y^{n}_{t}(\psi^{n}(x)))\big\|^{2p}\Big)\leq C_{p}\mathbb{P}(A^{c}_{J}).

(4.89)

The arbitrariness of
J
implies (
4.81
).

The proof of assertion (
4.14
) and (
4.15
) is complete.
∎

Now it remains to prove (
4.62
)-(
4.66
). The more difficult assertion will be
(
4.65
).

Proof of
(
4.62
).

Notice that by Page 444 and (4.15) of
[
76
]
,

\displaystyle\|Dh^{n}(\cdot,z)\|_{0}\leq c\|Du^{n}\|_{\gamma}(\mathds{1}_{B}(z)|z|^{\gamma}+\mathds{1}_{B^{c}}(z)),\ \ \ \forall z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{0\},\;\gamma=\alpha+\beta-1,

(4.90)

and

\displaystyle\|Dh(\cdot,z)\|_{0}\leq c\|Du\|_{\gamma}(\mathds{1}_{B}(z)|z|^{\gamma}+\mathds{1}_{B^{c}}(z)),\ \ \ \forall z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{0\}.

(4.91)

By (
4.18
), (
4.19
) and (
4.42
), it is easy to see that

\displaystyle\sup_{n}\|\tilde{b}^{n}\|_{0}+\|\tilde{b}\|_{0}\leq\lambda_{0}{}_{,M}C<\infty,

and

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\|D\tilde{b}^{n}\|_{0}\!\leq\!\lambda_{0}{}_{,M}\|Du^{n}\|_{0}\|D(\psi^{n})^{-1}\|_{0}\!\leq\!\frac{\lambda_{0}{}_{,M}}{2},\\
&\|D\tilde{b}\|_{0}\!\leq\!\lambda_{0}{}_{,M}\|Du\|_{0}\|D\psi^{-1}\|_{0}\!\leq\!\frac{\lambda_{0}{}_{,M}}{2}.\end{aligned}

(4.92)

For any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle|\tilde{b}^{n}(x)-\tilde{b}(x)|

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{0}{}_{,M}\Big(|u^{n}((\psi^{n})^{-1}(x))-u((\psi^{n})^{-1}(x))|+|u((\psi^{n})^{-1}(x))-u(\psi^{-1}(x))|\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{0}{}_{,M}\Big(\|u^{n}-u\|_{0}+\|Du\|_{0}\|(\psi^{n})^{-1}-\psi^{-1}\|_{0}\Big),

and, for any
x\in K
,

\displaystyle\|D\tilde{b}^{n}(x)-D\tilde{b}(x)\|=

\displaystyle\lambda_{0}{}_{,M}\|Du^{n}((\psi^{n})^{-1}(x))D(\psi^{n})^{-1}(x)-Du(\psi^{-1}(x))D\psi^{-1}(x)\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lambda_{0}{}_{,M}\|Du^{n}((\psi^{n})^{-1}(x))D(\psi^{n})^{-1}(x)-Du((\psi^{n})^{-1}(x))D(\psi^{n})^{-1}(x)\|

\displaystyle+\lambda_{0}{}_{,M}\|Du((\psi^{n})^{-1}(x))D(\psi^{n})^{-1}(x)-Du(\psi^{-1}(x))D(\psi^{n})^{-1}(x)\|

\displaystyle+\lambda_{0}{}_{,M}\|Du(\psi^{-1}(x))D(\psi^{n})^{-1}(x)-Du(\psi^{-1}(x))D\psi^{-1}(x)\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lambda_{0}{}_{,M}\sup_{y\in B_{M^{\prime}}}\|Du^{n}(y)-Du(y)\|\,\|D(\psi^{n})^{-1}\|_{0}

\displaystyle+\lambda_{0}{}_{,M}[Du]_{\gamma}\|(\psi^{n})^{-1}-\psi^{-1}\|_{0}^{\gamma}\|D(\psi^{n})^{-1}\|_{0}

\displaystyle+\lambda_{0}{}_{,M}\|Du\|_{0}\sup_{x\in K}\|D(\psi^{n})^{-1}(x)-D\psi^{-1}(x)\|,

here
M^{\prime}
is introduced in (
4.80
).
Hence, (
4.18
), (
4.19
), (
4.20
), (
4.42
), (
4.43
) and (
4.51
) imply that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\Big[\|\tilde{b}^{n}-\tilde{b}\|_{0}+\sup_{x\in K}\|D\tilde{b}^{n}(x)-D\tilde{b}(x)\|\Big]=0.

(4.93)

Similar arguments imply that, for any
z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{0\}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\Big[\|{h}^{n}(\cdot,z)-{h}(\cdot,z)\|_{0}+\sup_{x\in K}\|D{h}^{n}(x,z)-D{h}(x,z)\|\Big]=0.

(4.94)

Let
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
By (
4.57
) and (
4.59
),

\displaystyle|Y^{n}_{t}(y)-Y_{t}(y)|\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}|\tilde{b}^{n}(Y^{n}_{s}(y))-\tilde{b}(Y_{s}(y))|\,ds+\Big|\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}h^{n}(Y^{n}_{s-}(y),z)-h(Y_{s-}(y),z)\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}|\tilde{b}^{n}(Y^{n}_{s}(y))-\tilde{b}(Y^{n}_{s}(y))|\,ds+\int_{0}^{t}|\tilde{b}(Y^{n}_{s}(y))-\tilde{b}(Y_{s}(y))|\,ds

\displaystyle+\Bigl|\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}h^{n}(Y^{n}_{s-}(y),z)-h(Y^{n}_{s-}(y),z)\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)\Big|

\displaystyle+\Bigl|\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}h(Y^{n}_{s-}(y),z)-h(Y_{s-}(y),z)\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)\Big|

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle I_{1}(t)+I_{2}(t)+I_{3}(t)+I_{4}(t).

For
I_{1}(t)+I_{2}(t)
, we have

\displaystyle I_{1}(t)+I_{2}(t)\leq\|\tilde{b}^{n}-\tilde{b}\|_{0}t+\|D\tilde{b}\|_{0}\int_{0}^{t}|Y^{n}_{s}(y)-Y_{s}(y)|\,ds.

(4.95)

By (
4.18
), (
4.91
) and (
2.40
), for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big(\sup_{s\in[0,t]}|I_{4}(s)|^{p}\Big)\leq

\displaystyle C_{p,T}\|Du\|_{\gamma}^{p}\Big(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathds{1}_{B}(z)|z|^{\gamma p}+\mathds{1}_{B^{c}}(z)\,\nu(dz)+\Big(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathds{1}_{B}(z)|z|^{2\gamma}+\mathds{1}_{B^{c}}(z)\,\nu(dz)\Big)^{p/2}\Big)

\displaystyle\cdot\mathbb{E}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\sup_{r\in[0,s]}|Y^{n}_{r}(y)-Y_{r}(y)|^{p}ds\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{p,T}\mathbb{E}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\sup_{r\in[0,s]}|Y^{n}_{r}(y)-Y_{r}(y)|^{p}ds\Big).

(4.96)

The Gronwall lemma with the above three inequalities implies that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|Y^{n}_{t}(y)-Y_{t}(y)|^{p}\leq C_{p,T}\Big(\|\tilde{b}^{n}-\tilde{b}\|_{0}^{p}+{\mathbb{E}}(\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|I_{3}(t)|^{p})\Big).

(4.97)

By applying inequality (
2.40
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|I_{3}(t)|^{p}\Big)\leq\;

\displaystyle C_{p}\mathbb{E}\Big(\Bigl|\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|h^{n}(Y^{n}_{s}(y),z)-h(Y^{n}_{s}(y),z)|^{2}\nu(dz)ds\Big|^{p/2}\Big)

(4.98)

\displaystyle+C_{p}\mathbb{E}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|h^{n}(Y^{n}_{s}(y),z)-h(Y^{n}_{s}(y),z)|^{p}\nu(dz)ds\Big).

(4.99)

By (
4.94
) and using similar arguments as (
4
) and (
4
), we may invoke the Dominated Convergence Theorem to get

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbb{E}\Big(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|I_{3}(t)|^{p}\Big)=0.

(4.100)

Hence, we have (
4.62
).
∎

Next we move to prove (
4.63
).

Proof of (
4.63
).

By (
4.57
), we have, for any
y,y^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle|Y_{t}(y)-Y_{t}(y^{\prime})|\leq

\displaystyle|y-y^{\prime}|+\Big|\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{b}(Y_{s}(y))-\tilde{b}(Y_{s}(y^{\prime}))\,ds\Big|

\displaystyle+\Big|\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}h(Y_{s-}(y),z)-h(Y_{s-}(y^{\prime}),z)\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)\Big|.

(4.101)

A similar argument to that used for the proof of (
4.95
) and (
4
) shows that

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{b}(Y_{s}(y))-\tilde{b}(Y_{s}(y^{\prime}))\,ds\Big|\leq\|D\tilde{b}\|_{0}\int_{0}^{t}|Y_{s}(y)-Y_{s}(y^{\prime})|\,ds,

(4.102)

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big(\sup_{l\in[0,t]}\Big|\int_{0}^{l}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}h(Y_{s-}(y),z)-h(Y_{s-}(y^{\prime}),z)\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)\Big|^{p}\Big)\leq C_{p,T}\mathbb{E}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\sup_{r\in[0,s]}|Y_{r}(y)-Y_{r}(y^{\prime})|^{p}ds\Big).

(4.103)

Applying (
4.101
)–(
4.103
) and Gronwall’s inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|Y_{t}(y)-Y_{t}(y^{\prime})|^{p}\Big)\leq C_{p,T}|y-y^{\prime}|^{p},

(4.104)

which gives (
4.63
).
∎

Proof of (
4.64
).

Let us observe that
[
61
, Theorem 3.4]
, see also Page 445 of
[
76
]
, implies that the following equation for
DY_{t}(y)
and
DY^{n}_{t}(y)
holds,

\displaystyle DY_{t}(y)=I

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}D\tilde{b}(Y_{s}(y))DY_{s}(y)\,ds+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}Dh(Y_{s-}(y),z)DY_{s-}(y)\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz),\quad

(4.105)

and

\displaystyle DY^{n}_{t}(y)=I

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}D\tilde{b}^{n}(Y^{n}_{s}(y))DY^{n}_{s}(y)\,ds+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}Dh^{n}(Y^{n}_{s-}(y),z)DY^{n}_{s-}(y)\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz).\quad\quad

(4.106)

It is not difficulty to prove that, by (
4.18
), (
4.90
), (
4.91
) and (
4.92
),

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq 1}\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|DY^{n}_{t}(y)\|^{p}+\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|DY_{t}(y)\|^{p}<\infty.

(4.107)

which implies (
4.64
).
∎

Proof of (
4.65
).

We will prove that (recall that
K\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
is any compact set)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{y\in K}{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|DY^{n}_{t}(y)-DY_{t}(y)\|^{p}=0.

(4.108)

By (
4.105
) and (
4.106
),

\displaystyle\|DY^{n}_{t}(y)-DY_{t}(y)\|\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\|D\tilde{b}^{n}(Y^{n}_{s}(y))DY^{n}_{s}(y)-D\tilde{b}(Y_{s}(y))DY_{s}(y)\|\,ds

\displaystyle+\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}Dh^{n}(Y^{n}_{s-}(y),z)DY^{n}_{s-}(y)-Dh(Y_{s-}(y),z)DY_{s-}(y)\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)\Big\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\|D\tilde{b}^{n}(Y^{n}_{s}(y))DY^{n}_{s}(y)-D\tilde{b}^{n}(Y_{s}(y))DY^{n}_{s}(y)\|\,ds

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\|D\tilde{b}^{n}(Y_{s}(y))DY^{n}_{s}(y)-D\tilde{b}(Y_{s}(y))DY_{s}^{n}(y)\|\,ds

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\|D\tilde{b}(Y_{s}(y))DY_{s}^{n}(y)-D\tilde{b}(Y_{s}(y))DY_{s}(y)\|\,ds

\displaystyle+\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}Dh^{n}(Y^{n}_{s-}(y),z)DY^{n}_{s-}(y)-Dh^{n}(Y_{s-}(y),z)DY^{n}_{s-}(y)\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)\Big\|

\displaystyle+\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}Dh^{n}(Y_{s-}(y),z)DY^{n}_{s-}(y)-Dh(Y_{s-}(y),z)DY^{n}_{s-}(y)\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)\Big\|

\displaystyle+\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}Dh(Y_{s-}(y),z)DY^{n}_{s-}(y)-Dh(Y_{s-}(y),z)DY_{s-}(y)\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)\Big\|

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{6}J_{i}(t,y,n).

(4.109)

Before proceeding, we note that
by (
4.18
), (
4.55
), (
4.42
) and Page 444 Lines 9-24 of
[
76
]
,

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq 1}[D\tilde{b}^{n}]_{\gamma}\leq\sup_{n\geq 1}\,\lambda_{0}{}_{,M}\Big(\|Du^{n}\|_{0}[D(\psi^{n})^{-1}]_{\gamma}+[Du^{n}]_{\gamma}\|D(\psi^{n})^{-1}\|_{0}^{1+\gamma}\Big)\leq C<\infty,

(4.110)

with
\gamma=\alpha+\beta-1.

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle[D\tilde{b}]_{\gamma}\leq\lambda_{0}{}_{,M}\Big(\|Du\|_{0}[D\psi^{-1}]_{\gamma}+[Du]_{\gamma}\|D\psi^{-1}\|_{0}^{1+\gamma}\Big)\leq C<\infty.

(4.111)

For
J_{6}(t,y,n)
, using the Burkholder inequality (
2.40
), we have for any
0\leq S\leq T
,
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,S]}|J_{6}(t,y,n)|^{p}

(4.112)

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\int_{0}^{S}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|Dh(Y_{s}(y),z)\|^{2}\|DY^{n}_{s}(y)-DY_{s}(y)\|^{2}\nu(dz)ds\Big)^{p/2}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{p}{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\int_{0}^{S}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|Dh(Y_{s}(y),z)\|^{p}\|DY^{n}_{s}(y)-DY_{s}(y)\|^{p}\nu(dz)ds\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\int_{0}^{S}\|DY^{n}_{s}(y)-DY_{s}(y)\|^{2}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|Dh(\cdot,z)\|^{2}_{0}\nu(dz)ds\Big)^{p/2}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{p}{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\int_{0}^{S}\|DY^{n}_{s}(y)-DY_{s}(y)\|^{p}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|Dh(\cdot,z)\|^{p}_{0}\nu(dz)ds\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C_{p,T}{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\int_{0}^{S}\|DY^{n}_{s}(y)-DY_{s}(y)\|^{p}ds\Big)\Big[\Big(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|Dh(\cdot,z)\|^{2}_{0}\nu(dz)\Big)^{p/2}+\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|Dh(\cdot,z)\|^{p}_{0}\nu(dz)\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C_{p,T}{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\int_{0}^{S}\|DY^{n}_{s}(y)-DY_{s}(y)\|^{p}ds\Big).

(4.113)

For the last inequality, we have used the fact that
(
4.91
) implies that

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|Dh(\cdot,z)\|^{2}_{0}\nu(dz)+\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|Dh(\cdot,z)\|^{p}_{0}\nu(dz)<\infty.

(4.114)

The term
J_{3}(t,y,n)
can be easily estimated using the bound (
4.92
). We get a similar estimate:

{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,S]}|J_{3}(t,y,n)|^{p}\leq M_{p,T}{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{S}\|DY^{n}_{s}(y)-DY_{s}(y)\|^{p}ds.

(4.115)

In order to estimate
J_{1}(t,y,n)
, we use (
4.110
). We find

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|J_{1}(t,y,n)|^{p}

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\|D\tilde{b}^{n}(Y^{n}_{s}(y))-D\tilde{b}^{n}(Y_{s}(y))\|\|DY^{n}_{s}(y)\|\,ds\Big)^{p}

(4.116)

\displaystyle\leq C{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}|Y^{n}_{s}(y))-Y_{s}(y)|^{\gamma}\|DY^{n}_{s}(y)\|\,ds\Big)^{p}

(4.117)

\displaystyle\leq C_{T}\Big({\mathbb{E}}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|DY_{s}^{n}(y)\|^{2p}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big({\mathbb{E}}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}|Y^{n}_{s}(y)-Y_{s}(y)|^{2\gamma p}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(4.118)

Hence, by (
4.62
) and (
4.64
),

\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|J_{1}(t,y,n)|^{p}\leq M_{p,T}\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Big({\mathbb{E}}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}|Y^{n}_{s}(y)-Y_{s}(y)|^{2\gamma p}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\to 0,\quad\text{as }n\to\infty.

Let us estimate
J_{4}(t,y,n)
. For any
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}|J_{4}(s,y,n)|^{p}

(4.119)

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|Dh^{n}(Y^{n}_{s}(y),z)-Dh^{n}(Y_{s}(y),z)\|^{2}\|DY^{n}_{s}(y)\|^{2}\nu(dz)ds\Big)^{p/2}

(4.120)

\displaystyle\quad+C_{p}{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|Dh^{n}(Y^{n}_{s}(y),z)-Dh^{n}(Y_{s}(y),z)\|^{p}\|DY^{n}_{s}(y)\|^{p}\nu(dz)ds\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}\Big({\mathbb{E}}\Big(\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|DY^{n}_{s}(y)\|^{2p}\Big)\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big({\mathbb{E}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|Dh^{n}(Y^{n}_{s}(y),z)-Dh^{n}(Y_{s}(y),z)\|^{2}\nu(dz)ds\Big)^{p}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{p}\Big({\mathbb{E}}\Big(\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|DY^{n}_{s}(y)\|^{2p}\Big)\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big({\mathbb{E}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|Dh^{n}(Y^{n}_{s}(y),z)-Dh^{n}(Y_{s}(y),z)\|^{p}\nu(dz)ds\Big)^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}\Big({\mathbb{E}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|Dh^{n}(Y^{n}_{s}(y),z)-Dh^{n}(Y_{s}(y),z)\|^{2}\nu(dz)ds\Big)^{p}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{p}\Big({\mathbb{E}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|Dh^{n}(Y^{n}_{s}(y),z)-Dh^{n}(Y_{s}(y),z)\|^{p}\nu(dz)ds\Big)^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

For the last inequality, we have used (
4.64
).
Note that

Dh^{n}(x,z)=Du^{n}((\psi^{n})^{-1}(x)+z)D(\psi^{n})^{-1}(x)-Du^{n}((\psi^{n})^{-1}(x))D(\psi^{n})^{-1}(x).

(4.121)

Arguing as in (4.15) in
[
76
]
, we obtain that
there exists some
\delta>0
and
\mathcal{K}(z)
with
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{0\}}\mathcal{K}^{p}(z)\,\nu(dz)<\infty
for any
p\geq 2
such that, for any
n\geq 1
,

[Dh^{n}(\cdot,z)]_{\delta}\leq\mathcal{K}(z),\ \ \ \forall z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{0\}.

(4.122)

Hence, for any
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}|J_{4}(s,y,n)|^{p}\leq

\displaystyle C_{p}\Big({\mathbb{E}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|Y^{n}_{s}(y)-Y_{s}(y)|^{2\delta}\,\mathcal{K}^{2}(z)\nu(dz)ds\Big)^{p}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+C_{p}\Big({\mathbb{E}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|Y^{n}_{s}(y)-Y_{s}(y)|^{p\delta}\,\mathcal{K}^{p}(z)\nu(dz)ds\Big)^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(4.123)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{p,T}\Big({\mathbb{E}}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}|Y^{n}_{s}(y)-Y_{s}(y)|^{2p\delta}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Using (
4.62
) it follows easily that

\displaystyle\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}|J_{4}(s,y,n)|^{p}\leq C_{p,T}\Big(\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}|Y^{n}_{s}(y)-Y_{s}(y)|^{2p\delta}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\to 0

as
n\to\infty
.

To estimate the other terms, we consider the compact set
K
. Let us estimate
J_{2}(t,y,n)
. For any
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|J_{2}(t,y,n)|^{p}

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\|D\tilde{b}^{n}(Y_{s}(y))-D\tilde{b}(Y_{s}(y))\|\|DY^{n}_{s}(y)\|\,ds\Big)^{p}

(4.124)

\displaystyle\leq C_{T}\Big({\mathbb{E}}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|DY_{s}^{n}(y)\|^{2p}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big({\mathbb{E}}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|D\tilde{b}^{n}(Y_{s}(y))-D\tilde{b}(Y_{s}(y))\|^{2p}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(4.125)

\displaystyle\leq C_{T}\Big({\mathbb{E}}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|D\tilde{b}^{n}(Y_{s}(y))-D\tilde{b}(Y_{s}(y))\|^{2p}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(4.126)

For the last inequality, we have used (
4.64
). Note that we have

\sup_{y\in K}{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|D\tilde{b}^{n}(Y_{s}(y))-D\tilde{b}(Y_{s}(y))\|^{2p}\leq{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\sup_{y\in K}\|D\tilde{b}^{n}(Y_{s}(y))-D\tilde{b}(Y_{s}(y))\|^{2p}.

Since we know that
\sup_{n\geq 1}\|D\tilde{b}^{n}\|_{0}+\|D\tilde{b}\|_{0}
is bounded, see (
4.92
), we can prove that

{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\sup_{y\in K}\|D\tilde{b}^{n}(Y_{s}(y))-D\tilde{b}(Y_{s}(y))\|^{2p}\to 0

as
n\to\infty
by the dominated convergence theorem. To this purpose, we shall apply the fact that, for
\mathbb{P}
-a.s
\omega
,

\sup_{y\in K}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|Y_{t}(y)(\omega)|=M(\omega)<\infty,

(4.127)

and

\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{|r|\leq M(\omega)}\|D\tilde{b}^{n}(r)-D\tilde{b}(r)\|=0,

see (
4.93
). It remains to prove (
4.127
).
We note that that by (
4.56
) we have,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s., for any
t\in[0,T]
,
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\psi(\phi_{t}(\psi^{-1}(y)))=Y_{t}(y).

(4.128)

Recall that
\phi_{t}(x)
solves the SDE (
3.9
) with
s=0
. Since
b
is globally bounded, we get that for
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.
\omega
, for any compact set
F\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
, there exists
M_{F,T}(\omega)\in(0,\infty)
such that

\sup_{x\in F}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\phi_{t}(x)(\omega)|=M_{F,T}(\omega).

Using also the continuity of
\psi
and
\psi^{-1}
we obtain (
4.127
). Finally we get

\sup_{y\in K}{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|J_{2}(t,y,n)|^{p}\leq C_{T}\Big({\mathbb{E}}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\sup_{y\in K}\|D\tilde{b}^{n}(Y_{s}(y))-D\tilde{b}(Y_{s}(y))\|^{2p}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\to 0

(4.129)

as
n\to\infty.

We still have to deal with
J_{5}(t,y,n)
. By (
4.64
), we have, for any
y\in K,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}|J_{5}(s,y,n)|^{p}\leq

\displaystyle C_{p}{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|Dh^{n}(Y_{s}(y),z)-Dh(Y_{s}(y),z)\|^{2}\|DY^{n}_{s}(y)\|^{2}\nu(dz)ds\Big)^{p/2}

(4.130)

\displaystyle+C_{p}{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|Dh^{n}(Y_{s}(y),z)-Dh(Y_{s}(y),z)\|^{p}\|DY^{n}_{s}(y)\|^{p}\nu(dz)ds\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{p}\Big({\mathbb{E}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\sup_{y\in K}\|Dh^{n}(Y_{s}(y),z)-Dh(Y_{s}(y),z)\|^{2}\nu(dz)ds\Big)^{p}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+C_{p}\Big({\mathbb{E}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\sup_{y\in K}\|Dh^{n}(Y_{s}(y),z)-Dh(Y_{s}(y),z)\|^{p}\nu(dz)ds\Big)^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

In order to get
\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{y\in K}{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}|J_{5}(s,y,n)|^{p}=0
we need to apply the dominated convergence theorem in the previous formula. To this purpose, we first note that by (
4.94
) for any compact set
K^{\prime}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
we have

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{x\in K^{\prime}}\|D{h}^{n}(x,z)-D{h}(x,z)\|=0,\;\;z\not=0.

(4.131)

And by (
4.18
), (
4.90
) and (
4.91
), there exists
\mathcal{L}(z)
such that

\sup_{n\geq 1}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|Dh^{n}(x,z)\|+\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|Dh(x,z)\|\leq\mathcal{L}(z),\ \ \ \forall z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{0\},

with
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{0\}}\mathcal{L}^{p}(z)\,\nu(dz)<\infty
for any
p\geq 2
. Therefore, we get,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.,
n\geq 1,

z\not=0,

s\in[0,T]
,
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d},

\|Dh^{n}(Y_{s}(y),z)-Dh(Y_{s}(y),z)\|^{2}\leq C\mathcal{L}^{2}(z).

Recall (
4.127
) that for
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.
\omega
,

\sup_{y\in K}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|Y_{t}(y)(\omega)|=M(\omega)<\infty.

(4.132)

Hence, for
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.
\omega
,
z\not=0,

s\in[0,T]
, we get

\sup_{y\in K}\|Dh^{n}(Y_{s}(y)(\omega),z)-Dh(Y_{s}(\omega)(y),z)\|^{2}\leq\sup_{x\in B_{M(\omega)}}\|Dh^{n}(x,z)-Dh(Y_{s}(x,z)\|^{2}\to 0

as
n\to\infty
. We can apply the dominated convergence theorem and get

\lim_{n\to\infty}{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\sup_{y\in K}\|Dh^{n}(Y_{s}(y),z)-Dh(Y_{s}(y),z)\|^{2}\nu(dz)ds\Big)^{p}=0.

Similarly, we get

\lim_{n\to\infty}{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\sup_{y\in K}\|Dh^{n}(Y_{s}(y),z)-Dh(Y_{s}(y),z)\|^{p}\nu(dz)ds\Big)^{2}=0.

We arrive at

\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{y\in K}{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}|J_{5}(s,y,n)|^{p}=0.

(4.133)

Finally let us come back to (
4
).
By the previous estimates on
J_{i}(t,y,n)
and the Gronwall lemma, we obtain assertion (
4.65
).
∎

Now let us prove (
4.66
).

By (
4.105
), we have, for any
y,y^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\|DY_{t}(y)-DY_{t}(y^{\prime})\|

(4.134)

\displaystyle\leq\|\int_{0}^{t}D\tilde{b}(Y_{s}(y))DY_{s}(y)-D\tilde{b}(Y_{s}(y^{\prime}))DY_{s}(y^{\prime})\,ds\|

\displaystyle\quad+\big\|\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}Dh(Y_{s-}(y),z)DY_{s-}(y)-Dh(Y_{s-}(y^{\prime}),z)DY_{s-}(y^{\prime})\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)\big\|

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\int_{0}^{t}D\tilde{b}(Y_{s}(y))DY_{s}(y)-D\tilde{b}(Y_{s}(y^{\prime}))DY_{s}(y)\,ds\big\|

\displaystyle\quad+\big\|\int_{0}^{t}D\tilde{b}(Y_{s}(y^{\prime}))DY_{s}(y)-D\tilde{b}(Y_{s}(y^{\prime}))DY_{s}(y^{\prime})\,ds\big\|

\displaystyle\quad+\big\|\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}Dh(Y_{s-}(y),z)DY_{s-}(y)-Dh(Y_{s-}(y^{\prime}),z)DY_{s-}(y)\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)\big\|

\displaystyle\quad+\big\|\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}Dh(Y_{s-}(y^{\prime}),z)DY_{s-}(y)-Dh(Y_{s-}(y^{\prime}),z)DY_{s-}(y^{\prime})\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)\big\|

\displaystyle\leq T[D\tilde{b}]_{\gamma}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|DY_{s}(y)\|\sup_{s\in[0,T]}|Y_{s}(y)-Y_{s}(y^{\prime})|^{\gamma}+\|D\tilde{b}\|_{0}\int_{0}^{t}\|DY_{s}(y)-DY_{s}(y^{\prime})\|\,ds

\displaystyle\quad+\big\|\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}Dh(Y_{s-}(y),z)DY_{s-}(y)-Dh(Y_{s-}(y^{\prime}),z)DY_{s-}(y)\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)\big\|

\displaystyle\quad+\big\|\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}Dh(Y_{s-}(y^{\prime}),z)DY_{s-}(y)-Dh(Y_{s-}(y^{\prime}),z)DY_{s-}(y^{\prime})\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)\big\|.

(4.135)

A similar argument to that used for the proof of (
4.112
) and (
4
) shows that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big\|\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}Dh(Y_{s-}(y^{\prime}),z)DY_{s-}(y)-Dh(Y_{s-}(y^{\prime}),z)DY_{s-}(y^{\prime})\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)\big\|^{p}

(4.136)

\displaystyle\leq C{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\|DY_{s}(y)-DY_{s}(y^{\prime})\|^{p}ds\Big),

(4.137)

where (
4.114
) has been used, and there exists
\delta>0
such that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big\|\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}Dh(Y_{s-}(y),z)DY_{s-}(y)-Dh(Y_{s-}(y^{\prime}),z)DY_{s-}(y)\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)\big\|^{p}

\displaystyle\leq C_{T}\Big({\mathbb{E}}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}|Y_{s}(y)-Y_{s}(y^{\prime})|^{2\delta p}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(4.138)

Combining (
4.64
), (
4.92
), (
4.111
), (
4.63
), and (
4.134
), (
4.136
), (
4
), and choosing
q>1
such that
\delta pq>2
, the Gronwall inequality and the
\rm H\ddot{o}lder
inequality show that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|DY_{t}(y)-DY_{t}(y^{\prime})\|^{p}\leq C\Big(|y-y^{\prime}|^{p\gamma}+|y-y^{\prime}|^{\delta p}\Big).

(4.139)

Here the constant
C
is independent on
y
and
y^{\prime}
.

The proof of (
4.66
) is complete.

∎

The proof of Theorem
4.1
is complete.
∎

If
\eta:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
is a
C^{1}
-diffeomorphism, we will denote by
J\eta(x)=\det[D\eta(x)]
its Jacobian determinant.

Lemma 4.6
.

Assume
{\operatorname{div}\,}b(\cdot)=0
in the sense of distributions. Then the
stochastic flow
\phi
is
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. measure-preserving, i.e.,

J\phi_{s,t}(x)=1
, for all
0\leq s\leq t\leq T
and all

x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s..

Proof.

This proof is adapted from the proof of
[
39
, Lemma 9]
and
[
40
]
.
Let
b_{n}
be
C_{\mathrm{b}}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})
vector fields that converges to

b
in
C_{\mathrm{b}}^{\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})
and such that

{\operatorname{div}\,}b_{n}=0
. The functions
b_{n}
can be
constructed as in (6) of
[
39
]
. Let
\phi^{n}
be the associated
smooth diffeomorphism. Applying
[
61
, Theorem 3.4]
and the
well known Liouville theorem, we get that the diffeomorphism

\phi^{n}
preserves the Lebsegue measure since
b_{n}
is a
divergenceless vector field.

Then
\det[D\phi_{s,t}^{n}(x)]=1
for all
0\leq s<t\leq T
and
all
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.. Fix
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
and
s\in[0,T]
. By
(
4.3
), there exists a subsequence (possibly depending on

x,s
and still denoted by
D\phi_{s,t}^{n}
) such that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.

\sup_{s\leq t\leq T}\|D\phi_{s,t}^{n}(x)-D\phi_{s,t}(x)\|^{2}\to 0,\;\;\mbox{as}\;\;n\to\infty.

We find that
J\phi_{s,t}(x)=1
, for every
t\in[s,T]
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.. Hence, by Theorem
3.23
,
\phi
is a measure-preserving diffeomorphism,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s..
∎

5
Definition and existence of weak solution to the stochastic transport equation

Let us fix
T>0.
Before introducing the definition of solution
u
of our transport equation, we point out two differences
with respect to the definition of solution given in
[
39
, Definition 12]
.

These differences are due to the fact that here we need to consider stochastic integrals with respect to compensated Poisson random measures of the following form

\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s-,x)\,[\theta(x+z)-\theta(x)]\,dx\Bigr)\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz),

(5.1)

where
\theta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
; see the next formula (
5.26
).

Let us clarify these two differences.

Differences 5.1
.

Instead of requiring, as in
[
39
]
, that
u\in L^{\infty}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\Omega)
,
where
L^{\infty}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\Omega)
is the set of equivalence classes of all
dt\otimes dx\otimes\mathbb{P}(d\omega)
essentially bounded functions, we only require that

u:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\Omega\to\mathbb{R}

is a
{\mathcal{B}}([0,T])\otimes{\mathcal{B}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\otimes{{\mathscr{F}}}/\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R})
-measurable function which is essentially bounded in the following sense (this is slightly stronger than requiring the usual essential boundedness).
We are assuming that there exists
M>0
such

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|u(t,\cdot,\omega)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq M,\quad\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.}

(5.2)

This means that there exists
M>0
and
a
\mathbb{P}
-full set
\Omega^{\prime}\subset\Omega
such that for any
t\in[0,T]
,
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime},

\displaystyle|u(t,x,\omega)|\leq M,\;\;\forall x\in A_{t,\omega},

(5.3)

where
A_{t,\omega}
is a Borel set of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that
\mathrm{Leb}(A_{t,\omega}^{c})=0
.

We require that,
for every test function
\theta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, the process
u_{\cdot}(\theta):\Omega\times[0,T]\to\mathbb{R}
defined by

u_{t}(\theta)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\theta(x)u(t,x)\,dx

(5.4)

is
\mathbb{F}
-adapted and it has càdlàg paths,
{\mathbb{P}}
-a.s. Recall that
\mathbb{F}:=(\mathscr{F}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
.

This is different from
[
39
, Definition 12]
where
u_{\cdot}(\theta)

is an equivalent class in
L^{\infty}(\Omega\times[0,T])
which
has an
\mathbb{F}
-adapted càdlàg modification (possibly depending on
\theta
).

But note that the exceptional set in item (ii) above may depend on the test function
\theta
.

Remark 5.2
.

It should be not difficult to prove that item (ii) above implies the following stronger one with

\theta\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

for every test function
\theta\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, the process
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\theta(x)u(t,x)\,dx
,
t\in[0,T]
is
\mathbb{F}
-adapted and with càdlàg paths,
{\mathbb{P}}
-a.s..
This is quite standard, thanks to (
5.2
).
If
\theta\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
(\theta_{n})
is a
C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
-valued
sequence convergent to
\theta
in
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, then
{\mathbb{P}}
-a.s,

u_{\cdot}(\theta_{n})\to u_{\cdot}(\theta)
uniformly on
[0,T]
. Therefore, the process
u_{\cdot}(\theta)
is
\mathbb{F}
-adapted, see Corollary (i) to Theorem 1.14 in
[
81
]
and
it has càdlàg paths
{\mathbb{P}}
-a.s.
Such a stronger version is needed because of, for instance, the second term in the second line of equality (
5.26
).

Beside items (i) and (ii) stated above, we also use the following two lemmata.

Lemma 5.3
.

Assume that a measurable and essentially bounded process

u:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\Omega\to\mathbb{R},

(5.5)

see (
5.2
),
satisfies the following condition, see (ii) in Differences
5.1
,
for any
\theta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, the process
u_{\cdot}(\theta):[0,T]\times\Omega\to\mathbb{R}

is
\mathbb{F}
-adapted and it has càdlàg paths,
{\mathbb{P}}
-a.s..

Then there exists a
\mathbb{P}
-full set
\Omega^{\prime}\subset\Omega
such that for every
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime}
,
for every test function
\theta\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,

the function

u_{\cdot}(\theta)(\omega):[0,T]\ni t\mapsto\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\theta(x)u(t,x,\omega)\,dx

(5.6)

is càdlàg.

Proof.

Since
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
is separable we find that
C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\subset L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
is separable as well. Now we argue similarly to Remark
5.2
.

Let us choose a countable dense subset
\mathcal{A}
of
C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with respect to the
L^{1}
-norm. Thus for any
\eta\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
there exists

(\theta_{n})_{n=1}^{\infty}\subset\mathcal{A}
such that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\theta_{n}(x)-\eta(x)|dx=0.

(5.7)

Then
there exists a
\mathbb{P}
-full set
\Omega^{\prime}\subset\Omega
such that for every
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime}
,
for every test function
\theta\in\mathcal{A}
, condition (iii) is satisfied.
Moreover we can assume that for any
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime}
property (
5.2
) holds.

Let us choose and fix an arbitrary
\eta\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and consider the
\mathbb{F}
-adapted process

u_{\cdot}(\eta)=\{[0,T]\ni t\mapsto u_{t}(\eta)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\eta(x)u(t,x,\cdot)\,dx\in\mathbb{R}\}

(5.8)

defined on our stochastic basis. This process exists by the assumptions of this lemma and Remark
5.2
. Let us choose
(\theta_{n})_{n=1}^{\infty}\subset\mathcal{A}
such that (
5.7
) holds.
For any
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime}
using (
5.2
) we have:

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|u_{t}(\theta_{n})(\omega)-u_{t}(\eta)(\omega)|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|u(t,x,\omega)|\,|\theta_{n}(x)-\eta(x)|dx

(5.9)

\displaystyle\leq M\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\theta_{n}(x)-\eta(x)|dx\to 0

as
n\to\infty
. Since
\big(u_{t}(\theta_{n})(\omega)\big)_{n\geq 1}
converges uniformly w.r.t.
t\in[0,T]
to
u_{t}(\eta)(\omega)
as
n\to\infty
and each function
[0,T]\ni t\mapsto u_{t}(\theta_{n})(\omega)
is càdlàg on
[0,T]

we infer that
t\mapsto u_{t}(\eta)(\omega)
is càdlàg as well.
∎

We prepare the next lemma with some considerations.

Remark 5.4
.

The previous Lemma
5.3
implies that for any
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime}
, any test function
\theta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
any
t\in[0,T)
:

\displaystyle\lim_{s\to t^{+}}u_{s}(\theta)(\omega)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\theta(x)u(t,x,\omega)dx=u_{t}(\theta)

and for any
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime}
, any test function
\theta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
any
t\in(0,T]
, there exists a finite limit

\lim_{s\to t^{-}}u_{s}(\theta)(\omega).

(5.10)

We denote such limit by
u_{t-}(\theta)(\omega)
. To simplify, with
some abuse of notation
we will sometimes write

u_{t-}(\theta)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\theta(x)u(t-,x)dx.

However, note that
u(t-,x,\omega)
is not well-defined.

It is clear that, for any test function
\theta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
the process
(u_{t-}(\theta))_{t\in(0,T]}
is
\mathbb{F}
-adapted and left-continuous with finite right-limits,
{\mathbb{P}}
-a.s.. In fact, for the path regularity one can consider
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime}
.

Let us fix
t\in(0,T]
and
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime}
. By Lemma
5.3
again,
for any
\theta\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, there exists a finite limit

\displaystyle\lim_{s\to t^{-}}u_{s}(\theta)(\omega)

which we still denote by
u_{t-}(\theta)(\omega).

Note that identifying the dual space
(L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))^{\prime}
with
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
we infer that

\{u_{s}(\cdot)(\omega):0\leq s<t\}\subset(L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))^{\prime}.

Hence, by the Banach–Steinhaus Theorem we deduce that
u_{t-}(\cdot)(\omega)\in(L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))^{\prime}
. It follows, see Theorem 6.16 in
[
81
]
, that there exists a unique element

g_{t,\omega}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

such that

u_{t-}(\theta)(\omega)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\theta(x)g_{t,\omega}(x)dx,\;\;\forall\theta\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

(5.11)

In particular, (
5.11
) holds for any test function
\theta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
t\in(0,T]
,
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime}.

But we do not assert any measurability of the function

(0,T]\times\Omega\ni(t,\omega)\mapsto g_{t,\omega}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. On the other hand,
\|g_{t,\omega}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq M
.

In order to give a precise meaning to stochastic integrals as in (
5.1
)
we prove
the following lemma. We will use the notation
\theta(\cdot+z)=\tau_{-z}\theta
and
the predictable
\sigma
-field
\mathcal{P}
on
[0,T]\times\Omega
.

Lemma 5.5
.

Let
T>0.
Assume that
\Omega^{\prime}
is the event from Lemma
5.3
and Remark
5.4
.
Let
\theta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Then the following assertions are satisfied.

For every
(t,\omega)\in(0,T]\times\Omega^{\prime}
, the function

\mathbb{R}^{d}\ni z\mapsto u_{t-}(\theta(\cdot+z))\in\mathbb{R}

is continuous.

The map

[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\Omega^{\prime}\ni(t,z,\omega)\mapsto u_{t-}(\theta(\cdot+z))(\omega)\in\mathbb{R}

(5.12)

is
\mathcal{P}\otimes{\mathcal{B}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})/{\mathcal{B}}(\mathbb{R})
-measurable.

Proof.

To prove (i), let us choose and fix
(t,\omega)\in(0,T]\times\Omega^{\prime}
. Using (
5.11
) the assertion follows by the dominated convergence theorem. Indeed, we have

u_{t-}(\theta(\cdot+z))=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}g_{t,\omega}(x)\theta(x+z)dx.

Now let us prove (ii). We first note that for any fixed
z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, the process :
[0,T]\times\Omega^{\prime}\to u_{t-}(\theta(\cdot+z))(\omega)
is
\mathbb{F}
-adapted and left-continuous with finite right-limits;
see Remark
5.4
.
Hence, by the definition of predictable
\sigma
-field, see for instance, page 21 in
[
49
]
,
we
infer that for any
z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, the function

\ [0,T]\times\Omega^{\prime}\ni(t,\omega)\mapsto u_{t-}(\theta(\cdot+z))(\omega)\in\mathbb{R}

is
\mathcal{P}
-measurable. Thus, by (i) and Definition 4.50 in
[
1
]
, this function is a Carathéodory function and therefore, by
Lemma 4.51 in
[
1
]
to the map (
5.12
)

(\omega,t,z)\mapsto u_{t-}(\theta(\cdot+z))(\omega)
,
we deduce the assertion.
∎

After this preparation, we introduce our definition of solutions.

5.1
Definition of solutions to the stochastic transport equation

The definition involves a general Lévy process
(L_{t})_{t\geq 0}
.
We take into account the discussion in Remark
5.4
.

Definition 5.6
.

Assume that
b\in L_{loc}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d})
satisfies
\operatorname{div}\,b\in L_{loc}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. If
u_{0}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
then
a weak
∗
-
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}
-solution to the stochastic transport equation problem (
2.28
)
is a
measurable and essentially bounded stochastic process
