u:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\Omega\to\mathbb{R}

(5.13)

in the sense that
for some
M>0
,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|u(t,\cdot,\omega)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq M,\quad\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.}

(5.14)

(cf. (
5.3
)) and the following two conditions are satisfied.

For every test function
\theta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, the process

u_{\cdot}(\theta):[0,T]\times\Omega\to\mathbb{R}
defined by

u_{t}(\theta)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\theta(x)u(t,x)\,dx,\;\;t\in[0,T],

(5.15)

is
\mathbb{F}
-adapted and càdlàg,
\mathbb{P}
-almost surely.

For every test function
\theta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, for every
t\in[0,T]
, the following identity is satisfied
\mathbb{P}
-almost surely,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(t,x)\,\theta(x)\,dx=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(x)\,\theta(x)\,dx+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s,x)\,[b(x)\cdot D\theta(x)+\operatorname{div}\,b(x)\,\theta(x)]\,dx\,ds

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{d}\int_{0}^{t}\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s-,x)D_{i}\theta(x)\,dx\Bigr)\diamond dL_{s}^{i}.

Let us emphasise that the exceptional set in item (ds2) may depend on both
\theta
and
t\in[0,T]
. On the other hand, the exceptional set in item (ds1) does not depend on
\theta
(cf. Lemma
5.3
).

Remark 5.7
.

Let us observe that
given a Borel measurable function
f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
, a function

\Phi(f):[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\ni(t,z,y)\to\Phi(t,z,f)(y):=f(y-tz)\in\mathbb{R}

(5.16)

is a
weak solution to the following transport equation

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\Phi}{\partial t}(t,z,f)=-\sum_{i=1}^{d}z_{i}D_{i}\Phi(t,z,f),

(5.17)

\displaystyle\Phi(0,z,f)=f\mbox{ on }\mathbb{R}^{d},

(5.18)

where

D_{i}\Phi(t,z,f)(y)
is the weak derivative of
\Phi(t,z,f)
at
y
in the
i
-th coordinate direction.

Let us denote

\Phi(z,f)(\cdot)=\Phi(1,z,f)(\cdot)=f(\cdot-z).

(5.19)

Thus, a strong version of the Marcus canonical integral in the stochastic transport equation
(
2.28
) is of the following form

\displaystyle-\sum_{i=1}^{d}e_{i}\cdot Du(s-,x)\diamond dL_{s}^{i}=

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\bigl[\Phi(z,u(s-))(x)-u(s-,x)\bigr]\,\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)

(5.20)

\displaystyle+\int_{B^{\mathrm{c}}}\bigl[\Phi(z,u(s-))(x)-u(s-,x)\bigr]\,\mu(ds,dz)

(5.21)

\displaystyle+\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\bigl[\Phi(z,u(s))(x)-u(s,x)+\sum_{i=1}^{d}z_{i}D_{i}u(s,x)\bigr]\,\nu(dz)\,ds,

(5.22)

where
B
is the unit ball in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, cf.
[
47
]
.
In other words, in case when
u
is a classical regular solution, equation (
2.28
) should be understood in the following way

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\frac{\partial{u(s,x)}}{\partial s}+b(x)\cdot{D}u(s,x)\,ds\\
=&\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\bigl[\Phi(z,u(s-))(x)-u(s-,x)\bigr]\,\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)+\int_{B^{\mathrm{c}}}\bigl[\Phi(z,u(s-))(x)-u(s-,x)\bigr]\,\mu(ds,dz)\\
&+\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\bigl[\Phi(z,u(s))(x)-u(s,x)+\sum_{i=1}^{d}z_{i}D_{i}u(s,x)\bigr]\,\nu(dz)\,ds,\\
&u(0,x)=u_{0}(x),\ \ x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.\end{aligned}

(5.23)

In what follows, we rewrite equation (
5.23
) in the weak form, and thus we get an equivalent version of Definition
5.6
.

Definition 5.8
.

A weak
∗
-
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}
-solution to the stochastic transport equation
(
2.28
) is

a measurable and essentially bounded function

u:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\Omega\to\mathbb{R}

(5.24)

in the sense that there exists
M>0
satisfying

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|u(t,\cdot,\omega)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq M,\quad\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.}

(5.25)

together with the following two additional conditions

for every test function
\theta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, the process
u_{t}(\theta)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\theta(x)u(t,x)\,dx
,
t\in[0,T]
is
\mathbb{F}
-adapted and it has càdlàg paths,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.;

for every test function
\theta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, the process
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\theta(x)u(t,x)\,dx
,
t\in[0,T]
, satisfies that, for every
t\in[0,T]
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(t,x)\,\theta(x)\,dx

(5.26)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(x)\,\theta(x)\,dx+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s,x)\,[b(x)\cdot D\theta(x)+\operatorname{div}\,b(x)\,\theta(x)]\,dx\,ds

(5.27)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s-,x)\,[\theta(x+z)-\theta(x)]\,dx\Bigr)\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)

(5.28)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{\mathrm{c}}}\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s-,x)\,[\theta(x+z)-\theta(x)]\,dx\Bigr)\mu(ds,dz)

(5.29)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s,x)\,[\theta(x+z)-\theta(x)-\sum_{i=1}^{d}z_{i}D_{i}\theta(x)]\,dx\Bigr)\,\nu(dz)\,ds.

(5.30)

The
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. means as usual that we have a

\mathbb{P}
-full set involved.
Actually, one could make this
\mathbb{P}
-full set independent of
t
and
\theta
by Lemma
5.3
.

Recall that
according to Remark
5.4
we should write more precisely

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s-,x)\,[\theta(x+z)-\theta(x)]\,dx=u_{s-}(\theta(\cdot+z)-\theta).

Remark 5.8
.

Following the terminology used in
[
39
]
we should have used a name “weak-
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}
solution. However we have decided to use a different name, i.e., weak
{}^{\ast}\text{-}\mathrm{L}^{\infty}
, since our
L^{\infty}
-valued process
u
is càdlàg with respect to the weak
∗
topology on
L^{\infty}
.

We also have the following notion of uniqueness of the stochastic transport equation.

Definition 5.9
.

We say that uniqueness holds for the stochastic transport equation if for a fixed
u_{0}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

given any two weak
∗
-
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}
-solutions
u^{1}
and
u^{2}

we have:

for any test function
\theta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, it holds:

u^{1}_{t}(\theta)=u^{2}_{t}(\theta),

\mathbb{P}
-a.s., for any
t\in[0,T]
.

Let us recall that by
\theta^{\prime}(a)=d_{a}\theta\in\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R})
we denote the Fréchet derivative of
\theta
at
a\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, see
[
24
]
. By
D\theta(a)
or
\nabla\theta(a)
, we denote the gradient of
\theta
at
a\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, i.e., the unique element of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that

\theta^{\prime}(a)(y)=\langle D\theta(a),y\rangle=\langle\nabla\theta(a),y\rangle,\;\;y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

5.2
Existence of weak solution to the stochastic transport equation

The following theorem is one of the main results of our paper. It is a result about the existence
of weak solutions given by the stochastic flow. We will also assume some additional integrability condition on
\operatorname{div}b

(interpreted in the distributional sense).

Theorem 5.10
.

Assume that
\alpha\in[1,2)
or
\alpha\in(0,1)
. Assume that
L=(L_{t})_{t\geq 0}
is a Lévy process satisfying Hypotheses
1
in the former case or Hypothesis
3
in the latter case. Assume that
\beta\in(0,1)
satisfies condition (
2.25
) and

b\in C_{\mathrm{b}}^{\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})
is such that

\operatorname{div}b\in L^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

If a function
u_{0}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
is Borel measurable and essentially bounded, then the stochastic process
u
defined by the following formula

u(t,x,\omega):=u_{0}(\phi_{t}^{-1}(\omega)x),\;\;t\in[0,T],x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

(5.31)

is a weak
∗
-
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}
-solution to the problem (
1.1
) in the sense of Definition
5.8
.

Remark 5.11
.

By applying arguments similar to those in Lemma
3.6
, our solutions to the stochastic transport equation and the associated stochastic flow can be extended to the infinite time horizon
t\geq 0
. However, to simplify the presentation, we will focus on the interval
[0,T]
, for an arbitrary
T>0
.

Proof of Theorem
5.10
.

Let
T>0.
Let us define a random field
u
by formula (
5.31
).
It is obvious that

u:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\Omega\to\mathbb{R}

is a
{\mathcal{B}}([0,T])\otimes{\mathcal{B}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\otimes{{\mathscr{F}}}/\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R})
-measurable function using the measurability properties of our stochastic flow given in Section
3
.

We prove directly that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|u(t,\cdot,\omega)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq M,\quad\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.}

(5.32)

holds. This gives that
condition (o) of Definition
5.8
is verified.

Since
u_{0}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d}
is essentially bounded, we have

|u_{0}(y)|\leq M\quad\text{for a.e. }y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

Consider the set where
u_{0}
is bounded:

E=\{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:|u_{0}(y)|\leq M\}.

Then
\mathrm{Leb}(E^{c})=0
. To simplify notation let us define

h_{t}(x)(\omega)=\phi_{t}^{-1}(x)(\omega),

so that we are considering
u(\omega,t,x):=u_{0}(h_{t}(x)(\omega)),\;\;t\in[0,T],x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\omega\in\Omega.

Since by Theorem
3.8
, there exists an almost sure event
\Omega^{\prime}
such that for every
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime}
,
t\in[0,T]
, the mapping
x\mapsto h_{t}(x)(\omega)
is a homeomorphism, we infer the preimage

B_{t,\omega}:=[h_{t}(\omega)]^{-1}(E^{c})=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:h_{t}(\omega)(x)\in E^{c}\}

has Lebesgue measure zero. That is
\mathrm{Leb}(B_{t,\omega})=0
, for every
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime}
and
t\in[0,T]
. For each
t\in[0,T]
,
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime}
, let
A_{t,\omega}:=B_{t,\omega}^{c}
. Then for all
x\in A_{t,\omega}
,
|u(t,x,\omega)|\leq M
. Consequently,
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|u(t,\cdot,\omega)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq M
, for all
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime}
. So

u
satisfies condition (o) of Definition
5.8
.

Moreover,
by the Change of Measure Theorem, we infer that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(t,x)\theta(x)\,dx=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(y)\theta(\phi_{t}(y))J_{t}(y)\,dy,

(5.33)

for any test function
\theta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
where
J_{t}
is the corresponding Jacobian determinant

J_{t}(y)=\det[D\phi_{t}(y)],\;\;(t,y)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}.

(5.34)

By using the properties of the processes
\phi_{t}(y)
and
J_{t}(y)
proved before, see Theorems
3.5
and
3.23
, this easily will yield condition (i) in Definition
5.8
.

We will show the
\mathbb{R}
-valued process

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\theta(x)u(t,x)\,dx,\;\;\;t\geq 0,

satisfies equality (
5.26
). For this purpose, let us introduce some standard techniques and notation. First of all we set

B(r):=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\;|x|<r\},\;\;r>0.

Secondly, let
\bar{\vartheta}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to[0,1]\subset\mathbb{R}
be a symmetric
C^{\infty}_{c}
-class function such that

\mathds{1}_{B(\frac{1}{4})}\leq\bar{\vartheta}\leq\mathds{1}_{B(2)}\mbox{ and }\quad c_{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bar{\vartheta}(x)dx=1,

(5.35)

where
c_{d}
is a positive constant dependent only on
d
.

Define
\vartheta(x)=c_{d}\,\bar{\vartheta}(x)
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. We then introduce an approximation of functions
(\vartheta_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon>0}
defined by the following formula

\displaystyle\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(x)=\,\varepsilon^{-d}\vartheta(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}),\;\;x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\;\;\varepsilon>0.

(5.36)

Next we define a
C_{\mathrm{b}}^{\infty}
approximation
b^{\varepsilon}
of the vector field
b
by formula

\displaystyle b^{\varepsilon}=b\ast\vartheta_{\varepsilon},\mbox{ i.e., }b^{\varepsilon}(x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}b(x-y)\,\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y)\,dy,\;\;\;x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

(5.37)

For the completeness of our exposition, let us state the following result, which holds because
b\in C_{\mathrm{bu}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, i.e.,
b
is bounded and uniformly continuous, and
\operatorname{div}b\in L^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

Lemma 5.12
.

Using the notation introduced above, we have

b^{\varepsilon}\to b\mbox{ in }\;C_{\mathrm{b}}^{\beta^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d}).

(5.38)

with
0<\beta^{\prime}<\beta

and

\operatorname{div}b^{\varepsilon}\to\operatorname{div}b\mbox{ in }L^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

(5.39)

In the sequel, we will assume that, cf. Remark
4.2
,

\displaystyle\beta^{\prime}+\frac{\alpha}{2}>1.

Let
\phi^{\varepsilon}_{s,t}
be the stochastic flow associated with the equation (
2.34
) with the drift
b
replaced by
b^{\varepsilon}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\phi^{\varepsilon}_{s,t}(y)=y+\int_{s}^{t}b^{\varepsilon}(\phi_{s,r}^{\varepsilon}(y))\,dr+L_{t}-L_{s},\;\;t\geq s,\;\;y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

(5.40)

Denote by
(\phi^{\varepsilon}_{s,t})^{-1}
the inverse of
\phi^{\varepsilon}_{s,t}
. Then similar arguments as proving (
3.27
), we have

(\phi^{\varepsilon}_{s,t})^{-1}(y)=y-\int_{s}^{t}b^{\varepsilon}((\phi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})^{-1}(y))dr-(L_{t}-L_{s}),\;\;0\leq s\leq t.

(5.41)

Finally, let
J_{s,t}^{\varepsilon}
and
(J^{\varepsilon}_{s,t})^{-1}
be the corresponding Jacobian to
\phi^{\varepsilon}_{s,t}
and
(\phi^{\varepsilon}_{s,t})^{-1}
, i.e.,

J^{\varepsilon}_{s,t}(y)=\det[D\phi^{\varepsilon}_{s,t}(y)],\;\;(t,y)\in[s,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{d},

(5.42)

(J^{\varepsilon}_{s,t})^{-1}(y)=\det[D(\phi^{\varepsilon}_{s,t})^{-1}(y)],\;\;(s,y)\in[0,t]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}.

(5.43)

We begin with the following lemma which is the stochastic version of
[
24
, Theorem 3.6.1]
.

Lemma 5.13
.

There exists a
\mathbb{P}
-full set
\Omega^{(0)}
such that
the following equality holds for every
\omega\in\Omega^{(0)}
and every
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
, for any fixed
s\geq 0
,

\displaystyle J_{s,t}^{\varepsilon}(x)=1+\int_{s}^{t}\operatorname{div}b^{\varepsilon}(\phi^{\varepsilon}_{s,r}(x))\,J_{s,r}^{\varepsilon}(x)\,dr,\;\;t\geq s,\;x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},

(5.44)

and for any fixed
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle(J^{\varepsilon}_{s,t})^{-1}(x)=1-\int_{s}^{t}\operatorname{div}b^{\varepsilon}((\phi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})^{-1}(x))\,(J^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})^{-1}(x)\,dr,\;\;s\in[0,t],\;x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

(5.45)

Proof.

Here we only prove (
5.44
) with
s=0
, and the other cases are similar.

Since the noise is additive, this lemma can be proved in an elementary way by
the
\omega
-wise application of the classical deterministic results to the equation

\rho_{t}^{\varepsilon}(x)=x+\int_{0}^{t}g^{\varepsilon}(s,\rho_{s}^{\varepsilon}(x))ds,

(5.46)

where

\rho_{t}^{\varepsilon}(x):=\phi^{\varepsilon}_{0,t}(x)-L_{t},\;\;g^{\varepsilon}(t,x):=b^{\varepsilon}(x+L_{t}).

(5.47)

We can deduce from the above that

J_{0,t}^{\varepsilon}(x)=\det[D\rho_{t}^{\varepsilon}(x)]\mbox{ and }\operatorname{div}g^{\varepsilon}(t,x)=\operatorname{div}b^{\varepsilon}(x+L_{t}).

(5.48)

and hence the result follows.
∎

Now, let
\phi^{\varepsilon}_{t}=\phi^{\varepsilon}_{0,t}
and
J_{t}^{\varepsilon}=J_{0,t}^{\varepsilon}
, i.e.,

J^{\varepsilon}_{t}(y)=\det[D\phi^{\varepsilon}_{t}(y)],\;\;(t,y)\in[0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{d}.

(5.49)

In the following result we assume that
\Upsilon
is countable subset of
(0,1]
whose boundary
\partial(\Upsilon)\ni 0
, and the boundary of every infinite subset
of
\Upsilon
also contains
\{0\}
. When we write
\lim_{\varepsilon\in\Upsilon}
we mean
\lim_{\Upsilon\ni\varepsilon\to 0}
.

Lemma 5.14
.

There exists a
\mathbb{P}
-full set
\Omega^{(1)}\subset\Omega^{(0)}
such that
the following equality holds for every
\omega\in\Omega^{(1)}
,
and all
t\geq 0
,
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\varepsilon\in\Upsilon
,

\displaystyle\theta(\phi^{\varepsilon}_{t}(y))J_{t}^{\varepsilon}(y)=

\displaystyle\,\theta(y)+\int_{0}^{t}b^{\varepsilon}_{s}(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y))\cdot D\theta(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y))J_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y)\,ds

(5.50)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\,\theta(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y))\operatorname{div}b_{s}^{\varepsilon}(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y))J_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y)ds

(5.51)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\big[\theta(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y)+z)-\theta(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y))-\sum_{i=1}^{d}z_{i}D_{i}\theta(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y))\big]{J_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y)}\;\nu(dz)\,ds

(5.52)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}\big[\theta(\phi_{s-}^{\varepsilon}(y)+z)-\theta(\phi_{s-}^{\varepsilon}(y))\big]{J_{s-}^{\varepsilon}(y)}\mu(ds,dz)

(5.53)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\big[\theta(\phi_{s-}^{\varepsilon}(y)+z)-\theta(\phi_{s-}^{\varepsilon}(y))\big]{J_{s-}^{\varepsilon}(y)}\;\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz).

(5.54)

The set
\Omega^{(1)}
depends on the function
\theta
.

Proof of Lemma
5.14
.

It is enough to apply the classical Itô formula, see, for instance,
[
49
, Theorem II.5.1]
,
to the
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}
-valued process
(\phi^{\varepsilon}_{t}(y),J_{t}^{\varepsilon}(y))

and a function
F
defined by

F:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}\ni(x,p)\mapsto\theta(x)p\in\mathbb{R}.

∎

Since the set
K:=\textrm{supp }(\theta)
is compact in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, by Corollary
3.9
and Theorem
3.8
, there is a
\mathbb{P}
-full set (without loss of generality, denoted by
\Omega^{(1)}
, which is the same set as in Lemma
5.14
) such that, for all
(\varepsilon,T,\omega)\in\Upsilon\times[0,\infty)\times\Omega^{(1)}
,
\cup_{t\in[0,T]}\textrm{supp }(\theta\circ\phi^{\varepsilon}_{t})=\cup_{t\in[0,T]}(\phi_{t}^{\varepsilon})^{-1}(K)
is bounded.
Also notice that Theorem
3.19
implies that

[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\ni(s,y)\longmapsto J_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y)\in\mathbb{R}

(5.55)

is continuous, and
b^{\varepsilon}\in C_{\mathrm{b}}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
Therefore, every term of the equality (
5.52
) multiplying
u_{0}(y)
is integrable over
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, since
u_{0}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

Next, by multiplying the equality above by
u_{0}(y)
and then integrating over
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, using some classical arguments we deduce that, for every
\omega\in\Omega^{(1)}
, and all
t\geq 0
,
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\varepsilon\in\Upsilon
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(y)\,\theta(\phi^{\varepsilon}_{t}(y))J_{t}^{\varepsilon}(y)\,dy

(5.56)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(y)\,\theta(y)\,dy+\int_{0}^{t}\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(y)\big[b^{\varepsilon}(\phi^{\varepsilon}_{s}(y))\cdot D\theta(\phi^{\varepsilon}_{s}(y))+\theta(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y))\operatorname{div}b^{\varepsilon}(\phi^{\varepsilon}_{s}(y))\big]J_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y)\,dy\Bigr)ds

(5.57)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(y)\big[\theta(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y)+z)-\theta(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y))-\sum_{i=1}^{d}z_{i}D_{i}\theta(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y))\big]J_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y)dy\;\nu(dz)\,ds

(5.58)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(y)\big[\theta(\phi_{s-}^{\varepsilon}(y)+z)-\theta(\phi_{s-}^{\varepsilon}(y))\big]J_{s-}^{\varepsilon}(y)dy\;\mu(ds,dz)

(5.59)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(y)\big[\theta(\phi_{s-}^{\varepsilon}(y)+z)-\theta(\phi_{s-}^{\varepsilon}(y))\big]J_{s-}^{\varepsilon}(y)dy\;\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz),

(5.60)

with
D\theta(x)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
being the gradient of
\theta
at
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

Next, we will list and prove a series of Lemmata that we will need in order to pass with
\varepsilon\searrow 0
.
All the convergence results from Lemmata
5.16
-
5.22
are then applied along a limit over an infinite subset
\tilde{\Upsilon}\subset\Upsilon
to obtain the following limit

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(y)\,\theta(\phi_{t}(y))J_{t}(y)\,dy

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(y)\,\theta(y)\,dy+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(y)\Bigl(b(\phi_{s}(y))\cdot D\theta(\phi_{s}(y))+\theta(\phi_{s}(y))\operatorname{div}\;b(\phi_{s}(y))\Bigr)J_{s}(y)\,dyds

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(y)\big[\theta(\phi_{s}(y)+z)-\theta(\phi_{s}(y))-\sum_{i=1}^{d}z_{i}D_{i}\theta(\phi_{s}(y))\big]J_{s}(y)dy\;\nu(dz)\,ds

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(y)\big[\theta(\phi_{s-}(y)+z)-\theta(\phi_{s-}(y))\big]J_{s-}(y)dy\;\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(y)\big[\theta(\phi_{s-}(y)+z)-\theta(\phi_{s-}(y))\big]J_{s-}(y)dy\;\mu(ds,dz).

By changing variable
y=\phi_{t}^{-1}(x)
or
y=\phi_{s}^{-1}(x)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(\phi_{t}^{-1}(x))\,\theta(x)\,dx

(5.61)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(x)\,\theta(x)\,dx+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(\phi_{s}^{-1}(x))\Bigl(b(x)\cdot D\theta(x)+\theta(x)\operatorname{div}\;b(x)\Bigr)\,dxds

(5.62)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(\phi_{s}^{-1}(x))\big[\theta(x+z)-\theta(x)-\sum_{i=1}^{d}z_{i}D_{i}\theta(x)\big]dx\;\nu(dz)\,ds

(5.63)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(\phi_{s-}^{-1}(x))\big[\theta(x+z)-\theta(x)\big]dx\;\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)

(5.64)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(\phi_{s-}^{-1}(x))\big[\theta(x+z)-\theta(x)\big]dx\;\mu(ds,dz)

(5.65)

which shows that
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(\phi_{t}^{-1}(x))\,\theta(x)\,dx
satisfies (
5.26
).
∎

Of course, in order to fully complete the proof of Theorem
5.10
we need to prove the remaining results.

Notation 5.15
.

In what follows we put
\varepsilon_{\infty}=0
so that in particular
\phi^{\varepsilon_{\infty}}_{t}=\phi_{t}^{0}=\phi_{t}
and
b^{\varepsilon_{\infty}}=b^{0}=b
.

The proofs of the following two Lemmata are postponed till Appendix
E
.

Let us recall that in the following parts of our paper, whenever we mention
\mathbb{P}
-full set, we mean a set which may depend on
\theta
. If this is not the case, we will mention this explicitly.

Lemma 5.16
.

There exists a
\mathbb{P}
-full set
\Omega^{(iv)}\subset\Omega^{(1)}
and an infinite set
\Upsilon_{1}\subset\Upsilon
such that for
every
\omega\in\Omega^{(iv)}
and every
T>0
,

\lim_{\varepsilon\in\Upsilon_{1}\rightarrow 0}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(y)\,\theta(\phi^{\varepsilon}_{t}(y))J_{t}^{\varepsilon}(y)\,dy-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(y)\,\theta(\phi_{t}(y))J_{t}(y)\,dy\Big|=0.

(5.66)

Proof of Lemma
5.16
.

We refer to Appendix
E
which is based on Corollary
4.3
, see also Remark
4.2
.
∎

Remark 5.17
.

Let us observe that Lemma
5.16
implies that for every
\omega\in\Omega^{(iv)}
and every
T>0
,

u_{0}\circ(\phi^{\varepsilon}_{t})^{-1}\to u_{0}\circ\phi_{t}^{-1},\text{ as }\varepsilon\in\Upsilon_{1}\rightarrow 0,

weakly
∗
in
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, uniformly in
t\in[0,T]
.

Indeed, for every
\theta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
we
have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big|\int_{{}{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}(u_{0}\circ(\phi^{\varepsilon}_{t})^{-1})(x)\theta(x)dx-\int_{{}{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}(u_{0}\circ(\phi_{t})^{-1})(x)\theta(x)dx\Big|

(5.67)

\displaystyle=\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big|\int_{{}{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}u_{0}(y)\theta(\phi^{{\varepsilon}}_{t}(y))J_{t}^{\varepsilon}(y)dy-\int_{{}{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}u_{0}(y)\theta(\phi_{t}(y))J_{t}(y)dy\Big|\to 0\text{ as }\varepsilon\in\Upsilon_{1}\rightarrow 0.

(5.68)

Since the space
C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
is dense in
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
by employing the classical density argument we can prove that the
convergence (
5.67
) holds for every
\theta\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

Lemma 5.18
.

For every
T>0
and every
\omega\in\Omega^{(iv)}
,
where
\Omega^{(iv)}
and
\Upsilon_{1}
have been introduced in Lemma
5.16
, the following holds

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\in\Upsilon_{1}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}

\displaystyle\Bigl|\int_{0}^{t}\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(y)b^{\varepsilon}(\phi^{\varepsilon}_{s}(y))\cdot D\theta(\phi^{\varepsilon}_{s}(y))J_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y)\,dy\Bigr)\,ds

(5.69)

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{t}\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(y)b(\phi_{s}(y))\cdot D\theta(\phi_{s}(y))J_{s}(y)\,dy\Bigr)\,ds\Bigr|=0.

Proof of Lemma
5.18
.

See Appendix
E
.

∎

Lemma 5.19
.

There exists a
\mathbb{P}
-full set
\Omega^{(v)}\subset\Omega^{(iv)}
and an infinite set
\Upsilon_{2}\subset\Upsilon_{1}
such that
for every
\omega\in\Omega^{(v)}
and every
T>0
,

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\in\Upsilon_{2}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(y)\theta(\phi^{\varepsilon}_{s}(y))\operatorname{div}\,b^{\varepsilon}(\phi^{\varepsilon}_{s}(y))J_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y)\,dyds

(5.70)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(y)\theta(\phi_{s}(y))\operatorname{div}\,b(\phi_{s}(y))J_{s}(y)\,dyds\Big|=0.

(5.71)

Proof of Lemma
5.19
.

In the proof below we set

K:=\textrm{supp }\theta.

(5.72)

Obverse that

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(y)\theta(\phi^{\varepsilon}_{s}(y))\operatorname{div}\,b^{\varepsilon}(\phi^{\varepsilon}_{s}(y))J_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y)\,dyds-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(y)\theta(\phi_{s}(y))\operatorname{div}\,b(\phi_{s}(y))J_{s}(y)\,dyds\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{K}u_{0}((\phi^{\varepsilon}_{s})^{-1}(x))\theta(x)\operatorname{div}\,b^{\varepsilon}(x)\,dxds-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{K}u_{0}((\phi^{\varepsilon}_{s})^{-1}(x))\theta(x)\operatorname{div}\,b(x)\,dxds\Big|

\displaystyle\quad+\Big|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{K}[u_{0}((\phi^{\varepsilon}_{s})^{-1}(x))-u_{0}((\phi_{s})^{-1}(x))]\theta(x)\operatorname{div}\,b(x)\,dxds\Big|.

Now since
u_{0}
is bounded and
\operatorname{div}b^{\varepsilon}\to\operatorname{div}b
in

L^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

by (
5.39
) from Lemma
5.12
, we infer that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{K}u_{0}((\phi^{\varepsilon}_{s})^{-1}(x))\theta(x)\operatorname{div}\,b^{\varepsilon}(x)\,dxds-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{K}u_{0}((\phi^{\varepsilon}_{s})^{-1}(x))\theta(x)\operatorname{div}\,b(x)\,dxds\Big|\to 0.

(5.73)

On the other hand, using Remark
5.17
, we find that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{K}[u_{0}((\phi^{\varepsilon}_{s})^{-1}(x))-u_{0}((\phi_{s})^{-1}(x))]\theta(x)\operatorname{div}\,b(x)\,dxds\Big|\to 0.

(5.74)

The proof is complete.
∎

Lemma 5.20
.

There exists
\mathbb{P}
-full set
\Omega^{(vi)}\subset\Omega^{(v)}
and an infinite set
\Upsilon_{3}\subset\Upsilon_{2}
such that for every
T\geq 0
and every
\omega\in\Omega^{(vi)}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\in\Upsilon_{3}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big|\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(y)\big[\theta(\phi_{s-}^{\varepsilon}(y)+z)-\theta(\phi_{s-}^{\varepsilon}(y))\big]J_{s-}^{\varepsilon}(y)dy\;\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(y)\big[\theta(\phi_{s-}(y)+z)-\theta(\phi_{s-}(y))\big]J_{s-}(y)dy\;\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)\Big|=0.

Proof of Lemma
5.20
.

Set
K^{1}=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\mathrm{dist}(K,\{x\})\leq 1\}
. Then we can find
R_{0}>0
such that
K^{1}\subset B_{R_{0}}:=\{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:|y|\leq R_{0}\}
. By the Burkholder inequality, see Proposition
2.7
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big|\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(y)\big[\theta(\phi_{s-}^{\varepsilon}(y)+z)-\theta(\phi_{s-}^{\varepsilon}(y))\big]J_{s-}^{\varepsilon}(y)dy\;\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)

(5.75)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad-\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(y)\big[\theta(\phi_{s-}(y)+z)-\theta(\phi_{s-}(y))\big]J_{s-}(y)dy\;\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)\Big|^{2}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big|\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\int_{K^{1}}u_{0}((\phi_{s-}^{\varepsilon})^{-1}(x))\big[\theta(x+z)-\theta(x)\big]dx\;\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad-\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\int_{K^{1}}u_{0}(\phi_{s-}^{-1}(x))\big[\theta(x+z)-\theta(x)\big]dx\;\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)\Big|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Big|\int_{K^{1}}\big[u_{0}((\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon})^{-1}(x))-u_{0}(\phi_{s}^{-1}(x))\big]\big[\theta(x+z)-\theta(x)\big]dx\Big|^{2}\nu(dz)ds\Big).

By Remark
5.17
, on
\Omega^{(iv)}

\displaystyle\int_{K^{1}}\big[u_{0}((\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon})^{-1}(x))-u_{0}(\phi_{s}^{-1}(x))\big]\big[\theta(x+z)-\theta(x)\big]dx\to 0,

(5.76)

for every
z\in B
, uniformly w.r.t.
s\in[0,T]
.

Moreover, with
C_{0}=\mathrm{Leb}(B_{R_{0}})
, we also have the following estimate: for any
(z,s)\in B\times[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{K^{1}}\big[u_{0}((\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon})^{-1}(x))-u_{0}(\phi_{s}^{-1}(x))\big]\big[\theta(x+z)-\theta(x)\big]dx\Big|^{2}\leq 4C_{0}^{2}\|u_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\|D\theta\|^{2}_{0}|z|^{2}.

(5.77)

Since
\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{2}\nu(dz)<\infty
, we can finally apply the Lebesgue DCT and deduce that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Big|\int_{K^{1}}\big[u_{0}((\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon})^{-1}(x))-u_{0}(\phi_{s}^{-1}(x))\big]\big[\theta(x+z)-\theta(x)\big]dx\Bigr|^{2}\nu(dz)ds\Big)\to 0.

(5.78)

Since convergence in the mean implies the a.s. convergence of a subsequence,
the result follows.
∎

Lemma 5.21
.

There exists
\mathbb{P}
-full set
\Omega^{(vii)}\subset\Omega^{(vi)}
and an infinite set
\Upsilon_{4}\subset\Upsilon_{3}
such that for all
T\geq 0
and every
\omega\in\Omega^{(vii)}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\in\Upsilon_{4}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(y)\big[\theta(\phi_{s-}^{\varepsilon}(y)+z)-\theta(\phi_{s-}^{\varepsilon}(y))\big]J_{s-}^{\varepsilon}(y)dy\;\mu(ds,dz)

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(y)\big[\theta(\phi_{s-}(y)+z)-\theta(\phi_{s-}(y))\big]J_{s-}(y)dy\;\mu(ds,dz)\Big|=0.

Proof of Lemma
5.21
.

Using similar argument of proving (
5.75
), we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(y)\big[\theta(\phi_{s-}^{\varepsilon}(y)+z)-\theta(\phi_{s-}^{\varepsilon}(y))\big]J_{s-}^{\varepsilon}(y)dy\;\mu(ds,dz)

(5.79)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(y)\big[\theta(\phi_{s-}(y)+z)-\theta(\phi_{s-}(y))\big]J_{s-}(y)dy\;\mu(ds,dz)\Big|

(5.80)

\displaystyle=\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}((\phi_{s-}^{\varepsilon})^{-1}(x))\big[\theta(x+z)-\theta(x)\big]dx\;\mu(ds,dz)

(5.81)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(\phi_{s-}^{-1}(x))\big[\theta(x+z)-\theta(x)\big]dx\;\mu(ds,dz)\Big|

(5.82)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\int_{B^{c}}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}((\phi_{s-}^{\varepsilon})^{-1}(x))\big[\theta(x+z)-\theta(x)\big]dx

(5.83)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(\phi_{s-}^{-1}(x))\big[\theta(x+z)-\theta(x)\big]dx\Big|\;\mu(ds,dz).

(5.84)

Let us fix
z\in B^{c}
, and set
K^{1}_{z}=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\mathrm{dist}(K,\{x\})\leq|z|+1\}
. Since by Remark
5.17
, on
\Omega^{(iv)}
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big[u_{0}((\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon})^{-1}(x))-u_{0}(\phi_{s}^{-1}(x))\big]\big[\theta(x+z)-\theta(x)\big]dx

(5.85)

\displaystyle=\int_{K^{1}_{z}}\big[u_{0}((\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon})^{-1}(x))-u_{0}(\phi_{s}^{-1}(x))\big]\big[\theta(x+z)-\theta(x)\big]dx\to 0,

(5.86)

uniformly w.r.t.
s\in[0,T]
, by recalling that

\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}|F_{\varepsilon}(s,z)|\mu(ds,dz)=\sum_{0<s\leq t\;}|F_{\varepsilon}(s,\triangle L_{s})|\mathds{1}_{B^{c}}(\triangle L_{s})

as a random finite sum, with

\displaystyle F_{\varepsilon}(s,z)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big[u_{0}((\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon})^{-1}(x))-u_{0}(\phi_{s}^{-1}(x))\big]\big[\theta(x+z)-\theta(x)\big]dx,

(5.87)

we deduce
the assertion.
∎

Lemma 5.22
.

There exists
\mathbb{P}
-full set
\Omega^{(viii)}\subset\Omega^{(vii)}
and an infinite set
\Upsilon_{5}\subset\Upsilon_{4}

such that for all
T\geq 0
and every
\omega\in\Omega^{(viii)}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\in\Upsilon_{5}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big|\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(y)\big[\theta(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y)+z)-\theta(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y))-\sum_{i=1}^{d}z_{i}D_{i}\theta(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y))\big]J_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y)dy\,\nu(dz)\,ds

(5.88)

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(y)\big[\theta(\phi_{s}(y)+z)-\theta(\phi_{s}(y))-\sum_{i=1}^{d}z_{i}D_{i}\theta(\phi_{s}(y))\big]J_{s}(y)dy\,\nu(dz)\,ds\Big|=0.

Proof of Lemma
5.22
.

We use notations introduced in the proof of Lemma
5.20
. Arguing as before we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big|\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(y)\big[\theta(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y)+z)-\theta(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y))-\sum_{i=1}^{d}z_{i}D_{i}\theta(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y))\big]J_{s}^{\varepsilon_{n}}(y)dy\;\nu(dz)\,ds

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad-\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(y)\big[\theta(\phi_{s}(y)+z)-\theta(\phi_{s}(y))-\sum_{i=1}^{d}z_{i}D_{i}\theta(\phi_{s}(y))\big]J_{s}(y)dy\;\nu(dz)\,ds\Big|

\displaystyle=\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big|\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}((\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon})^{-1}(x))\big[\theta(x+z)-\theta(x)-\sum_{i=1}^{d}z_{i}D_{i}\theta(x)\big]dx\;\nu(dz)\,ds

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad-\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(\phi_{s}^{-1}(x))\big[\theta(x+z)-\theta(x)-\sum_{i=1}^{d}z_{i}D_{i}\theta(x)\big]dx\;\nu(dz)\,ds\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Big|\int_{K^{1}}[u_{0}((\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon})^{-1}(x))-u_{0}(\phi_{s}^{-1}(x))]\big[\theta(x+z)-\theta(x)-\sum_{i=1}^{d}z_{i}D_{i}\theta(x)\big]dx\Big|\nu(dz)\,ds.

Let us fix
z\in B
. By Remark
5.17
, we have on
\Omega^{(iv)}
,

\displaystyle\int_{K^{1}}\big[u_{0}((\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon})^{-1}(x))-u_{0}(\phi_{s}^{-1}(x))\big]\big[\theta(x+z)-\theta(x)-\sum_{i=1}^{d}z_{i}D_{i}\theta(x)\big]dx\to 0,

(5.89)

uniformly w.r.t.
s\in[0,T]
.
We also have the estimate, for
z\in B
and
s\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{K^{1}}\big[u_{0}((\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon})^{-1}(x))-u_{0}(\phi_{s}^{-1}(x))\big]\big[\theta(x+z)-\theta(x)-\sum_{i=1}^{d}z_{i}D_{i}\theta(x)\big]dx\Big|\leq 2C_{0}\|u_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|D^{2}\theta\|_{0}|z|^{2},

where
C_{0}=Leb(B_{R_{0}})
.
Since
\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{2}\nu(dz)<\infty
, we can finally apply the Lebesgue DCT and get, on
\Omega^{(iv)}
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Big|\int_{K^{1}}\big[u_{0}((\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon})^{-1}(x))-u_{0}(\phi_{s}^{-1}(x))\big]\big[\theta(x+z)-\theta(x)-\sum_{i=1}^{d}z_{i}D_{i}\theta(x)\big]dx\Big|\nu(dz)ds\to 0.

The proof of this lemma is complete.
∎

Now the proof of Theorem
5.10
is complete.

6
Uniqueness of weak solutions to the stochastic transport equation

The two main results in this section are Theorems
6.16
and
6.18
about the
uniqueness of weak
∗
-
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}
-solutions to the problem (
1.1
)
in the sense of Definition
5.8
under different assumptions.

6.1
New auxiliary regularity type results

We begin with recalling a basic definition.

Definition 6.1
.

Suppose that
u:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
is locally Lebesgue essentially bounded and
b:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
is locally Lebesgue integrable such that
\operatorname{div}b:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
is locally Lebesgue integrable.

We define a distribution
b\cdot Du\in{\mathscr{D}}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
by the following formula

\langle b\cdot Du,\rho\rangle:=-\langle ub,D\rho\rangle-\langle u\,\operatorname{div}b,\rho\rangle,\;\;\rho\in{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),

(6.1)

where
{\mathscr{D}}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
is the space of distributions.

Remark 6.2
.

The above formula is true when the functions
b
and
u
are of
C^{1}
class. Let us also observe that the bilinear map

(b,u)\mapsto b\cdot Du\in{\mathscr{D}}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

(6.2)

is continuous with respect to the topology on
(b,u)
as required above.

Below we present an equivalent form of the definition of
b\cdot Du
.

Proposition 6.3
.

Suppose that
u:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
is a locally Lebesgue essentially bounded function and
b:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
is a locally Lebesgue integrable vector field such that the distributional derivative
\operatorname{div}b:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
is locally Lebesgue integrable.

Then the distribution
b\cdot Du
defined in Definition
6.1
satisfies the following identity

b\cdot Du:=-u\,\operatorname{div}b+\operatorname{div}(ub),

(6.3)

where

(i)
u\,\operatorname{div}b
is a point-wise product of
u
and
\operatorname{div}b
and so is a locally Lebesgue integrable function,

(ii)
ub
is a locally Lebesgue integrable vector field,

(iii)
\operatorname{div}(ub)
is the distributional divergence of
ub
.

Remark 6.4
.

Let us note that if a locally Lebesgue integrable vector field
b:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
is such that
\operatorname{div}b:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
is locally Lebesgue integrable, then for every
C^{1}
-class function
u:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
the following formula holds in the sense of distributions

\operatorname{div}(ub)=b\cdot Du+u\,\operatorname{div}b,

(6.4)

where now each term is a well defined distribution. Indeed, by assumptions
ub
is a locally Lebesgue integrable vector field and functions
u\,\operatorname{div}b
and
b\cdot Du
are locally Lebesgue integrable functions.

Definition 6.5
.

Suppose that
u:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
is locally Lebesgue essentially bounded and
b:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
is locally Lebesgue integrable such that
\operatorname{div}b:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
is locally Lebesgue integrable. We define an expression
\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon}[b,u]
by the following formula

\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon}[b,u]:=\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\ast(b\cdot Du)-b\cdot D(\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\ast u),

(6.5)

where
b\cdot Du
is defined in Definition
6.1
and
\vartheta_{\varepsilon}
is defined by (
5.36
), and we call
\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon}[b,u]
the
\varepsilon
-commutator.

Let us observe that although the first term on the RHS of (
6.5
) is a
C^{\infty}
function, the second is not even
C^{1}
.

Let
\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
be the space of smooth functions on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
rapidly decreasing at infinity.
For
s\in\mathbb{R}
, we define the Bessel potential of order
s
to be the continuous bijective linear operator
\mathcal{J}^{s}:\mathcal{S}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)\to\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
by

\mathcal{J}^{s}u=\mathscr{F}^{-1}\left(1+|\cdot|^{2}\right)^{s/2}\mathscr{F}u,

(6.6)

where
\mathscr{F}
is the Fourier transform and
\mathscr{F}^{-1}
is the inverse Fourier transform.

For
p\in(1,\infty)
and
s\geq 0
, we use
H^{s,p}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)
to denote the Bessel potential space

H^{s,p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=\left\{u\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}):\mathcal{J}^{s}u\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\right\}

(6.7)

which is equipped with the norm

\|u\|_{H^{s,p}}:=\left\|\mathcal{J}^{s}u\right\|_{L^{p}}.

For positive integers
m
and
1\leq p<\infty
, let

W^{m,p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=\left\{u\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}):D^{\alpha}u\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\text{ for }0\leq|\alpha|\leq m\right\}.

Then
W^{m,p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=H^{m,p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
Let
W^{s,p}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)
,
0\leq s<1
,
p\geq 1
consist of all functions
u
in
L^{p}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)
for which the norm

\|u\|_{W^{s,p}}:=\|u\|_{L^{p}}+\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{|u(x)-u(y)|^{p}}{|x-y|^{d+sp}}dxdy\right)^{1/p}:=\|u\|_{L^{p}}+[u]_{W^{s,p}}

(6.8)

is finite. Then
W^{s,p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=(L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),W^{1,p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{s,p}
by the real interpolation. By
C_{r}^{\infty}:=C_{0}^{\infty}(B_{r})
we denote the space of smooth functions with compact support in
B_{r}=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:|x|\leq r\}
.
Similarly we use the following notations. For any
0<s<1
and
p\in[1,\infty]
,

\displaystyle L_{r}^{p}

\displaystyle\coloneq L^{p}(B_{r}),

(6.9)

\displaystyle W_{r}^{s,p}

\displaystyle\coloneq W^{s,p}(B_{r}).

(6.10)

Let
\|\cdot\|_{L_{r}^{p}}
and
\|\cdot\|_{W_{r}^{s,p}}
denote the
L^{p}
-norm and the
W^{s,p}
-norm on
B_{r}
,
0<s<1
,
p\in[1,\infty]
, respectively.

We will analyze the regularity of the Jacobian of the stochastic flow in Theorem
6.13
and Theorem
6.14
. Under some integrability assumption on
\mathrm{div}\,b
, we will show that the Jacobian
J\phi_{\cdot}(\cdot,\omega)
of the stochastic flow is in
L^{2}(0,T;W_{r}^{\delta,p})
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for some
p>1
.
Before moving to the regularity of Jacobian, let us establish first some results on the distributional commutator.

Remark 6.6
.

Comparing Proposition
6.8
below with
[
41
, Lemma 1]
, we observe that the latter requires
J\phi_{t}\in W^{1-\beta,1}_{r}
which is not sufficient for our purpose. Because even for bounded domain
\mathcal{O}
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, the embedding
W^{\delta,p}(\mathcal{O})\subset W^{\delta,1}(\mathcal{O})
,
p>1
no longer holds when
\delta
is not an integer, see
[
72
]
.
So we generalize the result in Proposition
6.8
to prove that
J\phi_{t}\in W^{1-\beta,p}_{r}
,
p\geq 1
.

The proofs of the following Lemma
6.7
and Proposition
6.8
are similar in spirit to
[
41
, Corollary 1 and Lemma 1]
.

Lemma 6.7
.

Assume
v\in L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d}),\,\operatorname{div}v\in L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\,g\in L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. If there exists
\beta\in(0,1)
such that
v\in C_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})
, then for any
\rho\in C_{r}^{\infty}
and for sufficiently small
\varepsilon
, we have, for
p\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon}[v,g](x)\rho(x)dx\right|\leq C_{r}\|g\|_{L_{r+1}^{\infty}}\left(\|\rho\|_{L_{r}^{\infty}}\|\operatorname{div}v\|_{L_{r+1}^{1}}+[v]_{C_{r+1}^{\beta}}[\rho]_{W_{r}^{1-\beta,p}}\right).

(6.11)

Proof.

The case
p=1
is proved in
[
41
, Corollary 1]
. We only need to consider
p>1
.
On account of
[
39
, Lemma 22]
and for sufficiently small
\varepsilon
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon}[v,g](x)\rho(x)dx\right|

(6.12)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{r}\|g\|_{L_{r+1}^{\infty}}\|\rho\|_{L_{r}^{\infty}}\|\operatorname{div}v\|_{L_{r+1}^{1}}+\left|\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}g(x^{\prime})D_{x}\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(x-x^{\prime})(\rho(x)-\rho(x^{\prime}))\left[v(x)-v(x^{\prime})\right]dxdx^{\prime}\right|.

(6.13)

Let
q=\frac{p}{p-1}
be the Hölder conjugate of
p
.
By applying the Hölder inequality, we find

\displaystyle\left|\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}g(x^{\prime})D_{x}\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(x-x^{\prime})\left(\rho(x)-\rho(x^{\prime})\right)\left[v(x)-v(x^{\prime})\right]dxdx^{\prime}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon}[v]_{C_{r+1}^{\beta}}\|g\|_{L_{r+1}^{\infty}}\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{d}}\Big(\iint_{B_{r+1}\times B_{r+1}}\left|D_{x}\vartheta(\frac{x-x^{\prime}}{\varepsilon})\right|^{q}dxdx^{\prime}\Big)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\cdot\Big(\iint_{B_{r+1}\times B_{r+1}}\left|\rho(x)-\rho(x^{\prime})\right|^{p}|x-x^{\prime}|^{p\beta}\mathds{1}_{[0,2\varepsilon]}(|x-x^{\prime}|)dxdx^{\prime}\Big)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{r}\frac{\varepsilon^{\beta}}{\varepsilon}[v]_{C_{r+1}^{\beta}}\|g\|_{L_{r+1}^{\infty}}\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{d}}\Big(\iint_{B_{r+1}\times B_{r+1}}\left|D_{x}\vartheta(\frac{x-x^{\prime}}{\varepsilon})\right|^{q}dxdx^{\prime}\Big)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\cdot\Big(\iint_{B_{r+1}\times B_{r+1}}\frac{\left|\rho(x)-\rho(x^{\prime})\right|^{p}}{\left|x-x^{\prime}\right|^{(1-\beta)p+d}}\left|x-x^{\prime}\right|^{(1-\beta)p+d}\mathds{1}_{[0,2\varepsilon]}(|x-x^{\prime}|)dxdx^{\prime}\Big)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle=C_{r}[v]_{C_{r+1}^{\beta}}\|g\|_{L_{r+1}^{\infty}}\Big(\iint_{B_{r+1}\times B_{r+1}}\left|D_{x}\vartheta(\frac{x-x^{\prime}}{\varepsilon})\right|^{q}\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{d}}dxdx^{\prime}\Big)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\cdot\Big(\iint_{B_{r+1}\times B_{r+1}}\frac{|\rho(x)-\rho(x^{\prime})|^{p}}{|x-x^{\prime}|^{(1-\beta)p+d}}\left|x-x^{\prime}\right|^{(1-\beta)p+d}\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{(1-\beta)p+d}}\mathds{1}_{[0,2\varepsilon]}(|x-x^{\prime}|)dxdx^{\prime}\Big)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{r}[v]_{C_{r+1}^{\beta}}\|D\vartheta\|_{0}\|g\|_{L_{r+1}^{\infty}}[\rho]_{W_{r+1}^{1-\beta,p}},

where the constant
C_{r}
is independent on
\varepsilon
and its value may change from line to line.
For the last inequality, we have used that

\displaystyle\quad\ \iint_{B_{r+1}\times B_{r+1}}\left|D_{x}\vartheta(\frac{x-x^{\prime}}{\varepsilon})\right|^{q}\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{d}}dxdx^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\iint_{B_{r+1}\times B_{r+1}}\left|D_{x}\vartheta(\frac{x-x^{\prime}}{\varepsilon})\right|^{q}\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{d}}\mathds{1}_{[0,2\varepsilon]}(|x-x^{\prime}|)dxdx^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\|D\vartheta\|^{q}_{0}\iint_{B_{r+1}\times B_{r+1}}\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{d}}\mathds{1}_{[0,2\varepsilon]}(|x-x^{\prime}|)dxdx^{\prime}\leq C_{r}\|D\vartheta\|^{q}_{0}.

∎

In the following, we always assume that
\varepsilon
is sufficiently small. The constants appearing in the following two propositions are independent of
\varepsilon
.

Proposition 6.8
.

Let
\phi
be a
C^{1}
-diffeomorphism of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Assume that there exist
\beta\in(0,1)
and
p\geq 1
such that

v\in C_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})
,

\operatorname{div}v\in L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,

g\in L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and

J\phi^{-1}\in W_{\mathrm{loc}}^{1-\beta,p}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)
.
Then for all
r>0
and any
R>0
,
there exist constants
C_{R}>0
and
C_{R,p}>0
such that if

\rho\in C_{r}^{\infty}
such that
\operatorname{supp}\left(\rho\circ\phi^{-1}\right)\subseteq B_{R}
, then

\displaystyle\quad\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon}[v,g](\phi(x))\rho(x)dx\right|

(6.14)

\displaystyle\leq C_{R}\|g\|_{L_{R+1}^{\infty}}\|\rho\|_{L_{r}^{\infty}}\|\operatorname{div}v\|_{L_{R+1}^{1}}\|J\phi^{-1}\|_{L_{R}^{\infty}}

(6.15)

\displaystyle\quad+C_{R,p}\|g\|_{L_{R+1}^{\infty}}[v]_{C_{R+1}^{\beta}}\left(\|D\rho\|_{L_{r}^{p}}\left\|D\phi^{-1}\right\|_{L_{R}^{\infty}}\left\|J\phi^{-1}\right\|_{L_{R}^{\infty}}+\left[J\phi^{-1}\right]_{W_{R}^{1-\beta,p}}\|\rho\|_{L_{r}^{\infty}}\right).

(6.16)

Moreover,

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon}[v,g](\phi(x))\rho(x)dx=0.

(6.17)

Proof.

By changing variable, we obtain

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon}[v,g](\phi(x))\rho(x)dx=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon}[v,g](y)\rho_{\phi}(y)dy,

(6.18)

where the function
\rho_{\phi}
, which is defined by

\rho_{\phi}(y)=\rho\left(\phi^{-1}(y)\right)J\phi^{-1}(y),

(6.19)

has the support strictly contained in the ball of radius
R
. Observe that

\|\rho_{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}_{R}}\leq\|\rho\|_{L^{\infty}_{r}}\|J\phi^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{R}}.

Now let us check that
\rho_{\phi}\in W^{1-\beta,p}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. By
[
20
, Corollary 2.2]
,

\displaystyle[\rho_{\phi}]_{W_{R}^{1-\beta,p}}\leq C_{p}\Big([\rho\circ\phi^{-1}]_{W_{R}^{1-\beta,p}}\|J\phi^{-1}\|_{L_{R}^{\infty}}+[J\phi^{-1}]_{W_{R}^{1-\beta,p}}\|\rho\|_{L_{r}^{\infty}}\Big).

(6.20)

In view of the Sobolev embedding
W^{1,p}_{R}\subset W^{1-\beta,p}_{R}
, we infer

[\rho\circ\phi^{-1}]_{W_{R}^{1-\beta,p}}\leq C_{R}\left\|D\left(\rho\circ\phi^{-1}\right)\right\|_{L_{R}^{p}}\leq C_{R}\|D\rho\|_{L_{r}^{p}}\left\|D\phi^{-1}\right\|_{L_{R}^{\infty}}.

(6.21)

Combining the above estimates gives

[\rho_{\phi}]_{W_{R}^{1-\beta,p}}\leq C_{R,p}\Big(\|D\rho\|_{L_{r}^{p}}\left\|D\phi^{-1}\right\|_{L_{R}^{\infty}}\left\|J\phi^{-1}\right\|_{L_{R}^{\infty}}+\left[J\phi^{-1}\right]_{W_{R}^{1-\beta,p}}\|\rho\|_{L_{r}^{\infty}}\Big).

(6.22)

Applying Lemma
6.7
, the proof is complete.
∎

We recall the following commutator estimates result from
[
39
]
. As a minor remark, we note that the commutator should be bounded by
\|D\phi^{-1}\|^{2}_{C_{R+1}^{1-\theta}}
instead of
\|D\phi^{-1}\|_{C_{R}^{1-\theta}}
by examining the proof of
[
39
, Corollary 23]
.

Proposition 6.9
.

[
39
, Corollary 23]
Let
\phi
be a
C^{1}
-diffeomorphism of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Assume that there exists
\theta\in(0,1)
such that
v\in C_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
\operatorname{div}v\in

L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),g\in L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Moreover, assume that
J\phi\in C_{\mathrm{loc}}^{1-\theta}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)
. Then, for any
\rho\in C_{r}^{\infty}
and any
R>0
such that
\operatorname{supp}\left(\rho\circ\phi^{-1}\right)\subseteq B_{R}
, we have the uniform bound

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon}[v,g](\phi(x))\rho(x)dx\right|

\displaystyle\leq C_{R}\|g\|_{L_{R+1}^{\infty}}\|\rho\|_{L_{r}^{\infty}}\left\|J\phi^{-1}\right\|_{L_{R}^{\infty}}\|\operatorname{div}v\|_{L_{R+1}^{1}}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{R}\|g\|_{L_{R+1}^{\infty}}[v]_{C_{R+1}^{\theta}}\left(\|D\phi^{-1}\|^{2}_{C_{R+1}^{1-\theta}}\|D\rho\|_{L_{r}^{\infty}}+\|\rho\|_{L_{r}^{\infty}}\left[D\phi^{-1}\right]_{C_{R+1}^{1-\theta}}\right),

and
in addition,

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon}[v,g](\phi(x))\rho(x)dx=0.

(6.23)

Next we will prove some Sobolev type estimates of the Jacobian of the stochastic flow.
We begin with a result which can be deduced from
[
95
]
; see

[
36
, Theorem 2.1]
for a related result.

Theorem 6.10
.

Let
L=(L_{t})_{t\geq 0}
be a Lévy process with Lévy triplet
(0,0,\nu)
satisfying Hypotheses
2
with
\alpha\geq 1
. Let us consider

\displaystyle\lambda v-{\mathscr{A}}v-b\cdot Dv=f,\;\;\lambda>0,

(6.24)

with
b\in C_{\mathrm{b}}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
f\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\cap C_{\mathrm{b}}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, for some
p>1
. Let
v\in C^{2}_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
be the classical bounded solution to (
6.24
).
Then, the following assertions hold.

1) If
\alpha\in(1,2)
there exists
c=c(d,\alpha,\lambda,p,\|b\|_{\infty})
such that
the solution
v
satisfies

\|v\|_{H^{\alpha,p}}\leq c\|f\|_{L^{p}}.

(6.25)

2) If
\alpha=1
,
\beta\in(0,1)
there exists
c=c(d,\alpha,\lambda,p,\|b\|_{\beta})

such that

\|v\|_{H^{1,p}}\leq c\|f\|_{L^{p}}.

(6.26)

Proof.

Recall that
H^{1,p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

=W^{1,p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. The existence of
v
follows by the classical approach of pages 173-177 in
[
45
]
which are dedicated to the study of non-local Kolmogorov equations with smooth coefficients.

The first assertion follows easily from Corollary 4.4 in
[
95
]
noting that
b\cdot Dv
is a “small perturbation” of

{\mathscr{A}}v
. Indeed, by
[
95
, Corollary 4.4]
the domain
\mathcal{D}({\mathscr{A}})
of the operator
{\mathscr{A}}
on the
L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
is equal to the Sobolev space
H^{\alpha,p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with equivalent norms.

The second assertion can be deduced by
[
95
, Theorem 5.3]
, which deals with a related parabolic problem. Let us explain the proof.
We first consider

\displaystyle\partial_{t}u(t,x)={\mathscr{A}}u(t,x)+b(x)\cdot Du(t,x)+e^{\lambda t}f(x),\;\;t\in]0,T],

u(0,x)=0
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. By
[
95
, Theorem 5.3]

the unique bounded classical solution
u
belongs to

C([0,T];W^{1-1/p,p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))\cap L^{p}([0,T];W^{1,p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
.
Moreover it holds:

\int_{0}^{T}\|Du(t)\|_{L^{p}}^{p}dt\leq C_{T}\|f\|_{L^{p}}^{p}.

(6.27)

Let us define
v(t,x)=e^{-\lambda t}u(t,x)
. We find that
v
solves

\displaystyle\partial_{t}v(t,x)={\mathscr{A}}v(t,x)+b(x)\cdot Dv(t,x)+f(x)-\lambda v(t,x),\;\;t\in]0,T],\;\;v(0,x)=0.

By the maximum principle it follows that
v(t,x)=v(x)
,
(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
where
v
solves (
6.24
). We find by (
6.27
)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|Du(t)\|_{L^{p}}^{p}dt=\int_{0}^{T}e^{\lambda pt}dt\,\|Dv\|_{L^{p}}^{p}\leq C_{T}\|f\|_{L^{p}}^{p}.

The assertion is proved.
∎

The next result is a consequence of
[
34
, Theorem 1.1]
or
[
17
, Lemma A.1]
.

Proposition 6.11
.

There exists a constant
C>0
, depending only on
d
, such that for every
p\in[1,+\infty]
,
s\in[0,1]
, and
u\in W^{s,p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
the following estimate holds

\displaystyle\|u(\cdot+y)-u\|_{L^{p}}\leq C[u]_{W^{s,p}}|y|^{s}.

(6.28)

Lemma 6.12
.

Assume the assumptions of Theorem
3.5
.
Assume that
p>1
,
\delta\in(0,1)
and
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
. Let
\phi^{\varepsilon}=\left(\phi_{t}^{\varepsilon}\right)_{t\geq 0}
be the flow corresponding to equation (
2.34
) with the vector field
b
replaced by
b^{\varepsilon}=b\ast\vartheta_{\varepsilon}
. Assume that
\tilde{p}>p
, let
\tilde{\delta}=\delta+d(\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{\tilde{p}})
. Then, the following assertion holds.

If
f\in W^{\tilde{\delta},\tilde{p}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, then for all
r>0
and
s\in[0,T]
,
f\circ\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}\in L^{p}(\Omega;W_{r}^{\delta,p})
, and there exists a constant
M_{r,T,\tilde{p},d,\delta,p}
independent of
f
and
\varepsilon
, such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|f(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon})\|^{p}_{W_{r}^{\delta,p}}\leq M_{r,T,\tilde{p},d,\delta,p}\|f\|^{p}_{W^{\tilde{\delta},\tilde{p}}},

(6.29)

and, for all
0\leq s\leq t\leq T
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|f((\phi^{\varepsilon}_{s,t})^{-1})\|^{p}_{W_{r}^{\delta,p}}\leq M_{r,T,\tilde{p},d,\delta,p}\|f\|^{p}_{W^{\tilde{\delta},\tilde{p}}},

(6.30)

where
(\phi^{\varepsilon}_{s,t})^{-1}
satisfies (
5.41
).

Proof.

Here we only prove (
6.29
) because we can prove (
6.30
) by a similar argument.
Let us choose and fix
p>1
,
\delta\in(0,1)
,
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
,
\tilde{p}>p
and
\tilde{\delta}:=\delta+d(\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{\tilde{p}})
.

By using the definition of the space
W^{\delta,p}_{r}
, we infer

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|f(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(\cdot))\|^{p}_{W^{\delta,p}_{r}}\leq C_{p}\mathbb{E}\int_{B(r)}|f(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(x))|^{p}dx+C_{p}\mathbb{E}\int_{B(r)}\int_{B(r)}\frac{\big|f(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(x))-f\big(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y))\big|^{p}}{|x-y|^{d+\delta p}}\,dxdy.

Since
\tilde{p}>p
by the Hölder inequality and the change of measure theorem we have

\displaystyle\quad\mathbb{E}\int_{B(r)}\int_{B(r)}\frac{\big|f(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(x))-f\big(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y))\big|^{p}}{|x-y|^{d+\delta p}}\,dxdy

(6.31)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\int_{B(r)}\int_{B(r)}\frac{\big|f(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(x))-f\big(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y))\big|^{p}}{|\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(x)-\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y)|^{d+\delta p}}\frac{|\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(x)-\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y)|^{d+\delta p}}{|x-y|^{d+\delta p}}\,dxdy

(6.32)

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\mathbb{E}\int_{B(r)}\int_{B(r)}\frac{\big|f(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(x))-f\big(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y))\big|^{\tilde{p}}}{|\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(x)-\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y)|^{d+\tilde{\delta}\tilde{p}}}\,dxdy\Big)^{\frac{p}{\tilde{p}}}

(6.33)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\Big(\mathbb{E}\int_{B(r)}\int_{B(r)}\Big(\frac{|\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(x)-\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y)|^{d+\delta p}}{|x-y|^{d+\delta p}}\Big)^{\frac{\tilde{p}}{\tilde{p}-p}}\,dxdy\Big)^{\frac{\tilde{p}-p}{\tilde{p}}}.

(6.34)

The first factor (inside the bracket) above we estimate by

\displaystyle\quad\mathbb{E}\int_{\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(B(r))}\int_{\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(B(r))}\frac{\big|f(x^{\prime})-f\big(y^{\prime})\big|^{\tilde{p}}}{|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|^{d+\tilde{\delta}\tilde{p}}}J(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon})^{-1}(x^{\prime})J(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon})^{-1}(y^{\prime})\,dx^{\prime}dy^{\prime}

(6.35)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\big|f(x^{\prime})-f\big(y^{\prime})\big|^{\tilde{p}}}{|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|^{d+\tilde{\delta}\tilde{p}}}\mathbb{E}\Big(J(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon})^{-1}(x^{\prime})J(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon})^{-1}(y^{\prime})\mathds{1}_{\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(B(r))}(x^{\prime})\mathds{1}_{\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(B(r))}(y^{\prime})\Big)\,dx^{\prime}dy^{\prime}

(6.36)

\displaystyle\leq[f]^{\tilde{p}}_{W^{\tilde{\delta},\tilde{p}}}\sup_{(\varepsilon,s,x)\in(0,1)\times[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbb{E}\Big(|J(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon})^{-1}(x)|^{2}\Big),

(6.37)

while, using
\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(x)-\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y)=\int_{0}^{1}D\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y+\theta(x-y))(x-y)d\theta
, we get the following estimate for second factor

\displaystyle\Big(\mathbb{E}\int_{B(r)}\int_{B(r)}\Big(\frac{|\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(x)-\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y)|^{d+\delta p}}{|x-y|^{d+\delta p}}\Big)^{\frac{\tilde{p}}{\tilde{p}-p}}\,dxdy\Big)^{\frac{\tilde{p}-p}{\tilde{p}}}

(6.38)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big(\int_{B(r)}\int_{B(r)}\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}\Big(\|D\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y+\theta(x-y))\|^{\frac{(d+\delta p)\tilde{p}}{\tilde{p}-p}}\Big)d\theta\,dxdy\Big)^{\frac{\tilde{p}-p}{\tilde{p}}}

(6.39)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big(\sup_{\varepsilon\in(0,1)}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbb{E}\Big(\|D\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(x)\|^{\frac{(d+\delta p)\tilde{p}}{\tilde{p}-p}}\Big)\Big)^{\frac{\tilde{p}-p}{\tilde{p}}}\Big(\int_{B(r)}\int_{B(r)}1\,dxdy\Big)^{\frac{\tilde{p}-p}{\tilde{p}}}.

(6.40)

Hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\int_{B(r)}\int_{B(r)}\frac{\big|f(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(x))-f\big(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y))\big|^{p}}{|x-y|^{d+\delta p}}\,dxdy\leq C_{r,T,\tilde{p},d,\delta,p}[f]^{p}_{W^{\tilde{\delta},\tilde{p}}}.

(6.41)

Here

\displaystyle C_{r,T,\tilde{p},d,\delta,p}:=

\displaystyle\Big(\sup_{\varepsilon\in(0,1)}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbb{E}|J(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon})^{-1}(x)|^{2}\Big)^{\frac{p}{\tilde{p}}}

(6.42)

\displaystyle\Big(\sup_{\varepsilon\in(0,1)}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbb{E}\Big(\|D\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(x)\|^{\frac{(d+\delta p)\tilde{p}}{\tilde{p}-p}}\Big)\Big)^{\frac{\tilde{p}-p}{\tilde{p}}}\Big(\int_{B(r)}\int_{B(r)}1\,dxdy\Big)^{\frac{\tilde{p}-p}{\tilde{p}}}.

(6.43)

Meanwhile, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\int_{B(r)}|f(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(x))|^{p}dx

\displaystyle\leq C_{p,\tilde{p},r}\Big(\mathbb{E}\int_{B(r)}|f(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(x))|^{\tilde{p}}dx\Big)^{\frac{p}{\tilde{p}}}

\displaystyle=C_{p,\tilde{p},r}\Big(\mathbb{E}\int_{\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(B(r))}|f(y)|^{\tilde{p}}J(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon})^{-1}(y)dy\Big)^{\frac{p}{\tilde{p}}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{p,\tilde{p},r}\Big(\sup_{\varepsilon\in(0,1)}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbb{E}|J(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon})^{-1}(x)|\Big)^{\frac{p}{\tilde{p}}}\|f\|^{p}_{L^{\tilde{p}}}

\displaystyle=C_{p,\tilde{p},r,T}\|f\|^{p}_{L^{\tilde{p}}}.

Here
C_{p,\tilde{p},r,T}:=C_{p,\tilde{p},r}\Big(\sup_{\varepsilon\in(0,1)}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbb{E}|J(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon})^{-1}(x)|\Big)^{\frac{p}{\tilde{p}}}.

Note that according to Theorem
4.1
and Remark
4.4
,

C_{r,T,\tilde{p},d,\delta,p}+C_{\tilde{p},r,T}<\infty
. Obviously
C_{r,T,\tilde{p},d,\delta,p}+C_{\tilde{p},r,T}
is independent on
f
and
\varepsilon
.

The result follows.
∎

Theorem 6.13
.

Let us assume that
p\geq 2
and that the parameters
\alpha\in[1,2)
and

\beta\in(0,1)
satisfy condition (
2.25
). Let
L=(L_{t})_{t\geq 0}
be a Lévy process with Lévy triplet
(0,0,\nu)
satisfying Hypothesis
2
, i.e.,

L
is a symmetric
\alpha
-stable process, which is non-degenerate in the sense that it satisfies (
2.32
).

Assume that
b\in C_{\mathrm{b}}^{\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\operatorname{div}b\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Then,
for all
\delta\in[0,\frac{\alpha}{2})
and
r>0
,

\displaystyle J\phi\in L^{2}(0,T;W_{r}^{\delta,p}),\;\;\mathbb{P}\mbox{-a.s.}

(6.44)

Proof.

Let us choose and fix numbers
p\geq 2
,

\alpha\in[1,2)
and

\beta\in(0,1)
satisfying condition
(
2.25
).
Assume also that that
b\in C_{\mathrm{b}}^{\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that
\operatorname{div}b\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
Let us choose and fix
r>0
and
\delta\in[0,\frac{\alpha}{2})
.

Step 1
In view of the chain rule for fractional Sobolev spaces, we infer that

[J\phi_{t}]_{W_{r}^{\delta,p}}=[e^{\log(J\phi_{t})}]_{W_{r}^{\delta,p}}\leq\Big(\sup_{x\in B(r)}\big|J\phi_{t}(x)\big|\Big)[\log J\phi_{t}]_{W_{r}^{\delta,p}},\;\;t\in[0,T].

(6.45)

Since by
Theorem
3.23

\sup_{t\in[0,T],\,x\in B(r)}|J\phi_{t}(x)|<\infty
, it is enough to prove that

\log J\phi_{\cdot}(\cdot)\in L^{2}(0,T;W_{r}^{\delta,p}).

Step 2
: Define, for
0<\varepsilon<1
, as that in the proof of Theorem
5.10
,
b^{\varepsilon}=b*\vartheta_{\varepsilon}
. Let us also set
b^{0}=b
. Let
\phi_{t}^{\varepsilon}
be the flow corresponding to (
2.34
) with
b
replaced by
b^{\varepsilon}
. According to Lemma
5.13
, we have

\log J\phi_{t}^{\varepsilon}(x)=\int_{0}^{t}\operatorname{div}b^{\varepsilon}\left(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(x)\right)ds.

(6.46)

Define

\psi_{\varepsilon}(t,x)=\int_{0}^{t}\operatorname{div}b^{\varepsilon}\left(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(x)\right)ds.

(6.47)

By Theorem
4.1
, we have for every
R>0
,
p\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\varepsilon\in(0,1)}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\|D\phi_{t}^{\varepsilon}\|^{p}_{L_{R}^{p}}dt+\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\|D\phi_{t}\|^{p}_{L_{R}^{p}}dt<\infty,

(6.48)

and

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\|D\phi_{t}^{\varepsilon}-D\phi_{t}\|^{p}_{L_{R}^{p}}dt=0.

(6.49)

Then we can extract a sequence
\{\varepsilon_{n}\}
such that

\displaystyle D\phi_{t}^{\varepsilon_{n}}(x)\to D\phi_{t}(x),\quad\text{a.e. in }t,\,x,\,\omega,\;\text{as }\varepsilon_{n}\to 0.

(6.50)

By the continuity of the determinant function, we have

\displaystyle\log J\phi_{t}^{\varepsilon_{n}}(x)=\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t,x)\to\log J\phi_{t}(x),\quad\text{a.e. in }t,\,x,\,\omega,\;\text{as }\varepsilon_{n}\to 0.

(6.51)

In view of Remark
4.4
, we see, for
p\geq 2

\displaystyle\quad\ \sup_{\varepsilon\in(0,1)}\Big(\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{B(r)}|\psi_{\varepsilon}(t,x)|^{p}dxdt\Big)

\displaystyle=\sup_{\varepsilon\in(0,1)}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\ \int_{B(r)}\Big|\int_{0}^{t}\operatorname{div}b^{\varepsilon}(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(x))ds\Big|^{p}dxdt

\displaystyle\leq C_{p,T}\sup_{\varepsilon\in(0,1)}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{B(r)}\big|\operatorname{div}b^{\varepsilon}\big(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(x)\big)\big|^{p}dxds

\displaystyle\leq C_{p,T}\sup_{\varepsilon\in(0,1)}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big|\operatorname{div}b^{\varepsilon}(y)\big|^{p}J(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon})^{-1}(y)dyds

\displaystyle\leq C_{p,T}\sup_{\varepsilon\in(0,1)}\sup_{s\in[0,T],y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbb{E}\Big[J\big(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}\big)^{-1}(y)\Big]\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Big|\operatorname{div}b^{\varepsilon}(y)\Big|^{p}dyds\leq C_{p,T}<\infty.

This shows that
\left(\psi_{\varepsilon}\right)_{\varepsilon\in(0,1)}
is uniformly bounded in
L^{p}(\Omega\times(0,T);L^{p}_{r})
and hence
\left(\psi_{\varepsilon}\right)_{\varepsilon\in(0,1)}
is uniformly integrable on
\Omega\times(0,T)\times B(r)
. Up to extraction of a subsequence
\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}
,
the Vitali theorem ensures that
\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}
converges strongly in
L^{1}(\Omega\times(0,T)\times B(r))
to
\log J\phi
. Notice that the dual space of the Banach space
W_{r}^{-\delta,p^{\prime}}
,
p^{\prime}=\frac{p}{p-1}
is
W_{r}^{\delta,p}
. If we can prove that
(\psi_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon\in(0,1)}
is uniformly bounded in
L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,T);W_{r}^{\delta,p})
, what will be done in
Step 3
, then according to the Banach-Alaoglu Theorem, we can extract a subsequence
(\psi_{\varepsilon_{n_{k}}})
of
(\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}})
such that
\psi_{\varepsilon_{n_{k}}}
converges weakly star in
L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,T);W_{r}^{\delta,p})
to some
\psi^{\prime}\in L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,T);W_{r}^{\delta,p})
. Consequently,
\psi_{\varepsilon_{n_{k}}}
converges weakly star in
L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,T);L^{p}_{r})
to
\psi^{\prime}
. By the uniqueness of the limit, we infer
\psi^{\prime}=\log J\phi
. Hence
\log J\phi\in L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,T);W_{r}^{\delta,p})
.

Step 3
: In this step, we shall prove that
(\psi_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon\in(0,1)}
is uniformly bounded in
L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,T);W_{r}^{\delta,p})
.

Consider first the case when

\delta\in(\alpha-1,\frac{\alpha}{2})
.
Recall that
b^{\varepsilon}\in C_{\mathrm{b}}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})
. We introduce the following Kolmogorov problem, for
0<\varepsilon<1
,

\displaystyle\lambda v^{\varepsilon}-{\mathscr{A}}v^{\varepsilon}-b^{\varepsilon}\cdot Dv^{\varepsilon}=\operatorname{div}b^{\varepsilon}.

(6.52)

By Theorem
6.10
, there exists
c>0
such that

\displaystyle\|v^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{\alpha,p}}\leq c\|\operatorname{div}b^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{p}}\leq c\|\operatorname{div}b\|_{L^{p}},\;\;\varepsilon\in(0,1].

(6.53)

Moreover,
by applying the Itô formula,
we obtain

\displaystyle v^{\varepsilon}\left(\phi_{t}^{\varepsilon}(x)\right)-v^{\varepsilon}(x)-\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}v^{\varepsilon}\left(\phi_{s-}^{\varepsilon}(x)+z\right)-v^{\varepsilon}\left(\phi_{s-}^{\varepsilon}(x)\right)\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)-\lambda\int_{0}^{t}v^{\varepsilon}(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(x))ds

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{t}\operatorname{div}b^{\varepsilon}\left(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(x)\right)ds=-\psi_{\varepsilon}(t,x).

(6.54)

To this purpose, note that since
b^{\varepsilon}
and div
b^{\varepsilon}
are
C_{\mathrm{b}}^{\infty}
-functions, the solutions
v^{\varepsilon}
are
C_{\mathrm{b}}^{2}
-functions, see the proof of Theorem

6.10
where we mention

[
45
]
.
Alternatively, one can remark that by Theorem
3.11
, we have
v^{\varepsilon}\in C^{\alpha+\beta}_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and we can apply the Itô formula as in
[
76
, (4.6)]
.

Since the space
W_{r}^{\delta,p}
,
p\geq 2
is a martingale type
2
Banach space, we infer (cf.
[
96
]
or
[
97
]
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}v^{\varepsilon}\big(\phi_{s-}^{\varepsilon}(\cdot)+z\big)-v^{\varepsilon}(\phi_{s-}^{\varepsilon}(\cdot))\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)\Big\|^{2}_{W^{\delta,p}_{r}}dt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{p,T}\mathbb{E}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|v^{\varepsilon}\big(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(\cdot)+z\big)-v^{\varepsilon}(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(\cdot))\|^{2}_{W_{r}^{\delta,p}}\nu(dz)ds\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{p,T}\mathbb{E}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\|v^{\varepsilon}(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(\cdot)+z)-v^{\varepsilon}(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(\cdot))\|^{2}_{W_{r}^{\delta,p}}\nu(dz)ds\Big)

\displaystyle+C_{p,T}\mathbb{E}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{B^{c}}\|v^{\varepsilon}(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(\cdot)+z)-v^{\varepsilon}(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(\cdot))\|^{2}_{W_{r}^{\delta,p}}\nu(dz)ds\Big)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle I_{1}+I_{2}.

Since
\alpha\in[1,2)
and
\delta\in(\alpha-1,\frac{\alpha}{2})
, we can always choose
\tilde{p}>p
such that
\frac{\alpha-\delta-1}{2}<d(\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{\tilde{p}})<\frac{\alpha-2\delta}{4}
. It follows that
s:=\alpha-\delta-2d(\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{\tilde{p}})\in(0,1)
.
By applying Lemma
6.12
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|v^{\varepsilon}(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(\cdot)+z)-v^{\varepsilon}(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(\cdot))\|^{2}_{W_{r}^{\delta,p}}\leq M_{r,T,\tilde{p},d,\delta,p}\|v^{\varepsilon}(\cdot+z)-v^{\varepsilon}(\cdot)\|^{2}_{W^{\tilde{\delta},\tilde{p}}}

(6.55)

where
\tilde{\delta}=\delta+d(\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{\tilde{p}})
.
Using the Sobolev embedding
H^{\tilde{\delta},\tilde{p}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\subset W^{\tilde{\delta},\tilde{p}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, for
\tilde{p}>2
(c.f.
[
88
, p. 155, Theorem 5]
,
[
90
, p. 49 (9)]
) gives

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{p,T}M_{r,T,\tilde{p},d,\delta,p}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\|v^{\varepsilon}(\cdot+z)-v^{\varepsilon}(\cdot)\|^{2}_{W^{\tilde{\delta},\tilde{p}}}\nu(dz)ds\Big)

(6.56)

\displaystyle\leq C_{r,T,\tilde{p},d,\delta,p}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\|v^{\varepsilon}(\cdot+z)-v^{\varepsilon}(\cdot)\|^{2}_{H^{\tilde{\delta},\tilde{p}}}\nu(dz)ds\Big)

(6.57)

\displaystyle=C_{r,T,\tilde{p},d,\delta,p}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\big\|\mathcal{J}^{\tilde{\delta}}\big(v^{\varepsilon}\left(\cdot+z\right)\big)-\mathcal{J}^{\tilde{\delta}}\big(v^{\varepsilon}(\cdot)\big)\|^{2}_{L^{\tilde{p}}}\nu(dz)ds\Big).

(6.58)

By using the translation estimate in the fractional Sobolev space (
6.28
) with

s=\alpha-\delta-2d(\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{\tilde{p}})\in(0,1)
, the Sobolev embedding
H^{s,\tilde{p}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\subset W^{s,\tilde{p}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
\tilde{p}>2
, the fact that
\mathcal{J}^{\tilde{\delta}}
is an isomorphism of
H^{s+\tilde{\delta},\tilde{p}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
to
H^{s,\tilde{p}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
(c.f.
[
90
, Section 2.3.8, Theorem]
) and also the Sobolev embedding
H^{\alpha,p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\subset H^{\tilde{\delta}+s,\tilde{p}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
(which holds because the parameters satisfy the relation
\alpha-\frac{d}{p}=\tilde{\delta}+s-\frac{d}{\tilde{p}}
), we infer

\displaystyle\big\|\mathcal{J}^{\tilde{\delta}}\big(v^{\varepsilon}\left(\cdot+z\right)\big)-\mathcal{J}^{\tilde{\delta}}\big(v^{\varepsilon}(\cdot)\big)\|^{2}_{L^{\tilde{p}}}

\displaystyle=\big\|\mathcal{J}^{\tilde{\delta}}(v^{\varepsilon})(\cdot+z)-\mathcal{J}^{\tilde{\delta}}(v^{\varepsilon})(\cdot)\|^{2}_{L^{\tilde{p}}}

(6.59)

\displaystyle\leq C_{d}\big\|\mathcal{J}^{\tilde{\delta}}(v^{\varepsilon})\|^{2}_{W^{s,\tilde{p}}}|z|^{2s}

(6.60)

\displaystyle\leq C_{d}\big\|\mathcal{J}^{\tilde{\delta}}(v^{\varepsilon})\|^{2}_{H^{s,\tilde{p}}}|z|^{2s}

(6.61)

\displaystyle=C_{d}\big\|v^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{s+\tilde{\delta},\tilde{p}}}|z|^{2s}

(6.62)

\displaystyle\leq C_{d}\big\|v^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{\alpha,p}}|z|^{2s}

(6.63)

\displaystyle\leq C_{d}\big\|\operatorname{div}b\|^{2}_{L^{p}}|z|^{2s},

(6.64)

where we used (
6.53
) in the last inequality.
Observe that
2s>\alpha
.
By Hypothesis
2
, we have
\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{2s}\nu(dz)<\infty
. It follows that

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{r,T,\tilde{p},d,\delta,p}\|\operatorname{div}b\|_{L^{p}}^{2}\Big(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{2s}\nu(dz)\Big)\leq C<\infty,

where
C_{r,T,\tilde{p},d,\delta,p}
is independent of
\varepsilon
.

For the other term
I_{2}
, choose
\tilde{p}>p
such that
\alpha-\frac{d}{p}\geq\tilde{\delta}-\frac{d}{\tilde{p}}
, where
\tilde{\delta}=\delta+d(\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{\tilde{p}})
and then apply Lemma
6.12
and the Sobolev embedding
H^{\alpha,p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\subset H^{\tilde{\delta},\tilde{p}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
to get

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle=C_{p,T}\mathbb{E}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{B^{c}}\|v^{\varepsilon}(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(\cdot)+z)-v^{\varepsilon}(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(\cdot))\|^{2}_{W_{r}^{\delta,p}}\nu(dz)ds\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C_{r,T,\tilde{p},d,\delta,p}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{B^{c}}\|v^{\varepsilon}(\cdot+z)-v^{\varepsilon}(\cdot)\|^{2}_{W^{\tilde{\delta},\tilde{p}}}\nu(dz)ds\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C_{r,T,\tilde{p},d,\delta,p}\|v^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{W^{\tilde{\delta},\tilde{p}}}\big(\nu(\{|z|>1\})\big)

\displaystyle\leq C_{r,T,\tilde{p},d,\delta,p}\|v^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{\alpha,p}}^{2}\big(\nu(\{|z|>1\})\big)

\displaystyle\leq C_{r,T,\tilde{p},d,\delta,p}\|\operatorname{div}b\|_{L^{p}}^{2}\big(\nu(\{|z|>1\})\big)<\infty.

Combining the above estimates, we find

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}v^{\varepsilon}\left(\phi_{s-}^{\varepsilon}(\cdot)+z\right)-v^{\varepsilon}\left(\phi_{s-}^{\varepsilon}(\cdot)\right)\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)\Big\|^{2}_{W^{\delta,p}_{r}}dt\leq C<\infty,

where the constant
C
is independent of
\varepsilon
.

Similarly to
I_{2}
, we apply Lemma
6.12
to get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\Big\|\lambda\int_{0}^{t}v^{\varepsilon}(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(\cdot))ds\Big\|_{W_{r}^{\delta,p}}^{2}dt
