\displaystyle\leq\lambda^{2}T\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\|v^{\varepsilon}(\phi_{t}^{\varepsilon}(\cdot))\|_{W_{r}^{\delta,p}}^{2}dt

(6.65)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\lambda,r,T,\tilde{p},d,\delta,p}\|v^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{W^{\tilde{\delta},\tilde{p}}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\lambda,r,T,\tilde{p},d,\delta,p}\|\operatorname{div}b\|_{L^{p}}^{2}\leq C<\infty.

(6.66)

Consequently,

(\psi_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon\in(0,1)}

is bounded in
L^{2}((\Omega\times(0,T);W_{r}^{\delta,p})
, for

\delta\in(\alpha-1,\frac{\alpha}{2})
. Since
W_{r}^{\delta_{2},p}\subset W_{r}^{\delta_{1},p}
when
\delta_{1}<\delta_{2}
, the uniform boundedness of
(\psi_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon\in(0,1)}
also holds for all
\delta\in[0,\frac{\alpha}{2})
.

∎

Theorem 6.14
.

Let us assume that
\alpha\in[1,2)
and

\beta\in(0,1)
be such that (
2.25
) holds. Let
L=(L_{t})_{t\geq 0}
be a Lévy process with Lévy triplet
(0,0,\nu)
satisfying Hypothesis
2
.
Assume that
b\in C_{\mathrm{b}}^{\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})
and there exists

p\in(\alpha,2]
such that
\operatorname{div}b\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

Then for any
\delta\in[0,\frac{p-1}{p}\alpha)
and
r>0
,

\displaystyle J\phi\in L^{p}(0,T;W_{r}^{\delta,p}),\;\;\mathbb{P}\mbox{-a.s.}

The first two steps of the proof are similar to the proof of Theorem
6.13
. The crucial part is step 3.

Proof.

Step 1
We may apply similar arguments as in the proof of Theorem
6.13
to get

[J\phi_{t}]_{W_{r}^{\delta,p}}^{p}\leq\Big(\sup_{x\in B(r)}|J\phi_{t}(x)|\Big)^{p}[\log J\phi_{t}]_{W_{r}^{\delta,p}}^{p}.

(6.67)

Hence it is enough to prove that

\log J\phi_{\cdot}(\cdot)\in L^{p}(0,T;W_{r}^{\delta,p}).

(6.68)

Step 2.
Similarly, we define as in Theorem
6.13
,
b^{\varepsilon}(x)=\left(b*\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\right)(x),\varepsilon\in(0,1)
and

\psi_{\varepsilon}(t,x)=\int_{0}^{t}\operatorname{div}b^{\varepsilon}\left(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(x)\right)ds.

(6.69)

Observe that

\displaystyle\quad\ \sup_{\varepsilon\in(0,1)}\Big(\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{B(r)}|\psi_{\varepsilon}(t,x)|^{p}dxdt\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C_{p,T}\sup_{\varepsilon\in(0,1)}\sup_{s\in[0,T],y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbb{E}\left[J\left(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}\right)^{-1}(y)\right]\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left|\operatorname{div}b^{\varepsilon}(y)\right|^{p}dyds\leq C_{p,T}<\infty.

By the Vitali theorem, there exists a subsequence
\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}
converges strongly in
L^{1}(\Omega\times(0,T)\times B(r))
to
\log J\phi
. Therefore, we only need to prove that
(\psi_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon\in(0,1)}
is uniformly bounded in
L^{p}(\Omega\times(0,T);W_{r}^{\delta,p})
.

Step 3
To prove the uniform boundedness of
(\psi_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon\in(0,1)}
in
L^{p}(\Omega\times(0,T);W_{r}^{\delta,p})
, in view of (
6.1
), we now need only to show

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}v^{\varepsilon}\left(\phi_{s-}^{\varepsilon}(\cdot)+z\right)-v^{\varepsilon}\left(\phi_{s-}^{\varepsilon}(\cdot)\right)\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)\Big\|^{p}_{W^{\delta,p}_{r}}dt\leq C<\infty.

(6.70)

Since the space
W_{r}^{\delta,p}
is a martingale type
p
Banach space, by using
[
97
, Propositon 2.2]
, we infer

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}v^{\varepsilon}(\phi_{s-}^{\varepsilon}(\cdot)+z)-v^{\varepsilon}(\phi_{s-}^{\varepsilon}(\cdot))\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)\Big\|^{p}_{W^{\delta,p}_{r}}dt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{T}\mathbb{E}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|v^{\varepsilon}(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(\cdot)+z)-v^{\varepsilon}(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(\cdot))\|^{p}_{W_{r}^{\delta,p}}\nu(dz)ds\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{T}\mathbb{E}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\|v^{\varepsilon}(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(\cdot)+z)-v^{\varepsilon}(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(\cdot))\|^{p}_{W_{r}^{\delta,p}}\nu(dz)ds\Big)

\displaystyle+C_{T}\mathbb{E}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{B^{c}}\|v^{\varepsilon}(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(\cdot)+z)-v^{\varepsilon}(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(\cdot))\|^{p}_{W_{r}^{\delta,p}}\nu(dz)ds\Big)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle I_{1}+I_{2}.

Let’s consider
I_{1}
first. Since
\alpha\in[1,2)
,
p\in(\alpha,2]
and
\delta\in[0,\frac{p-1}{p}\alpha)
, we can always choose
\tilde{p}>p
such that
\frac{\alpha-\delta-1}{2}<d(\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{\tilde{p}})<\frac{\frac{p-1}{p}\alpha-\delta}{2}
. It follows that
s:=\alpha-\delta-2d(\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{\tilde{p}})\in(0,1)
.
By applying Lemma
6.12
, we obtain

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{T}M_{r,T,\tilde{p},d,\delta,p}\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\|v^{\varepsilon}(\cdot+z)-v^{\varepsilon}(\cdot)\|^{p}_{W^{\tilde{\delta},\tilde{p}}}\nu(dz)ds.

Here
\tilde{\delta}=\delta+d(\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{\tilde{p}})
.
Since
d(\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{\tilde{p}})<\frac{\frac{p-1}{p}\alpha-\delta}{2}
, we have
sp>\alpha
. Choosing
\varepsilon_{0}>0
such that

(s-2\varepsilon_{0})p>\alpha
,
which implies that
\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{(s-2\varepsilon_{0})p}\nu(dz)<\infty
by
Hypothesis
2
.
Since
H^{s+\varepsilon^{\prime},\kappa}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\subset W^{s,\kappa}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\subset H^{s-\varepsilon^{\prime},\kappa}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
for any
s>0
,
\kappa\in(1,+\infty)
and
0<\varepsilon^{\prime}<s
(cf.
[
89
, Section 2.3.3]
),
we obtain

\displaystyle\big\|v^{\varepsilon}(\cdot+z)-v^{\varepsilon}(\cdot)\|^{p}_{W^{\tilde{\delta},\tilde{p}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big\|v^{\varepsilon}(\cdot+z)-v^{\varepsilon}(\cdot)\|^{p}_{H^{\tilde{\delta}+\varepsilon_{0},\tilde{p}}}

(6.71)

\displaystyle=C\|J^{\tilde{\delta}+\varepsilon_{0}}v^{\varepsilon}(\cdot+z)-J^{\tilde{\delta}+\varepsilon_{0}}v^{\varepsilon}(\cdot)\|^{p}_{L^{\tilde{p}}}

(6.72)

\displaystyle\leq C_{d}\|J^{\tilde{\delta}+\varepsilon_{0}}v^{\varepsilon}\|_{W^{s-2\varepsilon_{0},\tilde{p}}}^{p}|z|^{(s-2\varepsilon_{0})p}

(6.73)

\displaystyle\leq C_{d}\|J^{\tilde{\delta}+\varepsilon_{0}}v^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{s-\varepsilon_{0},\tilde{p}}}^{p}|z|^{(s-2\varepsilon_{0})p}

(6.74)

\displaystyle=C_{d}\|v^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{\tilde{\delta}+s,\tilde{p}}}^{p}|z|^{(s-2\varepsilon_{0})p}

(6.75)

\displaystyle\leq C_{d}\|v^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{\alpha,p}}^{p}|z|^{(s-2\varepsilon_{0})p}

(6.76)

\displaystyle\leq C_{d}\|\operatorname{div}b\|_{L^{p}}^{p}|z|^{(s-2\varepsilon_{0})p},

(6.77)

where we used the translation estimate (
6.28
) in the second inequality, the Sobolev embedding
H^{\alpha,p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\subset H^{\tilde{\delta}+s,\tilde{p}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, and (
6.53
). It follows that

\displaystyle I_{1}\leq C_{T,d}M_{r,T,\tilde{p},d,\delta,p}\|\operatorname{div}b\|_{L^{p}}^{p}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{(s-2\varepsilon_{0})p}\nu(dz)\leq C<\infty,

(6.78)

where the constant
C
is independent of
\varepsilon
.

For
\delta\in[0,\frac{p-1}{p}\alpha)
, choose
\tilde{p}
such that
p<\tilde{p}
and
\alpha-\frac{d}{p}\geq\tilde{\delta}-\frac{d}{\tilde{p}}
, where
\tilde{\delta}=\delta+d(\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{\tilde{p}})
. Therefore, for the other term
I_{2}
, we can apply Lemma
6.12
and the embedding
H^{\alpha,p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\subset W^{\tilde{\delta},\tilde{p}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
to get

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{T}M_{r,T,\tilde{p},d,\delta,p}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{B^{c}}\|v^{\varepsilon}(\cdot+z)-v^{\varepsilon}(\cdot)\|^{p}_{W^{\tilde{\delta},\tilde{p}}}\nu(dz)ds

(6.79)

\displaystyle\leq C_{r,T,\tilde{p},d,\delta,p}\mathbb{E}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{B^{c}}\|v^{\varepsilon}(\cdot+z)-v^{\varepsilon}(\cdot)\|^{p}_{H^{\alpha,p}}\nu(dz)ds\Big)

(6.80)

\displaystyle\leq C_{r,T,\tilde{p},d,\delta,p}\|v^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{\alpha,p}}^{p}\big(\nu(\{|z|>1\})\big)

(6.81)

\displaystyle\leq C_{r,T,\tilde{p},d,\delta,p}\|\operatorname{div}b\|_{L^{p}}^{p}\big(\nu(\{|z|>1\})\big)\leq C<\infty.

(6.82)

Combining the above estimates for
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
gives (
6.70
).

∎

Remark 6.15
.

Using similar arguments, Theorems
6.13
and
6.14
also hold with
\phi
replaced by
\phi^{-1}
. In the process of proofs, we need to pay special attention to the following results/claim:

(1)

By Theorem
3.23
,
\sup_{t\in[0,T],\,x\in B(r)}|J\phi^{-1}_{t}(x)|<\infty
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s..

(2)

Recall
(\phi^{\varepsilon}_{s,t})^{-1}
in (
5.41
). By Lemma
5.13
, we have,
for any
0\leq s\leq t\leq T

\log J(\phi^{\varepsilon}_{s,t})^{-1}(x)=-\int_{s}^{t}\operatorname{div}b^{\varepsilon}\left((\phi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})^{-1}(x)\right)dr.

(6.83)

Alternatively, one can use that
D\phi^{-1}_{t}(x)=(D\phi_{t}(\phi^{-1}_{t}(x)))^{-1}
and so

J\phi^{-1}_{t}(x)=\det[D\phi^{-1}_{t}(x)]=(J\phi_{t}(\phi^{-1}_{t}(x)))^{-1}.

Therefore the fractional Sobolev regularity of
\log(J\phi_{t}^{-1}(x))
follows from the one of

y\mapsto\log(J\phi_{t}(y)).

6.2
Uniqueness results for our SPDEs

Now we prove the uniqueness results for our SPDE. To this purpose we will use the regularity results of Subsection
6.1
together with an application of the Ito-Wentzell formula in the jump case, see Theorem
B.3
. This is a delicate issue; see our Appendix

A
.

Theorem 6.16
.

Let us assume that
\alpha\in[1,2)
and

\beta\in(0,1)
be such that (
2.25
) holds.
Let
L=(L_{t})_{t\geq 0}
be a Lévy process with Lévy triplet
(0,0,\nu)
satisfying Hypothesis
2
.
Assume that
b\in C_{\mathrm{b}}^{\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
Assume also that one of the following conditions hold.

(1)Assume

\operatorname{div}b\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
for some
p\geq 2
when
d\geq 1
or simply
Db\in L^{1}_{loc}(\mathbb{R})
in the case
d=1
.

(2) Assume
\beta>1-\alpha+\frac{\alpha}{p}
and
\operatorname{div}b\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
for some

p\in(\alpha,2]
when
d\geq 1
or simply
Db\in L^{1}_{loc}(\mathbb{R})
in the case
d=1
.

Then, for every
u_{0}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, there exists a unique weak
∗
-
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}
-solution to the problem (
1.1
) of the form
u(\omega,t,x)=u_{0}(\phi_{t}^{-1}(\omega)x),\;\;t\in[0,T],x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},
in the sense of Definition
5.8
.

Remark 6.17
.

The uniqueness result from Theorem
6.16
is comparable to that of from
[
39
, Theorem 20]
, which requires the hypothesis that there exists
p\in(2,\infty)
such that
\operatorname{div}b\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. On the other hand, our result extends
[
39
, Theorem 20]
by allowing
p
to range also in
(\alpha,2]
.

Our next uniqueness result holds in particular under the general Hypothesis
1
when
\alpha\geq 1
. It does not require any global integrability condition on
\operatorname{div}b
. On the other hand (
6.84
) is stronger than condition (
2.25
).

It is quite unexpected that the result even holds when
\alpha\in(0,1)
in the case of rotationally invariant
\alpha
-stable Lévy processes.

Theorem 6.18
.

Assume Hypothesis
1

if
\alpha\in[1,2)
or assume Hypothesis
3

if
\alpha\in(0,1)
. Let
\beta\in(0,1)
be such that

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha}{4}+\beta>1.

(6.84)

Assume also that
b\in C_{\mathrm{b}}^{\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\operatorname{div}b\in L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
d\geq 1
.
Then, for every
u_{0}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, there exists a unique weak
∗
-
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}
-solution to the problem (
1.1
) of the form

u(\omega,t,x)=u_{0}(\phi_{t}^{-1}(\omega)x),\;\;t\in[0,T],x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},

in the sense of Definition
5.8
.

Remark 6.19
.

The uniqueness from Theorem
6.18
is comparable with the paper
[
39
, Theorem 21]
, whose uniqueness result in the case of Brownian Motion, i.e.,
\alpha=2
, requires
b\in C_{\mathrm{b}}^{\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})
with
\beta>\frac{1}{2}
.

Let us emphasize that Theorem
6.18
does not rely on the paper
[
95
]
(the previous result, i.e., Theorem
6.16
, depends on
[
95
]
).

Proof of Theorem
6.16
.

Let us assume that
u
is a weak
∗
-
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}
-solution of equation (
1.1
) in the sense of Definition
5.8
with
u_{0}=0
.
By linearity of the equation, the uniqueness follows from showing that for any test function
\theta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(t,x)\theta(x)dx=0,\quad\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s., for any }t\in[0,T].

(6.85)

In what follows, we establish the claim (
6.85
). Define an auxiliary
\mathbb{R}
-valued process
u^{\varepsilon}(t)
,
t\in[0,T]
by

u^{\varepsilon}(t,\cdot)=(\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\ast u)(t,\cdot),\;\;t\in[0,T],

(6.86)

i.e.,

u^{\varepsilon}(t,y)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x)u(t,x)\;dx,

where
\vartheta_{\varepsilon}
is defined by (
5.36
).
By (
6.86
), we have

u^{\varepsilon}(0,\cdot)=0.

Note that for every
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, the real-valued process
u^{\varepsilon}(t,y)
is well defined.

Let us fix
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and introduce an auxiliary test function
\theta
defined by

\theta_{y}=\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-\cdot)\;\;\mbox{ i.e., for }x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\,\;\;\theta_{y}(x)=\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x)\in\mathbb{R}.

(6.87)

Then, by the definition (
6.86
) of
u^{\varepsilon}
, we have

\displaystyle u^{\varepsilon}(t,y)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x)u(t,x)\,dx=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(t,x)\theta_{y}(x)\,dx.

(6.88)

Therefore, by (i) of Definition
5.8
and Lemma
5.3
, we know that the process
u^{\varepsilon}(t,y)
,
t\geq 0
, is well defined and for every
y
,
u^{\varepsilon}(t,y)
has càdlàg paths
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. Observe that
by the above equality (
6.88
) and the equality (
5.26
) from Definition
5.8
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} u^{\varepsilon}(t,y)=&\int_{0}^{t}\;\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s,x)\,[b(x)\cdot{D}\theta_{y}(x)+\operatorname{div}\,b(x)\,\theta_{y}(x)]\,dx\,ds\\
&+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s-,x)\,[\theta_{y}(x+z)-\theta_{y}(x)]\,dx\Bigr)\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)\\
&+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{\mathrm{c}}}\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s-,x)\,[\theta_{y}(x+z)-\theta_{y}(x)]\,dx\Bigr)\mu(ds,dz)\\
&+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s,x)\,[\theta_{y}(x+z)-\theta_{y}(x)-\sum_{i=1}^{d}z_{i}D_{i}\theta_{y}(x)]\,dx\Bigr)\,\nu(dz)\,ds.\end{aligned}

(6.89)

It follows that for
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, the real-valued process
u^{\varepsilon}(t,y)
satisfies
t\geq 0

\displaystyle u^{\varepsilon}(t,y)=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\;\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s,x)\,[b(x)\cdot D_{x}\bigl(\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x)\bigr)+\operatorname{div}\,b(x)\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x)]\,dx\,ds

(6.90)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s-,x)\,[\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-z-x)-\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x)]\,dx\Bigr)\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{\mathrm{c}}}\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s-,x)\,[\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-z-x)-\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x)]\,dx\Bigr)\mu(ds,dz)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s,x)\bigl[\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-z-x)-\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x)-\sum_{i=1}^{d}z_{i}D_{i}\bigl(\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x)\bigr)\bigr]\,dx\Bigr)\,\nu(dz)\,ds.

By
D_{x}(\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x))
we mean the Fréchet derivative at point
x
of the function
\theta:x\mapsto\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x)
, i.e.,

D_{x}(\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x))=D_{x}\theta_{y},

where the function
\theta_{y}
is defined by the formula (
6.87
).

Let us observe that the following identity holds:

D_{i}(\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x))=-D_{i}\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x),

where
D\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(a)
is the directional Fréchet derivative of
\vartheta_{\varepsilon}
at
a
and
D_{i}\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(a)=\langle D\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(a),e_{i}\rangle
. Similarly, when we write
D\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x)
we mean the Fréchet derivative of
\vartheta_{\varepsilon}
at the point
y-x
.

By Theorem
B.3
, we have

\mathbb{P}
-a.s., for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle u^{\varepsilon}(t,\phi_{t}(x))=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}Du^{\varepsilon}(s,\phi_{s}(x))\cdot b(\phi_{s}(x))\,ds

(6.91)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s,y)\,\bigl[b(y)\cdot D_{y}\bigl(\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(\phi_{s}(x)-y)\bigr)+\operatorname{div}\,b(y)\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(\phi_{s}(x)-y)\bigr]\,dy\,ds.

Step 2
Let us fix
x\in\mathbb{R}
. Since the function
\theta:y\mapsto\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(\phi_{s}(x)-y)
is of
C_{c}^{\infty}
-class, according to Definition
6.1
we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s,y)\,\bigl[b(y)\cdot D_{y}\bigl(\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(\phi_{s}(x)-y)\bigr)+\operatorname{div}\,b(y)\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(\phi_{s}(x)-y)\bigr]\,dy\,ds

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\Bigl[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s,y)\,b(y)\cdot D_{y}\theta\,dy\,+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s,y)\,\operatorname{div}\,b(y)\theta(y)\,dy\bigr]\,ds

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}b(y)\cdot D_{y}u(s,y)\,\theta(y)\,dy\,ds

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}b(y)\cdot D_{y}u(s,y)\,\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(\phi_{s}(x)-y)\,dy\,ds

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{t}[\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\ast(b\cdot Du(s,\cdot))](\phi_{s}(x))\,ds,

where
b(y)\cdot D_{y}u(s,y)
is the object defined in (
6.1
).
Hence, we infer, using notation
u_{s}=u(s,\cdot)
,

\displaystyle u^{\varepsilon}(t,\phi_{t}(x))=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}b(\phi_{s}(x))\cdot Du^{\varepsilon}(s,\phi_{s}(x))\,ds-\int_{0}^{t}\bigl[\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\ast(b\cdot Du_{s})\bigr](\phi_{s}(x))\,dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{t}\bigl[\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\ast(b\cdot Du_{s})-b\cdot D(\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\ast u_{s})\bigr](\phi_{s}(x))ds

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon}\bigl[b,u_{s}\bigr](\phi_{s}(x))ds.

(6.92)

Let
\rho\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. By changing the variable we find

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u^{\varepsilon}(t,\phi_{t}(x))\rho(x)dx=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u^{\varepsilon}(t,y)\rho(\phi_{t}^{-1}(y))J\phi_{t}^{-1}(y)dy.

(6.93)

Since
u
satisfies (o) of Definition
5.8
and
\rho\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, for any
t\in[0,T]
, there exists a subsequence
\varepsilon_{n}
such that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon_{n}\to 0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u^{\varepsilon_{n}}(t,\phi_{t}(x))\rho(x)dx

\displaystyle=\lim_{\varepsilon_{n}\to 0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u^{\varepsilon_{n}}(t,y)\rho(\phi_{t}^{-1}(y))J\phi_{t}^{-1}(y)dy

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(t,y)\rho(\phi_{t}^{-1}(y))J\phi_{t}^{-1}(y)dy

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(t,\phi_{t}(x))\rho(x)dx.

Therefore, in view of (
6.2
), we infer
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(t,\phi_{t}(x))\rho(x)dx=-\lim_{\varepsilon_{n}\to 0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon_{n}}\bigl[b,u_{s}\bigr]\left(\phi_{s}(x)\right)ds\Big)\rho(x)dx.

(6.94)

Step 3
Now assume
\rho\in C_{r}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
for some
r>0
. Define a random variable

R=\sup_{s\in[0,T],\,x\in B(r)}|\phi_{s}(x)|

(6.95)

so that the map
\phi_{s}
,
s\in[0,T]
sends the support of
\rho
in the ball of radius
R
.
By Theorem
3.5
, we have
R\leq r+T\|b\|_{0}+\sup_{s\in[0,T]}|L_{s}|<\infty
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. In view of Theorem
3.23
, Theorem
4.1
and Remark
4.4
, we get,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.,

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[0,T],\,x\in B(R)}\left|J\phi_{s}^{-1}(x)\right|<\infty,

(6.96)

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[0,T],\,x\in B(R)}\|D\phi_{s}^{-1}(x)\|<\infty.

(6.97)

Since
1-\frac{\alpha}{2}<\beta<1
, by applying Theorem
6.13
with
\delta=1-\beta\in(0,\frac{\alpha}{2})
we have
J\phi\in L^{2}(0,T;W_{r}^{1-\beta,p})
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for any
r>0
, when
p\geq 2
. In view of Remark
6.15
, we infer
J\phi^{-1}\in L^{2}(0,T;W_{r}^{1-\beta,p})
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for any
r>0
, when
p\geq 2
.

If

p\in(\alpha,2]
and
1-\alpha+\frac{\alpha}{p}<\beta<1
, we may apply Theorem
6.14
with
\delta=1-\beta\in(0,\frac{(p-1)\alpha}{p})
to yield that
J\phi\in L^{p}(0,T;W_{r}^{1-\beta,p})
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for any
r>0
and hence
J\phi^{-1}\in L^{p}(0,T;W_{r}^{1-\beta,p})
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for
r>0
, by Remark
6.15
.
Now we can invoke Proposition
6.8
to deduce that for
p>1
and
r>0
,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon_{n}}\bigl[b,u_{s}\bigr]\left(\phi_{s}(x)\right)\rho(x)dx\Big|\\
&\leq C_{R}\|u_{s}\|_{L_{R+1}^{\infty}}\|\rho\|_{L_{r}^{\infty}}\|\operatorname{div}b\|_{L_{R+1}^{1}}\|J\phi^{-1}_{s}\|_{L_{R}^{\infty}}\\
&\quad+C_{R,p}\|u_{s}\|_{L_{R+1}^{\infty}}[b]_{C_{R+1}^{\beta}}\left(\left\|D\phi^{-1}_{s}\right\|_{L_{R}^{\infty}}\|D\rho\|_{L_{r}^{p}}\left\|J\phi^{-1}_{s}\right\|_{L_{R}^{\infty}}+\left[J\phi^{-1}_{s}\right]_{W_{R}^{1-\beta,p}}\|\rho\|_{L_{r}^{\infty}}\right)\\
&\in L^{1}(0,T),\quad\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.}\end{aligned}

(6.98)

and

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \lim_{\varepsilon_{n}\to 0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon_{n}}\bigl[b,u_{s}\bigr]\left(\phi_{s}(x)\right)\rho(x)dx=0,\quad\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.}\end{aligned}

(6.99)

(the limit is in
L^{1}(0,T)
by the dominated convergence theorem).
In particular, when
d=1
, we can apply
[
39
, Corollary 19]
to obtain

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon_{n}}\bigl[b,u_{s}\bigr]\left(\phi_{s}(x)\right)\rho(x)dx\Big|\leq C_{\rho}\|u_{s}\|_{L_{R+2}^{\infty}}\left\|J\phi^{-1}_{s}\right\|_{L_{R}^{\infty}}\|b\|_{W^{1,1}_{R+2}}\in L^{1}(0,T)\quad\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.}\end{aligned}

(6.100)

and

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \lim_{\varepsilon_{n}\to 0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon_{n}}\bigl[b,u_{s}\bigr]\left(\phi_{s}(x)\right)\rho(x)dx=0,\quad\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.}\end{aligned}

(6.101)

(the limit is in
L^{1}(0,T
)).

Consequently, we
conclude from (
6.94
)–(
6.101
) that, for any test function
\rho\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(t,\phi_{t}(x))\rho(x)dx=0,\quad t\in[0,T].

(6.102)

For any test function
\theta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(t,y)\theta(y)dy=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(t,\phi_{t}(x))\tilde{\rho}(x)dx,\,t\in[0,T],

(6.103)

where
\tilde{\rho}=(\theta\circ\phi_{t})J\phi_{t}
is a continuous function with compact support. Since
C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
is dense in
C_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
in
L^{1}
-norm and (
6.102
) holds for any test function, by a standard density argument, we can conclude that
for any test function
\theta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(t,y)\theta(y)dy=0,\quad t\in[0,T],

(6.104)

which completes the proof of uniqueness.
∎

Proof of Theorem
6.18
.

The first two steps are the same as that in the proof of Theorem
6.16
.

Step 3

According to Theorem
3.23
,
D\phi^{-1}_{s}
is
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. locally
\theta
-Hölder continuous, uniformly in
s\in[0,T]
, for any
0<\theta<\frac{\alpha}{2}+\beta-1
. Since by assumption
1>\beta>1-\frac{\alpha}{4}
, we have
1-\beta<\frac{\alpha}{2}+\beta-1
. It follows that
D\phi^{-1}_{s}
and
J\phi^{-1}_{s}
are
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. locally
(1-\beta)
-Hölder continuous in
x
, uniformly in
s\in[0,T]
. Observe also that

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|D\phi_{s}^{-1}\|_{C_{R}^{1-\beta}}<\infty,\quad\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.}

(6.105)

Now we can apply Proposition
6.9
to get

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon_{n}}\bigl[b,u_{s}\bigr]\left(\phi_{s}(x)\right)\rho(x)dx\Big|\\
&\leq C_{R}\|u_{s}\|_{L_{R+1}^{\infty}}\|\rho\|_{L_{r}^{\infty}}\left\|J\phi^{-1}_{s}\right\|_{L_{R}^{\infty}}\|\operatorname{div}b\|_{L_{R+1}^{1}}\\
&\quad+C_{R}\|u_{s}\|_{L_{R+1}^{\infty}}[b]_{C_{R+1}^{\beta}}\left(\left\|D\phi^{-1}_{s}\right\|_{C_{R+1}^{1-\beta}}^{2}\|D\rho\|_{L_{r}^{\infty}}+\|\rho\|_{L_{r}^{\infty}}\left[D\phi^{-1}_{s}\right]_{C_{R+1}^{1-\beta}}\right)\\
&\in L^{1}(0,T),\quad\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.}\end{aligned}

(6.106)

and

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \lim_{\varepsilon_{n}\to 0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon_{n}}\bigl[b,u_{s}\bigr]\left(\phi_{s}(x)\right)\rho(x)dx=0,\quad\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.}\end{aligned}

(6.107)

(the limit is in
L^{1}(0,T)
by the dominated convergence theorem). Consequently, using (
6.94
), (
6.106
) and (
6.107
), an application of dominated convergence theorem shows

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(t,\phi_{t}(x))\rho(x)dx=0.

(6.108)

Now we conclude as in the proof of
Theorem
6.16
.
We have thus proved the theorem.

∎

Appendix A
Appendix: The Itô-Wentzell formula in the jump case

In this section, we present in Theorem

A.1
the Itô-Wentzell formula in the form taken from

[
64
]
, see their Proposition 4.16, see also
[
71
]
. This concerns the computation of the stochastic differential of
F_{t}(\xi_{t})
where
F_{t}(y)
is an adapted random field with jumps and
\xi_{t}
is in general
an adapted quasi-left continuous semimartingale. To the best of our knowledge, only the references
[
64
]
and
[
71
]
contain complete proofs of such Itô-Wentzell formula.

We will simplify the assumptions on
\xi_{t}
and
F_{t}(y)
(see below)
with respect to
[
64
]
.
The obtained simplified Itô-Wentzell formula can still cover our situation, cf. Appendix
B
. However, we stress that even if such a simplified form contains some natural assumptions taken from
[
64
]
, it is still quite difficult to apply in concrete
examples related to SDEs. Indeed, it is a difficult task to check when the several assumptions
hold and to understand the consequences of the Itô-Wentzell formula in the jump case, see for instance our computations in Appendix
B
. Therefore, the situation appears to be very different with respect to the one of the well-known Itô-Wentzell formula in the continuous case, c.f.
[
60
]
.

We fix a stochastic basis or a complete
probability space
\left(\Omega,\mathscr{F},\mathbb{F}\right)
. Let
S=(S_{t})_{t\geq 0}
be an adapted pure-jump Lévy process with values in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
Moreover,
A=(A_{t})_{t\geq 0}
is an
\mathbb{R}^{d}
-valued continuous adapted process of finite variation with
A_{0}=0
.

Assume that
\xi=(\xi_{t}(x))_{t\geq 0}
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

verifies
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for all
t\geq 0

\displaystyle\xi_{t}(x)=x+A_{t}+S_{t}.

(A.1)

Then
\xi=(\xi_{t}(x))_{t\geq 0}
is a special Itô process with jumps.
In particular, our
stochastic flow
\phi_{t}(x)
is a particular process of type (
A.1
)
. In the sequel, we will often omit the dependence of
\xi
by
x
and simply write
\xi_{t}
.

According to
[
4
]
, it is known that

\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for all
t>0
,

\sum_{0<s\leq t}|\Delta\xi_{s}|^{2}<\infty.

(A.2)

Indeed, using the Poisson random measure
\mu^{S}
associated to
S
, we have by the Lévy-Itô formula:

\displaystyle\sum_{s\in(0,t]}|\Delta\xi_{s}|^{2}=\sum_{s\in(0,t]}|\Delta S_{s}|^{2}=\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{2}\mu^{S}(ds,dz)+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{\mathrm{c}}}|z|^{2}\mu^{S}(ds,dz)<\infty.

(A.3)

Here
B=\{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:|z|\leq 1\}
and
B^{c}=\{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:|z|>1\}
as before.
Let us now introduce the hypotheses on the random field
F_{t}(y)
in a simplified form with respect to
[
64
]
. In the following, the Lévy measure of
S
is denoted by
\nu^{S}
. We point out that

S_{t}=\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}z\tilde{\mu}^{S}(ds,dz)+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{\mathrm{c}}}z\mu^{S}(ds,dz),

here
\tilde{\mu}^{S}(ds,dz)=\mu^{S}(ds,dz)-\nu^{S}(dz)ds
.

To this purpose, we introduce another pure-jump Lévy process
L=(L_{t})_{t\geq 0}
with values in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and adapted to the previous stochastic basis:

L_{t}=\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}z\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}z\mu(ds,dz),

(A.4)

where
\mu
is the related Poisson random measure and
\tilde{\mu}
is the compensated Poisson random measure. Moreover, the Lévy measure of
L
is denoted by
\nu
.

Let us fix
T>0
.
In the sequel, as in
[
64
]
, we will use the predictable
\mathscr{P}=\mathscr{P}_{T}

and the optional, i.e., well-measurable,
\mathscr{O}=\mathscr{O}_{T}
,
\sigma
-fields, see
[
49
, Definition I.5.2, page 20]
.
The former one is generated by all real adapted left-continuous processes on
[0,T]\times\Omega
; while the latter one is generated by all real adapted right-continuous processes on
[0,T]\times\Omega
.
It is well-known

\displaystyle\mathscr{P}\subset\mathscr{O}.

We first assume

Hypothesis 4
.

a function

f:\Omega\times[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{m}

is

\mathscr{O}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d})/\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
-measurable, and

a function

h:\Omega\times[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{m}

is
\mathscr{P}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\otimes\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d})/\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
-measurable.

Moreover, assume that,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for all
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\int_{0}^{T}|f_{t}(y)|dt+\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|h_{t}(y,z)|^{2}\nu(dz)dt+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{B^{c}}|h_{t}(y,z)|\nu(dz)dt<\infty.

Let

F:\Omega\times[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{m}

(A.5)

be
\mathscr{O}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d})/\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
-measurable and assume that for each
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s., for all
t\in[0,T]
,

F(t,y)=F_{0}(y)+\int_{0}^{t}f_{s}(y)ds+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}h_{s}(y,z)[\mathds{1}_{B\setminus\{0\}}(z)\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)+\mathds{1}_{B^{c}}(z)\mu(ds,dz)].

(A.6)

For each
n\in\{1,2\}
, let
{C}^{n}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{m})
be the space of
n
-times continuously differentiable functions
g:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{m}
. The topology is defined via suitable semi-norms over balls of natural radii.

We now state our version of the Itô-Wentzell formula. For each
\omega,t
and
y
, we denote

\Delta F({t},y,\omega)=F(t,y,\omega)-F(t-,y,\omega)

(as usual in the sequel we omit the dependence on
\omega
).
The following result is a reformulation of Proposition 4.16
[
64
]
(with
\alpha=2
in the notation of
[
64
]
) which in turn is based on Proposition 1 in
[
71
]
.

Note that for a Fréchet space
{\mathcal{X}}
,
D([0,T];{\mathcal{X}})
is the space of
{\mathcal{X}}
-valued càdlàg
functions on
[0,T];
it is endowed with
semi-norms involving
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}
.

Theorem A.1
.

Let
(\xi_{t})_{t\geq 0}
be as before, i.e., in (
A.1
). In addition to Hypothesis
4
, let us assume that:

(1a)

\mathbb{P}
-a.s.
F\in D([0,T];{C}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{m}))
;

(1b)

for
\mathbb{P}\otimes\mathrm{Leb}
-almost-all
(\omega,t)\in\Omega\times[0,T]
, the function

\mathbb{R}^{d}\ni y\mapsto f_{t}(y)\in\mathbb{R}^{m}

is continuous and, for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\lim_{y\rightarrow x}\left[\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|h_{t}(y,z)-h_{t}(x,z)|^{2}\nu(dz)+\int_{B^{c}}|h_{t}(y,z)-h_{t}(x,z)|\nu(dz)\right]=0;

(A.7)

(2)

for each compact and convex subset
K
of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.

\int_{0}^{T}\sup_{y\in K}\left(|f_{t}(y)|+\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|h_{t}(y,z)|^{2}\nu(dz)+\int_{B^{c}}|h_{t}(y,z)|\nu(dz)\right)dt<\infty,

(A.8)

and

\sum_{t\leq T}[\Delta F(t,\cdot)]_{{1};K}|\Delta\xi_{t}|<\infty,

(A.9)

where the notation
[u]_{{1};K}
used above is defined at the beginning of the paper. We recall that

\displaystyle[\Delta F(t,\cdot)]_{{1};K}

\displaystyle=\sup_{x,y\in K,\,x\not=y}\frac{|\Delta F(t,x)-\Delta F(t,y)|}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{x\in K}|\Delta DF(t,x)|=\sup_{x\in K}|DF(t,x)-DF(t-,x)|.

Then
\mathbb{P}
-a.s., for all
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle F(t,\xi_{t})

\displaystyle=F_{0}(\xi_{0})+\int_{0}^{t}f_{s}(\xi_{s})ds

(A.10)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}h_{s}(\xi_{s-},z)[\mathds{1}_{B\setminus\{0\}}(z)\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)+\mathds{1}_{B^{c}}(z)\mu(ds,dz)]

\displaystyle\quad+{\int_{0}^{t}DF(s-,\xi_{s-})d{\xi_{s}}}

(A.11)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{s\leq t}\Big(F(s-,\xi_{s})-F(s-,\xi_{s-})-DF(s-,\xi_{s-})\cdot\Delta\xi_{s}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{s\leq t}\Big(\Delta F(s,\xi_{s})-\Delta F(s,\xi_{s-})\Big).

Remark A.2
.

In the special case when
F=F_{0}
, the Itô-Wentzell formula (
A.10
) reduces to the Itô formula as in Theorem 1.12 in the paper
[
61
]
rather then
the Itô formula as in Theorem II.5.1 in the monograph
[
49
]
. However, when
\xi
is a semimartigale of the form (5.1) in
[
49
, Section II]
, then one can give a form of
equality (
A.10
) in a form analogous to formula (5.2) therein.

Calculations in this directions are presented in the following Remark
A.3
.

Remark A.3
.

In our understanding of the Itô-Wentzell formula from
[
64
]
, the term
{\int_{0}^{t}DF(s-,\xi_{s-})d{\xi_{s}}}
in line 3 of (
A.10
) should be as it is and not
{\int_{0}^{t}DF(s-,\xi_{s-})d{A_{s}}}
as it is written in paper
[
64
]
. This agrees with the corresponding formula from the original 1983 paper
[
71
]
. Let us show, by an additional argument, why this is the correct form.

Let us consider the simplified case

F(t,y)=F_{0}(y),

where
F_{0}(y)\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. In this case
f_{s}=h_{s}=0
. Then the above Itô-Wentzell formula (
A.10
) (with
d\xi_{s}
) becomes

\displaystyle F_{0}(\xi_{t})

\displaystyle=F_{0}(\xi_{0})+{\int_{0}^{t}DF_{0}(\xi_{s-})d{\xi_{s}}}+\sum_{s\leq t}\Big(F_{0}(\xi_{s})-F_{0}(\xi_{s-})-DF_{0}(\xi_{s-})\cdot\Delta\xi_{s}\Big)

\displaystyle=F_{0}(\xi_{0})+{\int_{0}^{t}DF_{0}(\xi_{s})dA_{s}+\int_{0}^{t}DF_{0}(\xi_{s-})dS_{s}}

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Big(F_{0}(\xi_{s-}+z)-F_{0}(\xi_{s-})-DF_{0}(\xi_{s-})\cdot z\Big)\mu^{S}(ds,dz)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}\Big(F_{0}(\xi_{s-}+z)-F_{0}(\xi_{s-})-DF_{0}(\xi_{s-})\cdot z\Big)\mu^{S}(ds,dz)

\displaystyle=F_{0}(\xi_{0})+{\int_{0}^{t}DF_{0}(\xi_{s})dA_{s}+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}DF_{0}(\xi_{s-})\cdot z\tilde{\mu}^{S}(ds,dz)}

\displaystyle\quad+{\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}DF_{0}(\xi_{s-})\cdot z\mu^{S}(ds,dz)}

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Big(F_{0}(\xi_{s-}+z)-F_{0}(\xi_{s-})-DF_{0}(\xi_{s-})\cdot z\Big)\mu^{S}(ds,dz)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}\Big(F_{0}(\xi_{s-}+z)-F_{0}(\xi_{s-})-DF_{0}(\xi_{s-})\cdot z\Big)\mu^{S}(ds,dz)

\displaystyle=F_{0}(\xi_{0})+{\int_{0}^{t}DF_{0}(\xi_{s})dA_{s}+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}DF_{0}(\xi_{s-})\cdot z\tilde{\mu}^{S}(ds,dz)}

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Big(F_{0}(\xi_{s-}+z)-F_{0}(\xi_{s-})-DF_{0}(\xi_{s-})\cdot z\Big)\mu^{S}(ds,dz)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}\Big(F_{0}(\xi_{s-}+z)-F_{0}(\xi_{s-})\Big)\mu^{S}(ds,dz),

where the two terms
\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}DF_{0}(\xi_{s-})\cdot z\mu^{S}(ds,dz)
cancel each other out.

Next, let us observe that by the Taylor formula in the integral form, see e.g.,
[
24
]
,

\displaystyle\Big|F_{0}(\xi_{s-}+z)-F_{0}(\xi_{s-})-DF_{0}(\xi_{s-})\cdot z\Big|\leq\int_{0}^{1}|D^{2}F_{0}(\xi_{s-}+\delta z)||z|^{2}\,d\delta.

Hence by applying formula
[
49
, II. (3.8) on page 62]
we can rewrite the fourth term on the right-hand side of the above formula as

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Big(F_{0}(\xi_{s-}+z)-F_{0}(\xi_{s-})-DF_{0}(\xi_{s-})\cdot z\Big)\mu^{S}(ds,dz)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Big(F_{0}(\xi_{s-}+z)-F_{0}(\xi_{s-})-DF_{0}(\xi_{s-})\cdot z\Big)\tilde{\mu}^{S}(ds,dz)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Big(F_{0}(\xi_{s-}+z)-F_{0}(\xi_{s-})-DF_{0}(\xi_{s-})\cdot z\Big)\nu^{S}(dz)ds.

Thus, by simplifying through cancellations,
we infer

\displaystyle F_{0}(\xi_{t})=

\displaystyle F_{0}(\xi_{0})+{\int_{0}^{t}DF_{0}(\xi_{s})dA_{s}}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Big(F_{0}(\xi_{s-}+z)-F_{0}(\xi_{s-})\Big)\tilde{\mu}^{S}(ds,dz)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Big(F_{0}(\xi_{s-}+z)-F_{0}(\xi_{s-})-DF_{0}(\xi_{s-})\cdot z\Big)\nu^{S}(dz)ds

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}\Big(F_{0}(\xi_{s-}+z)-F_{0}(\xi_{s-})\Big)\mu^{S}(ds,dz).

This is consistent with the Itô formula given in
[
49
]
, see Theorem II.5.1 on page 66.
The above argument can be reversed.

Proof of Theorem
A.1
.

We give a sketch of proof for the sake of completeness, assuming
m=1
. We also provide some additional details and clarify the point indicated in Remark
A.3
.

Recall that, for fixed
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, in particular, the process
(F(t,y),\xi_{t})_{t\in[0,T]}
is
a semimartingale, see , for instance,
[
80
]
and
[
61
]
.

Since both sides of formula (
A.10
) have identical jumps (more precisely, we are considering the jumps of both
t\mapsto\xi_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
t\mapsto F(t,\cdot)\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R})
) and we can always interlace a finite set of jumps.
In particular we may assume that
|\Delta\xi_{t}|\leq 1
for all
t\in[0,\infty)
.
It also suffices to assume that for some
K>0
,

|\xi_{0}|\leq K\mbox{ for all $\omega$ }.

For each
R>K
, let us define

\tau_{R}=\inf\Big(t\in[0,\infty):|A|_{t}+\sum_{s\leq t}|\Delta\xi_{s}|^{2}+|\xi_{t}|>R\Big)\wedge T.

(A.12)

Here
|A|_{t}
indicates the total variation of the random function:
s\to A_{s}
on
[0,t]
.
Then we note that
\tau_{R}
is a stopping time and
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.

\tau_{R}\nearrow T\mbox{ as }R\to\infty.

Moreover, since the jumps are limited to size 1,
|\Delta\xi_{t}|\leq 1
for all
t\in[0,\infty)
, we have for every
t\in[0,\tau_{n}]
,

|A|_{t}+\sum_{s\leq t}|\Delta\xi_{s}|^{2}+|\xi_{t}|\leq R+1.

(A.13)

Next we observe that the assumptions of the theorem hold for the stopped processes

\xi_{\cdot\wedge\tau_{R}}
,
f\mathds{1}_{(0,\tau_{R}]}
,
h\mathds{1}_{(0,\tau_{R}]}
,
F\mathds{1}_{(0,\tau_{R}]}
.
In particular, the process
h\mathds{1}_{(0,\tau_{R}]}
is predictable, c.f., Proposition I.4.4 in
[
70
]
.

Moreover, if we can prove (
A.10
) for this new set of processes, then by taking the limit as
R
tends to infinity, we obtain (
A.10
). Therefore, without loss of generality, we may assume that for some
R>0
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for all
t\in[0,\infty)
,

|A|_{t}+\sum_{s\leq t}|\Delta\xi_{s}|^{2}+|\xi_{t}|\leq R.

(A.14)

In fact, this
R
can be taken to be the previous bound
R+1
in (
A.13
) (enlarging the constant if necessary).

Let
\phi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R})
have support in the unit ball in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and satisfy
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\phi(x)dx=1,\phi(x)=\phi(-x),
and
\phi(x)\geq 0
, for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. For
each
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, let
\phi_{\varepsilon}(x)=\varepsilon^{-d}\phi\left(x/\varepsilon\right),x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

Let us first fix
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. By applying Itô’s formula to the semimartingale
(F(t,y),\xi_{t})
using the smooth function
(u,v)\mapsto G(u,v)=u\phi_{\varepsilon}(y-v)
we deduce, see for instance, Theorem 1.12
[
61
]
or Section II.7 in
[
80
]
, that

\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for all
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle G(F(t,y),\xi_{t})

\displaystyle=F(t,y)\phi_{\varepsilon}(y-\xi_{t})

\displaystyle=F_{0}(y)\phi_{\varepsilon}(y-\xi_{0})+\int_{0}^{t}\phi_{\varepsilon}\left(y-\xi_{s-}\right)dF(s,y)-\int_{0}^{t}F(s-,y)D\phi_{\varepsilon}\left(y-\xi_{s-}\right)d\xi_{s}

\displaystyle\ \ \ +\sum_{s\leq t}\Big(F(s,y)\phi_{\varepsilon}(y-\xi_{s})-F(s-,y)\phi_{\varepsilon}(y-\xi_{s-})

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad-\phi_{\varepsilon}(y-\xi_{s-})\Delta F(s,y)+F(s-,y)D\phi_{\varepsilon}\left(y-\xi_{s-}\right)\Delta\xi_{s}\Big).

Now, writing

\displaystyle F(s,y)\phi_{\varepsilon}(y-\xi_{s})-F(s-,y)\phi_{\varepsilon}(y-\xi_{s-})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle F(s,y)\phi_{\varepsilon}(y-\xi_{s})-F(s-,y)\phi_{\varepsilon}(y-\xi_{s})+F(s-,y)\phi_{\varepsilon}(y-\xi_{s})-F(s-,y)\phi_{\varepsilon}(y-\xi_{s-})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Delta F(s,y)\phi_{\varepsilon}(y-\xi_{s})+F(s-,y)\phi_{\varepsilon}(y-\xi_{s})-F(s-,y)\phi_{\varepsilon}(y-\xi_{s-}),

we infer that

\displaystyle F(t,y)\phi_{\varepsilon}(y-\xi_{t})

\displaystyle=F_{0}(y)\phi_{\varepsilon}(y-\xi_{0}){-\sum_{i=1}^{d}\int_{0}^{t}F(s-,y)\partial_{i}\phi_{\varepsilon}(y-\xi_{s-})d\xi_{s}^{i}}

(A.15)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\phi_{\varepsilon}\left(y-\xi_{s}\right)f_{s}(y)ds

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\phi_{\varepsilon}\left(y-\xi_{s-}\right)h_{s}(y,z)[\mathds{1}_{B\setminus\{0\}}(z)\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)+\mathds{1}_{B^{c}}(z)\mu(ds,dz)]

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{s\leq t}\Delta F(s,y)\left(\phi_{\varepsilon}(y-\xi_{s})-\phi_{\varepsilon}\left(y-\xi_{s-}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{s\leq t}F(s-,y)\big(\phi_{\varepsilon}(y-\xi_{s})-\phi_{\varepsilon}(y-\xi_{s-})+\sum_{i=1}^{d}\partial_{i}\phi_{\varepsilon}\left(y-\xi_{s-}\right)\Delta\xi_{s}^{i}\big).

(A.16)

Recall that (cf. (
A.1
))

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}F(s-,y)\partial_{i}\phi_{\varepsilon}(y-\xi_{s-})d\xi_{s}^{i}=\int_{0}^{t}F(s,y)\partial_{i}\phi_{\varepsilon}(y-\xi_{s})dA_{s}^{i}+\int_{0}^{t}F(s-,y)\partial_{i}\phi_{\varepsilon}(y-\xi_{s-})dS_{s}^{i}.

Let us define, for each
\omega,t,y,
and
z
,

\displaystyle F^{(\varepsilon)}(t,y)

\displaystyle:=[\phi_{\varepsilon}\ast F(t,\cdot)](y),

\displaystyle f^{(\varepsilon)}_{t}(y)

\displaystyle=[\phi_{\varepsilon}\ast f_{t}](y),

\displaystyle h^{(\varepsilon)}_{t}(y,z)

\displaystyle=[\phi_{\varepsilon}\ast h_{t}(\cdot,z)](y),

where
\ast
denotes the convolution operator on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
Appealing to assumption (2) and (
A.14
) (i.e., for the integrals against
F
), we integrate both sides of the above (
A.15
) in
y
, apply the stochastic Fubini theorem,
see Corollary 4.13 from
[
64
]
, see also, Remark 4.14 therein, and the deterministic Fubini Theorem, and then integrate by parts to get that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for all
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle F^{(\varepsilon)}(t,\xi_{t})

\displaystyle=F^{(\varepsilon)}_{0}(\xi_{0}){+\int_{0}^{t}DF^{(\varepsilon)}(s-,\xi_{s-})d\xi_{s}}+\int_{0}^{t}f^{(\varepsilon)}_{s}(\xi_{s})ds

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}h^{(\varepsilon)}_{s}(\xi_{s-},z)[\mathds{1}_{B\setminus\{0\}}(z)\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)+\mathds{1}_{B^{c}}(z)\mu(ds,dz)]

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{s\leq t}\left(\Delta F^{(\varepsilon)}(s,\xi_{s})-\Delta F^{(\varepsilon)}(s,\xi_{s-})\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{s\leq t}\left(F^{(\varepsilon)}(s-,\xi_{s})-F^{(\varepsilon)}(s-,\xi_{s-})-DF^{(\varepsilon)}(s-,\xi_{s-})\Delta\xi_{s}\right).

(A.17)

Let

B_{r}=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:|x|\leq r\},\;\;r>0.

(A.18)

Owing to Assumption (1a) and standard properties of mollifiers, we infer that for each multi-index
\gamma
with
|\gamma|\leq 2
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.,

\sup_{t\leq T}|\partial^{\gamma}F^{(\varepsilon)}(t,\xi_{t})|\leq\sup_{t\leq T}\sup_{x\in B_{R+1}}|\partial^{\gamma}F(t,x)|<\infty,

(A.19)

and for each
K\in\mathbb{N}
,

\lim_{\varepsilon\searrow 0}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sup_{|y|\leq K}|\partial^{\gamma}F^{(\varepsilon)}(t,y)-\partial^{\gamma}F(t,y)|=0.

(A.20)

Similarly, by Assumptions (1b) and (2),
\mathbb{P}\otimes\mathrm{Leb}
-almost-all
(\omega,t)\in\Omega\times[0,T]
,

\displaystyle|f^{(\varepsilon)}_{t}(\xi_{t})|\leq\sup_{x\in B_{R+1}}|f_{t}(x)|<\infty,\quad

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|h^{\varepsilon}_{t}(\xi_{t},z)|^{2}\nu(dz)\leq\sup_{x\in B_{R+1}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|h_{t}(x,z)|^{2}\nu(dz),

\displaystyle\int_{B^{c}}|h^{\varepsilon}_{t}(\xi_{t},z)|\nu(dz)\leq\sup_{x\in B_{R+1}}\int_{B^{c}}|h_{t}(x,z)|\nu(dz),

and for each
K\in\mathbb{N}
,

\mathbb{P}\otimes\mathrm{Leb}-\lim_{\varepsilon\searrow 0}\sup_{|y|\leq K}|f^{(\varepsilon)}_{t}(y)-f_{t}(y)|=0,

(A.21)

and,
\mathbb{P}\otimes\mathrm{Leb}
-almost everywhere,

\lim_{\varepsilon\searrow 0}\sup_{|y|\leq K}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}[\mathds{1}_{B\setminus\{0\}}(z)|h^{(\varepsilon)}_{t}(y,z)-h_{t}(y,z)|^{2}+\mathds{1}_{B^{c}}(z)|h^{(\varepsilon)}_{t}(y,z)-h_{t}(y,z)|]\nu(dz)=0,

(A.22)

where in the last-line we have also used
a standard mollifying convergence argument.

Owing to Assumptions (1a) and (
A.14
),
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.  for any
s\in[0,T]
,

|F^{(\varepsilon)}(s-,\xi_{s})-F^{(\varepsilon)}(s-\xi_{s-})-DF^{(\varepsilon)}(s-,\xi_{s-})\cdot\Delta\xi_{s}|\leq\sup_{t\leq T}[F_{t}]_{2;B_{R+1}}|\Delta\xi_{s}|^{2}

and
\sum_{s\leq T}|\Delta\xi_{s}|^{2}\leq R
.

Since
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.
F\in D([0,T];C^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}))
, it follows that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s., for each
K\in\mathbb{N}
,

\lim_{\varepsilon\searrow 0}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sup_{|y|\leq K}|\Delta F^{\varepsilon}(t,y)-\Delta F(t,y)|=0.

(A.23)

By Assumption (2),
\mathbb{P}
-a.s for all
s\in[0,T]
, we have

\left|\Delta F^{(\varepsilon)}(s,\xi_{s-}+\Delta\xi_{s})-\Delta F^{(\varepsilon)}(s,\xi_{s-})\right|\leq[\Delta F({s},\cdot)]_{{1};B_{R+1}}|\Delta\xi_{s}|

and,

\sum_{s\leq T}[\Delta F({s},\cdot)]_{{1};B_{R+1}}|\Delta\xi_{s}|<\infty.

(A.24)

Combining the above and using Assumptions (1a) and (2) and the bounds given in (
A.14
) and the
dominated convergence theorem, we obtain convergence of all the terms in (
A
), which completes the proof.
∎

Appendix B
A crucial application of the Itô-Wentzell formula

Recall (cf. (
6.86
)) that

\displaystyle u^{\varepsilon}(t,y)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x)u(t,x)\,dx=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(t,x)\theta_{y}(x)\,dx,

(B.1)

with
u(0,\cdot)=0
, where the function
\theta_{y}
is defined by

\theta_{y}=\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-\cdot)\;\;\mbox{ i.e., for }x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\,\;\;\theta_{y}(x)=\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x)\in\mathbb{R}.

For any
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, the real-valued process
u^{\varepsilon}(t,y)
satisfies:
\mathbb{P}
-a.s., for any
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle u^{\varepsilon}(t,y)=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\;\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s,x)\,[b(x)\cdot D_{x}\bigl(\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x)\bigr)+\operatorname{div}\,b(x)\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x)]\,dx\,ds

(B.2)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s-,x)\,[\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x-z)-\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x)]\,dx\Bigr)\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{\mathrm{c}}}\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s-,x)\,[\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x-z)-\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x)]\,dx\Bigr)\mu(ds,dz)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s,x)\bigl[\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x-z)-\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x)-\sum_{i=1}^{d}z_{i}D_{i}\bigl(\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x)\bigr)\bigr]\,dx\Bigr)\,\nu(dz)\,ds.

Formula (
B.2
) can be rewritten in the following compact form, for each
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
t\geq 0,

u^{\varepsilon}(t,y)=\int_{0}^{t}G^{\varepsilon}_{s}(y)\,ds+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}h^{\varepsilon}_{s}(z,y)\,\mu(ds,dz)+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}h^{\varepsilon}_{s}(z,y)\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz),

(B.3)

where the functions

\displaystyle G^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle:\Omega\times[0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to{\mathbb{R}},

\displaystyle h^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle:\Omega\times[0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to{\mathbb{R}},

are defined by the following formulae, for
s\in[0,\infty)
,
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle G^{\varepsilon}_{s}(y)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s,x)\,[b(x)\cdot D_{x}\bigl(\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x)\bigr)+\operatorname{div}\,b(x)\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x)]\,dx

(B.4)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s,x)\,[\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x-z)-\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x)-\sum_{i=1}^{d}z_{i}D_{i}\bigl(\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x)\bigr)]\,dx\Bigr)\,\nu(dz),

\displaystyle h^{\varepsilon}_{s}(y,z)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s-,x)\,[\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x-z)-\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x)]\,dx.

(B.5)

Note that, for
s\in[0,\infty)
,
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we have

\displaystyle h^{\varepsilon}_{s}(y,z)

\displaystyle=u^{\varepsilon}(s-,y-z)-u^{\varepsilon}(s-,y),

(B.6)

and

\displaystyle G^{\varepsilon}_{s}(y)=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s,x)\,[b(x)\cdot D_{x}\bigl(\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x)\bigr)+\operatorname{div}\,b(x)\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x)]\,dx

\displaystyle+\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Big[u^{\varepsilon}(s,y-z)-u^{\varepsilon}(s,y)+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s,x)z\cdot D\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x)\,dx\Big]\nu(dz)

(B.7)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s,x)\,[b(x)\cdot D_{x}\bigl(\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x)\bigr)+\operatorname{div}b(x)\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x)]\,dx

\displaystyle+\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Big[u^{\varepsilon}(s,y-z)-u^{\varepsilon}(s,y)+Du^{\varepsilon}(s,y)\cdot z\Big]\nu(dz).

(B.8)

We have a series of important lemmas.

Lemma B.1
.

Let
T>0.

The process
F=u^{\varepsilon}
satisfies all assumptions of Theorem
A.1

with

\xi_{t}(x)=\phi(t,x)=\phi_{t}(x),\;\;\;x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\;\;t\in[0,T],

where
\phi
is the stochastic flow we constructed in Section
3
, i.e.,

\phi_{t}(x)=x+\int_{0}^{t}b(\phi_{r}(x))\,dr+L_{t}.

(B.9)

Proof of Lemma
B.1
.

Note that we take
A_{t}=A_{t}(x)=\int_{0}^{t}b(\phi_{r}(x))\,dr
,
S_{t}=L_{t}
in (
A.1
) and
F(t,y)=u^{\varepsilon}(t,y)
with

\displaystyle f_{t}(y)

\displaystyle=G^{\varepsilon}_{t}(y),

\displaystyle h_{s}(y,z)

\displaystyle=h^{\varepsilon}_{s}(y,z)=u^{\varepsilon}(s-,y-z)-u^{\varepsilon}(s-,y).

First, we have to check that (i), (ii), (iii) and (iv) in Hypothesis
4
hold.

We will use formula (
B.1
):

u^{\varepsilon}(t,y)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x)u(t,x)\,dx=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(t,x)\theta_{y}(x)\,dx.

By Lemma
5.3
and (o) in Definition
5.8
, we know that there exists a
\mathbb{P}
-full set
\Omega^{\prime}\subset\Omega
such that
for any fixed
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle u^{\varepsilon}(t,\omega,y):[0,T]\times\Omega\to\mathbb{R}\text{ is c\`{a}dl\`{a}g on }\Omega^{\prime}\text{ and adapted,}

(B.10)

and

\displaystyle\text{for every }\omega\in\Omega^{\prime},\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|u(\omega,t,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq M<\infty.

(B.11)

Moreover, by the dominated convergence theorem, we find that, for any
(t,\omega)\in[0,T]\times\Omega^{\prime}:

\displaystyle y\mapsto u^{\varepsilon}(t,\omega,y)\;\;\text{is continuous on $\mathbb{R}^{d}$.}

(B.12)

Hence, we know in particular that for any
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
u^{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\cdot,y)
is

\mathscr{O}
-measurable and the function
(t,\omega)\mapsto u^{\varepsilon}(t-,\omega,y)
is
\mathscr{P}
-measurable. Furthermore,
for any
y,z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

(t,\omega)\mapsto h^{\varepsilon}_{t}(y,z,\omega)\;\;\text{is predictable on $[0,T]\times\Omega$.}

(B.13)

Using (
B.5
) and Remark
5.4
, we deduce that, for a fixed
(t,\omega)\in(0,T]\times\Omega^{\prime}
and any

z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we have

y\mapsto h^{\varepsilon}_{t}(y,z)\;\;\text{is continuous on $\mathbb{R}^{d}$}.

(B.14)

Applying Lemma 4.51 in
[
1
]
we deduce that the function
(t,\omega,y)\mapsto u^{\varepsilon}(t,\omega,y)\in\mathbb{R}
is
\mathscr{O}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
-measurable and the function
(t,\omega,y,z)\mapsto h^{\varepsilon}_{t}(y,z,\omega)
is
\mathscr{P}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\otimes\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
-measurable. Similar arguments yield that the function
(t,\omega,y)\mapsto G^{\varepsilon}_{t}(y,\omega)
is
\mathscr{O}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
-measurable.
It remains to check (iii), which follows by observing that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for every
y,z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle|h^{\varepsilon}_{t}(y,z)|

\displaystyle=|u^{\varepsilon}(t-,y-z)-u^{\varepsilon}(t-,y)|

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{s\uparrow t}\left(\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\theta_{y-z}(x)u(s,x)dx\right|+\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\theta_{y}(x)u(s,x)dx\right|\right)\leq 2M\mathrm{Leb}(B(2\varepsilon))\|\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\|_{0},

\displaystyle|h^{\varepsilon}_{t}(y,z)|

\displaystyle=|u^{\varepsilon}(t-,y-z)-u^{\varepsilon}(t-,y)|

\displaystyle\leq M\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{0}^{1}|D\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-\rho z-x)|d\rho\;dx\,|z|\leq M\mathrm{Leb}(B(2\varepsilon))\|\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\|_{1}|z|,

where
D\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(a)
is the directional Fréchet derivative of
\vartheta_{\varepsilon}
at
a
as before, and we used (
B.11
) and
\mathds{1}_{B(\frac{1}{4}\varepsilon)}\leq\varepsilon^{d}\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\leq\mathds{1}_{B({2}{\varepsilon})}
.

Next, we shall prove that the other assumptions in Theorem
A.1
hold. First we will show that Assumption (1a) of Theorem
A.1
is satisfied. We have to check that

\mathbb{P}
-a.s.
u^{\varepsilon}\in D([0,T];{C}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}))
.

(B.15)

Keep in mind that the topology on
{C}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R})
is defined via suitable semi-norms over balls of natural radii.
Recall (
B.11
).
In the sequel, we set
\Omega^{\prime}=\Omega
to simplify the notation.
Note that for any
y_{1},y_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{(t,\omega)\in[0,T]\times\Omega}|u^{\varepsilon}(t,y_{1},\omega)-u^{\varepsilon}(t,y_{2},\omega)|

\displaystyle=\sup_{(t,\omega)\in[0,T]\times\Omega}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y_{1}-x)u(t,x,\omega)\;dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y_{2}-x)u(t,x,\omega)\;dx\Big|

\displaystyle\leq M\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{0}^{1}|D\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y_{2}+\rho(y_{1}-y_{2})-x)|d\rho\;dx\,|y_{1}-y_{2}|

(B.16)

\displaystyle\leq M\,\mathrm{Leb}(B(2\varepsilon))\|\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\|_{1}|y_{1}-y_{2}|\leq{M_{\varepsilon}}\|\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\|_{1}|y_{1}-y_{2}|.

(B.17)

If
K\in\mathbb{N}
, we consider the process
{u}^{\varepsilon}|_{\mathbb{K}}
, the restriction of the process
{u}^{\varepsilon}
to the set
[0,T]\times\mathbb{K}\times\Omega
, with
\mathbb{K}=[-K,K]^{d}
,
defined by

{u}^{\varepsilon}|_{\mathbb{K}}:[0,T]\times\mathbb{K}\times\Omega\ni(t,x,\omega)\mapsto{u}^{\varepsilon}(t,x,\omega)\in\mathbb{R}.

We claim that

Claim 1.     for any
\omega\in\Omega,

{u}^{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\cdot,\omega)\in D([0,T];C(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}))
.

To prove Claim 1, it is sufficient to prove that for any
\omega\in\Omega
,
{u}^{\varepsilon}|_{\mathbb{K}}(\cdot,\cdot,\omega)\in D([0,T];C(\mathbb{K},\mathbb{R}))
, for an arbitrary
K\in\mathbb{N}
.
Let us choose and fix
K\in\mathbb{N}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
\Pi_{n}
be the set of all lattice points in
\mathbb{K}
of the form

(\frac{i_{1}K}{2^{n}},\dots,\frac{i_{d}K}{2^{n}}),\mbox{ where }-2^{n}\leq i_{1},\dots,i_{d}\leq 2^{n}\mbox{ are integers}.

Then by (
B
) and (
B.10
), for any
\omega\in\Omega
,
s\geq 0
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{t\searrow s}\Big|{u}^{\varepsilon}|_{\mathbb{K}}(t,\cdot,\omega)-{u}^{\varepsilon}|_{\mathbb{K}}(s,\cdot,\omega)\Big|_{C(\mathbb{K},\mathbb{R})}

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{t\searrow s}\sup_{y\in\Pi_{n}}|{u}^{\varepsilon}(t,y,\omega)-{u}^{\varepsilon}(s,y,\omega)|+2dM\|\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\|_{1}\mathrm{Leb}(B(2\varepsilon))\frac{K}{2^{n}}

\displaystyle\leq 2dM\|\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\|_{1}\mathrm{Leb}(B(2\varepsilon))\frac{K}{2^{n}}.

As
n
is arbitrary, this implies that

\displaystyle\lim_{t\searrow s}\Big|{u}^{\varepsilon}|_{\mathbb{K}}(t,\cdot,\omega)-{u}^{\varepsilon}|_{\mathbb{K}}(s,\cdot,\omega)\Big|_{C(\mathbb{K},\mathbb{R})}=0.

(B.18)

Hence, for every
\omega\in\Omega
and
s_{0}\geq 0
,
the function
[0,\infty)\ni s\mapsto{u}^{\varepsilon}|_{\mathbb{K}}(s,\cdot,\omega)\in C(\mathbb{K},\mathbb{R})
is right continuous at
s_{0}\geq 0
.

Now for any
\omega\in\Omega
,
s_{0}>0
, using similar arguments as above, for any
0<s,t<s_{0}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{s,t\nearrow s_{0}}\Big|{u}^{\varepsilon}|_{\mathbb{K}}(t,\cdot,\omega)-{u}^{\varepsilon}|_{\mathbb{K}}(s,\cdot,\omega)\Big|_{C(\mathbb{K},\mathbb{R})}

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{s,t\nearrow s_{0}}\sup_{y\in\Pi_{n}}|{u}^{\varepsilon}(t,y,\omega)-{u}^{\varepsilon}(s,y,\omega)|+2dM\|\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\|_{1}\mathrm{Leb}(B(2\varepsilon))\frac{K}{2^{n}}

\displaystyle\leq 2dM\|\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\|_{1}\mathrm{Leb}(B(2\varepsilon))\frac{K}{2^{n}}.

As
n
is arbitrary, this implies that

\displaystyle\lim_{s,t\nearrow s_{0}}\Big|{u}^{\varepsilon}|_{\mathbb{K}}(t,\cdot,\omega)-{u}^{\varepsilon}|_{\mathbb{K}}(s,\cdot,\omega)\Big|_{C(\mathbb{K},\mathbb{R})}=0.

(B.19)

Hence, for any
\omega\in\Omega
and
s_{0}>0
,
{u}^{\varepsilon}|_{\mathbb{K}}(s_{0}-,\cdot,\omega)
is well-defined in
C(\mathbb{K},\mathbb{R})
. This proves Claim 1.
By using the fact that

\displaystyle Du^{\varepsilon}(s,y)

\displaystyle=D\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s,x)\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x)\,dx=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s,x)D\bigl(\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x)\bigr)\,dx

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s,x)D_{x}\bigl(\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x)\bigr)\,dx,\;\;s\in[0,\infty),\;y\in\mathbb{R}^{d},

(B.20)

and applying similar arguments as above, we can prove that for any
\omega\in\Omega
,
{u}^{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\cdot,\omega)\in D([0,T];C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}))
, and then for any
\omega\in\Omega
,
{u}^{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\cdot,\omega)\in D([0,T];C^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}))
.
Then the verification of Assumption (1a) of Theorem
A.1
is complete.

Now we will verify Assumption (1b) of Theorem
A.1
, that is,
for
\mathbb{P}\otimes\mathrm{Leb}
-almost-all
(\omega,t)\in\Omega\times[0,T]
, the function

\mathbb{R}^{d}\ni y\mapsto G_{t}^{\varepsilon}(y)\in\mathbb{R}

is continuous and for any
y_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\lim_{y\rightarrow y_{0}}\left[\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|h^{\varepsilon}_{t}(y,z)-h^{\varepsilon}_{t}(y_{0},z)|^{2}\nu(dz)+\int_{B^{c}}|h^{\varepsilon}_{t}(y,z)-h^{\varepsilon}_{t}(y_{0},z)|\nu(dz)\right]=0.

(B.21)

Let us choose and fix
y_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. We choose
K>0
such that
y_{0}\in\mathbb{K}:=[-K,K]^{d}
.
Then, if
y\in[-K-1,K+1]^{d}
, we have

\displaystyle|G_{t}^{\varepsilon}(y)-G_{t}^{\varepsilon}(y_{0})|

\displaystyle\leq\Bigl\{M\|\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\|_{2}\int_{\mathbb{K}_{\varepsilon}}|b(x)|+|\operatorname{div}b(x)|dx

(B.22)

\displaystyle\quad+M\|\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\|_{3}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{2}\nu(dz)\mathrm{Leb}(B(2\varepsilon))\Bigr\}\times|y-y_{0}|,

(B.23)

where
\mathbb{K}_{\varepsilon}=[-K-1-2\varepsilon,K+1+2\varepsilon]^{d}
, and

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|h^{\varepsilon}_{t}(y,z)-h^{\varepsilon}_{t}(y_{0},z)|^{2}\nu(dz)+\int_{B^{c}}|h^{\varepsilon}_{t}(y,z)-h^{\varepsilon}_{t}(y_{0},z)|\nu(dz)

(B.24)

\displaystyle\leq\;\Bigl\{M^{2}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{2}\nu(dz)\|\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{2}\Big(\mathrm{Leb}(B(2\varepsilon))\Big)^{2}|y-y_{0}|+2M\nu(B^{c})\|\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\|_{1}\mathrm{Leb}(B(2\varepsilon))\Bigr\}\times|y-y_{0}|.

(B.25)

The above two inequalities imply that Assumption (1b) of Theorem
A.1
is satisfied.

We will now verify condition (
A.8
). It is sufficient to prove that, for every
K,T>0
and
\mathbb{K}=[-K,K]^{d}
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.

\int_{0}^{T}\sup_{y\in\mathbb{K}}\left(|G_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y)|+\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|h_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y,z)|^{2}\nu(dz)+\int_{B^{c}}|h_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y,z)|\nu(dz)\right)ds<\infty.

(B.26)

Note that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\sup_{y\in\mathbb{K}}|G_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y)|ds

\displaystyle\leq TM\|\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\|_{1}\int_{[-K-2\varepsilon,K+2\varepsilon]^{d}}|b(x)|+|\operatorname{div}b(x)|dx

(B.27)

\displaystyle+TM\|\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\|_{2}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{2}\nu(dz)\mathrm{Leb}(B(2\varepsilon))<\infty,

(B.28)

and

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\sup_{y\in\mathbb{K}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|h_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y,z)|^{2}\nu(dz)ds\leq TM^{2}\|\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\|_{1}^{2}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{2}\nu(dz)\Big(\mathrm{Leb}(B(2\varepsilon))\Big)^{2}<\infty,

(B.29)

and

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\sup_{y\in\mathbb{K}}\int_{B^{c}}|h_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y,z)|\nu(dz)ds\leq 2TM\|\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\|_{0}\mathrm{Leb}(B(2\varepsilon))\nu(B^{c})<\infty.

(B.30)

Hence (
B.26
) follows.

Finally we will verify condition (
A.9
). Let us recall that
F={u}^{\varepsilon}
and
\xi_{t}=\phi_{t}
as in the initial statement of the lemma.
For this aim, it is sufficient to prove that, for every
K,T>0
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.

\displaystyle\sum_{s\leq T}\;\sup_{y\in\mathbb{K}}|DF(s,y)-DF(s-,y)||\xi_{s}-\xi_{s-}|<\infty,

(B.31)

where as before
\mathbb{K}=[-K,K]^{d}
, and
DF(s,y)=D_{{y}}F(s,y)
.

Observe that

\displaystyle D_{{y}}F(s,y)=D\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s,x)\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x)\,dx=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s,x)D_{x}\bigl(\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x)\bigr)\,dx,\;\;s\in[0,T],\;y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

(B.32)

We also claim that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s., for any
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
s\in(0,T]
,

\displaystyle D_{{y}}F(s,y)-D_{{y}}F(s-,y)

\displaystyle=\tilde{h}^{\varepsilon}_{s}(y,\Delta L_{s})\mathds{1}_{B^{c}}(z)(\Delta L_{s})+\tilde{h}^{\varepsilon}_{s}(y,\Delta L_{s})\mathds{1}_{B\backslash\{0\}}(\Delta L_{s}),

(B.33)

\displaystyle\xi_{s}-\xi_{s-}

\displaystyle=\Delta L_{s}\mathds{1}_{B^{c}}(z)(\Delta L_{s})+\Delta L_{s}\mathds{1}_{B\backslash\{0\}}(\Delta L_{s}),

(B.34)

where for
s\in(0,T]
,
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\tilde{h}^{\varepsilon}_{s}(y,z)

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s-,x)\,[D_{x}(\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x-z))-D_{x}(\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x))]\,dx.

It follows directly from (
B
) and Lemma
B.2
that this claim holds.

Then we have

\displaystyle\sum_{s\leq T}\;\sup_{y\in\mathbb{K}}|DF(s,y)-DF(s-,y)||\xi_{s}-\xi_{s-}|

(B.35)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\leq T}\;\sup_{y\in\mathbb{K}}\Big[|\tilde{h}^{\varepsilon}_{s}(y,\Delta L_{s})||\Delta L_{s}|\mathds{1}_{B^{c}}(z)(\Delta L_{s})+|\tilde{h}^{\varepsilon}_{s}(y,\Delta L_{s})||\Delta L_{s}|\mathds{1}_{B\backslash\{0\}}(\Delta L_{s})\Big]

(B.36)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{B^{c}}\sup_{y\in\mathbb{K}}\big|\tilde{h}^{\varepsilon}_{s}(y,z)\big||z|\,\mu(ds,dz)+\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\sup_{y\in\mathbb{K}}\big|\tilde{h}^{\varepsilon}_{s}(y,z)\big||z|{\mu}(ds,dz).

(B.37)

Since
\nu(B^{c})<\infty
and

\sup_{\omega\in\Omega}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\sup_{y\in\mathbb{K}}\big|\tilde{h}^{\varepsilon}_{s}(y,z)\big|\leq 2M\|\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\|_{1}\mathrm{Leb}(B(2\varepsilon))<\infty,

we infer that,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.,

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{B^{c}}\sup_{y\in\mathbb{K}}\big|\tilde{h}^{\varepsilon}_{s}(y,z)\big||z|\,\mu(ds,dz)<\infty.

Define a function

f_{\varepsilon,y,x}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\ni v\mapsto D_{x}(\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x-v))\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

Then

\displaystyle\sup_{\omega\in\Omega}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\sup_{y\in\mathbb{K}}|\tilde{h}^{\varepsilon}_{s}(y,z)||z|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{\omega\in\Omega}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\sup_{y\in\mathbb{K}}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|u(s,x)|\,\Big|\int_{0}^{1}D_{v}f_{\varepsilon,y,x}(\delta z)\cdot z\,d\delta\Big|\,dx\Big)|z|

(B.38)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M\|\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\|_{2}\,\mathrm{Leb}(B(2\varepsilon))|z|^{2}.

(B.39)

Combining with
\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{2}\nu(dz)<\infty
, we have,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.,

\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\sup_{y\in\mathbb{K}}\big|\tilde{h}^{\varepsilon}_{s}(y,z)\big||z|{\mu}(ds,dz)<\infty.

Hence, (
B.31
) holds, which verifies condition (
A.9
). Therefore, all the assumptions of Theorem
A.1
are fulfilled, and the proof is complete.
∎

We now prove the following lemma, which implies (
B.33
) and helps clarify the following term appearing in the Itô-Wentzell formula

\sum_{s\leq t}\Big(\Delta u^{\varepsilon}(s,\phi_{s}(x))-\Delta u^{\varepsilon}(s,\phi_{s-}(x))\Big).

(B.40)

Lemma B.2
.

Let
T>0
.
There exists a
\mathbb{P}
-full set
\Omega_{1}\subset\Omega
such that for any
\omega\in\Omega_{1}
,
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
t\in(0,T]
,

\displaystyle u^{\varepsilon}(t,y)-u^{\varepsilon}(t-,y)=

\displaystyle\int_{\{t\}}\int_{B^{c}}h_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y,z)\mu(ds,dz)+\int_{\{t\}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}h_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y,z)\mu(ds,dz)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathds{1}_{\{|\Delta L_{t}|\neq 0\}}\,h_{t}^{\varepsilon}(y,\Delta L_{t}),

(B.41)

where
h^{\varepsilon}_{s}(y,z)=u^{\varepsilon}(s-,y-z)-u^{\varepsilon}(s-,y)
.

Proof.

Recall from (
B.3
) that

u^{\varepsilon}(t,y)=\int_{0}^{t}G^{\varepsilon}_{s}(y)\,ds+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}h^{\varepsilon}_{s}(y,z)\,\mu(ds,dz)+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}h^{\varepsilon}_{s}(y,z)\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz).

(B.42)

Then, for any
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s., for any
t\in(0,T]
, we have

\displaystyle u^{\varepsilon}(t,y)-u^{\varepsilon}(t-,y)

(B.43)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}h_{s}^{\varepsilon}(z,y)\mu(ds,dz)+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}h_{s}^{\varepsilon}(z,y)\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)

(B.44)

\displaystyle\quad-\lim_{l\nearrow t}\Big(\int_{0}^{l}\int_{B^{c}}h_{s}^{\varepsilon}(z,y)\mu(ds,dz)+\int_{0}^{l}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}h_{s}^{\varepsilon}(z,y)\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)\Big)

(B.45)

\displaystyle=\int_{\{t\}}\int_{B^{c}}h_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y,z)\mu(ds,dz)+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}h_{s}^{\varepsilon}(z,y)\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)-\lim_{l\nearrow t}\int_{0}^{l}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}h_{s}^{\varepsilon}(z,y)\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz).

(B.46)

Denote
B_{\delta}=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\,:\,\delta<|x|\leq 1\}
,
\delta\in(0,1).

Let us fix
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.
By combining the argument in the definition of the Itô integral for
f\in\mathbf{F}^{2}_{p}
given in
[
49
, page 63]

with the argument from the proof of Theorem 41 in
[
80
, page 31]
, we deduce that we can find a subsequence
\delta_{n}\to 0^{+}
(possibly depending on
y
) such that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta_{n}\to 0}\Big[\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B_{\delta_{n}}}h_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y,z)\mu(ds,dz)-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B_{\delta_{n}}}h_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y,z)\nu(dz)ds\Big]

(B.47)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}h_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y,z)\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz),\mbox{ uniformly w.r.t. }t\in[0,T].

Let us choose and fix such a subsequence. In what follows, instead of writing
\lim_{\delta_{n}\to 0}
, we will write
\lim_{\delta\to 0^{+}}
.

By the uniform convergence (
B.47
), we can change the order of limits
\lim_{\delta\to 0+}\lim_{l\nearrow t}=\lim_{l\nearrow t}\lim_{\delta\to 0+}
, and therefore, for any
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s., for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle u^{\varepsilon}(t,y)-u^{\varepsilon}(t-,y)

\displaystyle=\int_{\{t\}}\int_{B^{c}}h_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y,z)\mu(ds,dz)+\lim_{\delta\to 0^{+}}\Big[\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B_{\delta}}h_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y,z)\mu(ds,dz)-\lim_{l\nearrow t}\int_{0}^{l}\int_{B_{\delta}}h_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y,z)\mu(ds,dz)\Big]

\displaystyle=\int_{\{t\}}\int_{B^{c}}h_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y,z)\mu(ds,dz)+\lim_{\delta\to 0^{+}}\int_{\{t\}}\int_{B_{\delta}}h_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y,z)\mu(ds,dz).

We have,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s., for any
\delta\in(0,1)
and
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\int_{\{t\}}\int_{B_{\delta}}h_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y,z)\mu(ds,dz)

\displaystyle=\sum_{0<s\leq t}\mathds{1}_{\{t\}}(s)\,\mathds{1}_{\{\delta<|\Delta L_{s}|\leq 1\}}\,h_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y,\Delta L_{s})

(B.48)

\displaystyle=\mathds{1}_{\{\delta<|\Delta L_{t}|\leq 1\}}\,h_{t}^{\varepsilon}(y,\Delta L_{t}).

(B.49)

Hence, for any
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s., for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\lim_{\delta\to 0^{+}}\int_{\{t\}}\int_{B_{\delta}}h_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y,z)\mu(ds,dz)=\int_{\{t\}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}h_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y,z)\mu(ds,dz)=\mathds{1}_{\{0<|\Delta L_{t}|\leq 1\}}\,h_{t}^{\varepsilon}(y,\Delta L_{t}).

We arrive at, for any
t\in(0,T]
,

\displaystyle u^{\varepsilon}(t,y)-u^{\varepsilon}(t-,y)=

\displaystyle\int_{\{t\}}\int_{B^{c}}h_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y,z)\mu(ds,dz)+\int_{\{t\}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}h_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y,z)\mu(ds,dz)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathds{1}_{\{|\Delta L_{t}|\neq 0\}}\,h_{t}^{\varepsilon}(y,\Delta L_{t}).

(B.50)

Next, we shall prove that,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s., (
B
) holds for any
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
Let
D
be a countable dense set in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. From the above argument, we can find a
\mathbb{P}
-full event
\Omega^{\prime}\subset\Omega
such that for any
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime}
, (
B
) holds for any
y\in D
. Note that
\Omega^{\prime}
is independence of
t\in[0,T]
.

Also, by (
B.15
), we know that,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.,

{u}^{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\cdot)\in D([0,T];C(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}))
. It follows that,

\mathbb{P}
-a.s., for any
t\in(0,T]
,

y\mapsto u^{\varepsilon}(t,y)-u^{\varepsilon}(t-,y)\;\;\text{is continuous on $\mathbb{R}^{d}$}.

(B.51)

On the other hand, in view of (
B.14
),
\mathbb{P}
-a.s., for any
t\in(0,T]
,

y\mapsto\int_{\{t\}}\int_{B^{c}}h_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y,z)\mu(ds,dz)+\int_{\{t\}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}h_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y,z)\mu(ds,dz)=\mathds{1}_{\{|\Delta L_{t}|\neq 0\}}h_{t}^{\varepsilon}(y,\triangle L_{t})

(B.52)

is continuous on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
Taking both (
B.51
) and (
B.52
) into account, we can conclude that, there exists a
\mathbb{P}
-full set
\Omega_{1}\subset\Omega^{\prime}\subset\Omega
such that
for any

\omega\in\Omega_{1}
,
