y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, (
B
) holds. The lemma has been proved.
∎

Now we are in a position to apply the Itô-Wentzell formula to
u^{\varepsilon}(t,\phi_{t}(x))
.

Theorem B.3
.

Assume that
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Then,

\mathbb{P}
-a.s., for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle u^{\varepsilon}(t,\phi_{t}(x))=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}Du^{\varepsilon}(s,\phi_{s}(x))\cdot b(\phi_{s}(x))\,ds

(B.53)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s,y)\,\bigl[b(y)\cdot D_{y}\bigl(\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(\phi_{s}(x)-y)\bigr)+\operatorname{div}\,b(y)\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(\phi_{s}(x)-y)\bigr]\,dy\,ds.

(B.54)

Prof of Theorem
B.3
.

Since we have verified all the assumptions of Theorem
A.1
in Lemma
B.1
, we can apply the Itô-Wentzell formula (
A.10
) stated in Theorem
A.1
with
F=u^{\varepsilon}
given by (
B.3
) and
\xi_{t}=\phi_{t}
given by (
B.9
). The application of Itô-Wentzell formula (
A.10
) must be handled carefully, as it is not entirely straightforward.

We now calculate each term explicitly.
Keep in mind that

\xi_{t}=\xi_{t-}+\Delta L_{t}=\phi(t-,x)+\Delta L_{t}.

First, note that

\displaystyle F(t,\xi_{t})

\displaystyle=u^{\varepsilon}(t,\xi_{t}),

(B.55)

\displaystyle F(0,\xi_{0})

\displaystyle=u^{\varepsilon}(0,\xi_{0})=0,

(B.56)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}f_{s}(\xi_{s})ds

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}G^{\varepsilon}_{s}(\xi_{s})\,ds,

(B.57)

where by (
B
),

\displaystyle G^{\varepsilon}_{s}(y)=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s,x)\,[b(x)\cdot D_{x}\bigl(\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x)\bigr)+\operatorname{div}\,b(x)\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-x)]\,dx

\displaystyle+\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Big[u^{\varepsilon}(s,y-z)-u^{\varepsilon}(s,y)+Du^{\varepsilon}(s,y)\cdot z\Big]\nu(dz).

Since

h^{\varepsilon}_{s}(y,z)=u^{\varepsilon}(s-,y-z)-u^{\varepsilon}(s-,y)
, see (
B.6
), we get

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}h_{s}(\xi_{s-},z)[\mathds{1}_{B\setminus\{0\}}(z)\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)+\mathds{1}_{B^{c}}(z)\mu(ds,dz)]

(B.58)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}\Bigl(u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-}-z)-u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})\Bigr)\,\mu(ds,dz)

(B.59)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Bigl(u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-}-z)-u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})\Bigr)\,\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz).

(B.60)

Recall that
A_{t}=\int_{0}^{t}b(\phi_{r}(x))\,dr
and
S_{t}=L_{t}
in (
A.1
). It follows that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}DF(s-,\xi_{s-})d\xi_{s}=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}DF(s-,\xi_{s-})dA_{s}+\int_{0}^{t}DF(s-,\xi_{s-})dS_{s}

(B.61)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}b(\xi_{s})\cdot Du^{\varepsilon}(s,\xi_{s})ds+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}Du^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})\cdot z\,\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)

(B.62)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}Du^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})\cdot z\,\mu(ds,dz).

(B.63)

Moreover,

\displaystyle\sum_{s\leq t}\Big(F(s-,\xi_{s})-F(s-,\xi_{s-})-DF(s-,\xi_{s-})\Delta\xi_{s}\Big)

(B.64)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}\big(u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-}+z)-u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})-Du^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})\cdot z\big)\mu(ds,dz)

(B.65)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\big(u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-}+z)-u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})-Du^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})\cdot z\big)\mu(ds,dz),

(B.66)

which is meaningful since, for
z\in B,

s\in(0,t]
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.,

|u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-}+z)-u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})-Du^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})\cdot z|\leq C{\|u^{\varepsilon}\|_{2}}|z|^{2}.

The most delicate term to understand in the Itô-Wentzell formula is

\sum_{s\leq t}\Big(\Delta F(s,\xi_{s})-\Delta F(s,\xi_{s-})\Big).

(B.67)

By Lemma
B.2
, we have,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s., for any
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
s\in(0,T]
,

\displaystyle F(s,y)-F(s-,y)=

\displaystyle u^{\varepsilon}(s,y)-u^{\varepsilon}(s-,y)

(B.68)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\{s\}}\int_{B^{c}}h_{r}^{\varepsilon}(y,z)\mu(dr,dz)+\int_{\{s\}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}h_{r}^{\varepsilon}(y,z)\mu(dr,dz)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathds{1}_{\{|\Delta L_{s}|\neq 0\}}\,h_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y,\Delta L_{s}).

(B.69)

By substituting
y=\xi_{s}
or
\xi_{s-}
in (
B.68
) and combining

\displaystyle\Delta F(s,\xi_{s})-\Delta F(s,\xi_{s-}):=\Big(F(s,\xi_{s})-F(s-,\xi_{s})\Big)-\Big(F(s,\xi_{s-})-F(s-,\xi_{s-})\Big),

we infer that

\displaystyle\Delta F(s,\xi_{s})-\Delta F(s,\xi_{s-})

\displaystyle=\mathds{1}_{\{|\Delta L_{s}|\neq 0\}}\,h_{s}^{\varepsilon}(\xi_{s},\Delta L_{s})-\mathds{1}_{\{|\Delta L_{s}|\neq 0\}}\,h_{s}^{\varepsilon}(\xi_{s-},\Delta L_{s})

\displaystyle=\mathds{1}_{\{|\Delta L_{s}|\neq 0\}}\,h_{s}^{\varepsilon}(\xi_{s-}+\Delta L_{s},\Delta L_{s})-\mathds{1}_{\{|\Delta L_{s}|\neq 0\}}\,h_{s}^{\varepsilon}(\xi_{s-},\Delta L_{s})

\displaystyle=\mathds{1}_{\{0<|\Delta L_{s}|\leq 1\}}\,[h_{s}^{\varepsilon}(\xi_{s-}+\Delta L_{s},\Delta L_{s})-h_{s}^{\varepsilon}(\xi_{s-},\Delta L_{s})]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathds{1}_{\{|\Delta L_{s}|>1\}}\,[h_{s}^{\varepsilon}(\xi_{s-}+\Delta L_{s},\Delta L_{s})-h_{s}^{\varepsilon}(\xi_{s-},\Delta L_{s})]

\displaystyle=\int_{\{s\}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}[h_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\xi_{r-}+z,z)-h_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\xi_{r-},z)]\,\mu(dr,dz)+\int_{\{s\}}\int_{B^{c}}[h_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\xi_{r-}+z,z)-h_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\xi_{r-},z)]\,\mu(dr,dz)

(B.70)

\displaystyle=\int_{\{s\}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}([u^{\varepsilon}(r-,\xi_{r-})-u^{\varepsilon}(r-,\xi_{r-}+z)]-[u^{\varepsilon}(r-,\xi_{r-}-z)-u^{\varepsilon}(r-,\xi_{r-})])\mu(dr,dz)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\{s\}}\int_{B^{c}}([u^{\varepsilon}(r-,\xi_{r-})-u^{\varepsilon}(r-,\xi_{r-}+z)]-[u^{\varepsilon}(r-,\xi_{r-}-z)-u^{\varepsilon}(r-,\xi_{r-})])\mu(dr,dz).

(B.71)

Note that

\displaystyle\sum_{s\leq t}\int_{\{s\}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Big|[u^{\varepsilon}(r-,\xi_{r-})-u^{\varepsilon}(r-,\xi_{r-}+z)]-[u^{\varepsilon}(r-,\xi_{r-}-z)-u^{\varepsilon}(r-,\xi_{r-})]\Big|\mu(dr,dz)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Big|u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})-u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-}+z)-\Big(u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-}-z)-u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})\Big)\Big|\,\mu(ds,dz)

\displaystyle\leq\|u^{\varepsilon}\|_{2}\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{2}\mu(ds,dz),

and

\displaystyle\sum_{s\leq t}\int_{\{s\}}\int_{B^{c}}\Big|u^{\varepsilon}(r-,\xi_{r-})\Big|+\Big|u^{\varepsilon}(r-,\xi_{r-}+z)\Big|+\Big|u^{\varepsilon}(r-,\xi_{r-}-z)\Big|+\Big|u^{\varepsilon}(r-,\xi_{r-})\Big|\mu(dr,dz)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}\Big|u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})\Big|+\Big|u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-}+z)\Big|+\Big|u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-}-z)\Big|+\Big|u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})\Big|\,\mu(ds,dz)

\displaystyle\leq 4\|u^{\varepsilon}\|_{0}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}1\mu(ds,dz),

both of which are finite,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s., because

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{2}\mu(ds,dz)=t\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{2}\nu(dz)<\infty,

(B.72)

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}1\mu(ds,dz)=t\nu(B^{c})<\infty.

(B.73)

Hence, the term (
B.67
) is well defined and we have

\displaystyle\sum_{s\leq t}\Big(\Delta F(s,\xi_{s})-\Delta F(s,\xi_{s-})\Big)

(B.74)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}[u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})-u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-}+z)]\,\mu(ds,dz)-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}{[u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-}-z)-u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})]}\,\mu(ds,dz)

(B.75)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\{[u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})-u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-}+z)]{-}\big[u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-}-z)-u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})\big]\}\mu(ds,dz).

(B.76)

Applying the Itô-Wentzell formula and then using (
B.58
), (
B.61
), (
B.64
) and (
B.74
), we obtain

\displaystyle u^{\varepsilon}(t,\xi_{t})=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}G^{\varepsilon}_{s}(\xi_{s})\,ds+\int_{0}^{t}b(\xi_{s})\cdot Du^{\varepsilon}(s,\xi_{s})\,ds

(B.77)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}Du^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})\cdot z\,\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)

(B.78)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}Du^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})\cdot z\,\mu(ds,dz)

(B.79)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}\big(u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-}+z)-u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})\big)\mu(ds,dz)

(B.80)

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}Du^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})\cdot z\mu(ds,dz)

(B.81)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\big(u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-}+z)-u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})-Du^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})\cdot z\big)\mu(ds,dz)

(B.82)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}\Bigl[u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-}-z)-u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})\Bigr]\;\mu(ds,dz)

(B.83)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Bigl[u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-}-z)-u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})\Bigr]\,\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)

(B.84)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}{[u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})-u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-}+z)]}\,\mu(ds,dz)

(B.85)

\displaystyle{-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}{[u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-}-z)-u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})]}\,\mu(ds,dz)}

(B.86)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Bigl[[u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})-u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-}+z)]

(B.87)

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt-[u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-}-z)-u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})]\Bigr]\,\mu(ds,dz).

(B.88)

By combining the third term on the right hand side of (
B.77
) with the ninth term, canceling the fourth term with the sixth term, the eighth term with the eleventh term, and the fifth term with the tenth term, we arrive at

\displaystyle u^{\varepsilon}(t,\xi_{t})=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}G^{\varepsilon}_{s}(\xi_{s})\,ds+\int_{0}^{t}b(\xi_{s})\cdot Du^{\varepsilon}(s,\xi_{s})\,ds

(B.89)

\displaystyle+{\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Bigl[u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-}+z)-u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})-Du^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})\cdot z\Bigr]\;\mu(ds,dz)}

(B.90)

\displaystyle+{\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Bigl[u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-}-z)-u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})+Du^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})\cdot z\Bigr]\,\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)}

(B.91)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Bigl[u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})-u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-}+z)

(B.92)

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt-[u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-}-z)-u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})]\Big]\,\mu(ds,dz).

(B.93)

Now we use the expression of
G^{\varepsilon}
in (
B
) to obtain

\displaystyle u^{\varepsilon}(t,\xi_{t})=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}b(\xi_{s})\cdot Du^{\varepsilon}(s,\xi_{s})\,ds

(B.94)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s,x)\,[b(x)\cdot D_{x}\bigl(\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(\xi_{s}-x)\bigr)+\operatorname{div}\,b(x)\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(\xi_{s}-x)]\,dx\,ds

\displaystyle+{\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Big[u^{\varepsilon}(s,\xi_{s}-z)-u^{\varepsilon}(s,\xi_{s})+Du^{\varepsilon}(s,\xi_{s})\cdot z\Big]\nu(dz)\,ds}

(B.95)

\displaystyle+{\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Bigl[u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-}+z)-u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})-Du^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})\cdot z\Bigr]\;\mu(ds,dz)}

(B.96)

\displaystyle+{\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Bigl[u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-}-z)-u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})+Du^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})\cdot z\Bigr]\,\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)}

(B.97)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Bigl[u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})-u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-}+z)[u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-}-z)-u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})]\Big]\,\mu(ds,dz).

(B.98)

Note that the last integral is meaningful because
u^{\varepsilon}\in D([0,T];C^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})).

We find, using
\tilde{\mu}=\mu-\nu
, that

\displaystyle u^{\varepsilon}(t,\xi_{t})=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}b(\xi_{s})\cdot Du^{\varepsilon}(s,\xi_{s})\,ds

(B.99)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s,x)\,[b(x)\cdot D_{x}\bigl(\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(\xi_{s}-x)\bigr)+\operatorname{div}\,b(x)\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(\xi_{s}-x)]\,dx\,ds

\displaystyle+{\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Bigl[u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-}+z)-u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})-Du^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})\cdot z\Bigr]\;\mu(ds,dz)}

(B.100)

\displaystyle+{\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Bigl[u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-}-z)-u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})+Du^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})\cdot z\Bigr]\,\mu(ds,dz)}

(B.101)

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Bigl[u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-}+z)-u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})+u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-}-z)-u^{\varepsilon}(s-,\xi_{s-})\Big]\,\mu(ds,dz).

(B.102)

After performing some cancellations, we obtain

\displaystyle{u^{\varepsilon}(t,\xi_{t})}=\int_{0}^{t}b(\xi_{s})\cdot Du^{\varepsilon}(s,\xi_{s})\,ds+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s,x)\,[b(x)\cdot D_{x}\bigl(\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(\xi_{s}-x)\bigr)+\operatorname{div}\,b(x)\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(\xi_{s}-x)]\,dx\,ds.

(B.103)

This proves equality (
B.53
) and thus
the proof of Theorem
B.3
is complete.
∎

Appendix C
No blow-up of
C^{1}
-class solutions

Definition C.1
.

Assume
b\in L^{1}_{loc}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
u_{0}\in C_{\mathrm{b}}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
A measurable and essentially bounded function (in the sense of (
5.2
))
u:\Omega\times[0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
is a
C^{1}
-solution to the problem (
1.1
) if it
satisfies the following four conditions: for any
T>0
,

for almost every
\omega\in\Omega
,
u(\omega,t,\cdot)\in C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
for all
t\in[0,T]
;

for almost every
\omega\in\Omega
and every
R>0
, the gradient
Du(\omega,t,x)
satisfies

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|Du(\omega,t,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}(B(R))}<\infty.

This means that there exists a
\mathbb{P}
-full set
\Omega^{\prime}\subset\Omega
such that for any
R>0
, there exists
M_{R}\in(0,\infty)
such that
for any
t\in[0,T]
,
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime},

\displaystyle\|Du(\omega,t,x)\|\leq M_{R},\;\;\forall x\in A^{R}_{t,\omega},

where
A^{R}_{t,\omega}
is a Borel set of
B(R)
such that
\mathrm{Leb}((A^{R}_{t,\omega})^{c})=0
. Recall that
\|\cdot\|_{L^{\infty}(B(R))}
denotes the essential supremum norm on
B(R)
, the closed ball of radius
R
centered at
0
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
;

for every test function
\theta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, the process
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\theta(x)u(t,x)\,dx
,
t\geq 0
is
\mathbb{F}
-adapted and it has càdlàg paths
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.;

for every test function
\theta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, the process
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\theta(x)u(t,x)\,dx
,
t\geq 0
, satisfies that, for every
t\geq 0
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(t,x)\,\theta(x)\,dx=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(x)\,\theta(x)\,dx-\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}b(x)\cdot Du(s,x)\theta(x)\,dx\Big)ds

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s-,x)\,[\theta(x+z)-\theta(x)]\,dx\Bigr)\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{\mathrm{c}}}\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s-,x)\,[\theta(x+z)-\theta(x)]\,dx\Bigr)\mu(ds,dz)

(C.1)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s,x)\,[\theta(x+z)-\theta(x)-\sum_{i=1}^{d}z_{i}D_{i}\theta(x)]\,dx\Bigr)\,\nu(dz)\,ds.

The
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. means that our
\mathbb{P}
-full set depends on
t\in[0,\infty)
and
\theta
.

Theorem C.2
.

Assume Hypothesis
1

if
\alpha\in[1,2)
or assume Hypothesis
3

if
\alpha\in(0,1)
. Assume that
\beta\in(0,1)
satisfies condition (
2.25
) and

b\in C_{\mathrm{b}}^{\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Then for every
u_{0}\in C_{\mathrm{b}}^{1}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)
, there exists a unique classical
C^{1}
-solution for the transport equation (
1.1
) of the form

u(\omega,t,x)=u_{0}(\phi_{t}^{-1}(\omega)x),\;\;t\in[0,T],x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},

with probability one, in the sense of Definition (
C.1
).

Proof.

Step 1 (Existence). We will prove that
u_{0}(\phi_{t}^{-1}(\omega)x),t\geq 0,x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
is a
C^{1}
-solution to the problem (
1.1
), that is,
u_{0}(\phi_{t}^{-1}(\omega)x),t\geq 0,x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
satisfies all conditions in Definition (
C.1
).

Recall from Theorem
3.23
both
\phi_{t}
and its inverse
\phi^{-1}_{t}
are diffeomorphisms of
C^{1}
class, and for any
R>0
,
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sup_{x\in B(R)}\|D\phi^{-1}_{t}(x)\|<\infty

\mathbb{P}
-a.s.. Since
u_{0}\in C_{\mathrm{b}}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, the composition
u_{0}\circ\phi^{-1}_{t}\in C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
t\geq 0
,
D\Big(u_{0}(\phi^{-1}_{t}(x))\Big)=Du_{0}(\phi^{-1}_{t}(x))D\phi^{-1}_{t}(x)
. Hence Conditions (i) and (ii) in Definition
C.1
is satisfied. For
\theta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, using (
5.33
) and the properties of the processes
\phi_{t}(y)
and
J_{t}(y)
proved in Theorems
3.5
and
3.23
, it follows straightforwardly that Condition (iii) in Definition
C.1
is satisfied.

Now let us verify Condition (iv). Recall
b^{\varepsilon}
defined in (
5.37
) and the set
\Upsilon
introduced prior to Lemma
5.14
.
Then
b^{\varepsilon}
,
\varepsilon\in\Upsilon
, is a
C_{\mathrm{b}}^{\infty}
approximation of the vector field
b
. Let
\phi^{\varepsilon}_{s,t}
be the stochastic flow associated with equation (
2.34
) with the drift
b
replaced by
b^{\varepsilon}
. Then following the arguments in the proof of Theorem
5.10
, for every
\omega\in\Omega^{(1)}
with
\mathbb{P}(\Omega^{(1)})=1
, and all
t\geq 0
,
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\varepsilon\in\Upsilon
, we have, see (
5.56
),

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(y)\,\theta(\phi^{\varepsilon}_{t}(y))J_{t}^{\varepsilon}(y)\,dy

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(y)\,\theta(y)\,dy+\int_{0}^{t}\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(y)\big[b^{\varepsilon}(\phi^{\varepsilon}_{s}(y))\cdot D\theta(\phi^{\varepsilon}_{s}(y))+\theta(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y))\operatorname{div}b^{\varepsilon}(\phi^{\varepsilon}_{s}(y))]J_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y)\,dy\Bigr)ds

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(y)\big[\theta(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y)+z)-\theta(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y))-\sum_{i=1}^{d}z_{i}D_{i}\theta(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y))\big]J_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y)dy\;\nu(dz)\,ds

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(y)\big[\theta(\phi_{s-}^{\varepsilon}(y)+z)-\theta(\phi_{s-}^{\varepsilon}(y))\big]J_{s-}^{\varepsilon}(y)dy\;\mu(ds,dz)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(y)\big[\theta(\phi_{s-}^{\varepsilon}(y)+z)-\theta(\phi_{s-}^{\varepsilon}(y))\big]J_{s-}^{\varepsilon}(y)dy\;\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz).

Since
u_{0}\in C_{\mathrm{b}}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, by changing variable
y=(\phi_{t}^{\varepsilon})^{-1}(x)
or
x=\phi_{t}^{\varepsilon}(y)
, and integration by parts, we infer

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(y)\big[b^{\varepsilon}(\phi^{\varepsilon}_{s}(y))\cdot D\theta(\phi^{\varepsilon}_{s}(y))+\theta(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y))\operatorname{div}b^{\varepsilon}(\phi^{\varepsilon}_{s}(y))\big]J_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y)\,dy\Bigr)ds

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(y)b^{\varepsilon}(\phi^{\varepsilon}_{s}(y))\cdot D\theta(\phi^{\varepsilon}_{s}(y))J_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y)\,dy\Bigr)ds+\int_{0}^{t}\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}((\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon})^{-1}(x))\theta(x)\operatorname{div}b^{\varepsilon}(x)\,dx\Bigr)ds

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(y)b^{\varepsilon}(\phi^{\varepsilon}_{s}(y))\cdot D\theta(\phi^{\varepsilon}_{s}(y))J_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y)\,dy\Bigr)ds-\int_{0}^{t}\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}((\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon})^{-1}(x))D\theta(x)\cdot b^{\varepsilon}(x)\,dx\Bigr)ds

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{0}^{t}\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}Du_{0}((\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon})^{-1}(x))D(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon})^{-1}(x)\cdot b^{\varepsilon}(x)\theta(x)\,dx\Bigr)ds

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{t}\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}Du_{0}(y)\cdot b^{\varepsilon}(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y))\theta(\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y))J_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y)\,dy\Bigr)ds.

Applying similar arguments as in (
5.18
), we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}

\displaystyle\Bigl|\int_{0}^{t}\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}Du_{0}(y)\cdot b^{\varepsilon}(\phi^{\varepsilon}_{s}(y))\theta(\phi^{\varepsilon}_{s}(y))J_{s}^{\varepsilon}(y)\,dy\Bigr)\,ds

(C.2)

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{t}\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}Du_{0}(y)\cdot b(\phi_{s}(y))\theta(\phi_{s}(y))J_{s}(y)\,dy\Bigr)\,ds\Bigr|\rightarrow 0,

as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.
By combining this with Lemmata
5.16
,
5.20
,
5.21
and
5.22
, taking the limit, and then by changing variable
y=(\phi_{t})^{-1}(x)
or
x=\phi_{t}(y)
, we derive the identity (
C.1
).

Step 2 (Uniqueness). Assume that
u_{0}=0
. Following similar arguments as in Step 1 and 2 in the proof of Theorem
6.16
, see (
6.94
), for
\rho\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we have

\mathbb{P}
-a.s.

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(t,\phi_{t}(x))\rho(x)dx

\displaystyle=-\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon}\bigl[b,u_{s}\bigr](\phi_{s}(x))\Big)\rho(x)dx\Big)ds

(C.3)

\displaystyle=-\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon}[b,u_{s}](y)\rho\left(\phi_{s}^{-1}(y)\right)J\phi_{s}^{-1}(y)dy\Big)ds.

(C.4)

We will prove that the above limit equals 0. First, we provide some preliminary results.

Similar as in the proof of Theorem
6.16
, assume now
\rho\in C_{r}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
for some
r>0
. By (ii) of Theorem
3.5
,
we have

R=\sup_{s\in[0,T],\,x\in B(r)}|\phi_{s}(x)|\leq r+T\|b\|_{0}+\sup_{s\in[0,T]}|L_{s}|<\infty,\quad\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.}

(C.5)

In view of Theorem
3.23
, Theorem
4.1
and Remark
4.4
, we get

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[0,T],\,x\in B(R)}\left|J\phi_{s}^{-1}(x)\right|<\infty,\quad\sup_{s\in[0,T],\,x\in B(R)}\|\phi_{s}^{-1}(x)\|<\infty,\quad\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.}

(C.6)

Put
\theta_{s}(x)=\rho\left(\phi_{s}^{-1}(x)\right)J\phi_{s}^{-1}(x)
.
It follows that

\displaystyle\|\theta_{\cdot}(\omega,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T]\times B(R))}<\infty,\quad\text{for }\mathbb{P}\text{-a.e.}\;\omega\in\Omega.

Observe that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon}\left[b,u_{s}\right](y)\theta_{s}(y)dy\right)ds=\int_{0}^{t}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-z)\left[b(z)-b(y)\right]\theta_{s}(y)dy\Big)\cdot Du_{s}(z)dz\right)ds.

Recall the definition of
\vartheta_{\varepsilon}
in (
5.36
) and note that
\rho\left(\phi_{s}^{-1}(\cdot)\right)\in C_{R}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
for any
s\in[0,T]

\mathbb{P}
-a.s.. Then,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-z)|\|b(z)-b(y)\||\theta_{s}(y)|dy\Big)\|Du_{s}(z)\|dz\right)ds

\displaystyle\leq\|\theta_{\cdot}(\omega,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T]\times B(R))}\int_{0}^{T}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Big(\int_{B(R)}|\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-z)|\|b(z)-b(y)\|dy\Big)\|Du_{s}(z)\|dz\right)ds

\displaystyle=\|\theta_{\cdot}(\omega,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T]\times B(R))}\int_{0}^{T}\left(\int_{B(R+2\varepsilon)}\Big(\int_{B(R)}|\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-z)|\|b(z)-b(y)\|dy\Big)\|Du_{s}(z)\|dz\right)ds

\displaystyle\leq T\|\theta_{\cdot}(\omega,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T]\times B(R))}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|Du_{s}(z)\|_{L^{\infty}(B(R+2\varepsilon))}{[b]_{\beta}}\int_{B(R+2\varepsilon)}\Big(\int_{B(R)}|\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-z)||y-z|^{\beta}dy\Big)dz

\displaystyle=T\|\theta_{\cdot}(\omega,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T]\times B(R))}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\!\!\|Du_{s}(z)\|_{L^{\infty}(B(R+2\varepsilon))}{[b]_{\beta}}\!\!\int_{B(R+2\varepsilon)}\!\!\Big(\int_{B(R)\cap B(z,2\varepsilon)}\!\!\!|\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(y-z)||y-z|^{\beta}dy\Big)dz

\displaystyle\leq(2\varepsilon)^{\beta}T\|\theta_{\cdot}(\omega,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T]\times B(R))}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|Du_{s}(z)\|_{L^{\infty}(B(R+2\varepsilon))}{[b]_{\beta}}\int_{B(R+2\varepsilon)}1dz\rightarrow 0

as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
.

Hence we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon}[b,u_{s}](y)\rho\left(\phi_{s}^{-1}(y)\right)J\phi_{s}^{-1}(y)dy\Big)ds=0,

(C.7)

which proves
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(t,\phi_{t}(x))\rho(x)dx=0
. The proof is now complete.
∎

Appendix D
The perturbative equation

The next result has been proved in Appendix B of
[
39
]

in the case of the transport equation driven by a Brownian motion.
The proof in our jump case is more delicate. We believe that the result is of independent interest. It shows that the definition of weak
∗
-
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}
-solution can be formulated equivalently without using the Marcus integration form. This result is in the spirit of rough path theory (for recent developments of rough path theory for SDEs driven by Lévy processes see
[
55
]
and the references therein).

Theorem D.1
.

Let
b\in L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d}),\operatorname{div}b\in L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
u_{0}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. A measurable process
u\in L^{\infty}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\Omega)
is a weak
∗
-
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}
-solution of the SPDE (
1.1
) (in the sense of Definition
5.8
) if and only if,
there exists
M>0
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|u(t,\cdot,\omega)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq M,\quad\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.},

(D.1)

for every
\theta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
u_{t}(\theta):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\theta(x)u(t,x)dx
is
\mathbb{F}
-adapted and it has càdlàg paths
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. and the perturbative equation

\displaystyle u_{t}(\theta)=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\theta(x+L_{t})u_{0}(x)dx

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Big[b(x)\cdot D\theta(x+L_{t}-L_{r})+\operatorname{div}b(x)\theta(x+L_{t}-L_{r})\Big]u(r,x)dx\,dr

(D.2)

holds almost surely in
\omega\in\Omega
, for every
t\in[0,T]
.

Remark D.2
.

Arguing as in Lemma
5.3
one can show that there exists a
\mathbb{P}
-full set
\Omega^{\prime}\subset\Omega
such that for every
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime}
,
for every

\theta\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
the solution of the perturbative equation (
D.2
) verifies:

u_{t}(\theta)(\omega):[0,T]\ni t\mapsto\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\theta(x)u(t,x,\omega)\,dx

(D.3)

is càdlàg and the corresponding process is adapted.

It follows, using also the dominated convergence theorem, that for every
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime}
,
for any
\theta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, the mapping:

(r,y)\mapsto\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\theta(x+y)u_{0}(x)dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Big[b(x)\cdot D\theta(x+y)+\operatorname{div}b(x)\theta(x+y)\Big]u(r,x)dx

is càdlàg in
r\in[0,T]
for any fixed
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and continuous in
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
for any fixed
r\in[0,T]
. Hence it is easy to check that formula (
D.2
) is meaningful.

Proof.

Definition
\eqref{per-equ-app}\Longrightarrow
Definition
5.8
.

Let us fix
\theta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).
Taking into account Remark
D.2
,
define the function
F:\Omega\times[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R},

\displaystyle F(t,y)=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\theta(x+y)u_{0}(x)dx

(D.4)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Big[b(x)\cdot D\theta(x+y-L_{r})+\operatorname{div}b(x)\theta(x+y-L_{r})\Big]u(r,x)dx\,dr.

(D.5)

Clearly,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s., for any
t\in[0,T]
,
F(t,y)
is smooth in
y
and, for any
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d},

F(\cdot,y)
has no jumps.

Assume that
u_{t}(\theta)
satisfies the perturbation equation (
D.2
).
Then,
\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s},
for every
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle u_{t}(\theta)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\theta(x)u(t,x)dx=F(t,L_{t}).

(D.6)

Moreover, for any
t\in(0,T]
, using also the dominated convergence theorem

\displaystyle F(t,L_{t-})=\lim_{s\to t-}F(t,L_{s})=\lim_{s\to t-}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\theta(x)u(s,x)dx=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\theta(x)u(t-,x)dx.

(D.7)

In order to clarify the term
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\theta(x)u(t-,x)dx
, see the discussion around formula (
5.11
) in Remark
5.4
.

To proceed, we need to apply the Itô-Wentzell formula, see Theorem
A.1
) in a special case since

\displaystyle F(t,y)=F_{0}(y)+\int_{0}^{t}f_{r}(y)dr,

where
F_{0}(y)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\theta(x+y)u_{0}(x)dx
and
f_{r}(y)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Big[b(x)\cdot D\theta(x+y-L_{r})+\operatorname{div}b(x)\theta(x+y-L_{r})\Big]u(r,x)dx
.

Let us check the assumptions of the Itô-Wentzell formula.
First it is straightforward to check that
f:\Omega\times[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
and
F
are both

\mathscr{O}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
-measurable.
Moreover, it is clear that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for all
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\int_{0}^{T}|f_{r}(y)|dr<\infty.

Hence the assumptions of Hypothesis
4
hold. Let us check the other assumptions in Theorem
A.1
.
We deduce easily that

\mathbb{P}
-a.s.
F\in C([0,T];{C}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}))
.

We also have by the dominated convergence theorem,
for
\mathbb{P}\otimes\mathrm{Leb}
-almost-all
(\omega,r)\in\Omega\times[0,T]
, the function

\mathbb{R}^{d}\ni y\mapsto f_{r}(y)\in\mathbb{R}

is continuous. It remains to prove that
for every
T>0
and each compact subset
K
of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.,

\int_{0}^{T}\sup_{y\in K}|f_{r}(y)|dr<\infty.

(D.8)

Recall that

\displaystyle\sup_{r\in[0,T]}\|u(r,\omega,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq M,\quad\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.}

(D.9)

Let us fix
K
and consider
Supp(\theta)
. For each
\omega
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s,
we know that the image of
r\in[0,T]\mapsto L_{r}(\omega)
belongs to a compact set
N^{T}_{\omega}
of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

Therefore, defining the compact set
H^{T}_{\omega}=Supp(\theta)-K+N^{T}_{\omega}
we have, for any
y\in K
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Big[b(x)\cdot D\theta(x+y-L_{r})+\operatorname{div}b(x)\theta(x+y-L_{r})\Big]u(r,x)dx

\displaystyle=\int_{H^{T}_{\omega}}\Big[b(x)\cdot D\theta(x+y-L_{r})+\operatorname{div}b(x)\theta(x+y-L_{r})\Big]u(r,x)dx.

We find,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\sup_{y\in{K}}|f_{r}(y)|dr\leq TM\|\theta\|_{1}\int_{H^{T}_{\omega}}(|b(x)|+|\operatorname{div}b(x)|)dx<\infty.

In view of the Itô-Wentzell formula (Theorem
A.1
) and
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.
F\in C([0,T];{C}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}))
, we infer,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s., for any
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle F(t,L_{t})=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\theta(x)u_{0}(x)dx+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Big[b(x)\cdot D\theta(x+L_{r}-L_{r})+\operatorname{div}b(x)\theta(x+L_{r}-L_{r})\Big]u(r,x)dx\,dr

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}DF(s,L_{s-})dL_{s}+\sum_{s\leq t}\Big[F(s,L_{s})-F(s,L_{s-})-\sum_{i=1}^{d}D_{i}F(s,L_{s-})\Delta L^{i}_{s}\Big].

Hence

\displaystyle F(t,L_{t})=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\!\!\!\!\theta(x)u_{0}(x)dx+\int_{0}^{t}\!\!\!\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\!\!\!\!\Big[b(x)\cdot D\theta(x)+\operatorname{div}b(x)\theta(x)\Big]u(r,x)dx\,dr

(D.10)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}DF(s,L_{s-})\cdot z\,\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}DF(s,L_{s-})\cdot z\,\mu(ds,dz)

(D.11)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Big[F(s,L_{s-}+z)-F(s,L_{s-})-DF(s,L_{s-})\cdot z\Big]\mu(ds,dz)

(D.12)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}\Big[F(s,L_{s-}+z)-F(s,L_{s-})-DF(s,L_{s-})\cdot z\Big]\mu(ds,dz).

(D.13)

Now, we will prove that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Big[F(s,L_{s-}+z)-F(s,L_{s-})-DF(s,L_{s-})\cdot z\Big]\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)

(D.14)

and

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Big[F(s,L_{s}+z)-F(s,L_{s})-DF(s,L_{s})\cdot z\Big]\nu(dz)ds.

(D.15)

are well-defined.

Observe that, differentiating (
D.4
) and similar to (
D.6
), we get

\displaystyle D_{i}F(s,L_{s})

\displaystyle=D_{i}F(s,y)|_{y=L_{s}}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}D_{i}\theta(x)u(s,x)dx.

(D.16)

Moreover (cf. (
D.7
))
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for all
z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
s\geq 0
,

\displaystyle F(s,L_{s-}+z)-F(s,L_{s-})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\theta(x+z)u(s-,x)dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\theta(x)u(s-,x)dx.

(D.17)

Applying the Taylor expansion formula yields for some
\rho\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\big|F(s,L_{s-}+z)-F(s,L_{s-})-DF(s,L_{s-})\cdot z\big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{d}D_{i}F(s,L_{s-}+\rho z)z_{i}\Big|+\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{d}D_{i}F(s,L_{s-})z_{i}\Big|,

\displaystyle\big|F(s,L_{s-}+z)-F(s,L_{s-})-DF(s,L_{s-})\cdot z\big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\Big|\sum_{i,j=1}^{d}D_{i,j}F(s,L_{s-}+\rho z)z_{i}z_{j}\Big|,

where

\displaystyle D_{i}F(s,y)=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}D_{i}\theta(x+y)u_{0}(x)\,dx

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Big[\sum_{k=1}^{d}b_{k}(x)D_{ik}\theta(x+y-L_{r})+\operatorname{div}b(x)D_{i}\theta(x+y-L_{r})\Big]u(r,x)\,dx\,dr,

\displaystyle D_{i,j}F(s,y)=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}D_{ij}\theta(x+y)u_{0}(x)\,dx

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Big[\sum_{k=1}^{d}b_{k}(x)D_{ijk}\theta(x+y-L_{r})+\operatorname{div}b(x)D_{ij}\theta(x+y-L_{r})\Big]u(r,x)\,dx\,dr.

Notice that the image of
[0,T]\ni s\mapsto L_{s-}(\omega)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
belongs to a compact set
K^{T}_{\omega}
of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Define a compact set
J_{\omega}=\textrm{supp }(\theta)-K^{T}_{\omega}-B+N^{T}_{\omega}
.
It follows that,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s., for every
s\in[0,T]
,
z\in B
,

\displaystyle\Big|D_{i}F(s,L_{s-}+\rho z)\Big|\leq M\|\theta\|_{1}\mathrm{Leb}(\textrm{supp }(\theta))+MT\|\theta\|_{2}\int_{J_{\omega}}(|b(x)|+|\operatorname{div}b(x)|)dx<\infty,

\displaystyle\Big|D_{i,j}F(s,L_{s-}+\rho z)\Big|\leq M\|\theta\|_{2}\mathrm{Leb}(\textrm{supp }(\theta))+MT\|\theta\|_{3}\int_{J_{\omega}}(|b(x)|+|\operatorname{div}b(x)|)dx<\infty.

Hence for any
z\in B
and
s\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\big|F(s,L_{s-}+z)-F(s,L_{s-})-DF(s,L_{s-})\cdot z\big|\leq C_{1}|z|^{2},

\displaystyle\big|F(s,L_{s-}+z)-F(s,L_{s-})-DF(s,L_{s-})\cdot z\big|\leq C_{2}|z|.

where random variables
C_{1},C_{2}<\infty

\mathbb{P}
-a.s. depend on
M
and
\|\theta\|_{3}
.
Then we have,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\!\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\big|F(s,L_{s-}+z)-F(s,L_{s-})-DF(s,L_{s-})\cdot z\big|\nu(dz)ds\leq C_{1}T\;\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{2}\nu(dz)<\infty,

(D.18)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\big|F(s,L_{s-}+z)-F(s,L_{s-})-DF(s,L_{s-})\cdot z\big|^{2}\nu(dz)ds

(D.19)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\big|F(s,L_{s-}+z)-F(s,L_{s-})-DF(s,L_{s-})\cdot z\big|^{2}\nu(dz)ds\leq C_{2}^{2}T\,\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{2}\nu(dz)<\infty.

Hence, (
D.14
) and (
D.15
) are well-defined.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Big[F(s,L_{s-}+z)-F(s,L_{s-})-DF(s,L_{s-})\cdot z\Big]\mu(ds,dz)

(D.20)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Big[F(s,L_{s-}+z)-F(s,L_{s-})-DF(s,L_{s-})\cdot z\Big]\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)

(D.21)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Big[F(s,L_{s}+z)-F(s,L_{s})-DF(s,L_{s})\cdot z\Big]\nu(dz)ds.

(D.22)

After inserting (
D.20
) to (
D.10
) and performing some cancellations, we get

\displaystyle F(t,L_{t})=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\theta(x)u_{0}(x)dx+\int_{0}^{t}dr\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Big[b(x)\cdot D\theta(x)+\operatorname{div}b(x)\theta(x)\Big]u(r,x)dx

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Big[F(s,L_{s-}+z)-F(s,L_{s-})\Big]\,\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}\Big[F(s,L_{s-}+z)-F(s,L_{s-})\Big]\mu(ds,dz)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Big[F(s,L_{s}+z)-F(s,L_{s})-DF(s,L_{s})\cdot z\Big]\nu(dz)ds.

Combining (
D.6
), (
D.16
) and (
D.17
), the above equality yields that
u
satisfies (
5.26
) in the Definition
5.8
.

Definition
5.8

\Longrightarrow
Definition
(
D.2
)

On the other hand, assume that
u
is a weak
∗
-
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}
solution of the SPDE (
1.1
) in the sense of Definition
5.8
. Given
\theta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, consider the test function
\theta_{y}(x)=\theta(x+y)
.
Then
u
satisfies

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(t,x)\,\theta(x+y)\,dx

(D.23)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(x)\,\theta(x+y)\,dx+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s,x)\,[b(x)\cdot D\theta(x+y)+\operatorname{div}\,b(x)\,\theta(x+y)]\,dx\,ds

(D.24)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s-,x)\,[\theta(x+z+y)-\theta(x+y)]\,dx\Bigr)\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)

(D.25)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{\mathrm{c}}}\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s-,x)\,[\theta(x+z+y)-\theta(x+y)]\,dx\Bigr)\mu(ds,dz)

(D.26)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s,x)\,[\theta(x+z+y)-\theta(x+y)-\sum_{i=1}^{d}z_{i}D_{i}\theta(x+y)]\,dx\Bigr)\,\nu(dz)\,ds.

(D.27)

Next consider the random field
F(t,y):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(t,x)\,\theta(x+y)\,dx
. Note that

DF(t,y)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(t,x)\,D\theta(x+y)\,dx.

Applying similar arguments as in the proof of Lemma
B.1
, we can verify that
F(t,y)
satisfies the assumptions of Itô-Wentzell Theorem
A.1
.

Let
t\in(0,T]
. Now we apply Itô-Wentzell Theorem
A.1
to the function

F(t,y)=F_{0}(y)+\int_{0}^{t}f_{s}(y)ds+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}h_{s}(y,z)\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}h_{s}(y,z)\mu(ds,dz)

and
\xi_{t}=y-L_{t}
,
t\geq 0
,
where

\displaystyle F_{0}(y)=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(x)\,\theta(x+y)\,dx,

\displaystyle f_{s}(y)=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s,x)\,[b(x)\cdot D\theta(x+y)+\operatorname{div}\,b(x)\,\theta(x+y)]\,dx

\displaystyle+\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s,x)\,[\theta(x+z+y)-\theta(x+y)-\sum_{i=1}^{d}z_{i}D_{i}\theta(x+y)]\,dx\Bigr)\,\nu(dz),

\displaystyle h_{s}(y,z)=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s-,x)\,[\theta(x+z+y)-\theta(x+y)]\,dx.

We obtain,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle F(t,\xi_{t})

\displaystyle=F_{0}(\xi_{0})+\int_{0}^{t}f_{s}(\xi_{s})ds+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}h_{s}(\xi_{s-},z)[\mathds{1}_{B\setminus\{0\}}(z)\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)+\mathds{1}_{B^{c}}(z)\mu(ds,dz)]

(D.28)

\displaystyle\quad+{\int_{0}^{t}DF(s-,\xi_{s-})d{\xi_{s}}}+\sum_{s\leq t}\Big(F(s-,\xi_{s})-F(s-,\xi_{s-})-DF(s-,\xi_{s-})\cdot\Delta\xi_{s}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{s\leq t}\Big(\Delta F(s,\xi_{s})-\Delta F(s,\xi_{s-})\Big).

(D.29)

First observe that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}f_{s}(\xi_{s})ds

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s,x)\,[b(x)\cdot D\theta(x+y-L_{s})+\operatorname{div}\,b(x)\,\theta(x+y-L_{s})]\,dx\,ds

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s,x)\,[\theta(x+z+y-L_{s})-\theta(x+y-L_{s})-\sum_{i=1}^{d}z_{i}D_{i}\theta(x+y-L_{s})]\,dx\Bigr)\,\nu(dz)ds

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s,x)\,[b(x)\cdot D\theta(x+y-L_{s})+\operatorname{div}\,b(x)\,\theta(x+y-L_{s})]\,dx\,ds

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s-,x)\,[\theta(x+z+y-L_{s-})-\theta(x+y-L_{s-})-\sum_{i=1}^{d}z_{i}D_{i}\theta(x+y-L_{s-})]\,dx\Bigr)\,\nu(dz)ds

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s,x)\,[b(x)\cdot D\theta(x+y-L_{s})+\operatorname{div}\,b(x)\,\theta(x+y-L_{s})]\,dx\,ds

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s-,x)\,[\theta(x+z+y-L_{s-})-\theta(x+y-L_{s-})-\sum_{i=1}^{d}z_{i}D_{i}\theta(x+y-L_{s-})]\,dx\Bigr)\,\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s-,x)\,[\theta(x+z+y-L_{s-})-\theta(x+y-L_{s-})-\sum_{i=1}^{d}z_{i}D_{i}\theta(x+y-L_{s-})]\,dx\Bigr)\,\mu(ds,dz),

for the second equality we have used the relation: for any
z\in B
and almost all
s\in(0,T]
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s,x)\,[\theta(x+z+y-L_{s})-\theta(x+y-L_{s})-\sum_{i=1}^{d}z_{i}D_{i}\theta(x+y-L_{s})]dx

(D.30)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s,x)\,[\theta(x+z+y-L_{s-})-\theta(x+y-L_{s-})-\sum_{i=1}^{d}z_{i}D_{i}\theta(x+y-L_{s-})]dx

(D.31)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s-,x)\,[\theta(x+z+y-L_{s-})-\theta(x+y-L_{s-})-\sum_{i=1}^{d}z_{i}D_{i}\theta(x+y-L_{s-})]dx,

(D.32)

and the stochastic integrals with respect to the compensated Poisson random measure are well defined because, analogous to equations (
D.18
) and (
D.19
), we have
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s-,x)\,[\theta(x+z+y-L_{s-})-\theta(x+y-L_{s-})-\sum_{i=1}^{d}z_{i}D_{i}\theta(x+y-L_{s-})]\,dx\Big|\nu(dz)ds

\displaystyle\leq M\|\theta\|_{2}\mathrm{Leb}(supp(\theta))T\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{2}\nu(dz)<\infty,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s-,x)\,[\theta(x+z+y-L_{s-})-\theta(x+y-L_{s-})-\sum_{i=1}^{d}z_{i}D_{i}\theta(x+y-L_{s-})]\,dx\Big|^{2}\nu(dz)ds

\displaystyle\leq 4M^{2}\|\theta\|^{2}_{1}\Big(\mathrm{Leb}(supp(\theta))\Big)^{2}T\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}|z|^{2}\nu(dz)<\infty.

For the third term on the right-hand side of (
D.29
), by substituting
h_{s}
and
\xi_{s}
we find

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}h_{s}(\xi_{s-},z)[\mathds{1}_{B\setminus\{0\}}(z)\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)+\mathds{1}_{B^{c}}(z)\mu(ds,dz)]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s-,x)\,[\theta(x+z+y-L_{s-})-\theta(x+y-L_{s-})]\,dx\Bigr)\,\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s-,x)\,[\theta(x+z+y-L_{s-})-\theta(x+y-L_{s-})]\,dx\Bigr)\,\mu(ds,z).

The fourth term can be rewritten as

\displaystyle{\int_{0}^{t}DF(s-,\xi_{s-})d{\xi_{s}}}=

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s-,x)\,D\theta(x+y-L_{s-})\cdot z\,dx\tilde{\mu}(ds,dz)

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s-,x)\,D\theta(x+y-L_{s-})\cdot z\,dx\mu(ds,dz).

Moreover, similar considerations as in the proof of Lemma
B.1
, we can deduce

\displaystyle\sum_{s\leq t}\Big(F(s-,\xi_{s})-F(s-,\xi_{s-})-DF(s-,\xi_{s-})\Delta\xi_{s}\Big)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Big(F(s-,\xi_{s-}-z)-F(s-,\xi_{s-})-DF(s-,\xi_{s-})\cdot(-z)\mu(ds,dz)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}\Big(F(s-,\xi_{s-}-z)-F(s-,\xi_{s-})-DF(s-,\xi_{s-})\cdot(-z)\mu(ds,dz)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s-,x)\,\big(\theta(x-z+y-L_{s-})-\theta(x+y-L_{s-})+D\theta(x+y-L_{s-})\cdot z\big)dx\Bigr)\mu(ds,dz)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s-,x)\,\big(\theta(x-z+y-L_{s-})-\theta(x+y-L_{s-})+D\theta(x+y-L_{s-})\cdot z\big)dx\Bigr)\mu(ds,dz).

Furthermore, we have,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\sum_{s\leq t}\Big(\Delta F(s,\xi_{s})-\Delta F(s,\xi_{s-})\Big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\leq t}\mathds{1}_{\{0<|\Delta L_{s}|\leq 1\}}\,[h_{s}(\xi_{s-}-\Delta L_{s},\Delta L_{s})-h_{s}(\xi_{s-},\Delta L_{s})]

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{s\leq t}\mathds{1}_{\{|\Delta L_{s}|>1\}}\,[h_{s}(\xi_{s-}-\Delta L_{s},\Delta L_{s})-h_{s}(\xi_{s-},\Delta L_{s})]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}[h_{s}(\xi_{s-}-z,z)-h_{s}(\xi_{s-},z)]\,\mu(ds,dz)+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}[h_{s}(\xi_{s-}-z,z)-h_{s}(\xi_{s-},z)]\mu(ds,dz)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s-,x)\,[\theta(x+\xi_{s-})-\theta(x+\xi_{s-}-z)-\theta(x+z+\xi_{s-})+\theta(x+\xi_{s-})]\,dx\Big]\,\mu(ds,dz)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s-,x)\,[\theta(x+\xi_{s-})-\theta(x+\xi_{s-}-z)-\theta(x+z+\xi_{s-})+\theta(x+\xi_{s-})]\,dx\Big]\,\mu(ds,dz)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\times$}}\kern-7.08333pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}\kern-5.20834pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.4358pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}\kern-4.28824pt}}\!\int_{B}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s-,x)\,[\theta(x+y-L_{s-})-\theta(x+y-L_{s-}-z)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad-\theta(x+z+y-L_{s-})+\theta(x+y-L_{s-})]\,dx\Big]\,\mu(ds,dz)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B^{c}}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s-,x)\,[\theta(x+y-L_{s-})-\theta(x+y-L_{s-}-z)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad-\theta(x+z+y-L_{s-})+\theta(x+y-L_{s-})]\,dx\Big]\,\mu(ds,dz).

Substituting the above calculations into (
D.29
) and simplifying, we deduce that

\displaystyle F(t,y-L_{t})=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(x)\,\theta(x+y)\,dx

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}ds\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(s,x)\,[b(x)\cdot D\theta(x+y-L_{s})+\operatorname{div}\,b(x)\,\theta(x+y-L_{s})]\,dx.

Since both sides are smooth functions of
y

\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for any
t\in[0,T]
, setting
y=L_{t}
and substituting the definition of
F
yields the perturbation equation (
D.2
). The proof has now been completed.

∎

Appendix E
Proofs of some lemmata from Section
5

Proof of Lemma
5.66
.

By possibly employing the Heine diagonal procedure we can fix
T\in\mathbb{N}
and consider
t\in[0,T]
.
By Corollary
3.9
and Theorem
3.8
applied to vector fields
b
and
b^{\varepsilon_{n}}
, there exists an almost sure event
\Omega^{\prime\prime}
such that
for every
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime\prime}
there exists
C_{T}(\omega)>0
such that for every
n\in\overline{\mathbb{N}}:=\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
, the following inequality holds

|(\phi^{\varepsilon_{n}}_{s})^{-1}y-y|\leq s\|b^{\varepsilon_{n}}\|_{0}+C_{T}(\omega),\;\;y\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\;0\leq s\leq T.

(E.1)

Recall that
K:=\textrm{supp }\theta
. By the above, for very
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime\prime}
there exists a compact set
K_{0}(\omega)\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that

\bigcup_{n\in\overline{\mathbb{N}},s\in[0,T]}(\phi^{\varepsilon_{n}}_{s})^{-1}(K)\subset K_{0}(\omega).

Let us now fix
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime\prime}
. Then we have

\displaystyle|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(y)\,\theta(\phi^{\varepsilon_{n}}_{t}(y))J_{t}^{\varepsilon_{n}}(y)\,dy-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(y)\,\theta(\phi_{t}(y))J_{t}(y)\,dy|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|u_{0}(y)|\,|\theta(\phi^{\varepsilon_{n}}_{t}(y))J_{t}^{\varepsilon_{n}}(y)-\,\theta(\phi_{t}(y))J_{t}(y)|\,dy

\displaystyle=\int_{K_{0}(\omega)}|u_{0}(y)|\,|\theta(\phi^{\varepsilon_{n}}_{t}(y))J_{t}^{\varepsilon_{n}}(y)-\,\theta(\phi_{t}(y))J_{t}(y)|\,dy

\displaystyle\leq\|u_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\int_{K_{0}(\omega)}|\theta(\phi^{\varepsilon_{n}}_{t}(y))J_{t}^{\varepsilon_{n}}(y)-\,\theta(\phi_{t}(y))J_{t}(y)|\,dy.

(E.2)

Moreover,

\displaystyle\int_{K_{0}(\omega)}|\theta(\phi^{\varepsilon_{n}}_{t}(y))J_{t}^{\varepsilon_{n}}(y)-\theta(\phi_{t}(y))J_{t}(y)|\,dy

\displaystyle\leq\int_{K_{0}(\omega)}|\theta(\phi^{\varepsilon_{n}}_{t}(y))|\,|J_{t}^{\varepsilon_{n}}(y)-J_{t}(y)|\,dy+\int_{K_{0}(\omega)}|\theta(\phi^{\varepsilon_{n}}_{t}(y))-\,\theta(\phi_{t}(y))||J_{t}(y)|\,dy

\displaystyle\leq\|\theta\|_{0}\int_{K_{0}(\omega)}|J_{t}^{\varepsilon_{n}}(y)-J_{t}(y)|\,dy+\sup_{y\in K_{0}(\omega)}|J_{t}(y)|\int_{K_{0}(\omega)}|\theta(\phi^{\varepsilon_{n}}_{t}(y))-\theta(\phi_{t}(y))|\,dy.

(E.3)

Now applying Corollary
4.3
, there exists a subsequence of the sequence
\phi^{\varepsilon_{n}}
, still denoted by
\phi^{\varepsilon_{n}}
, and a
\mathbb{P}
-full set
\Omega^{1}\subset\Omega
such that for all
\omega\in\Omega^{1}
,
p\geq 1
and
M>0
,

\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{B_{M}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\,\Bigl[|\phi_{t}^{\varepsilon_{n}}(x)-\phi_{t}(x)|^{p}+\|D\phi_{t}^{\varepsilon_{n}}(x)-D\phi_{t}(x)\|^{p}\Bigr]dx=0,

(E.4)

here
B_{M}=\{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:|y|\leq M\}
.
By Theorem
3.19
, there exists
\mathbb{P}
-full set
\Omega^{2}
such that, for any
\omega\in\Omega^{2}
, and
M>0
,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},|x|\leq M}\|D\phi_{t}(x)\|<\infty.

(E.5)

Since the map
\det:\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}\to\mathbb{R}
is continuous, for any
\omega\in\Omega^{2}
, and
M>0
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},|x|\leq M}|J_{t}(x)|<\infty.

(E.6)

Let us take now an arbitrary
\omega\in\Omega^{(iv)}:=\Omega^{\prime\prime}\cap\Omega^{1}\cap\Omega^{2}
, which is a
\mathbb{P}
-full set. Note that
\theta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and there exists
M(\omega)<\infty
such that
K_{0}(\omega)\subset B_{M(\omega)}
, we infer by (
E.4
) and (
E.6
) that

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sup_{y\in K_{0}(\omega)}|J_{t}(y)|<\infty,

\displaystyle\hskip-17.07182pt\!\lim_{n\to\infty}\!\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\!\int_{K_{0}(\omega)}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!|\theta(\phi^{\varepsilon_{n}}_{t}(y))-\theta(\phi_{t}(y))|\,dy\!\leq\!\lim_{n\to\infty}\!\int_{B_{M(\omega)}}\!\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\theta(\phi^{\varepsilon_{n}}_{t}(y))-\theta(\phi_{t}(y))|\,dy=0,

(E.7)

and

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{K_{0}(\omega)}|J^{\varepsilon_{n}}_{t}(y)-J_{t}(y)|\,dy\leq\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{B_{M(\omega)}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|J^{\varepsilon_{n}}_{t}(y)-J_{t}(y)|\,dy=0.

(E.8)

To obtain the last inequality, we have used the fact again that the map
\det:\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}\to\mathbb{R}
is continuous, and the fact that there exists a constant
C_{d}
such that for any
A,B\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
,
|\det A-\det B|\leq C_{d}(\max\{\|A\|,\|B\|\})^{d-1}\|A-B\|
.
The above three inequalities combined with (
E
) and (
E
) complete the proof of this lemma.
∎

Proof of Lemma
5.18
.

We encounter here the same difficulty as in the proof of Lemma
5.16
.

Recall from the proof of Lemma
5.66
,
\Omega^{(iv)}=\Omega^{\prime\prime}\cap\Omega^{1}\cap\Omega^{2}
, and recall
K_{0}(\omega)\subset B_{M(\omega)}
, for
\omega\in\Omega^{(iv)}
.
Let us now fix
\omega\in\Omega^{(iv)}
. Then, for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\Bigl|\int_{0}^{t}\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(y)b^{\varepsilon_{n}}(\phi^{\varepsilon_{n}}_{s}(y))\cdot D\theta(\phi^{\varepsilon_{n}}_{s}(y))J_{s}^{\varepsilon_{n}}(y)\,dy\Bigr)ds-\int_{0}^{t}\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{0}(y)b(\phi_{s}(y))\cdot D\theta(\phi_{s}(y))J_{s}(y)\,dy\Bigr)ds\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\|u_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K_{0}(\omega)}|b^{\varepsilon_{n}}(\phi^{\varepsilon_{n}}_{s}(y))\cdot D\theta(\phi^{\varepsilon_{n}}_{s}(y))||J_{s}^{\varepsilon_{n}}(y)-J_{s}(y)|\,dy\,ds

\displaystyle\ \ \ +\|u_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K_{0}(\omega)}|b^{\varepsilon_{n}}(\phi^{\varepsilon_{n}}_{s}(y))||D\theta(\phi^{\varepsilon_{n}}_{s}(y))-D\theta(\phi_{s}(y))||J_{s}(y)|\,dy\,ds

\displaystyle\ \ \ +\|u_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K_{0}(\omega)}|b^{\varepsilon_{n}}(\phi^{\varepsilon_{n}}_{s}(y))-b(\phi^{\varepsilon_{n}}_{s}(y))||D\theta(\phi_{s}(y))||J_{s}(y)|\,dyds

\displaystyle\ \ \ +\|u_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K_{0}(\omega)}|b(\phi^{\varepsilon_{n}}_{s}(y))-b(\phi_{s}(y))||D\theta(\phi_{s}(y))||J_{s}(y)|\,dyds

\displaystyle\leq\|u_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|b^{\varepsilon_{n}}\|_{0}\|D\theta\|_{0}T\int_{B_{M(\omega)}}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}|J_{s}^{\varepsilon_{n}}(y)-J_{s}(y)|\,dy

\displaystyle\ \ \ +\|u_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|b^{\varepsilon_{n}}\|_{0}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\sup_{y\in B_{M(\omega)}}|J_{s}(y)|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K_{0}(\omega)}|D\theta(\phi^{\varepsilon_{n}}_{s}(y))-D\theta(\phi_{s}(y))|\,dy\,ds

\displaystyle\ \ \ +\|u_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|D\theta\|_{0}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\sup_{y\in B_{M(\omega)}}|J_{s}(y)|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K_{0}(\omega)}|b^{\varepsilon_{n}}(\phi^{\varepsilon_{n}}_{s}(y))-b(\phi^{\varepsilon_{n}}_{s}(y))|\,dyds

\displaystyle\ \ \ +\|u_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|D\theta\|_{0}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\sup_{y\in B_{M(\omega)}}|J_{s}(y)|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K_{0}(\omega)}|b(\phi^{\varepsilon_{n}}_{s}(y))-b(\phi_{s}(y))|\,dyds.

(E.9)

\lim_{n\to\infty}\|b^{\varepsilon_{n}}-b\|_{0}=0
implies that, for any
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime\prime}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K_{0}(\omega)}|b^{\varepsilon_{n}}(\phi^{\varepsilon_{n}}_{s}(y))-b(\phi^{\varepsilon_{n}}_{s}(y))|\,dyds=0.

(E.10)

Here we have used that for any
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime\prime}
,
K_{0}(\omega)
is a compact subset of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

Since
\theta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
b\in C_{\mathrm{b}}^{\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})
, a
similar argument to that used for the proof of (
E.7
) shows that, for any
\omega\in\Omega^{(iv)}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K_{0}(\omega)}|D\theta(\phi^{\varepsilon_{n}}_{s}(y))-D\theta(\phi_{s}(y))|\,dyds=0.

(E.11)

and

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K_{0}(\omega)}|b(\phi^{\varepsilon_{n}}_{s}(y))-b(\phi_{s}(y))|\,dyds=0.

(E.12)

Note that, by (
E.6
), for any
\omega\in\Omega^{2}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},|x|\leq M(\omega)}|J_{t}(x)|<\infty.

(E.13)

Let us observe that assertions (
E.8
),
(
E
)-(
E.10
), (
E.11
)-(
E.13
) imply (
5.69
), completing the proof of this lemma.

∎

Appendix F
Kolmogorov-Totoki-Cencov-type Theorem

The following result is known.

Lemma F.1
.

If
T>0
, then the map

D_{Skor}([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{d})\ni\omega\mapsto\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\omega(t)|\in\mathbb{R},

(F.1)

where
D_{Skor}([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{d})
is
the space
D([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{d})
of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
-valued càdlàg functions, endowed with the Skorokhod metric,
is measurable.

Proof.

It is enough to apply Lemma 1 and Theorem 12.5 from Section 12 in
[
12
]
.
∎

The following theorem extends the Kolmogorov–Chentsov continuity criterion, see Lemma A.2.37 in
[
11
]
, to a more general setting.

Theorem F.2
.

Assume that
(\Omega,\mathscr{F},\mathbb{P})
is a complete probability space,
X
is an infinite dimensional vector space and let
\rho
and
\|\cdot\|
be a metric and norm on
X
. The space
X
endowed with the metric
\rho
will be denoted by
E
and
the space
X
endowed with the norm
\|\cdot\|
will be denoted by
B
. We assume that
E
is separable,
B
is complete and that the natural embedding

i:B\hookrightarrow E

is continuous. We denote by
\mathscr{B}(E)
the Borel
\sigma
-field on
E
.

Assume that
D\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
is an open subset of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Assume that for every
x\in D
,
K(x)
is an
E
-valued random element, i.e.,
K(x):\Omega\to E
is

\mathscr{F}/\mathscr{B}(E)
-measurable. Assume that
p\in(0,\infty)
and
\delta,\gamma\in(0,1)
,
\delta<\gamma
.

Assume that for all
x,y\in D
, the functions

\displaystyle\Omega\ni\omega

\displaystyle\mapsto\|K(x,\omega)-K(y,\omega)\|\in[0,\infty),

(F.2)

\displaystyle\Omega\ni\omega

\displaystyle\mapsto\|K(x,\omega)\|\in[0,\infty)

are
\mathscr{F}/\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{R})
-measurable.
Assume that there exists
C>0
such that for all
x,y\in D
,

\mathbb{E}\|K(x)-K(y)\|^{p}\leq C\left[|x-y|^{d+\delta}+|x-y|^{d+\gamma}\right].

(F.3)

Then, there exists a family
K^{\prime}(x)
,
x\in D
, of
E
-valued random elements, such that

K^{\prime}
is a modification of
K
, i.e., for every
x\in D
,

\mathbb{P}(K(x)\not=K^{\prime}(x))=0;

for every
\omega\in\Omega
, the map

D\ni x\mapsto K^{\prime}(x,\omega)\in B

is continuous.
Moreover, the assertion (ii) can be strengthened as follows. For any
\beta<\frac{\delta}{p}
and every
R>0
, the map

D\cap B(0,R)\ni x\mapsto K^{\prime}(x,\omega)\in B

where
B(0,R)
is the closed ball of radius
R
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
is
\beta
-Hölder continuous (and hence bounded).

Remark F.3
.

The idea of the proof draws upon
[
61
, Theorem 4.1]
and
[
11
, Lemma A.2.37]
, but both results require substantial adjustments to fit current framework.

Proof.

We will prove the case that there exists
M\in\mathbb{N}:=\{1,2,3,\cdots\}
such that
D=[0,M]^{d}
. For the general case, please see
[
11
]
.

In order to prove the theorem, we will introduce a modulus of continuity of a map from
D
to
B
. Let
\Pi_{n}
be the set of all lattice points in
D
of the form
(i_{1}M/2^{n},\dots,i_{d}M/2^{n})
, where
0\leq i_{1},\dots,i_{d}\leq 2^{n}
are integers. We set
\Pi=\cup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\Pi_{n}
. It is a dense subset of
D
. For any
x\in D
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists a unique

x_{n}\in\Pi_{n}\text{ such that }0\leq x^{i}-x_{n}^{i}<2^{-n}M\text{ for all }\,i=1,\dots,d,

and we define
\Pi^{D}_{n}(x)=x_{n}
.

Given a map
f:\Pi\to B
, we define for each
n
a modulus of continuity and the modulus of
\beta
-
\rm H\ddot{o}lder
continuity of
f
by

\Delta_{n}(f)=\max_{x,y\in\Pi_{n},|x-y|=2^{-n}M}\|f(x)-f(y)\|,

\Delta_{n}^{\beta}(f)=2^{\beta n}M^{-\beta}\Delta_{n}(f).

Claim 1: The inequality

\displaystyle\|f(x)-f(y)\|\leq 2d\left(\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\Delta_{n}^{\beta}(f)\right)|x-y|^{\beta},\quad\forall x,y\in\Pi

(F.4)

holds for any map
f:\Pi\to B
.

If
\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\Delta_{n}^{\beta}(f)=\infty
, then the claim is obvious.
In the following, we assume that
\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\Delta_{n}^{\beta}(f)<\infty
.

Define a sequence of simple functions
g_{n}:D\to B,n=1,2,\ldots
by
g_{n}(x)=f(x_{n})
, where
x_{n}\in\Pi_{n}
is the point such that
0\leq x^{i}-x_{n}^{i}<2^{-n}M
holds for any
i=1,\ldots,d
, that is,
x_{n}=\Pi_{n}^{D}(x)
. Then it holds

\|g_{n+1}(x)-g_{n}(x)\|\leq\Delta_{n+1}(f)=2^{-\beta(n+1)}M^{\beta}\Delta_{n+1}^{\beta}(f),\quad\forall x\in D.

Then, for any
x\in D
and
k\in\mathbb{N}
,

\sum_{n=k}^{\infty}\|g_{n+1}(x)-g_{n}(x)\|\leq\sum_{n=k}^{\infty}\Delta_{n+1}(f)\leq 2^{-\beta(k+1)}M^{\beta}\sum_{n=k+1}^{\infty}\Delta_{n}^{\beta}(f)<\infty.

Then the sequence of simple functions
g_{n}(x)
converges on
D
. Let
g(x)
be the limit function. Then
g(x)=f(x)
holds valid for
x\in\Pi
, and for any
x\in D
and
k\in\mathbb{N}
,

\|g(x)-g_{k}(x)\|\leq 2^{-\beta(k+1)}M^{\beta}\sum_{n=k+1}^{\infty}\Delta_{n}^{\beta}(f)<\infty.

(F.5)

In the sequel, we prove this claim for
g
instead of
f
.

Now let
x\neq y
be any points in
D
. Without loss of generality, we assume that
x^{1}>y^{1}
and
x^{1}-y^{1}=\sup_{i=1,2,\cdots,d}|x^{i}-y^{i}|
. Set
z_{1}^{i}=\min\{x^{i},y^{i}\}
and
z_{2}^{i}=\max\{x^{i},y^{i}\}
,
i=1,2,\cdots,d
. Then there exist unique
k\in\mathbb{N}
and just one
j_{1}\in\{0,1,2,\cdots,2^{k}\}
such that

\frac{j_{1}M}{2^{k}}\in(y^{1},x^{1})\text{ and }x^{1}-y^{1}\geq\frac{M}{2^{k}}.

(F.6)

Note that
x^{1}
or
y^{1}
may belong to
\{\frac{jM}{2^{k}};j=0,1,2,\cdots,2^{k}\}
, that is why we consider the open set
(y^{1},x^{1})
.

Since
x^{1}-y^{1}=\sup_{i=1,2,\cdots,d}|x^{i}-y^{i}|
, then, for any
i=1,2,3,\cdots,d
, the number of the elements of the set

\{j\in\{0,1,2,\cdots,2^{k}\}:\frac{jM}{2^{k}}\in(z_{1}^{i},z_{2}^{i})\}
is 0 or 1. Set
H_{i}=\max\{\frac{jM}{2^{k}}<z_{2}^{i}:j\in\{0,1,2,\cdots,2^{k}\}\}
, and
H=(H_{1},\cdots,H_{d})
. Then
H
belongs to
\Pi_{k}
, and we have

|x-H|\leq\frac{dM}{2^{k}}\text{ and }|y-H|\leq\frac{dM}{2^{k}}.

(F.7)

By (
F.6
) and (
F.7
),

1\leq 2^{k}M^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{d}|\Pi_{k}^{D}(x)^{i}-\Pi_{k}^{D}(y)^{i}|\leq 2d,

(F.8)

and

\frac{M}{2^{k}}\leq|x-y|\leq\frac{2dM}{2^{k}}.

(F.9)

Hence, by (
F.5
) and (
F.9
),

\displaystyle\|g(x)-g_{k}(x)\|\leq 2^{-\beta(k+1)}M^{\beta}\sum_{n=k+1}^{\infty}\Delta_{n}^{\beta}(f)\leq 2^{-\beta}\sum_{n=k+1}^{\infty}\Delta_{n}^{\beta}(f)|x-y|^{\beta},

(F.10)

and

\displaystyle\|g(y)-g_{k}(y)\|\leq 2^{-\beta(k+1)}M^{\beta}\sum_{n=k+1}^{\infty}\Delta_{n}^{\beta}(f)\leq 2^{-\beta}\sum_{n=k+1}^{\infty}\Delta_{n}^{\beta}(f)|x-y|^{\beta}.

(F.11)

Further since (
F.8
) and (
F.9
) we have

\displaystyle\|g_{k}(x)-g_{k}(y)\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|g_{k}(\Pi_{k}^{D}(x))-g_{k}(\Pi_{k}^{D}(y))\|

(F.12)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{d}\|g_{k}(\vec{x}^{i-1})-g_{k}(\vec{x}^{i})\|

(F.13)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2d\Delta_{k}(f)=2d\Delta_{k}^{\beta}(f)2^{-\beta k}M^{\beta}\leq 2d\Delta_{k}^{\beta}(f)|x-y|^{\beta}.

(F.14)

Here

\displaystyle\vec{x}^{0}=\Pi_{k}^{D}(x),

(F.15)

\displaystyle\vec{x}^{1}=(\Pi_{k}^{D}(y)^{1},\Pi_{k}^{D}(x)^{2},\cdots,\Pi_{k}^{D}(x)^{d}),

(F.16)

\displaystyle\vec{x}^{2}=(\Pi_{k}^{D}(y)^{1},\Pi_{k}^{D}(y)^{2},\Pi_{k}^{D}(x)^{3},\cdots,\Pi_{k}^{D}(x)^{d}),

(F.17)

\displaystyle\cdots,

(F.18)

\displaystyle\vec{x}^{d}=\Pi_{k}^{D}(y).

(F.19)

Therefore

\displaystyle\|g(x)-g(y)\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|g(x)-g_{k}(x)\|+\|g_{k}(x)-g_{k}(y)\|+\|g_{k}(y)-g(y)\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2d\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\Delta_{k}^{\beta}(f)\right)|x-y|^{\beta}.

The proof of Claim 1 is complete.

By the above claim, the map
f:\Pi\to B
satisfying
\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\Delta_{k}^{\beta}(f)<\infty
for some
\beta>0
is uniformly continuous on
\Pi
and has a continuous extension
g:D\to B
i.e., there exists a continuous map
g:D\to B
such that
g(x)=f(x)
holds for
x\in\Pi
. The function
g
is
\beta
-
\rm H\ddot{o}lder
continuous.

We shall apply the above claim to the random field
K(x)
. Observe that for each
\omega
,
K(\cdot,\omega)
restricting
x
to
\Pi
can be regarded as a map from
\Pi
to
B
. Then we have

\|K(x,\omega)-K(y,\omega)\|\leq 2d\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\Delta_{k}^{\beta}(K(\omega))\right)|x-y|^{\beta},\quad\forall x,y\in\Pi.

(F.20)

Claim 2
Let
\beta
be a positive number satisfying
\beta p<\delta
. Then, there exists
a constant
C_{M,\gamma-\delta,p,\beta,\delta,d}
such that

\Big[\mathbb{E}(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\Delta_{k}^{\beta}(K))^{p}\Big]^{1/p}\leq\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}2^{-k(\delta/p-\beta)}\right)\cdot(2^{d}C_{M,\gamma-\delta,p,\beta,\delta,d})^{1/p}<\infty.

Proof:
We will consider the case
p\geq 1
only. Observe the inequality

(\Delta_{k}^{\beta}(K))^{p}=M^{-\beta p}(\sup_{x,y\in\Pi_{k},|x-y|=M/2^{k}}\|K(x)-K(y)\|{2^{k\beta}})^{p}\leq M^{-\beta p}\sum(\|K(x^{\prime})-K(y^{\prime})\|{2^{k\beta}})^{p},

where the summations are taken over all
x^{\prime},y^{\prime}\in\Pi_{k}
such that
|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|=M/2^{k}
. Then the number of summations are at most
d(2^{k}+1)^{d}<d2^{(k+1)d}
. Therefore

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{k}^{\beta}(K)^{p}]

\displaystyle\leq d2^{(k+1)d+kp\beta}M^{-\beta p}\mathbb{E}[\|K(x^{\prime})-K(y^{\prime})\|^{p}]

(F.21)

\displaystyle\leq d2^{(k+1)d+kp\beta}M^{-\beta p}C_{M,\gamma-\delta}(M/2^{k})^{d+\delta}

(F.22)

\displaystyle\leq C_{M,\gamma-\delta,p,\beta,\delta,d}2^{d}2^{k(p\beta-\delta)}.

(F.23)

Here we have used that by (
F.3
) for any
x,y\in D=[0,M]^{d}

\mathbb{E}\|K(x)-K(y)\|^{p}\leq C_{M,\gamma-\delta}|x-y|^{d+\delta}.

(F.24)

Therefore, by the Minkowski inequality we get

\Big[\mathbb{E}(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\Delta_{k}^{\beta}(K))^{p}\Big]^{1/p}\leq\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}[\mathbb{E}\Delta_{k}^{\beta}(K)^{p}]^{1/p}\leq\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}C^{1/p}_{M,\gamma-\delta,p,\beta,\delta,d}2^{d/p}2^{k(\beta-\delta/p)}<\infty.

The proof of Claim 2 is complete.

Now we are in position to finish the proof of Theorem
F.2
for the case
D=[0,M]^{d}
.

The random field
K(x)
restricting
x
on
\Pi
satisfies the inequality (
F.20
), where
\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\Delta_{k}^{\beta}(K)<\infty
holds a.s. Therefore
K(x),x\in\Pi
is uniformly
\beta
-
\rm H\ddot{o}lder
continuous in
B
a.s.. Then there exists a continuous random field
\tilde{K}(x)
in
E
defined on
D
such that
K(x)=\tilde{K}(x)
holds a.s. for any
x\in\Pi
. Since
K(x)
is continuous in probability, the equality
K(x)=\tilde{K}(x)
holds a.s for any
x\in D
. Thus we have proved the theorem.

∎
